===Chinese===

薄唇泛起一丝讥讽的冷笑。
“没错。我成了那个——呃——被拒且心碎的求婚者。按照惯例，我本该了断性命才合乎常理。但我早已过了演这出苦肉戏的年纪。相反，我依序动身——前往维也纳。然后我赢了。我亲爱的休，这便是恶德的酬劳。”
休倾斜着酒杯，凝视着烛光在深红酒液中的跃动。
“我听闻，”他缓缓说道，“那个被你赢走全部家当的人——是个年轻小伙子，贾斯汀——”
“——而且品行端正无可挑剔。”
“不错。听说那个年轻人——确实自己了断了性命。”
“亲爱的，你的消息有误。他是在一场决斗中中弹身亡的。这便是美德的报偿。我想，这番寓意已足够深刻了吧？”
“随后你便携着万贯家财来到了巴黎。”
“相当丰厚的一笔巨资。我买下了这处宅邸。”
“不错。真不知道你该如何面对自己的灵魂？”
“我根本没有灵魂，休。我还以为你早该心知肚明。”
“当初珍妮弗·博尚（Jennifer Beauchamp）嫁给安东尼·梅里维尔（Anthony Merivale）时，你身上还依稀有着接近灵魂的东西。”
“我有过吗？”贾斯汀兴味盎然地看着他。
休迎上他的目光。
“我倒也很好奇，如今珍妮弗·博尚于你而言究竟算什么？”
贾斯汀举起一只白皙优美的手。
“是珍妮弗·梅里维尔，休。她不过是一场溃败的记忆，一段迷狂岁月的幻影罢了。”
“然而打那以后，你便彻底变了个人。”
贾斯汀站起身来，此刻脸上的冷嘲已极为分明。
“半小时前我便同你说过，亲爱的，我向来竭力顺应你的期许。三年前——确切地说，当我从妹妹范妮那里获悉珍妮弗成婚的消息时——你便以一贯的天真说道，尽管她未曾应允我的求婚，但她重塑了我。仅此而已（Voilà tout）。”
“不。”休若有所思地隔着桌子注视着他，“我当时看走了眼，不过——”
“我亲爱的休，可千万别打破我对你的信任！”
“我虽看走了眼，却也并未错得离谱。我本该说，珍妮弗不过是为另一位能重塑你的女子铺平了道路罢了。”
贾斯汀合上了双眼。
“休，每当你故作高深莫测，便总叫我懊悔昔日为何要将你接纳进我屈指可数的朋友圈中。”
“你的朋友倒真多得很，不是吗？”休涨红了脸反唇相讥道。
“一点不错（Parfaitement）。”贾斯汀走到门前，“哪儿有金钱，哪儿自然少不了——朋友。”
达弗南特放下了酒杯。
“这话是在存心侮辱人吗？”他平缓地说道。
贾斯汀停下脚步，手按在门把上。
“说来奇怪，并非存心。不过你大可以向我提出决斗挑战。”
休忽然笑了起来。
“噢，去睡吧，贾斯汀！你简直不可理喻！”
“这话你早就同我说过不知多少遍了。

Good night, my dear.” He went out, but before he had shut the door bethought himself of something, and looked back, smiling. “Apropos, Hugh, I have got a soul. It has just had a bath, and is now asleep.”
“God help it!” Hugh said gravely.
“I am not sure of my cue. Do I say amen, or retire cursing?” His eyes mocked but the smile in them was not unpleasant. He did not wait for an answer, but shut the door, and went slowly up to bed.

## VI His Grace of Avon Refuses to Sell His Page / 阿翁公爵拒绝转让他的侍童
===Original===

When Davenant met his Grace at breakfast next morning he found that the Duke was in excellent spirits. He was more than usually urbane, and whenever his eye alighted on Léon he smiled, as if at some pleasant thought.
“Was the levée well attended?” asked Hugh, attacking a red sirloin. Unlike the Duke, who never ate more than a roll for breakfast, he made a hearty meal of eggs and bacon, and cold meats, washed down by English ale, especially imported by the Duke for his delectation.
The Duke poured himself out a second cup of coffee.
“Crowded, my dear Hugh. It was in honour of some birthday, or saint’s day, or something of the sort.”
“Did you see Armand?” Hugh reached out his hand for the mustard.
“I saw Armand, and the Comtesse, and the Vicomte, and everybody I least wished to meet.”
“One always does. I suppose La Pompadour was delighted to see you?”
“Oppressively so. The King sat on his throne and smiled benignantly. Just like a coin.”
Hugh suspended his fork in midair.
“Just like a what?”
“A coin. Léon will explain. Or possibly he has forgotten.”
Hugh looked inquiringly at the page.
“What is the joke, Léon? Do you know?”
Léon shook his head.
“No, m’sieur.”
“Ah, I thought perhaps you would not remember,” said his Grace. “Léon was quite satisfied with the King, Hugh. He confided to me that he was just like the coins.”
Léon blushed.
“I﻿—I am afraid I was asleep, Monseigneur.”
“Very nearly so. Do you always sleep as one dead?”
“N-no. That is﻿—I do not know, Monseigneur. I was put to bed in all my clothes.”
“Yes, I did that. Having wasted ten minutes in endeavouring to rouse you, I thought that the simplest plan would be to carry you up to bed. You are not all joy, my infant.”
“I am very sorry, Monseigneur; you should have made me wake up.”
“If you would tell me how that may be done I shall do so on the next occasion. Hugh, if you must eat beef, pray do not brandish it in my face at this hour.”
Davenant, whose fork was still suspended midway between his plate and mouth, laughed, and went on eating.
Justin began to sort the letters that lay beside his plate. Some he threw away, others he slipped into his pocket. One had come from England, and spread over several sheets. He opened them and started to decipher the scrawl.
“From Fanny,” he said. “Rupert is still at large, it seems. At Mistress Carsby’s feet.

===Chinese===

翌日清晨，达弗南特在早餐桌上见到公爵大人时，发现公爵的心情极佳。他比平日里更显温文尔雅，每当目光落在莱昂身上，嘴角总会漾起一丝笑意，仿佛想起了什么惬心乐事。
“朝觐仪式（levée）去的人多吗？”休一边切下一块红润多汁的牛里脊，一边问道。他不像公爵早餐向来只吃一只小面包，而是一顿丰盛的早膳：熏肉煎蛋、冷肉拼盘，再配上一大杯公爵特意为他进口以供享用的英格兰麦芽酒。
公爵为自己斟了第二杯咖啡。
“拥挤不堪，我亲爱的休。好像是为了庆祝哪个生辰、或者圣徒瞻礼日，诸如此类的由头吧。”
“见到阿尔芒了吗？”休伸手去拿芥末酱。
“见到了阿尔芒，还有伯爵夫人，还有子爵，凡是我最不愿碰到的人，全撞见了。”
“向来如此。想必蓬帕杜夫人（La Pompadour）见到你欣喜若狂吧？”
“殷勤得令人喘不过气来。国王高坐在王座上，露出慈祥仁爱的微笑。活像一枚硬币。”
休手中的餐叉停在了半空中。
“活像个什么？”
“一枚硬币。莱昂会给你解释的。或者他可能早就忘了。”
休询问地看向侍童。
“到底是什么笑话，莱昂？你知道吗？”
莱昂摇了摇头。
“不知道，先生。”
“啊，我猜你大概记不得了，”公爵大人说道，“莱昂对国王倒是满意得很，休。他私下跟我咬耳朵说，国王跟硬币上印的一模一样。”
莱昂涨红了脸。
“我——恐怕我当时睡着了，大人。”
“几乎睡死了。你每次睡觉都睡得跟死人一样吗？”
“不——不是。也就是说——我不知道，大人。我是和衣被抱上床的。”
“是啊，是我抱你上去的。花了十分钟愣是没能把你叫醒，我想最省事的法子莫过于直接把你抱上楼去睡觉。我的小家伙，你可真叫人不省心。”
“万分抱歉，大人；您本该狠狠把我弄醒的。”
“要是你肯告诉我到底怎样才能弄醒你，下回我一定照办。休，倘若你非吃牛肉不可，烦请别在这一大清早把肉叉在我眼前挥来晃去的。”
达弗南特手中的叉子仍悬在餐盘与嘴巴之间，闻言大笑起来，接着埋头大嚼。
贾斯汀开始翻拣盘子旁边的信件。有的随手扔在一旁，有的塞进衣袋。有一封从英格兰寄来的信，写了厚厚好几页。他拆开信函，开始辨认那潦草的字迹。
“是范妮寄来的，”他说，“看来鲁珀特依然逍遥法外，拜倒在卡斯比夫人（Mistress Carsby）的石榴裙下。

===Original===

When I saw him last he was madly in love with Julia Falkner. From one extreme to another.” He turned over the page. “Now, how interesting! Dear Edward has given Fanny a chocolate-coloured coach with pale blue cushions. The wheels are picked out in blue.” He held the sheet at arm’s length. “It seems strange, but no doubt Fanny is right. I have not been in England for such a time﻿—Ah, I beg her pardon! You will be relieved to hear, my dear Hugh, that the wheat in England still grows as ever it did. Ballentor has fought another duel, and Fanny won fifty guineas at play the other night. John is in the country because town air does not suit him. Now, is John her lapdog or her parrot?”
“Her son,” said Davenant.
“Is he? Yes, I believe you are right. What next? If I can find her a French cook she vows she will love me more than ever. Léon, tell Walker to find me a French cook.﻿—She wishes she could visit me as I suggested some time ago﻿—how rash of me!﻿—but it is quite impossible as she cannot leave her darling Edward alone, and she fears he would not accompany her to my hovel. Hovel. Not very polite of Fanny. I must remember to speak to her about it.”
“Hôtel,” suggested Hugh.
“Once more you are right. Hôtel it is. The rest of this enthralling communication concerns Fanny’s toilettes. I will reserve it. Oh, have you finished?”
“Finished and gone,” answered Davenant, rising. “I am riding out with D’Anvau. I shall see you later.” He went out.
Avon leaned his arms on the table, resting his chin on the back of his clasped hands.
“Léon, where does your remarkable brother live?”
Léon started, and fell back a pace.
“Mon﻿—Monseigneur?”
“Where is his inn?”
Suddenly Léon fell on his knees beside Avon’s chair, and clutched the Duke’s sleeve with desperate fingers. His face was upturned, pale and agonised, the great eyes swimming in tears.
“Oh no, no, no, Monseigneur! You would not﻿—Oh, please not that! I﻿—I will never go to sleep again! Please, please forgive me! Monseigneur! Monseigneur!”
Avon looked down at him with upraised brows. Léon had pressed his forehead against his master’s arm, and was shaking with suppressed sobs.
“You bewilder me,” complained the Duke. “What is it that I am not to do, and why will you never sleep again?”
“Don’t﻿—don’t give me back to Jean!” implored Léon, clinging tighter still. “Promise, promise!”
Avon loosened the clasp on his sleeve.

上次我见他时，他还疯狂迷恋着朱莉娅·福克纳（Julia Falkner）呢。真是从一个极端跳到另一个极端。”他翻过一页，“噢，这倒有趣！亲爱的爱德华送了范妮一辆巧克力色的马车，配着淡蓝色的坐垫。车轮是用蓝漆描边的。”他把信纸举在手臂一臂远的距离端详着。“听着颇显古怪，但想必范妮的眼光不会错。我离开英格兰实在太久了——啊，我错怪她了！我亲爱的休，听到英格兰的小麦依旧长势良好，想必你心里大为宽慰吧。巴伦托（Ballentor）又跟人决斗了一场，范妮前几天晚上玩牌赢了五十几尼。约翰（John）去乡下了，因为城里的空气不合他的身子。话说回来，这约翰到底是她的哈巴狗还是她的鹦鹉来着？”
“是她的亲儿子，”达弗南特说道。

“Nothing at all alarming, I assure you. Dry your tears.”
Léon hunted through his various pockets.
“I﻿—I have lost my handkerchief,” he apologised.
“Yes, you are very young, are you not?” commented his Grace. “I suppose I must give you mine.”
Léon took the fine lace handkerchief which the Duke held out, wiped his eyes, blew his nose, and gave it back again. The Duke received it gingerly, and eyed the crumpled ball through his quizzing glass.
“Thank you,” he said. “You are nothing if not thorough. I think you had better keep it now.”
Léon pocketed it cheerfully.
“Yes, Monseigneur,” he said. “Now I am happy again.”
“I am relieved,” said the Duke, and rose. “I shall not want you this morning.” He strolled out, and in half an hour’s time was in his coach, driving towards the Rue Sainte-Marie.
The street was very narrow, with refuse in the kennels on either side of the road; the houses were mostly tumbledown, projecting outward from the first storey. Hardly one had all its windows intact; there were cracked and missing panes on all sides, and where curtains hung they were ragged and dirty.

这条街道地处巴黎最破败荒凉的贫民区之一，狭窄泥泞，阴暗肮脏。

===Original===

Half a dozen partly clothed children were playing in the road, and scattered to right and left as the coach drove up, standing on the footway, and watched the progress of this fine equipage with astonished eyes, and many startled comments.
The tavern of the Crossbow was situated midway down the squalid street, and from its open door issued a smell of cooking, and of cabbage water, thrown carelessly out into the kennel. The coach drew up outside the inn, and one of the footmen sprang down to open the door for his Grace to alight. His countenance was quite impassive, and only by the lofty tilt of his chin did he betray his emotions.
His Grace came slowly down from the coach, his handkerchief held to his nose. He picked his way across the filth and garbage to the inn door, and entered what appeared to be the taproom and the kitchen. A greasy woman was bending over the fire at one end, a cooking-pot in her hand, and behind the counter opposite the door stood the man who had sold Léon to the Duke a month ago.
He gaped when he saw Avon enter, and for a moment did not recognise him. He came forward cringingly, rubbing his hands together, and desired to know Monseigneur’s pleasure.
“I think you know me,” said his Grace gently.
Bonnard stared, and suddenly his eyes dilated, and his full-blooded countenance turned a sickly grey.
“Léon! Milor’﻿—I﻿—”
“Precisely. I want two words with you in private.”
The man looked at him fearfully, passing his tongue between his lips.
“I swear by God﻿—”
“Thank you. In private I said.”
The woman, who had watched the encounter open-mouthed, came forward now, arms akimbo. Her soiled dress was in disorder, cut low across her scraggy bosom, and there was a smudge of dirt on her cheek.
“Now, if the little viper has said aught against us,” she began shrilly, but was cut short by Avon’s lifted hand.
“My good woman, I have no desire to speak with you. You may return to your stew-pots. Bonnard, in private!”
Charlotte would have interrupted again, but her husband hustled her back to the stove, whispering to her to hold her tongue.
“Yes, milor’, indeed yes! If milor’ will follow me?” He pushed open the crazy, rat-eaten door at the other end of the room, and ushered his Grace into the parlour. The room was scantily furnished, but it was not so dirty as the taproom.

六七个衣不蔽体的孩童正在路上嬉戏打闹，马车驶来时纷纷向左右四散跑开，站在步道上，用惊愕万分的双眼打量着这辆豪华气派的四轮大马车，嘴里发出阵阵诧异的议论。
十字弓酒馆位于这处污秽街道的半中腰，敞开的门洞里飘出一股油烟气，混合着随手泼在阴沟里的烂菜叶泔水味。马车在客栈门前停稳，一名男仆敏捷地跳下车，为公爵大人拉开车门。公爵神色如常，唯有微微昂起的下巴隐约流露出内心的一丝厌恶。
公爵大人用手帕掩住口鼻，缓缓步下马车。他在污秽与垃圾堆间小心翼翼地择路前行，跨入酒馆大门，来到了这处兼作小酒吧与厨房的逼仄堂间。屋子一头，一个油腻邋遢的妇人正端着一口烹饪锅俯身在炉火旁，而在正对大门的柜台后面，站着的正是整整一个月前把莱昂卖给公爵的那个汉子。
瞧见阿翁走进来，汉子瞠目结舌，愣了片刻才回过神来。他满脸谄媚地搓着双手迎上前来，躬身打听公爵大人有何吩咐。
“我想你认得我，”公爵和悦地说道。
博纳尔死死盯着他，双眼骤然睁大，那张满脸横肉的脸庞瞬间泛起一阵死灰般的惨白。
“莱昂！老爷——我——”
“正是。我有两句话要单独同你谈谈。”
汉子惶恐不安地望着他，舌头紧张地舔着嘴唇。
“我对天发誓——”
“多谢了。我说了，单独谈。”
那个原本张口结舌旁观的妇人此时双手叉腰走了过来。她那身肮脏的衣裳凌乱不堪，干瘪的胸脯前领口开得极低，脸颊上还蹭着一块污黑的烟灰。
“好啊，要是那条小毒蛇在背后说了咱们什么坏话，”她尖声嚷嚷起来，但被阿翁抬起的一只手硬生生截住了话头。
“我的好妇人，我没兴趣同你搭话。你回你的炖锅旁边去吧。博纳尔，单独谈！”
夏洛特（Charlotte）本还想插话，但她丈夫一把将她推回炉灶旁，低声呵斥她闭上嘴巴。
“是，老爷，千真万确！老爷这边请！”他推开堂间另一头那扇破烂不堪、被老鼠啃食得坑坑洼洼的门，引着公爵大人步入一间小会客室。屋内陈设简陋寒酸，但比起外面的小酒吧倒还算干净些。

===Original===

Avon went to the table that stood by the window, flicked the dust from its surface with a corner of his cloak, and sat down on the edge of the rickety structure.
“Now, my friend. That you may not misunderstand me, or seek to evade me, let me tell you that I am the Duke of Avon. Yes, I thought that you would be surprised. You realise, I am sure, that it would be very dangerous to play with me. I am going to ask you one or two questions about my page. I wish to know first where he was born.”
“I﻿—I think in the north, Monseigneur. In﻿—Champagne, but I am not sure. Our﻿—our parents never spoke of that time, and I can scarce remember﻿—I﻿—”
“No? It seems strange that you do not know why your worthy parents went so suddenly to live in Anjou.”
Bonnard looked at him helplessly.
“My﻿—my father told me that he had come into money! Indeed, I know no more, Monseigneur! I would not lie. I swear I would not!”
The fine lips curled sardonically.
“We will pass over that. How comes it that Léon is so unlike you in face and form?”
Bonnard rubbed his forehead. There was no mistaking the perplexity in his eyes.
“I do not know, Monseigneur. I have often wondered. He was ever a weakly child, petted and cosseted when I was made to work on the farm. My mother cared nothing for me beside him. It was all Léon, Léon, Léon! Léon must learn to read and write, but I﻿—the eldest﻿—must tend the pigs! A sickly, pert lad he was ever, Monseigneur! A viper, a﻿—”
Avon tapped the lid of his snuffbox with one very white finger.
“Do not let us misunderstand one another, my friend. There never was a Léon. A Léonie, perhaps. I want that explained.”
The man shrank.
“Ah, Monseigneur! Indeed, indeed I did it for the best! It was impossible to have a girl of that age here, and there was work to be done. It was better to dress her as a boy. My wife﻿—Monseigneur will understand﻿—women are jealous, milor’. She would not have a girl here. Indeed, indeed, if the boy﻿—girl﻿—has said aught against us, he lies! I could have turned him out into the streets, for he had no claim on me. Instead, I kept him, clothed him, fed him, and if he says he was ill-treated it is a lie! He is a wicked brat with a vicious temper. You could not blame me for hiding his sex, Monseigneur! It was for his sake, I swear! He liked it well enough. Never did he demand to be a girl!”
“No doubt he had forgotten,” said Avon dryly.

===Chinese===

阿翁走到窗旁的一张桌子前，用斗篷的一角掸去桌面上的尘土，随后坐在了那张摇摇欲晃的破桌子边缘。
“好了，我的朋友。免得你产生误会，或是妄图推诿搪塞，我且明确告知你，我乃阿翁公爵（Duke of Avon）。没错，我料定你会大吃一惊。我相信你心里清楚，同我耍滑头可是极其危险的。我要就我的侍童问你一两个问题。首先，我想知道他究竟出生在何处。”
“我——我想是在北方，大人。在——香槟省（Champagne），但我吃不大准。我——我们爹娘从不提起那段时候的事，我也记不太清了——我——”
“是吗？你家那两位可敬的父母当年为何如此突然地迁居安茹，你竟然毫不知情，这听来未免有些蹊跷吧。”
博纳尔无助地望着他。
“我——我爹当时同我说，他是发了一笔横财！大人，我知道的真的就只有这些了！我绝不敢撒谎。我对天发誓绝对没有！”
公爵那精致的薄唇勾起一抹讥诮的冷笑。
“这事先搁在一旁。莱昂的长相与身段为何同你截然不同？”
博纳尔揉了揉额头。他眼底流露出的困惑显然不是装出来的。
“我不知道，大人。我也经常犯嘀咕。他打小就身子骨单薄，整天被捧在手心里娇生惯养，而我却得在农场里累死累活干粗活。我娘眼里除了他根本没有我。张嘴闭嘴全是莱昂、莱昂、莱昂！莱昂得读书识字，而我——作为长子——却得去放猪！大人，他向来是个病怏怏又爱顶嘴的家伙！就是个灾星，是个——”
阿翁用一根白皙如玉的手指轻轻叩了叩鼻烟盒的盒盖。
“我的朋友，咱们可别各说各话。这世上根本就没有什么莱昂。兴许，倒是有个莱昂妮（Léonie）。这事我需要你好好解释一番。”
那汉子吓得缩成了一团。
“啊，大人！真的，真的，我全是一片好心啊！这里根本容不下那么大年纪的女孩子，而且家里有一堆活要干。把她扮作男孩子才最稳妥。内人她——大人您能体谅的——女人家爱嫉妒，老爷。她绝不容许家里留着个姑娘。真的，真的，要是那小子——姑娘——在背后说了咱们坏话，全是他血口喷人！他跟我非亲非故，我本可以直接把他扫地出门丢到大街上。相反，我收留了他，给他穿、给他吃，要是他说自己受了虐待，纯属撒谎！他就是个脾气暴虐的恶劣贱种。大人，您绝不能怪我隐瞒他的性别！我对天发誓，这全是为了他好！他自己也乐意得很。他从来没嚷嚷着非要做回姑娘！”
“想必她是早就给忘了吧，”阿翁冷冷地说道。

===Original===

“Seven years a boy﻿ ﻿… Now﻿—” He held up a louis. “Mayhap this will refresh your memory. What do you know of Léon?”
The man looked at him in a puzzled way.
“I﻿—do not understand, Monseigneur. What do I know of him?”
Avon leaned forward slightly, and his voice became menacing.
“It will not serve you to feign ignorance, Bonnard. I am very powerful.”
Bonnard’s knees shook.
“Indeed, Monseigneur, I do not understand! I cannot tell you what I do not know! Is﻿—is aught amiss with Léon?”
“You never thought that he was, perhaps, not your parents’ child?”
Bonnard’s jaw dropped.
“Not﻿—Why, Monseigneur, what do you mean? Not my parents’ child. But﻿—”
Avon sat back.
“Does the name Saint-Vire convey aught to you?”
“Saint-Vire﻿ ﻿… Saint-Vire﻿ ﻿… no. Stay, the name has a familiar ring! But﻿—Saint-Vire﻿—I do not know.” He shook his head hopelessly. “It may be that I have heard my father speak the name, but I cannot remember.”
“A pity. And when your parents died was there no document found belonging to them which concerned Léon?”
“If there was, milor’, I never saw it. There were old accounts and letters﻿—I cannot read, Monseigneur, but I have them all.” He looked at the louis, and licked his lips. “If Monseigneur would care to see for himself? They are here, in that chest.”
Avon nodded.
“Yes. All of them.”
Bonnard went to the chest and opened it. After some search he found a sheaf of papers, which he brought to the Duke. Avon went through them quickly. For the most part they were, as Bonnard had said, farm accounts, with one or two letters amongst them. But at the bottom of the pile was a folded slip of paper, addressed to Jean Bonnard, on the estate of M. le Comte de Saint-Vire, in Champagne. It was only a letter from some friend, or relation, and it held nothing of importance, save the address. The Duke held it up.
“This I will take.” He tossed the louis to Bonnard. “If you have lied to me, or deceived me, you will be sorry. At present I am willing to believe that you know nothing.”
“I have spoken naught but the truth, Monseigneur, I swear!”
“Let us hope that it is so. One thing, however”﻿—he produced another louis﻿—“you can tell me. Where shall I find the Curé at Bassincourt, and what is his name?”
“M. de Beaupré, Monseigneur, but he may be dead now, for aught I know. He was an old man when we left Bassincourt. He used to live in a little house beside the church.

===Chinese===

“当了整整七年的男孩子……那么——”他举起一枚路易金币，“或许这能帮你唤醒记忆。关于莱昂，你究竟知道些什么？”
那汉子满脸困惑地看着他。
“我——我不明白，大人。我知道他什么事？”
阿翁身子微微前倾，语气变得森冷骇人。
“装糊涂对你毫无益处，博纳尔。我的权势有多大，你应当清楚。”
博纳尔的双膝不由得打起战来。
“真的，大人，我实在不明白！我不知道的事，我怎么说得出来啊！莱昂他——他出什么事了吗？”
“你就从来没想过，他或许根本不是你父母亲生的骨肉？”
博纳尔惊得下巴都快掉下来了。
“不是——怎么会，大人，您这话什么意思？不是我爹娘亲生的？可是——”
阿翁靠回了椅背上。
“圣维尔（Saint-Vire）这个名字，你听着可有任何印象？”
“圣维尔……圣维尔……没有。慢着，这名字听着倒耳熟！可是——圣维尔——我想不起来。”他绝望地摇着头，“兴许我听我爹生前提过这名字，可我实在记不清了。”
“真是遗憾。那么令尊令堂过世时，可曾留下过任何与莱昂相关的文书字据？”
“要是真有，老爷，小人也从没见过。倒是有一些旧账本和信件——我不识字，大人，但我全收着呢。”他盯着那枚路易金币，舔了舔干裂的嘴唇，“要是大人不嫌弃，可愿亲自过目？全收在那儿，在那个樟木箱子里。”
阿翁点了点头。
“拿来。全部拿来。”
博纳尔走到箱子前翻找起来。一阵翻检之后，他捧出一叠泛黄的纸页，递到了公爵面前。阿翁飞快地翻阅着。正如博纳尔所说，大多是些农场账目，其间杂夹着一两封信件。然而在最底下，却压着一张折叠的便条，收信人写着：让·博纳尔（Jean Bonnard），香槟省圣维尔伯爵大人庄园。这不过是某位亲友寄来的一封普通家书，除却信封上的地址外并无要紧内容。公爵将便条抽了出来。
“这个我带走了。”他把那枚路易扔给博纳尔，“倘若你敢对我撒谎或有所欺瞒，你会后悔莫及的。眼下我暂且相信你当真毫不知情。”
“大人，小人说的全是大实话，我对天发誓！”
“但愿如此。不过还有一桩事”——他又拿出一枚路易金币——“你定能告诉我。巴桑库尔（Bassincourt）的那位本堂神父叫什么名字，我该去何处寻他？”
“德·博普雷先生（M. de Beaupré），大人，可据我所知，他老人家如今年纪大了，兴许早就过世了。当年我们离开巴桑库尔时他就已经是个风烛残年的老人了。他以前就住在教堂旁边的一栋小房子里。

===Original===

You cannot mistake it.”
Avon threw the louis into his eager hand.
“Very well.” He went to the door. “Be advised by me, my friend, and strive to forget that you ever had a sister. For you had not, and it might be that if you remember a Léonie there would be a reckoning to be paid for your treatment of her. I shall not forget you, I assure you.” He swept out, and through the taproom to his coach.
That afternoon, when Avon sat in the library of his house, writing to his sister, a footman came to him and announced that M. de Faugenac wished to see him.
The Duke raised his head.
“M. de Faugenac? Admit him.”
In a few minutes’ time there entered a tubby little gentleman with whom his Grace was but slightly acquainted. Avon rose as he came in and bowed.
“Monsieur!”
“Monsieur!” De Faugenac returned the bow. “Pardon the unseemly hour of this intrusion, I beg!”
“Not at all,” answered the Duke. “Fetch wine, Jules. Pray be seated, m’sieur.”
“No wine for me, I thank you! The gout you understand. A sad affliction!”
“Very,” agreed his Grace. “Is there something I can do for you, I wonder?”
De Faugenac stretched his hands to the fire.
“Yes, I come on business, m’sieur. Bah, the ugly word! M’sieur will pardon the interruption, I am sure! A splendid fire, Duc!”
Avon bowed. He had seated himself on the arm of a chair, and was looking at his visitor in mild surprise. He drew out his snuffbox and offered it to De Faugenac, who helped himself to a liberal pinch and sneezed violently.
“Exquisite!” he said enthusiastically. “Ah, the business! M’sieur, you will think I come upon a strange errand, but I have a wife!” He beamed at Avon, and nodded several times.
“I felicitate you, m’sieur,” said Avon gravely.
“Yes, yes! A wife! It will explain all.”
“It always does,” answered his Grace.
“Aha, the pleasantry!” De Faugenac broke into delighted laughter. “We know, we husbands, we know!”
“As I am not a husband I may be excused my ignorance. I am sure you are about to enlighten me.” His Grace was becoming bored, for he had remembered that De Faugenac was an impoverished gentleman usually to be found at the heels of Saint-Vire.
“Indeed yes. Yes, indeed. My wife. The explanation! She has seen your page, m’sieur!”
“Wonderful!” said the Duke. “We progress.”
“We﻿—? You said progress? We? Progress?”
“It seems I erred,” Avon sighed. “We remain at the same place.”

===Chinese===

您绝不会认错的。”
阿翁把金币抛进他贪婪的手掌中。
“很好。”他走到门前，“听我一句忠告，我的朋友，从此以后彻底忘掉你曾经有过一个妹妹。因为你根本就没有妹妹；而且倘若你记起了有个莱昂妮，你当年苛虐她的账，届时少不了要连本带利清算一番。我向你保证，我可绝不会忘了你。”他长袍一拂，大步穿过堂间，登上了马车。
当天下午，当阿翁坐在府邸书房内给妹妹提笔写信时，一名男仆走进来禀报，德·福热纳克先生（M. de Faugenac）求见。
公爵抬起头来。
“德·福热纳克先生？请他进来。”
不多时，走进来一位矮胖敦实的绅士，公爵同他素无深交。阿翁在他进门时起身致意。
“先生！”
“公爵大人！”德·福热纳克回礼道，“在如此不合时宜的时辰冒昧登门打扰，万祈恕罪！”
“无妨，”公爵答道，“于勒（Jules），奉酒。先生，请坐。”
“我就不喝酒了，多谢！痛风发作，您懂的。真是一场苦难！”
“确实，”公爵随声附和道，“不知先生此番前来，我有何能效劳之处？”
德·福热纳克把双手凑到壁炉火前烘烤着。
“正是，公爵，我是为一桩买卖而来的。呸，真俗气的词儿！我相信公爵大人定能宽恕我的冒昧打扰！好旺的炉火啊，公爵！”
阿翁微微欠身。他在一张椅子的扶手上坐下，带着几分淡淡的诧异打量着这位来客。他取出一只鼻烟盒递给德·福热纳克，后者毫不客气地抓了一大撮吸了进去，猛烈地打了个大喷嚏。
“极妙！”他热情洋溢地赞叹道，“啊，说正经事！先生，您或许会觉得我此行十分古怪，可我有个妻子！”他冲着阿翁满面春风地连连点头。
“恭喜先生，”阿翁神情自若地说道。
“正是，正是！一个妻子！这便能解释一切了。”
“向来如此，”公爵大人答道。
“哈哈，真幽默！”德·福热纳克欣喜若狂地笑了起来，“咱们这些做丈夫的，心里全门儿清！”
“既然我尚未婚娶，我对此一无所知倒也情有可原。我相信先生马上便会为我指点迷津。”公爵大人渐渐觉得无聊起来，因为他忽然想起来，德·福热纳克是个家境拮据的小贵族，向来像条跟屁虫似地跟在圣维尔屁股后头转悠。
“确实如此。千真万确。想必您早就猜到了吧？”
“我从不胡乱猜测，”阿翁说。
“啊，可您是何等精明睿智的人啊！”
“咱们还是直奔正题吧，”阿翁语气温和得令人感到一丝危险。
“那是自然！那是自然！”德·福热纳克从怀里扯出一方丝帕擦拭着额头的虚汗，“公爵，内人年方二十七岁。那可是个迷人的尤物，不是吗？绝色美人，地地道道的王后！她脑子里冒出了个奇思妙想。她瞧见了您的侍童。”
“瞧见过他的大有人在，”阿翁说道。
“她瞧见了您的侍童，”德·福热纳克重复道，“她还是个孩子心性！一个被宠坏的孩子！她要什么我都百依百顺。她看上了您的侍童。”
“那她就只能继续眼巴巴盼着了，”公爵说。
“啊，可您替她想想啊，先生！他不过是个侍童！您随随便便就能找来十来个侍童，可对我夫人而言——天底下唯独只有这一位！”
“这见解倒发人深省。”
“她茶饭不思！把眼睛都哭肿了！整个人痛不欲生！先生，我出五百法郎买他！”
阿翁啪的一声轻轻合上了鼻烟盒盖。
“他是非卖品，先生。”
“一千法郎！”
“他是非卖品。”
“一千五百法郎！”
阿翁用冷酷嫌恶的目光打量着这个矮胖汉子。
“先生，”他说，“难道你没听见我方才说这孩子是非卖品吗？”
“两千法郎，先生！您好好琢磨琢磨！花两千法郎买一个侍童！”
“不卖。”
“三千法郎！”
“不卖。”
“四千！五千！一万法郎！”
“哪怕你出一万法郎，十万法郎，我也绝不出让，”公爵平缓地说道。

===Original===

De Faugenac was puzzled for a moment, but all at once his face broke into fresh smiles.
“Another pleasantry! Yes, yes, I see!”
“I doubt it,” murmured Avon. “You were saying, m’sieur, that your wife had seen my page.”
De Faugenac clasped his hands to his breast.
“She is ravished! She is envious! She pines!”
“Dear me!”
“She gives me no peace!”
“They never do.”
“Aha! No, never, never! But you do not take my meaning, m’sieur, you do not take my meaning!”
“But then, that is hardly my fault,” said Avon wearily. “We have arrived at the point at which your wife gives you no peace.”
“That is the matter in a nutshell! She eats out her heart for your so lovely, your so enchanting, your so elegant﻿—”
Avon held up his hand.
“M’sieur, my policy has ever been to eschew married women.”
De Faugenac stared.
“But﻿—but﻿—what do you mean, m’sieur? Is it another pleasantry? My wife pines for your page.”
“How very disappointing!”
“Your page, your so elegant page! She plagues me day and night to come to you. And I am here! Behold me!”
“I have beheld you for the past twenty minutes, m’sieur,” said Avon rather tartly.
“She begs me to come to you, to ask you if you would part with your page! She cannot rest until she has him to hold her train for her, to carry her gloves and fan. She cannot sleep at night until she knows that he is hers!”
“It seems that madame is destined to spend many sleepless nights,” said Avon.
“Ah no, m’sieur! Consider! It is said that you bought your page. Now, is it not truly said that what may be bought may be sold?”
“Possibly.”
“Yes, yes! Possibly! M’sieur, I am as a slave to my wife.” He kissed the tips of his fingers. “I am as the dirt beneath her feet.” He clasped his hands. “I must bestow on her all that she desires, or die!”
“Pray make use of my sword,” invited his Grace. “It is in the corner behind you.”
“Ah no! M’sieur cannot mean that he refuses! It is impossible! M’sieur, you may name your own price and I will give it!”
Avon stood up. He picked up a silver handbell, and rang it.
“M’sieur,” he said silkily, “you may bear my compliments to the Comte de Saint-Vire, and tell him that Léon, my page, is not for sale. Jules, the door.”
De Faugenac rose, very crestfallen.
“M’sieur?”
Avon bowed.
“M’sieur. You mistake! You do not understand!”
“Believe me, I understand perfectly.”
“Ah, but you have no soul thus to thwart a lady’s wish!”

===Chinese===

德·福热纳克愣了片刻，随即脸上再度堆满了笑容。
“又在开玩笑了！对，对，我懂了！”
“我深表怀疑，”阿翁低语道，“先生方才提到，贵夫人见过了我的侍童。”
德·福热纳克双手合抱胸前。
“她神魂颠倒！嫉妒得发狂！相思成疾！”
“哎哟！”
“她吵得我永无宁日！”
“妇人家向来如此。”
“啊哈！对，永无宁日！可您没领会我的意思，先生，您压根没明白！”
“既然如此，那也怪不得我，”阿翁厌倦地说道，“咱们方才谈到尊夫人吵得你不得安宁。”
“一句话便能说透！她为了您那位娇俏迷人、风情万种、优雅绝伦的——”
阿翁抬手打断了他。
“先生，我生平的戒律向来是不招惹有夫之妇。”
德·福热纳克目瞪口呆。
“可——可——先生这话什么意思？难不成又是玩笑话？内人日思夜想的是您的侍童啊。”
“那可真叫人大失所望！”
“您的侍童，您那位优雅至极的侍童！她不分昼夜地缠着我来找您。如今我就在这儿！您瞧瞧我！”
“过去这整整二十分钟里，我一直在瞧着你呢，先生，”阿翁语气微带尖刻地说道。
“她苦苦哀求我来找您，问您可肯割爱出让侍童！不让他替她提裙裾、捧手套摇折扇，她就寝食难安。不确知那孩子归她所有，她夜里根本睡不着觉！”
“看来夫人注定要度过许多个辗转难眠的长夜了，”阿翁说。
“啊不，先生！您想想看！人人都传闻您的侍童是买来的。那么俗话不是说得好嘛，买得来的东西自然也卖得出去，难道不是吗？”
“或许吧。”
“对，对！或许吧！先生，我在内人面前就像个奴隶。”他亲吻着自己的指尖，“我卑微得如她脚下的泥土。”他双手合十作揖，“凡是她想要的，我若不能捧到她跟前，我宁可去死！”
“那就烦请借用我的佩剑吧，”公爵大人盛情相邀道，“就在您身后的角落里。”
“啊不！先生绝非当真要拒绝吧！这不可能！先生，您大可以漫天要价，我绝不还口！”
阿翁站起身来。他拿起银质手铃，摇响了铃铛。
“先生，”他语调柔滑如丝地说道，“烦请替我向圣维尔伯爵致意，转告他：莱昂，我的侍童，绝非待售之物。于勒，送客。”
德·福热纳克垂头丧气地站起身。
“先生？”
阿翁躬身致意。
“先生，您弄错了！您根本不明白！”
“相信我，我心里再清楚不过了。”
“啊，可您如此横加阻挠一位女士的心愿，当真毫无同情心！”

===Original===

“My misfortune entirely, m’sieur. I am desolated that you are unable to stay longer. M’sieur, your very obedient!” So he bowed De Faugenac out.
No sooner had the door closed behind the little man than it opened again to admit Davenant.
“Who in the name of all that’s marvellous was that?” he asked.
“A creature of no account,” replied his Grace. “He wished to buy Léon. An impertinence. I am going into the country, Hugh.”
“Into the country? Why?”
“I forget. No doubt I shall call the reason to mind some time. Bear with me, my dear; I am still moderately sane.”
Hugh sat down.
“You never were sane. ’Pon rep, you’re a casual host!”
“Ah Hugh, I crave your pardon on my knees! I encroach on your good nature.”
“Damme, you’re very polite! Is Léon to accompany you?”
“No, I leave him in your charge, Hugh, and I counsel you to have a care for him. While I am away he will not leave the house.”
“I thought there was some mystery. Is he in danger?”
“N-no. I can hardly say. But keep him close, and say naught, my dear. I should not be pleased if harm came to him. Incredible as it may seem, I am becoming fond of the child. I must be entering upon my dotage.”
“We are all fond of him,” said Hugh. “But he is an imp.”
“Undoubtedly. Do not allow him to tease you; he is an impertinent child. Unhappily he cannot be brought to realise that fact. And here he is.”
Léon came in and smiled confidingly as he met the Duke’s eyes.
“Monseigneur, you told me to be ready to accompany you out at three, and it is now half-past the hour,” he said.
Hugh’s shoulders shook with suppressed laughter; he turned away, coughing.
“It would appear that I owe you an apology,” said his Grace. “Pray hold me excused for once. I am not going out after all. Come here.”
Léon approached.
“Yes, Monseigneur?”
“I am going into the country for a few days, my infant, from tomorrow. Oblige me by looking on M. Davenant as master in mine absence, and do not, on any account, leave the house until I return.”
“Oh!” Léon’s face fell. “Am I not to come with you?”
“I am denying myself that honour. Please do not argue with me. That is all that I wished to say.”
Léon turned away and went with lagging steps to the door. A small sniff escaped him, and at the sound of it Avon smiled.
“Infant, the end of the world has not come. I shall return, I hope, within the week.”
“I wish﻿—oh, I wish that you would take me!”

===Chinese===

“这完全是在下的遗憾，先生。先生不能多留片刻，着实令我痛心疾首。先生，仆候效劳！”公爵就这样躬身把德·福热纳克送了出去。
矮个子男人的身影刚消失在门后，房门便再次被推开，达弗南特迈步走入。
“看在天下所有怪事的份上，刚才那位到底是何方神圣？”他问道。
“一个微不足道的小角色，”公爵大人答道，“妄图买下莱昂。不知天高地厚。休，我要去乡下一趟。”
“去乡下？为什么？”
“我给忘了。想必早晚会想起来的。担待着点吧，亲爱的；我神志大体上还算清醒。”
休坐了下来。
“你何曾清醒过。老天作证，你这东道主当得可真够随性的！”
“啊，休，我恨不得双膝跪地乞求你的宽恕！我仗着你性情宽厚多有叨扰。”
“见鬼，你这客套话可真够体面的！莱昂随你同去吗？”
“不，我把他托付给你照料，休，我劝你务必看护好他。在我离开期间，绝不许他踏出府邸半步。”
“我就料到其中有蹊跷。他有危险吗？”
“不——不算有。我也吃不大准。不过把他严加看管在家里，切莫多言，亲爱的。倘若他有个三长两短，我可不会高兴。说来荒唐，我竟然越来越喜欢这孩子了。我定是开始老糊涂了。”
“咱们大家谁不喜欢他呢，”休说，“可他就是个小皮猴。”
“确实如此。千万别由着他捉弄你；他是个没大没小的放肆孩子。令人遗憾的是，任凭怎么点拨他都意识不到这一点。瞧，他来了。”
莱昂走了进来，迎上公爵的目光，露出一抹信赖的微笑。
“大人，您吩咐我三点整预备好随您出门，现在已经三点半了，”他说道。
休忍俊不禁，肩膀直打颤；他转过脸去，用咳嗽掩饰笑意。
“看来我还欠你一个道歉呢，”公爵大人说道，“烦请下不为例地原谅我一回吧。我终究不打算出门了。过来。”
莱昂走上前去。
“大人有何吩咐？”
“从明儿起，我要去乡下住上几日，我的小家伙。请你务必把达弗南特先生当作主人看待，在我归来之前，无论如何不得离开府邸半步。”
“噢！”莱昂顿时耷拉下了脑袋，“我不陪您一道去吗？”
“我只能割爱谢绝这份荣幸了。请别同我争辩。我要吩咐的就只有这些。”
莱昂转身拖着沉重的步伐朝房门走去。一声轻轻的抽噎声从他鼻腔里溢出，听到这声抽泣，阿翁嘴角浮现出一抹笑意。
“小家伙，世界末日还没到呢。若无意外，我一周之内便会归来。”
“我真希望——噢，我真盼着您能带我一起去！”

===Original===

“That is hardly polite to M. Davenant. I do not think he is likely to ill-use you. I am not going out tonight, by the way.”
Léon came back.
“You﻿—you won’t go tomorrow without saying goodbye, will you, Monseigneur?”
“You shall hand me into my coach,” promised the Duke, and gave him his hand to kiss.

===Chinese===

“这对达弗南特先生可不太礼貌。我想他大概不至于亏待你。顺便提一句，我今晚不出门。”
莱昂折返了回来。
“您——您明儿启程前不会连声告别都不说就走吧，大人？”
“你可以亲自送我登车，”公爵许诺道，并把手递给他吻别。

## XII His Grace of Avon’s Ward / 阿翁公爵的受监护人
===Original===

A fortnight later, when Léonie was practising a court curtsy before the mirror in her room, Fanny entered with the announcement that Avon had come at last. Léonie arose from her curtsy with more haste than grace.
“Monseigneur!” she cried, and would have flown from the room, had it not been for Fanny, who resolutely barred her passage. “Let me go! Let me go! Where is he?”
“ ’Pon rep, Léonie, that is no way to receive a gentleman!” said her ladyship. “To run downstairs like a hoydenish miss, with your hair in a tangle, and your gown caught up! Come back to the mirror.”
“Oh, but﻿—”
“I insist!”
Léonie came reluctantly and was passive while Fanny arranged her gown of primrose silk, and combed out the unruly curls.
“Léonie, you tiresome creature, where is your ribbon?”
Léonie fetched it meekly.
“I do not like to feel a ribbon in my hair,” she complained. “I would rather﻿—”
“It is of no consequence at all,” said Fanny severely. “I am determined you shall look your best. Shake out your petticoat, and pick up your fan. And if you dare to run forward in an unmaidenly way I shall be so mortified﻿—”
“Let me go now! Please, I am ready!”
“Then follow me, child, so!” Out swept Fanny, and down the stairs. “Remember! A decorous curtsy, my love, and give him your hand to kiss.” As she spoke she opened the door into the withdrawing-room.
“Bah!” said Léonie.
His Grace was standing by the window, looking out.
“So my sister has not induced you to stop saying ‘bah’?” he said, and turned. For a moment he said nothing, but stood looking at his ward. “Infant, it is very well,” he said at last, slowly.
Léonie sank into a curtsy, talking all the time.
“I must do this because madame says so, and you bade me do as she told me, Monseigneur, but oh, I would rather bow to you!” She rose gracefully, and danced forward. “Monseigneur, Monseigneur, I thought that you would never come! I am so very pleased to see you!” She caught his hand to her lips. “I have been good and patient, and now will you take me, please?”
“Léonie!”
“Well, but madame, I want so much for him to take me.”
Avon raised his eyeglass.
“Stand still, child. Fanny, I kiss your hands and feet. I am almost surprised at the miracle you have wrought.”
“Monseigneur, do you think that I am nice?” asked Léonie, tiptoeing before him.
“It’s an inadequate word, child. You are no longer Léon.”
She sighed, shaking her head.

===Chinese===

两周之后，正当莱昂妮（Léonie）在房里的穿衣镜前练习宫廷屈膝礼时，范妮（Fanny）快步走进来宣布：阿翁（Avon）终于到了。莱昂妮收回屈膝礼的动作显得慌乱有余而优雅不足。
“大人（Monseigneur）！”她大声呼喊着，若不是范妮坚决地横在门前挡住去路，她早就飞奔出房间了。“让我去！让我去！他人呢？”
“老天作证，莱昂妮，哪有这样迎候一位绅士的！”夫人说道，“像个疯丫头似的往楼下冲，头发乱蓬蓬的，裙摆也撩得老高！快回到镜子前站好。”
“噢，可是——”
“听我的！”
莱昂妮老大不情愿地走回镜前，由着范妮替她整理好那身报春花黄的丝绸长裙，梳顺那一头倔强的卷发。
“莱昂妮，你这个磨人的淘气包，你的发带跑哪儿去了？”
莱昂妮乖乖地取来发带。
“我不喜欢头发上系着发带的感觉，”她抱怨道，“我宁愿——”
“这根本无关紧要，”范妮严肃地说道，“我打定主意要让你展现出最迷人的一面。把衬裙抖开，拿上折扇。要是你敢不知羞耻地乱跑上前去，我可真要羞得无地自容了——”
“现在放我去吧！求您了，我收拾好了！”
“那就跟在我身后，我的孩子，照这样做！”范妮裙裾一拂，快步走下楼梯。“记着！行一个端庄得体的屈膝礼，亲爱的，再伸手让他吻你的手。”她一边说着，一边推开了小会客室的房门。
“呸！”莱昂妮说。
公爵大人正伫立在窗前，凝视着窗外。
“这么说来，舍妹终究没能让你改掉说‘呸’的毛病咯？”他说着，转过身来。有那么一刻他什么也没说，只是静静打量着自己的受监护人。“小家伙，收拾得相当齐整，”他终于缓缓开口说道。
莱昂妮屈膝行礼，嘴里却一刻不停：
“我这么做纯粹是因为夫人非要我这样，而且是大人您吩咐过要我凡事听从她的安排，可噢，我真宁愿像以前那样向您躬身行礼！”她步态轻盈地起身，欢快地迎上前去。“大人，大人，我还以为您永远不来了呢！能再见到您我真是太高兴了！”她抓起他的手贴在唇边。“我一直很乖、很有耐心，现在您肯带我走了吗，拜托了？”
“莱昂妮！”
“哎呀，夫人，我心里巴不得他带我走嘛。”
阿翁举起单片眼镜。
“站稳了，孩子。范妮，我深吻你的双手双足。你所展现的神奇妙手简直令我叹为观止。”
“大人，您觉得我好看吗？”莱昂妮在他身前踮着脚尖问道。
“用好看二字未免太轻描淡写了，孩子。你再也不是莱昂了。”
她叹了口气，摇了摇头。

===Original===

“I wish I were Léon still. Monseigneur, do *you* understand what it is to be put into petticoats?”
Fanny started, and frowned direfully.
“Naturally I do not, my beautiful ward,” Justin answered gravely. “I can imagine that after the freedom of your breeches, petticoats are a little cramping.”
Léonie turned triumphantly to Fanny.
“Madame, he said it! You heard him! He spoke of breeches!”
“Léonie﻿—Justin, I’ll not have you let her bewail her﻿—her breeches﻿—as she is forever doing! And don’t, don’t say bah, Léonie!”
“She has fatigued you, my dear? I believe I warned you that she was something of a rogue.”
Fanny relented.
“Indeed, and we love her dearly! I could wish that you would leave her with us longer.”
Léonie took a firm hold on Avon’s coat sleeve.
“You won’t, will you, Monseigneur?”
He disengaged himself.
“My infant, you must strive to be more polite. One would infer that you had been unhappy with Lady Fanny.”
“Yes, Monseigneur, very unhappy. It is not because she is not kind, for she has been very kind to me, but I belong to you.”
Over her head Justin looked mockingly at his sister.
“It distresses you, my dear? I believe you are right, Léonie. I have come to fetch you.”
She was all smiles at once.
“*Voyons*, now I am happy! Where will you take me, Monseigneur?”
“Into the country, child. Ah, the worthy Edward! Your devoted servant, Edward.”
Marling had entered quietly. Stiffly he returned Avon’s bow.
“I would have a word with you an it please you, Alastair,” he said.
“But does it please me?” wondered his Grace. “No doubt you wish to speak concerning my ward?”
Edward looked annoyed.
“In private, sir.”
“Quite unnecessary, my dear Edward, I assure you.” He flicked Léonie’s cheek with one careless finger. “Mr. Marling has no doubt warned you that I am no fit companion for the young and﻿—ah﻿—innocent, infant?”
“No-no.” Léonie tilted her head. “I know all about that, you see. Me, I am not very innocent, do you think?”
“That will do, Léonie!” hastily interposed Fanny. “You’ll drink a dish of Bohea with me, Justin? Léonie shall be ready to accompany you tomorrow. Léonie, my love, I have left my handkerchief in your room. Be so good as to fetch it for me. And Edward may go too. Yes, Edward, please!” So she drove them out, and turned again to her brother. “Well, Justin, I’ve done as you desired me.”
“Admirably, my dear.”
Her eyes twinkled.

===Chinese===

“我倒巴不得自己依然是莱昂呢。大人，您能体会穿上裙子到底是什么滋味吗？”
范妮浑身一震，眉头紧锁得极为难看。
“我自然无法体会，我美丽的受监护人，”贾斯汀（Justin）神情严肃地答道，“但我可以想象，在体会过了穿马裤的无拘无束之后，穿上裙装未免有些束手束脚。”
莱昂妮得意洋洋地转向范妮。
“夫人，他可说了！您亲耳听见的！他也提起马裤了！”
“莱昂妮——贾斯汀，我不许你由着她整天为她的——她的马裤大倒苦水！还有，莱昂妮，不许说呸，绝对不许说！”
“亲爱的，她把你累坏了吧？我想我早就提醒过你，她是个难缠的小淘气。”
范妮心软了下来。
“话虽如此，我们大家可都疼爱她得紧！我倒真盼着你能让她在咱们这儿多住些日子。”
莱昂妮一把死死拽住阿翁的大衣衣袖。
“您不会把我留下的，对吧，大人？”
他轻轻抽出了衣袖。
“我的小家伙，你得学着懂礼貌些。外人听了，还以为你在范妮夫人这儿过得多委屈呢。”
“是的，大人，委屈得很。并不是因为夫人待我不好，她待我仁至义尽，可我是属于您的。”
贾斯汀越过她的头顶，带着嘲弄的神情看向妹妹。
“亲爱的，这让你感到难过了吧？我相信你说得在理，莱昂妮。我正是来接你回去的。”
她脸上顿时笑开了花。
“哎呀（Voyons），我现在可开心了！大人，您打算带我去哪儿？”
“去乡下，孩子。啊，可敬的爱德华（Edward）！愿随时效劳，爱德华。”
马林（Marling）悄无声息地走了进来。他冷冰冰地向阿翁回礼。
“阿拉斯泰尔（Alastair），若蒙允准，我想同你说句话，”他说。
“可我当真乐意听吗？”公爵若有所思道，“想必你是想谈谈关于我这名受监护人的事吧？”
爱德华神色略显不悦。
“单独谈谈，先生。”
“大可不必，我亲爱的爱德华，我向你保证。”他漫不经心地伸出一根手指在莱昂妮脸颊上弹了一下。“小家伙，马林先生想必早就告诫过你，对于年轻而——呃——天真的姑娘而言，我绝非良伴吧？”
“才没有呢。”莱昂妮歪着脑袋，“你看，关于这些我全一清二楚。再说了，你觉得我当真有多天真纯洁吗？”
“住嘴，莱昂妮！”范妮急忙从旁打岔，“贾斯汀，同我喝盏武夷红茶（Bohea）吧？莱昂妮明日准保预备好随你动身。莱昂妮，亲爱的，我的手帕落在你房里了。好心替我取一趟吧。爱德华也可以退下了。是的，爱德华，劳驾了！”就这样，她把两人打发了出去，随后再次转向哥哥。“好吧，贾斯汀，我已经按你的吩咐全办妥了。”
“办得无可挑剔，亲爱的。”
她的眼里闪烁着笑意。

===Original===

“At no small cost, Justin.”
“It is no matter, Fanny.”
She eyed him irresolutely.
“What now, Justin?”
“Now I take her to Avon.”
“With Cousin Field?”
“But could you doubt it?” he bowed.
“Easily.” She curled her lip. “Justin, what is it you intend? You’ve some scheme, I know. I’ll believe you mean no ill by Léonie.”
“It is always wise to believe the worst of me, Fanny.”
“I confess I don’t understand you, Justin. ’Tis most provoking.”
“It must be,” he agreed.
She drew nearer, coaxing him.
“Justin, I do wish that you would tell me what is in your mind!”
He took a pinch of snuff, and shut the box with a snap.
“You must learn, my dear Fanny, to curb your curiosity. Suffice it that I am as a grandfather to that child. It should suffice.”
“It does, in part, but I do so want to know what scheme you have in your head!”
“I am sure you do, Fanny,” he said sympathetically.
“You are very horrid,” she pouted. A sudden smile came. “Justin, what new whim is this? Léonie speaks of you as of a strict governor. ’Tis forever ‘Monseigneur would not like me to do that,’ or ‘Do you think that Monseigneur would mind?’ It’s not like you, my dear.”
“An I knew less of the world’s ways I should no doubt be a more lenient guardian,” he said. “As it is, Fanny﻿—” He shrugged, and drew his fan from one of his great pockets.
Léonie came back into the room, holding up her gown with one little hand.
“I could not find your handkerchief, madame,” she began, and then saw Avon’s fan. A look of disapproval came over her face; there was a measure of reproof in the candid blue eyes. Avon smiled.
“You will grow accustomed to it, my child.”
“Never,” said Léonie positively. “It does not please me at all.”
“But then,” murmured his Grace, “I do not use it to please you.”
“*Pardon, Monseigneur!*” she answered contritely, and peeped at him through her lashes. The irresistible dimple quivered.
“She’ll snare him,” thought Fanny. “She is all too fascinating.”
---
Justin took his ward down to Avon by coach the following day, in company with Madam Field, on whose amiable vapidity Léonie looked with scant respect. Justin was quick to read her opinion of the lady, and when they arrived at Avon, took her aside.
“This,” said Léonie buoyantly, “is a nice house. I like it.”
“I am rejoiced to hear you say so,” replied his Grace ironically.

===Chinese===

“代价可着实不菲呢，贾斯汀。”
“这无伤大雅，范妮。”
她满腹狐疑地端详着他。
“下一步又如何，贾斯汀？”
“眼下我要带她回阿翁庄园。”
“同菲尔德表姐（Cousin Field）一道去？”
“难不成你还心存怀疑？”他躬身致意。
“怀疑得很。”她撇了撇嘴，“贾斯汀，你到底打着什么算盘？我知道你心里必定有个全盘计划。我权且相信你对莱昂妮绝无恶意。”
“凡事总把我想得最坏，向来是最明智的做派，范妮。”
“我承认我实在捉摸不透你，贾斯汀。当真令人恼火。”
“想必确实如此，”他深表赞同。
她凑上前去，软语相求。
“贾斯汀，我真盼着你能告诉我你心里到底在琢磨什么！”
他吸了一撮鼻烟，啪的一声合上盒盖。
“我亲爱的范妮，你得学会克制你的好奇心。你只需知晓，我对那个孩子而言就如同祖父一般。这理当足够了。”
“按理说确实足够，可我就是按捺不住想知道你脑子里到底装的什么锦囊妙计！”
“我相信你必定想知道，范妮，”他深表同情地说道。
“你真讨人嫌，”她撅起嘴。忽而展颜一笑，“贾斯汀，这又是哪门子的新鲜念头？莱昂妮提起你来，活像在说一位严苛古板的严师。嘴里成天不是‘大人不会喜欢我那样做的’，就是‘你觉得大人会介意吗？’这可一点儿都不像你的做派，亲爱的。”
“倘若我对这人世间的门道少几分了解，想必我自会做一名宽仁随和的监护人，”他说，“而眼下呢，范妮——”他耸了耸肩，从宽大的衣袋里掏出折扇。
莱昂妮用一只小手提着裙裾走回屋内。
“夫人，我没找着您的手帕，”她刚开口，目光便落在了阿翁手中的折扇上。一丝不赞同的神情浮现在她脸上；坦率清澈的蓝眼睛里满含责备。阿翁微微一笑。
“你会看习惯的，我的孩子。”
“绝不可能，”莱昂妮语气坚定地说道，“我一点儿都不喜欢这东西。”
“可话说回来，”公爵低语道，“我摇折扇可不是为了讨你欢心的。”
“请大人恕罪（Pardon, Monseigneur）！”她愧疚地答道，透过浓密的睫毛怯生生瞄着他。脸颊上那抹令人难以抗拒的小酒窝微微颤动着。
“她早晚会把他牢牢套住的，”范妮心中暗想，“她实在太过惹人怜爱了。”
---
翌日，贾斯汀乘马车带着受监护人前往阿翁庄园，同行的还有菲尔德夫人（Madam Field）。莱昂妮对这位夫人和善平庸的乏味劲儿殊无敬意。贾斯汀敏锐地洞察到了侍童对这位夫人的评价，一抵达阿翁庄园，便将她拉到一旁。
“这里，”莱昂妮兴高采烈地说道，“是一栋很棒的宅子。我喜欢。”
“听到你这般评价，真叫我欣喜若狂，”公爵反唇讥讽道。

===Original===

Léonie looked round the panelled hall, with its carven chairs, its paintings, and tapestry, and the gallery above.
“Perhaps it is a little sombre,” she said. “Who is this gentleman?” She went to a suit of armour, and regarded it with interest.
“It is not a gentleman at all, my infant. It is the armour one of my ancestors wore.”
“*Vraiment?*” She wandered away to the foot of the stairs, and inspected an ancient portrait. “Is this another ancestor, this foolish woman?”
“A very famous one, my dear.”
“She has a stupid smile,” Léonie remarked. “Why was she famous? What for?”
“Principally for her indiscretions. Which reminds me, child, that I want to speak to you.”
“Yes, Monseigneur?” Léonie was staring now at a shield which hung above the fireplace. “ ‘*J’y serai.*’ That is French.”
“Your intelligence is remarkable. I wish to speak to you of my cousin, Madam Field.”
Léonie looked at him over her shoulder, grimacing.
“May I say what I think, Monseigneur?”
He sat down on the great carved table, swinging his eyeglass.
“To me, yes.”
“She is just a fool, Monseigneur.”
“Indubitably. And therefore, my infant, you must not only bear with her folly, but you must be at pains to cause her no trouble.”
Léonie seemed to debate within herself.
“Must I, Monseigneur?”
Justin looked at her, and recognised the naughty twinkle in her eye.
“Because I will it so, my child.”
The little straight nose wrinkled.
“Oh, *eh bien*!”
“I thought so,” remarked Avon beneath his breath. “It is a promise, Léonie?”
“I do not think that I will promise,” Léonie temporised. “I will try.” She came and stood before him. “Monseigneur, it is very kind of you to bring me to this beautiful place, and to give me everything just as though I were not the sister of an innkeeper. Thank you very much.”
Justin looked at her for a moment, and his lips twisted in a curious smile.
“You think me a paragon of all the virtues, don’t you, *ma fille*?”
“Oh no!” she answered candidly. “I think it is only to me that you are kind. With some women you are not good at all. I cannot help knowing these things, Monseigneur!”
“And yet, child, you are content to remain with me?”
“But of course!” she answered in some surprise.
“You are full of trust,” he remarked.
“Of course,” she said again.
“This,” said Avon, looking at the rings on his hand, “is a new experience. I wonder what Hugh would say?”

===Chinese===

莱昂妮环视着护墙板装点的宏伟门厅，打量着那些雕花木椅、油画、壁毯，以及二楼高耸的走廊。
“或许有那么一点点阴森沉闷，”她说，“这位绅士是谁？”她走到一具盔甲前，兴致盎然地端详着。
“这根本不是什么绅士，我的小家伙。这是我某位先祖穿过的盔甲。”
“当真（Vraiment）？”她漫步走到楼梯口，打量着一幅古老的肖像画，“这位蠢女人也是另一位先祖吗？”
“一位赫赫有名的先祖，亲爱的。”
“她的笑容好傻，”莱昂妮评述道，“她因何成名？为什么？”
“主要是因为她那些不检点的风流韵事。顺便提醒了我，孩子，我有话同你说。”
“大人请吩咐？”莱昂妮此时正凝视着壁炉上方悬挂的一面盾牌。“‘我将在此（J’y serai）。’那是法语。”
“你的聪明才智令人刮目相看。我想同你谈谈我的表姐菲尔德夫人。”
莱昂妮扭过头白了他一眼，做了个鬼脸。
“大人，我能实话实说吗？”
公爵在大雕花桌上坐下，晃动着手里的单片眼镜。
“在我面前，可以。”
“她纯粹是个蠢人，大人。”
“无可辩驳。因此，我的小家伙，你不仅要容忍她的愚蠢，更要尽力切莫给她招惹麻烦。”
莱昂妮似乎在内心暗自权衡。
“我非照办不可吗，大人？”
贾斯汀注视着她，辨认出她眼底那一抹顽皮狡黠的光芒。
“因为这是我的意志，我的孩子。”
秀气挺拔的小鼻子皱了起来。
“噢，好吧（eh bien）！”
“我就料定如此，”阿翁低声自语道。“这算是个承诺吗，莱昂妮？”
“我觉着自己还是别轻易许诺为妙，”莱昂妮推诿道，“我会尽力而为的。”她走到他身前站定。“大人，您待我实在太仁慈了，把我带到这般优美的地方，赐予我一切，全然不顾我曾是一个小客栈老板的妹妹。万分感谢您。”
贾斯汀凝神注视了她片刻，唇角勾起一抹异样的微笑。
“我亲爱的姑娘（ma fille），你把我想成一切美德的典范了，不是吗？”
“噢，绝没有！”她坦率地答道，“我觉得您唯独对我一个人好。对待某些女人，您可一点儿都不善良。这等事我心里怎能不知呢，大人！”
“即便如此，孩子，你依然甘愿留在我身边？”
“那是自然！”她略感诧异地答道。
“你倒是满怀信任，”他评述道。
“自然如此，”她再次说道。
“这，”阿翁凝视着手指上的戒指喃喃道，“倒是一番前所未有的新鲜体验。真不知休会作何感想？”

===Original===

“Oh, he would pull down his mouth, so! And shake his head. I think he is sometimes not very wise.”
He laughed, and laid a hand on her shoulder.
“I never thought, *ma fille*, to take unto me a ward so much after mine own heart. I beg you will be careful not to shock Madam Field.”
“But with you I may say what I please?”
“You always do,” he replied.
“And you will stay here?”
“For the present. I have to attend to your education, you see. There are things you have to learn that I can best teach you.”
“What, *par example*?”
“To ride?”
“On a horse? *Vraiment?*”
“The prospect pleases you?”
“Yes, oh yes! And will you teach me to fight with a sword, Monseigneur?”
“It’s not a ladylike occupation, *ma fille*.”
“But I do not always want to be a lady, Monseigneur! If I may learn to fight with a sword I will try very hard to learn the other silly things.”
He looked down at her, smiling.
“I believe you are trying to drive a bargain with me! What if I will not teach you to fence?”
She dimpled.
“Why, then I fear I shall be very stupid when you teach me to curtsy, Monseigneur. Oh, Monseigneur, say you will! Please say it quickly! Madame is coming.”
“You force my hand,” he bowed. “I will teach you, imp.”
Madam Field entered the hall in time to see her charge execute a neat step-dance. She murmured expostulations.

===Chinese===

“噢，他准会像这样撇着嘴！然后不住地摇头。我觉得他有时候可一点儿都不聪明。”
他轻笑出声，将一只手搭在她的肩头。
“我亲爱的姑娘（ma fille），我做梦也没想到，有朝一日竟能收养一位如此深合我心意的受监护人。我求你务必留心，切莫惊吓到了菲尔德夫人。”
“可跟您在一起时，我想说什么就能说什么，对吗？”
“你向来如此，”他答道。
“而且您会一直留在庄园里吗？”
“暂且留着。你瞧，我还得照料你的教养学业呢。有些事情必须得学，而且唯独由我来教最为妥当。”
“比如学什么（par exemple）？”
“学骑马？”
“骑在高头大马上？当真（Vraiment）？”
“这前景令你满意吧？”
“满意，噢，太满意了！那大人，您会教我如何挥剑击剑吗？”
“这可算不上淑女该干的行当，我的姑娘。”
“可我才不想成天当什么淑女呢，大人！要是能允许我学佩剑击剑，我保证拼命去把那些无聊透顶的闺秀规矩全学好。”
他俯视着她，唇边含笑。
“我看你是在存心同我讨价还价呢！倘若我不肯教你击剑又当如何？”
她脸上浮现出两只浅浅的酒窝。
“哎呀，那我可真担心大人教我屈膝礼时，我脑子会笨得转不过弯来呢。噢，大人，答应我吧！快些答应吧！夫人走过来了。”
“你倒真会逼我妥协，”他欠身道，“我教你便是，小皮猴。”
菲尔德夫人迈入门厅，正巧撞见她的这位受监护人欢快地跳起了一段轻巧利落的踢踏步。她嘴里不由得低声吐出几句责备唠叨。

## XX His Grace of Avon Takes Command of the Game / 阿翁公爵掌控全局
===Original===

The colour came flooding back to Léonie’s cheeks. “Monseigneur!” she gasped, and flew across the room towards him, laughing and crying at once. “Oh, Monseigneur, you have come, you have come!” She landed breathless in his arms, and clung to him.
“Why, *ma fille*!” said his Grace gently. “What is all this? Did you doubt I should come?”
“Take that pistol from her,” recommended Rupert faintly, but with a smile.
The pistol was pressed to his Grace’s heart. He removed it from Léonie’s clutch, and pocketed it. He looked down at the curly head with a curious smile, and presently stroked it.
“My dear infant, you must not cry. Come, it is in very truth Monseigneur! There is nothing to frighten you.”
“Oh, I am n-not *frightened*!” said Léonie. “I am so very glad!”
“Then I beg you will signify your gladness in a more becoming manner. May I ask what you are doing in those clothes?”
Léonie kissed his hand, and mopped her eyes.
“I like them, Monseigneur,” she said, with a twinkle.
“I doubt it not.” Avon went past her to the bed, and bent over it, laying a cool white hand over Rupert’s galloping pulse. “You are hurt, boy?”
Rupert managed to smile.
“It’s naught. A hole in my shoulder, plague take it!”
His Grace produced a flask from one pocket, and put it to Rupert’s lips. Rupert drank, and the blue shade went from about his mouth.
“I believe I have to thank you,” said the Duke, and removed a pillow. “You did well, my child. In fact, you have surprised me. I am in your debt.”
Rupert flushed.
“Pooh, ’twas nothing! I did precious little. ’Twas Léonie got us off. ’Fore Gad, I’m devilish pleased to see you, Justin!”
“Yes, so you remarked.” His Grace put up his quizzing glass and eyed the coins that lay scattered over the bed. “What, may I ask, is all this wealth?”
“Oh, that’s our money, Monseigneur!” said Léonie. “We were counting it when you came.”
“*Our* money!” ejaculated Rupert. “That’s rich, ’pon my soul it is! There’s some on the floor still.”
“And what,” said his Grace, turning to the broken bowl, “is this?”
“Rupert did it,” said Léonie. “It is his broth, but when we heard you coming he threw it on the floor.”
“My appearance seems to have produced a strange effect upon you,” remarked his Grace. “Can either of you tell me where is my very dear friend Saint-Vire?”
Rupert struggled up on his elbow.
“Tare an’ ouns, how did you know ’twas he?”

===Chinese===

红晕再次涌上莱昂妮（Léonie）的面颊。“大人（Monseigneur）！”她倒抽了一口凉气，飞奔穿过房间扑向他，又哭又笑。“噢，大人，您来了，您真的来了！”她上气不接下气地跌入他的怀中，死死搂住他。
“哎呀，我的姑娘（ma fille）！”公爵大人温和地说道，“这都是怎么了？难道你还怀疑我不会来吗？”
“把她手里的手枪拿走，”鲁珀特（Rupert）虚弱地提议道，脸上却带着一丝微笑。
那柄手枪正紧紧抵在公爵大人的心口上。他从莱昂妮紧攥的小手里拿过手枪，揣入衣袋。他低头注视着那头卷发，脸上浮现出一抹异样的笑意，随后轻轻抚了抚她的秀发。
“我亲爱的孩子，可不能再哭了。快瞧瞧，当真是我这位公爵大人！没什么好害怕的。”
“噢，我才没——没有害怕呢！”莱昂妮说，“我只是太高兴了！”
“既然如此，我求你换个更合体统的法子来表达你的欣喜。我能问问你身上怎么穿成了这副模样吗？”
莱昂妮吻了吻他的手，擦干了眼泪。
“我喜欢这身衣服，大人，”她眨着促狭的眼睛说道。
“这我毫不怀疑。”阿翁越过她走向床边，俯下身，将一只清凉白皙的手搭在鲁珀特飞速跳动的脉搏上。“受了伤，孩子？”
鲁珀特勉强挤出一丝微笑。
“算不得什么。肩膀上被开了个洞，真他妈倒霉！”
公爵大人从口袋里掏出一只小扁酒壶，递到鲁珀特唇边。鲁珀特喝了一口，嘴角那一抹青紫之色渐渐褪去。
“我想我该好好向你道声谢，”公爵说着，抽走了一个枕头。“干得漂亮，我的孩子。实不相瞒，你真让我刮目相看。我欠你一个人情。”
鲁珀特涨红了脸。
“嗨，这算得了什么！我其实没派上多大用场。是莱昂妮把咱们给救出来的。老天在上，能见着你我真是高兴坏了，贾斯汀（Justin）！”
“是啊，你刚才已经说过了。”公爵大人举起单片眼镜，打量着散落在床上的硬币。“我能问问，这笔巨款又是怎么回事吗？”
“噢，那是我们的盘缠，大人！”莱昂妮说，“您进来那会儿我们正在数钱呢。”
“我们的盘缠！”鲁珀特叫嚷起来，“这话可真新鲜，我对天发誓！地上还掉着几枚呢。”
“那这又是什么？”公爵大人转向地上的破碗问道。
“是鲁珀特干的，”莱昂妮说，“那是他的肉汤，可听到您进门的脚步声，他一把将碗掀翻在地上了。”
“我的现身似乎在你们身上激起了异乎寻常的反应啊，”公爵大人评述道，“不知你们两位谁能告诉我，我那位极亲爱的朋友圣维尔（Saint-Vire）如今身在何处？”
鲁珀特挣扎着撑起手肘。
“见鬼了，你怎么知道是他？”

===Original===

His Grace put him back on his pillows.
“It is my business always to know, Rupert.”
“Well, I always swore you were at the bottom of it! But how the deuce did you find out that he’d got Léonie? Where were you? How did you guess I was after them?”
“Yes, and how did you know where to find us?” asked Léonie. “Why did he take me?”
The Duke took off his greatcoat, and smoothed a wrinkle from the velvet sleeve beneath.
“You bewilder me, my children. One question at a time, I beg of you.”
“How did you know who had run off with Léonie?”
The Duke sat down by the bed, and snapped his fingers to Léonie, who came at once to sit at his feet.
“It was really quite simple,” he said.
“Simple, was it, egad! Then for the love of God, Justin, tell us what we’ve been doing, for I’ll be hanged if I know!”
Avon twisted his rings.
“Oh, I think you do!” he said. “Léonie was abducted by a very pretty rogue, and you rescued her.”
“She rescued herself,” chuckled Rupert.
“Yes. I did,” Léonie nodded. “When the wheel came off I slipped out of the coach, and ran down the road. *Then* Rupert came.”
“Yes, but there’s more to it than that,” interrupted Rupert. “What did Saint-Vire want with Léonie? Do you know that?”
“I do, my dear boy.”
“Well, *I* think it was a great piece of impudence,” said Léonie. “Why did he want me?”
“My children, you cannot expect me to tell you all my secrets.”
“But, Monseigneur, I do *not* see that that is fair! We have been on a big adventure, and we have done it all by ourselves, and we do not know what it is about in the very least, and now you will not tell us!”
“I think you might tell us, Justin,” said Rupert. “We can be discreet, you know.”
“No, my children. My opinion of your discretion is not so great as my opinion of your courage and resource. By the way, what did you do with Mr. Manvers’ roan?”
Rupert stared.
“Lord, is there anything you don’t know? Who told you that?”
“Mr. Manvers himself,” replied the Duke. “I arrived at Avon on the evening of the day you﻿—er﻿—left. Mr. Manvers came to retrieve his property.”
“Curse his impudence!” said Rupert. “I left him a message! Does the fellow think I’m not to be trusted with a horse?”
“That was rather the impression he gave me,” said his Grace. “What did you do with it?”
“Well, to tell the truth, I sold it,” replied Rupert, grinning.
The Duke lay back in his chair.
“Then I very much fear that Mr.

公爵大人把他按回枕头上。
“凡事洞若观火，向来是我的看家本领，鲁珀特。”
“得，我早就发誓这事背后一定有你的影子！可你究竟是怎么打听到他掳走了莱昂妮的？你当时人在哪儿？你又是怎么猜到我在后头穷追不舍的？”
“对啊，您又是怎么知道来这儿找我们的？”莱昂妮问，“他为何要抓我？”
公爵脱下长大衣，抚平了底下天鹅绒衣袖上的褶皱。
“你们把我弄糊涂了，孩子们。一个一个问，我求求你们了。”
“您是怎么知道拐走莱昂妮的人是谁的？”
公爵在床旁坐下，冲着莱昂妮打了个响指，后者立刻顺从地走过来坐在他脚边。
“这其实相当简单，”他说。
“简单？见鬼，还真亏你说得出口！看在上帝的份上，贾斯汀，快告诉我们咱们这几天到底干了些什么吧，我要是搞得明白就叫我上绞架！”
阿翁转动着手指上的戒指。
“噢，我想你心里很清楚！”他说，“莱昂妮被一个相当标致的恶棍拐走了，而你把她救了出来。”
“是她自己把自己救出来的，”鲁珀特吃吃笑了起来。
“没错。是我自己逃出来的，”莱昂妮点头道，“马车轮子掉下来的时候，我从车厢里溜了出去，顺着大路一路狂奔。然后鲁珀特才赶到的。”
“对，可事情远不止这么简单，”鲁珀特打岔道，“圣维尔抓莱昂妮到底图什么？你知道内情吗？”
“我知道，我亲爱的孩子。”
“依我看，这简直是放肆狂妄到了极点，”莱昂妮说，“他到底要我做什么？”
“我的孩子们，你们可不能指望我把所有秘密都抖落给你们。”
“可是，大人，我觉得这太不公平了！我们经历了一场天大的冒险，全凭我们自己闯过来了，可从头到尾我们甚至连怎么回事都一头雾水，而现在您居然还不肯告诉我们！”
“我觉得你真该告诉我们，贾斯汀，”鲁珀特说，“我们可是懂得分寸、守口如瓶的，你懂的。”
“不，我的孩子们。比起你们的勇气与机智，我对你们守口如瓶的能耐可没那么高的指望。顺便问一句，你把曼弗斯先生（Mr. Manvers）的那匹红毛马弄到哪儿去了？”
鲁珀特瞪大了双眼。
“老天，这世上还有什么是你不知道的吗？谁告诉你的？”
“曼弗斯先生本人，”公爵答道，“就在你们——呃——离开的那天傍晚，我赶回了阿翁庄园。曼弗斯先生特意登门来索要他的私产。”
“去他娘的狂妄！”鲁珀特说，“我给他留了口信的！难道那家伙以为我是去

===Original===

Manvers will be satisfied with nothing less than our lives,” he sighed. “Pray do not imagine that I disapprove of your action, but I should like to know why you disposed of his roan thus speedily?”
“Well, you see, I’d no money,” explained Rupert. “I forgot I’d my pin to sell. Besides, what else could I do with the animal? I didn’t want to bring it to France.”
The Duke looked at him in some amusement.
“Did you set out on this venture penniless?” he inquired.
“No, I’d a couple of crowns in my pocket,” Rupert answered.
“You make me feel incredibly old,” complained his Grace. He smiled down at Léonie. “What happened to you, my infant?”
“Oh, I was just teasing Rupert!” Léonie replied buoyantly. “That is why I am in these clothes. I put them on to make him angry. And I ran away from him into the wood, and that pig-person was there﻿—”
“One moment, my infant. You will pardon my ignorance, but I do not know who the﻿—er﻿—pig-person is meant to be.”
“Why, the wicked Comte!” said Léonie. “He is a pig-person, Monseigneur.”
“I see. I do not think I admire your choice of adjective, though.”
“Well, I think it is a very good name for him,” said Léonie, unabashed. “He seized me, and threw me into his coach, and I bit him till there was blood.”
“You distress me, child. But proceed.”
“I called to Rupert as loud as I could, and I kicked the pig-person﻿—”
“The Comte de Saint-Vire.”
“Yes, the pig-person﻿—on his legs a great many times. He did not like it at all.”
“That,” said his Grace, “does not altogether surprise me.”
“No. If I had had my dagger I would have killed him, for I was very angry﻿—oh, but *very* angry! But I had no dagger, so I could only call to Rupert.”
“The Comte de Saint-Vire has yet something to be thankful for,” murmured his Grace. “He little knows the temper of my ward.”
“Well, but would not you have been angry, Monseigneur?”
“Very, infant; but continue.”
“Oh, you know the rest, Monseigneur! He gave me an evil drink﻿—pig-wash! He called it coffee.”
“Then let us also call it coffee, child, I beg of you. I can support ‘pig-person,’ but ‘pig-wash’ I will not endure.”
“But it *was*, Monseigneur! I threw it at him, and he swore.”
His Grace regarded her inscrutably.
“You seem to have been a pleasant travelling companion,” he remarked. “What then?”
“*Then* he brought more pig﻿—coffee, and he made me drink it. It was drugged, Monseigneur, and it made me go to sleep.”

===Chinese===

偷他的马不成？我买下了那匹畜生！”
“可是你并没有付钱给人家，”阿翁温和地提醒道，“那匹马现在在哪儿？”
“卖了，”鲁珀特答道。
阿翁挑起了双眉。
“卖了？卖给谁了？”
“卖给一个叫弗莱彻（Fletcher）的老头了。他开着一艘运煤船。”
公爵长叹了一口气。
“我想曼弗斯先生唯有要了我们俩的性命才肯罢休了，”他叹道，“请别以为我是在责备你的举动，我只是想知道，你为何如此神速地把他的红毛马给脱手了？”
“哎，你瞧，我身上分文没有，”鲁珀特解释道，“我把身上那枚领针能换钱的事给忘了个精光。再说了，那匹畜生我还能怎么处置？我总不能把它大老远牵到法国来吧。”
公爵颇感好笑地打量着他。
“难道你身无分文就敢踏上这趟冒险之途？”他询问道。
“没有，我兜里当时还揣着两枚克朗呢，”鲁珀特答道。
“你真让我觉得自己老得不成样子了，”公爵大人抱怨道。他低头微笑着看向莱昂妮。“我的小家伙，你身上又出了什么事？”
“噢，我当时只是在故意捉弄鲁珀特！”莱昂妮神采飞扬地答道，“所以我才穿成这副模样。我穿这身衣服就是为了气他。我从他身边跑开，钻进了树林里，那个猪头人就候在那儿——”
“慢着，我的小家伙。请原谅我的孤陋寡闻，我实在不知这所谓的——呃——猪头人到底是指哪一位。”
“哎呀，就是那个恶毒的伯爵呀！”莱昂妮说，“他就是个猪头人，大人。”
“我明白了。不过我想我并不怎么欣赏你选的这个形容词。”
“可我觉得这名字安在他头上再合适不过了，”莱昂妮毫无愧色地说道，“他一把揪住我，把我扔进了他的马车里，我狠狠咬了他一口，咬得他直淌血。”
“你真叫我心惊肉跳，孩子。接着说。”
“我拼命扯着嗓子呼喊鲁珀特，还用脚死命踢那个猪头人——”
“是圣维尔伯爵。”
“对，就是那个猪头人——往他的腿上狠狠踹了不知多少脚。他难受得直咧嘴。”
“这，”公爵大人说道，“倒也在我的意料之中。”
“就是嘛。要是我当时身上带着短匕首，我非杀了他不可，因为我当时气炸了——噢，真的气炸了！可我没带匕首，只能大声向鲁珀特呼救。”
“这么说来，圣维尔伯爵倒还真该谢天谢地了，”公爵大人低语道，“他可丝毫不知我这位受监护人的暴烈脾气。”
“可换作是您，大人，难道您就不会动怒吗？”
“会非常生气，小家伙；接着往下讲。”
“噢，后来的事大人您都知道了！他端给我一碗难喝得要命的鬼东西——简直就是泔水！他硬说那是咖啡。”
“那我也求求你，咱们还是管它叫咖啡吧，孩子。‘猪头人’我还勉强能忍受，可‘泔水’我实在听不下去。”
“可那真的就是泔水嘛，大人！我把碗里的东西全泼到他身上了，他破口大骂。”
公爵大人神情莫测地注视着她。
“看来你真算得上一位令人愉悦的旅伴呢，”他评述道，“然后呢？”
“然后他又端来了一些猪——咖啡，硬逼着我喝下去。里头下了迷药，大人，喝得我昏睡了过去。”

===Original===

“Poor infant!” His Grace tweaked one curl. “But a most indomitable infant withal.”
“There is nothing more to tell you, Monseigneur. I woke up next day at the inn at Le Havre, and I pretended to be asleep. Then the coach broke, and I escaped.”
“And what of Rupert?” The Duke smiled across at his brother.
“Faith, I don’t think I stopped running till I came here!” said Rupert. “I am still something out of breath.”
“Oh, Rupert was very clever!” Léonie struck in. “Monseigneur, he even sold his diamond to follow me, and he came to France in a dirty old boat, without a hat or a sword!”
“Nonsense, silly chit, Fletcher gave me his Sunday beaver. You talk too much, Léonie. Stop it!”
“I do not talk too much, do I, Monseigneur? And it is as I say. I do not know what would have happened to me but for Rupert.”
“Nor I, *ma fille*. We owe him a very big debt of gratitude. It is not often that I put my faith in another, but I did so these last two days.”
Rupert blushed and stammered.
“ ’Twas Léonie did it all. She brought me here, wherever we are. Where are we, Justin?”
“You are at Le Dennier, some ten miles from Le Havre, my children.”
“Well, that’s one mystery solved, at all events!” said Rupert. “Léonie went ’cross country till the head turned on my shoulders. Oh, she diddled Saint-Vire finely, I give you my word!”
“But if you had not come I could not have got away,” Léonie pointed out.
“If it comes to that,” said Rupert, “the Lord alone knows what would have happened if you’d not caught us, Justin.”
“I understand that my bloodthirsty ward would have shot the so dear Comte﻿—er﻿—dead.”
“Yes, I would,” Léonie averred. “*That* would have taught him a lesson!”
“It would indeed,” agreed his Grace.
“Will you shoot him for me, please, Monseigneur?”
“Certainly not, infant. I shall be delighted to see the dear Comte.”
Rupert looked at him sharply.
“I’ve sworn to have his blood, Justin.”
His Grace smiled.
“I am before you, my dear, by some twenty years, but I bide my time.”
“Ay, so I guessed. What’s your game, Avon?”
“One day I will tell you, Rupert. Not today.”
“Well, I don’t envy him if you’ve your claws on him,” said Rupert frankly.
“No, I think he is not to be envied,” said his Grace. “He should be here soon now. Infant, a trunk has been carried to your chamber. Oblige me by dressing yourself once more *à la jeune fille*.

===Chinese===

“可怜的小家伙！”公爵大人轻轻揪了揪她的一缕卷发。“不过终究是个百折不挠的小家伙。”
“剩下的也没什么好对大人细说的了。第二天我在勒阿弗尔（Le Havre）的客栈里醒过来，一直假装还在熟睡。后来马车轴断了，我就趁机逃了出来。”
“那鲁珀特呢？”公爵隔着床铺冲弟弟微微一笑。
“说真的，我觉得自己一路狂奔直到跑进这儿才停下脚步！”鲁珀特说，“我现在喘气都还没倒匀实呢。”
“噢，鲁珀特机灵得很呢！”莱昂妮插嘴道，“大人，他甚至把自己的钻石领针卖了换钱来追我，连帽子和佩剑都没带，就坐着一条又脏又破的旧船渡海来到了法国！”
“胡说八道，傻丫头，弗莱彻把他的体面海狸皮礼帽借给我了。你话太多了，莱昂妮。快闭嘴！”
“我才没有话多呢，对吧，大人？而且我说的全是实话。要不是鲁珀特，我真不知道自己会落得什么下场。”
“我也难以想象，我的姑娘。咱们确实欠了他一份天大的人情。我生平极少对旁人寄予厚望，但过去这两天里，我确实将希望托付在了他身上。”
鲁珀特顿时面红耳赤，结结巴巴起来。
“全凭莱昂妮自己有能耐。是她一路把我领到这儿来的，不管这儿到底是哪儿。贾斯汀，咱们现在到底在哪儿？”
“你们现在在勒德涅（Le Dennier），我的孩子们，距勒阿弗尔约莫十英里地。”
“好吧，好歹解开了一个谜团！”鲁珀特说，“莱昂妮抄乡间小路走得我脑袋都转不过弯来了。噢，她把圣维尔耍得团团转，我敢向你保证！”
“可要是你没赶来，我一个人根本逃不脱，”莱昂妮指出。
“话说到这份上，”鲁珀特说，“要是你没及时追上咱们，贾斯汀，天晓得会闹出什么大乱子。”
“据我所知，我这位嗜血好斗的受监护人本打算把那位亲爱的伯爵一枪——呃——击毙的。”
“没错，我绝对干得出来，”莱昂妮断言道，“唯有那样才能给他个教训！”
“确实如此，”公爵大人深表赞同。
“大人，您能替我开枪杀了他吗，求您了？”
“绝无可能，小家伙。我倒巴不得能亲眼见见那位亲爱的伯爵呢。”
鲁珀特机警地看着他。
“我可是发过誓要让他血债血偿的，贾斯汀。”
公爵大人微微一笑。
“在向他复仇这件事上，亲爱的，我比你早了大概二十年，可我一直在静待时机。”
“是啊，我早就猜到了。阿翁，你到底在下一盘什么大棋？”
“有朝一日我会告诉你的，鲁珀特。但绝非今天。”
“行吧，要是被你的利爪按住了，我可真不同情他，”鲁珀特坦率地说道。
“是啊，我想他确实不值得同情，”公爵大人说，“他眼下应该很快就要找过来了。小家伙，已经有一只大皮箱搬进你房里了。劳驾你去换回大家闺秀（à la jeune fille）的装束吧。

===Original===

You will find a package sent by my Lady Fanny, which contains, I believe, a sprigged muslin. Put it on: it should suit you.”
“Why, Monseigneur, did you bring my clothes?” cried Léonie.
“I did, my child.”
“By Gad, you’re an efficient devil!” remarked Rupert. “Come Justin! Tell us your part in the venture.”
“Yes, Monseigneur, please!” Léonie seconded.
“There is very little to tell,” sighed his Grace. “My share in the chase is woefully unexciting.”
“Let’s have it!” requested Rupert. “What brought you down to Avon so opportunely? Damme, there’s something uncanny about you, Satanas, so there is!”
Léonie fired up at that.
“You shall not call him by that name!” she said fiercely. “You only dare to do it because you are ill and I cannot fight you!”
“My esteemed ward, what is this lamentable talk of fighting? I trust you are not in the habit of fighting Rupert?”
“Oh no, Monseigneur, I only did it once! He just ran and hid behind a chair. He was afraid!”
“Small wonder!” retorted Rupert. “She’s a wildcat, Justin. It’s Have-at-you! before you know where you are, ’pon my oath it is!”
“It seems I stayed away too long,” said his Grace sternly.
“Yes, Monseigneur, much, much too long!” said Léonie, kissing his hand. “But I was good﻿—oh, many times!”
His Grace’s lips twitched. At once the dimple peeped out.
“I knew you were not really angry!” Léonie said. “Now tell us what you did.”
The Duke flicked her cheek with one finger.
“I came home, my infant, to find my house invaded by the Merivales, your duenna being prostrate with the vapours.”
“Bah, she is a fool!” said Léonie scornfully. “Why was Milor’ Merivale there?”
“I was about to tell you, my dear, when you interrupted me with your stricture upon my cousin. My Lord and Lady Merivale were there to help find you.”
“Faith, it must have been a merry meeting!” put in the irrepressible Rupert.
“It was not without its amusing side. From them I learned of your disappearance.”
“Did you think we had eloped?” Rupert inquired.
“That explanation did present itself to me,” admitted the Grace.
“Eloped?” Léonie echoed. “With *Rupert*? Ah, bah, I would as soon elope with the old goat in the field!”
“If it comes to that, I’d as soon elope with a tigress!” retorted Rupert. “Sooner, by Gad!”
“When this interchange of civilities is over,” said his Grace languidly, “I will continue. But do not let me interrupt you.”
“Ay, go on,” said Rupert.

===Chinese===

你会看到范妮夫人寄来的一个包裹，里头如果我没记错的话，是一袭带小碎花图案的细平布长裙。穿上它吧，应该很衬你。”
“哎呀，大人，您竟然把我的衣服都带过来了？”莱昂妮叫出声来。
“是的，我的孩子。”
“天哪，你真是一个无所不能的魔鬼！”鲁珀特评述道，“快说吧贾斯汀！跟咱们讲讲你在这场追击战里的经历。”
“是啊，大人，拜托了！”莱昂妮从旁附和道。
“实在没什么好说的，”公爵大人叹道，“我在这场追击中所扮演的角色平淡乏味得很。”
“快讲讲吧！”鲁珀特催促道，“究竟是什么风把你在这般恰到好处的时节吹到阿翁庄园去的？见鬼，撒旦，你身上总有种神鬼莫测的邪门劲儿，真的！”
莱昂妮一听这话顿时火冒三丈。
“不许你用那个名字叫他！”她恶狠狠地说道，“你唯独敢趁着自己卧病在床、我没法跟你决斗的时候才敢这么叫！”
“我可敬的受监护人，这又是哪门子令人扼腕的打斗狂言？我相信你平日里没有跟鲁珀特动手过招的坏毛病吧？”
“噢没有，大人，我只跟他动过一回手！他就只知道一溜烟逃跑，躲在椅子后头不敢出来。他害怕了！”
“这有什么好奇怪的！”鲁珀特反唇相讥道，“她就是个小野猫，贾斯汀。还没等你回过神来，她的‘看剑！’就已经刺到你眼前了，我敢对天发誓！”
“看来我离开的时间确实太长了些，”公爵大人神色严厉地说道。
“是的，大人，太长太长了！”莱昂妮一边吻着他的手一边说道，“可我一直很听话——噢，听话了不知多少回呢！”
公爵大人的唇角微微抽动了一下。那抹可爱的小酒窝顿时又从她脸上浮现出来。
“我就知道您不是当真动怒！”莱昂妮说，“现在快告诉我们您做了些什么吧。”
公爵伸出一根手指轻弹了弹她的脸颊。
“我回到家中，我的小家伙，却发现我的宅邸被梅里维尔一家彻底占领了，你的女管事因郁闷发作正瘫软在床上。”
“呸，她纯粹是个蠢人！”莱昂妮轻蔑地说道，“梅里维尔勋爵跑那儿去干嘛？”
“我正打算细说呢，亲爱的，却被你对我表姐的一通严厉评判给打断了。梅里维尔勋爵与夫人是为了帮着搜寻你的下落才赶到那儿的。”
“哈，那场面一定热闹非凡！”生性不羁的鲁珀特插嘴道。
“倒也确实不乏妙趣横生之处。从他们口中，我获悉了你的失踪。”
“你当时该不会以为我们俩私奔了吧？”鲁珀特打听道。
“这种合情合理的解释确实曾在我脑海里闪现过，”公爵承认道。
“私奔？”莱昂妮尖叫道，“同鲁珀特私奔？啊，呸，我还不如跟田地里那头老山羊私奔呢！”
“真要说到这份上，我还宁可同一头母老虎私奔呢！”鲁珀特回敬道，“老天作证，绝对宁愿选母老虎！”
“等二位这番彬彬有礼的寒暄告一段落，”公爵大人懒洋洋地说道，“我再继续往下讲。不过可别让我打断了你们二位的雅兴。”
“行啦，快讲吧，”鲁珀特说。

===Original===

“What next?”
“Next, my children, Mr. Manvers bounced in upon us. I fear that Mr. Manvers is not pleased with you, Rupert, or with me, but let that pass. From him I gathered that you, Rupert, had gone off in pursuit of a coach containing a French gentleman. After that it was easy. I journeyed that night to Southampton﻿—you did not think to board the *Queen*, boy?”
“I remembered her, but I was in no mood to waste time riding to Southampton. Go on.”
“For which I thank you. You would undoubtedly have sold her had you taken her to France. I crossed in her yesterday, and came into Le Havre at sundown. There, my children, I made sundry inquiries, and there also I spent the night. From the innkeeper I learned that Saint-Vire had set off with Léonie by coach for Rouen at two in the afternoon, and further that you, Rupert, had hired a horse half an hour or more later﻿—by the way, have you still that horse, or has it already gone the way of its fellow?”
“No, it’s here right enough,” chuckled Rupert.
“You amaze me. All this, I say, I learned from the innkeeper. It was rather too late then for me to set out in search of you, and, moreover, I half expected you to arrive at Le Havre. When you did not arrive I feared that you, Rupert, had failed to catch my very dear friend Saint-Vire. So this morning, my children, I took a coach along the road to Rouen, and came upon a derelict.” His Grace produced his snuffbox, and opened it. “My very dear friend’s coach, with his arms blazoned upon the door. It was scarcely wise of my very dear friend to leave his coach lying about for me to find, but it is possible of course, that he did not expect me.”
“He is a fool, Monseigneur. He did not know even that I was pretending to be asleep.”
“According to you, my infant, the world is peopled by fools. I believe you have reason. To resume. It seemed probable that Léonie had escaped; further, it seemed probable that she had escaped towards Le Havre. But since neither of you had arrived at that port I guessed that you were concealed somewhere on the road to Le Havre. Therefore, *mes enfants*, I drove back along the road until I came to a lane that gave on to it. Down this lane I proceeded.”
“We went across the fields,” Léonie cut in.
“A shorter way, no doubt, but one could hardly expect a coach to take it. At the hamlet I came upon they knew nothing of you.

===Chinese===

“接下来呢？”
“接下来，我的孩子们，曼弗斯先生气势汹汹地破门而入。恐怕曼弗斯先生对你很是不满，鲁珀特，对我同样毫无好感，不过这事且搁在一旁。从他口中我推断出，你，鲁珀特，策马追赶一辆载着法国绅士的马车而去了。在此之后，事情便水到渠成了。当天夜里我便赶赴南安普敦——你这小子就没想过去登‘王后号’（Queen）？”
“我记起那艘船了，可我哪有闲心大老远骑马绕道南安普敦去。接着说。”
“为此我深表感激。倘若你真把船带到了法国，你十有八九会把它给卖了换钱。昨天我乘船渡海，日落时分驶入了勒阿弗尔港。在港口，我的孩子们，我四处打探了一番，并在当地歇宿了一夜。从客栈老板那里我得知，圣维尔在下午两点便带着莱昂妮乘马车前往鲁昂（Rouen）了，而且还打听到你，鲁珀特，在半个多小时后租了一匹马——顺便问一句，那匹马眼下还在吗，还是早就步了它前任的后尘了？”
“没有，好好呆在楼下呢，”鲁珀特吃吃笑了起来。
“真叫我刮目相看。正如我所言，这些消息全是我从客栈老板那里打听到的。那会儿天色已晚，我动身追赶已然不及，况且我心里还隐隐期盼着你们或许会折返勒阿弗尔。见你们迟迟未归，我唯恐你，鲁珀特，未能拦截住我那位极亲爱的朋友圣维尔。因此今晨，我的孩子们，我雇了一辆马车沿着通往鲁昂的大道前行，途中撞见了一处残骸遗迹。”公爵大人掏出鼻烟盒，打开盒盖。“正是我那位极亲爱的朋友的马车，车门上赫然印着他的家族徽章。我那位极亲爱的朋友将马车随手扔在路旁由着我去找寻，这未免有些欠妥，不过想必，他压根没料到我会现身。”
“他是个蠢货，大人。他甚至连我一直在装睡都不知道。”
“照你看来，我的小家伙，普天之下全长满了蠢人。我想你说得不无道理。言归正传。当时看来，莱昂妮多半已经逃脱了；而且极有可能是朝着勒阿弗尔的方向逃逸的。但鉴于你们俩谁也没有抵达港口，我便推测你们定是潜藏在通往勒阿弗尔大道的某处隐蔽之地。因此，我的孩子们（mes enfants），我驾车原路折返，直到瞧见了一条岔入大路的小巷。我便沿着这条小巷一路寻来。”
“我们当时是抄穿田野走过来的，”莱昂妮插嘴道。
“想必路程要近得多，但谁也不能指望一辆四轮马车也能横穿田野吧。在途经的一处村落里，村民们对你们的行踪一无所知。

===Original===

I drove on, and came at length, by devious ways, to this place. The luck, you see, favoured me. Let us hope that my very dear friend will be equally fortunate. Infant, go and change your clothes.”
“Yes, Monseigneur. What are we going to do now?”
“That remains to be seen,” said Avon. “Away with you!”
Léonie departed. His Grace looked at Rupert.
“My young madman, has a surgeon seen your wound?”
“Ay, he came last night, confound him!”
“What said he?”
“Oh, naught! He’ll come again today.”
“From your expression I am led to infer that he prophesied some days in bed for you, child.”
“Ten, plague take him! But I shall be well enough by tomorrow.”
“You will remain there, nevertheless, until the worthy surgeon permits you to arise. I must send for Harriet.”
“Lord, must you? Why?”
“To chaperon my ward,” said his Grace calmly. “I hope my letter will not bring about a fresh attack of the vapours. Gaston had best start for Le Havre at once.” He rose. “I want pen, ink, and paper. I suppose I shall find them downstairs. You would be the better for an hour’s sleep, my dear.”
“But what of Saint-Vire?” Rupert asked.
“The so dear Comte is in all probability scouring the countryside. I hope to see him soon.”
“Ay, but what will you do?”
“I? I shall do precisely nothing.”
“I’d give a pony to see his face when he finds you here!”
“Yes, I do not think he will be pleased,” said his Grace, and went out.

===Chinese===

我继续驱车前行，兜兜转转，终于辗转来到了这个地方。你们瞧，运气终究偏向了我这一边。但愿我那位极亲爱的朋友届时也能享有同等的福气吧。小家伙，去换身衣裳吧。”
“遵命，大人。咱们接下来要做什么？”
“这还得拭目以待呢，”阿翁说，“去吧！”
莱昂妮退下了。公爵大人看向鲁珀特。
“我的小疯子，外科大夫给你的伤口瞧过了吗？”
“瞧过了，昨晚来的，真他妈讨人嫌！”
“大夫怎么说？”
“嗨，什么也没说！他今天还会再来。”
“从你的脸色我便能推断出，大夫定是断言你得在床上躺上几天了，孩子。”
“整整十天，真他妈见鬼！可我明儿一早准能痊愈。”
“尽管如此，在可敬的外科大夫允许你下床之前，你休想离开床铺半步。我必须写信唤哈丽雅特（Harriet）过来。”
“老天，非叫她来不可吗？为什么？”
“来给我这名受监护人当伴护监督人，”公爵大人神色平静地说道，“希望我这封信不至于引得她再次郁闷发作。加斯东最好立刻启程赶赴勒阿弗尔。”他站起身来。“我需要纸墨笔砚。我想楼下应该找得着。去睡上一个小时吧，亲爱的，这对你有好处。”
“可圣维尔那边怎么办？”鲁珀特问。
“那位亲爱的伯爵十有八九正在乡间四处搜寻呢。我希望能早点见着他。”
“是啊，可你到底打算怎么做？”
“我？我什么都不打算做。”
“我倒真乐意出二十五英镑（pony），就为了亲眼瞧瞧他发现你在这儿时脸上的精彩神情！”
“是啊，我想他大概不会太高兴，”公爵大人说道，随即迈步走了出去。

## XXX His Grace of Avon Trumps the Comte’s Ace / 阿翁公爵破掉伯爵的王牌
===Original===

The Marlings came early to Madame du Deffand’s house, and were followed shortly by Merivale and Hugh Davenant. Madame du Deffand wanted to know what had become of Léonie, and was informed that she was indisposed, and had remained at home. Rupert presently arrived in company with d’Anvau and Lavoulère, and was twitted by several people, Madame du Deffand included, on his appearance at such a function.
“Doubtless you are come to read us a madrigal or a rondeau,” Madame teased him. “*Faites voir*, milor’, *faites voir*!”
“I? No, b’Gad!” Rupert said. “I’ve never written a verse in my life! I’m come to listen, madame.”
She laughed at him.
“You will be so bored, my poor friend! Bear with us!” She moved away to greet a fresh arrival.
Under the wail of the violins which played at one end of the room, Merivale spoke to Davenant.
“Where’s Avon?”
Hugh shrugged.
“I’ve scarce set eyes on him all day. He starts for Anjou immediately after this party.”
“Then he means to strike tonight.” Merivale looked round. “I saw Armand de Saint-Vire a moment ago. Is the Comte here?”
“Not yet, I think, but I am told that both he and his wife are coming. Justin will have a large audience.”
The rooms were filling speedily. Merivale presently heard a footman announce Condé. Behind the Prince came the Saint-Vires, and the Marchérands, and the Duc and the Duchesse de la Roque. A young exquisite approached Fanny and demanded Mademoiselle de Bonnard. On being told that she was not present his face fell considerably, and he confided mournfully to my lady that he had written a madrigal to Léonie’s eyes which he had intended to read tonight. My lady commiserated him, and turned to find Condé at her elbow.
“Madame!” he bowed. “But where is *la petite*?”
Lady Fanny repeated Léonie’s excuses, and was requested to bear a graceful message to her charge. Then Condé moved away to join in a game of *bouts-rhymés*, and the wail of the violins died down to a murmur.
It was just as Madame du Deffand had called upon M. de la Douaye to read his latest poems that some slight stir arose by the door, and his Grace of Avon came in. He wore the dress he had once worn in Versailles, cloth of gold, shimmering in the candlelight. A great emerald in the lace at his throat gleamed balefully, another flashed on his finger.

===Chinese===

马林夫妇早早便来到了杜·德芳夫人（Madame du Deffand）的府邸，不多时梅里维尔（Merivale）与休·达弗南特（Hugh Davenant）也随之而至。杜·德芳夫人打听起莱昂妮（Léonie）的下落，得到的答复是她身子微有不适，留在家中歇息。鲁珀特（Rupert）随后同丹沃（d’Anvau）与拉武莱尔（Lavoulère）结伴赶来，因他罕见地出现在这等文雅聚会上，引得包括杜·德芳夫人在内的好几个人打趣取笑。
“想必勋爵是特意来为咱们朗诵一首牧歌或回旋诗的吧，”夫人打趣道，“快拿出来给咱们瞧瞧，勋爵，拿出来瞧瞧吧（Faites voir, milor, faites voir）！”
“我？不，老天作证！”鲁珀特说，“我这辈子连半句诗都没写过！我是来当听众的，夫人。”
她冲他笑了起来。
“那你可要受大罪无聊死啦，我可怜的朋友！多担待着点吧！”她说着转身迎候新到的宾客。
在房间一角小提琴凄婉如诉的琴声掩映下，梅里维尔同达弗南特低声攀谈起来。
“阿翁人在哪儿？”
休耸了耸肩。
“我今天几乎一整天都没见着他的人影。这场聚会一结束，他便要立刻动身赶赴安茹（Anjou）。”
“这么说来，他今晚便打算动手了。”梅里维尔环顾四周，“我方才瞧见了阿尔芒·德·圣维尔（Armand de Saint-Vire）。伯爵在场吗？”
“我想还没到呢，不过听闻他和夫人今晚皆会到场。贾斯汀（Justin）今晚可少不了满堂看客。”
大厅里很快宾客盈门。梅里维尔不多时便听见仆役高声通报孔代亲王（Condé）驾到。紧随亲王身后的是圣维尔一家、马尔谢兰一家（Marchérands），以及德·拉罗克公爵与公爵夫人（Duc and Duchesse de la Roque）。一位年轻俊美的纨绔子弟凑到范妮身旁打听波纳尔小姐（Mademoiselle de Bonnard）的芳踪。获悉她今晚并未出席，那年轻人顿时大失所望，神情凄恻地向夫人吐露，他特意为莱昂妮的双眸题写了一首牧歌，本打算今晚当众朗诵。夫人对他深表同情，一转身却发现孔代亲王正立在身旁。
“夫人！”亲王欠身道，“我们那位娇俏的小人儿（la petite）呢？”
范妮夫人再次代莱昂妮告假致歉，亲王便托她向受监护人转达一番体面的慰问。随后亲王走开去加入一局嵌名填字诗游戏（bouts-rhymés），小提琴的琴声也渐渐由哀婉转为低吟。
正当杜·德芳夫人邀请德·拉杜埃先生（M. de la Douaye）朗诵他的最新诗篇之际，门前忽然泛起一阵微小的骚动，阿翁公爵大人信步迈入。他身上穿着昔日在凡尔赛穿过的那套锦衣，织金面料在烛光掩映下熠熠生辉。喉头蕾丝上佩戴的一颗硕大祖母绿泛着森冷的幽光，手指上的另一颗宝石同样璀璨夺目。

===Original===

At his side was a light dress sword; in one hand he carried his scented handkerchief, and a snuffbox studded with tiny emeralds, and from one wrist hung a fan of painted chicken-skin mounted upon gold sticks.
Those who were near the door drew back to let him pass, and for a moment he stood alone, a tall, haughty figure, dwarfing the Frenchmen about him. He was completely at his ease, even a little disdainful. He raised his quizzing glass, and swept a glance round the room.
“By Gad, he’s a magnificent devil, ’pon my soul he is!” said Rupert to Merivale. “Damme if I’ve ever seen him look more regal!”
“What a dress!” said Fanny, in her husband’s ear. “You cannot deny, Edward, that he is truly handsome.”
“He has a presence,” conceded Marling.
Avon went forward across the room, and bowed over his hostess’s hand.
“Late as usual!” she scolded him. “Oh, and you still have a fan, I see! Poseur! You are just in time to hear M. de la Douaye read to us his poems.”
“The luck always favours me, madame,” he said, and inclined his head to the young poet. “May we beg m’sieur to read us his lines addressed to the Flower in her Hair?”
La Douaye flushed with pleasure, and bowed.
“I am honoured that that so poor trifle should still be remembered,” he said, and went to stand before the fireplace with a roll of papers in his hand.
His Grace crossed slowly to the Duchesse de la Roque’s couch, and sat down beside her. His eyes flickered to Merivale’s face, and from thence to the door. Unostentatiously Merivale linked his arm in Davenant’s and moved with him to a sofa that stood by the door.
“Avon makes me feel nervous,” murmured Davenant. “An impressive entrance, a striking dress, and that in his manner that sends a chill down one’s back. You feel it?”
“I do. He means to hold the stage tonight.” Merivale spoke lower still, for La Douaye’s liquid voice sounded in the first line of his poem. “He sent me to sit here. If you can catch Rupert’s eye signal to him to go to the other door.” He crossed his legs, and fixed his attention on La Douaye.
A storm of applause greeted the verses. Davenant craned his neck to see where Saint-Vire was, and caught a glimpse of him by the window. Madame de Saint-Vire was at some distance from him, and several times she looked across at him with wide apprehensive eyes.

===Chinese===

他腰间配着一柄轻巧的佩剑；一只手握着熏香手帕和一只镶嵌着细碎祖母绿的鼻烟盒，手腕上悬着一把绘有精美图画的羊皮折扇，扇骨为纯金镶制。
门旁的宾客纷纷向后退让，为他闪出一条通路。有那么片刻，他独自伫立在那里，身形修长傲岸，衬得身旁的法国绅士们皆相形见绌。他神态自若至极，眉宇间甚至隐隐带着一丝倨傲与不屑。他举起单片眼镜，目光在全场缓缓扫视了一圈。
“天哪，他真是一个神采飞扬的魔鬼，我对天发誓！”鲁珀特低声对梅里维尔说道，“我要是见过他比此刻更具帝王威仪的模样，就叫我遭天打雷劈！”
“好一身华服啊！”范妮贴在丈夫耳边轻语道，“你不得不承认，爱德华，他生得当真英俊极了。”
“确实很有威仪气度，”马林承认道。
阿翁缓步穿过大厅，俯身在女主人的手背上印下一吻。
“照例又迟到了！”她嗔怪道，“噢，我瞧见你手里居然还拿着折扇呢！爱摆花架子的家伙！你正好赶上听德·拉杜埃先生为咱们朗诵他的新作诗篇。”
“运气向来眷顾于我，夫人，”他说着，向年轻的诗人微微欠身致意，“不知咱们是否有幸能请先生为咱们朗诵那首《赠佳人鬓边繁花》？”
拉杜埃满面喜色，躬身回礼。
“拙劣之作承蒙公爵大人念念不忘，实乃在下之荣幸，”他说着，手中握着一卷诗稿，走上前在壁炉前站定。
公爵大人缓缓移步走到德·拉罗克公爵夫人的长榻前，在她身侧落座。他的目光在梅里维尔脸上一扫而过，随即投向大门方向。梅里维尔不露声色地挽起达弗南特的手臂，同他一同移步到门边的一张沙发旁坐下。
“阿翁真叫我心里直打鼓，”达弗南特低声耳语道，“这般引人注目的出场，如此华贵的锦袍，还有他骨子里透出的那股森然神气，直叫人脊梁骨发凉。你感觉到了吗？”
“感觉到了。他今晚摆明了要唱主角，”梅里维尔将声音压得更低，因为拉杜埃清亮圆润的嗓音已然念出了诗作的第一行，“是他示意我坐在这儿的。如果你能对上鲁珀特的目光，给他递个眼色，让他去守着另一扇门。”他翘起二郎腿，将注意力转向拉杜埃。
诗篇朗诵完毕，全场爆发出雷鸣般的掌声。达弗南特伸长脖子张望圣维尔身在何处，在窗边瞥见了他的身影。圣维尔夫人坐得离他有些距离，好几次战战兢兢地抬起满含忧惧的大眼睛朝他望去。

===Original===

“If Saint-Vire’s seen that Léonie’s not here he’ll be feeling that chill down his back too, methinks,” said Merivale. “I wish I knew what Avon means to do. Look at Fanny! Egad, Avon’s the only one of us who’s at his ease!”
La Douaye began to read again; followed praise, and elegant discussion. Avon complimented the poet, and moved away to the adjoining salon where some were still playing at *bouts-rhymés*. In the doorway he met Rupert. Merivale saw him pause for an instant, and say something. Rupert nodded, and lounged over to the two by the main door. He leaned over the back of the couch, and chuckled gleefully.
“Mysterious devil, an’t he?” he said. “I’ve orders to watch the other door. I’m agog with excitement, stap me if I’m not! Tony, I’ll lay you a monkey Justin wins this last round!”
Merivale shook his head.
“I’ll not bet against a certainty, Rupert,” he said. “Before he came I was assailed by doubts, but faith, the sight of him is enough to end them! The sheer force of his personality should carry the day. Even I feel something nervous. Saint-Vire, with the knowledge of his own guilt, must feel a thousand times more so. Rupert, have you any idea what he means to do?”
“Devil a bit!” answered Rupert cheerfully. He lowered his voice. “I’ll tell you something, though. This is the last soirée I’ll attend. Did you hear that fellow mouthing out his rhymes?” He shook his head severely. “Y’know it ought not to be allowed. An undersized little worm like that!”
“You’ll agree that he is something of a poet nevertheless?” smiled Hugh.
“Poet be damned!” said Rupert. “He’s walking about with a rose in his hand! A rose, Tony!” He snorted indignantly, and saw to his horror that a portly gentleman was preparing to read an essay on Love. “God save us all, who’s this old Turnip-Top?” he demanded irreverently.
“Hush, child!” whispered Lavoulère, who was standing near by. “It is the great M. de Foquemalle!”
M. de Foquemalle began to roll forth impressive periods. Rupert edged along the wall towards the smaller salon, with a look of comical dismay on his face. He came upon the Chevalier d’Anvau, who pretended to bar his passage.
“What, Rupert?” The Chevalier’s shoulders shook. “Whither away, *mon vieux*?”
“Here, let me pass!” whispered Rupert. “Damme if I can stand this!

拉杜埃再次开嗓朗诵；随之而来的是满堂赞誉与典雅精妙的高谈阔论。阿翁向诗人致以赞许，随后移步走向相连的另一间客厅，那里还有好些人在兴致勃勃地玩着嵌名填字诗游戏。在门道处他迎面撞见了鲁珀特。梅里维尔瞧见他停下脚步片刻，低声对弟弟吩咐了几句，随后微笑着走开了。鲁珀特神色变得肃穆起来。他朝四下里望了望，走到梅里维尔跟前，俯身低语道：
“阿翁吩咐我守住这扇门。他说今晚任何人都休想脱身离场。托尼，究竟要出什么大事？”
“我若是清楚就好了。贾斯汀既然这般吩咐，你只管照办便是。你身上带家伙了吗？”
“带了把随身小佩剑。阿翁打算同圣维尔摊牌吗？”
“大概是吧。你最好回到你负责的那扇门旁去，鲁珀特。拉杜埃已经念完了，德·福克马勒（de Foquemalle）正预备宣读一篇长篇大论呢。”
鲁珀特露出嫌恶的神情。
“那我就更得守在这扇门前了，”他说，“上一位念诗的成天拿朵玫瑰花在鼻子底下闻来闻去，而眼前这个老无赖眼里透着股叫我不舒服的凶光，我可不爱看。我闪人啦！”他冲着范妮大幅度地挤了挤眼，范妮正同两三位贵妇围坐在房间中央，神情虔敬地仰视着德·福克马勒先生。

The last one kept snuffing at a rose, and this old ruffian’s got a nasty look in his eye which I don’t like. I’m off!” He winked broadly at Fanny, who was sitting with two or three ladies in the middle of the room, soulfully regarding M. de Foquemalle.
In the other salon Rupert found an animated party gathered about the fire. Condé was reading his stanza amid laughter, and mock applause. A lady beckoned to Rupert.
“Come, milor’, and join us! Oh, is it my turn to read?” She picked up her paper and read out her lines. “There! It goes not well when one has heard M. le Duc’s verse, I fear. Do you leave us, Duc?”
Avon kissed her hand.
“My inspiration fails, madame. I believe I must go speak with Madame du Deffand.”
Rupert found a seat beside a lively brunette.
“Take my advice, Justin, and keep away from the other room. There’s an ill-favoured old rascal reading an essay on Love, or some such nonsense.”
“De Foquemalle, I’ll lay a pony!” cried Condé, and went to peep through the doorway. “Shall you brave it, Duc?”
M. de Foquemalle came at last to his peroration; Madame du Deffand headed the compliments that showered upon him; de Marchérand started a discussion on M. de Foquemalle’s opinions. A lull fell presently, and lackeys came in with refreshments. Learned arguments gave way to idle chatter. Ladies, sipping negus and ratafie, talked of toilettes, and the new mode of dressing the hair; Rupert, near the door he guarded, produced a dice-box, and began surreptitiously to play with a few intimates. His Grace strolled over to where Merivale stood.
“More commands?” inquired my lord. “I see Fanny has Madame de Saint-Vire in close conversation.”
His Grace waved his fan languidly to and fro.
“But one more command,” he sighed. “Just keep our amiable friend away from his wife, my dear.” He passed on to speak to Madame de Vauvallon, and was presently lost in the crowd.
Lady Fanny was complimenting Madame de Saint-Vire on her gown.
“I declare, that shade of blue is positively ravishing!” she said. “I searched the town for just such a taffeta not so long ago. La, there is that lady in puce again! Pray who may she be?”
“It is﻿—I believe it is Mademoiselle de Cloué,” Madame replied. The Vicomte de Valmé came up. “Henri, you have seen your father?”
“Yes, madame, he is with de Châtalet and another, over there.” He bowed to Fanny. “It is Milor’ Merivale, I think.
