“So you have found a use for him, Hugh? Was I not wise to bring him? Your servant, Lavoulère.”
The soft voice startled Saint-Vire, and his hand shook, so that a little liquid was spilled from his glass. Avon stood at his side, quizzing-glass raised.
“A very prince of pages,” smiled Lavoulère. “How is your luck tonight, Justin?”
“Wearisome,” sighed the Duke. “For a week it has been impossible to lose. From the dreamy expression on Hugh’s face I infer that it is not so with him.” He went to stand behind Hugh’s chair, laying a hand on his shoulder. “Belike, my dear Hugh, I shall bring you better luck.”
“I have never known you do that yet,” retorted Davenant. He set down his emptied glass. “Shall we play again?”
“By all means,” nodded Saint-Vire. “You and I are in a sad way, Davenant.”
“And shall soon be in a sadder,” remarked Hugh, shuffling the pack. “Remind me, Lavoulère, that in future I only play with you as my partner.” He dealt the cards round, and as he did so, spoke quietly to the Duke, in English. “Send the child downstairs, Alastair. You have no need of him.”
“I am as wax in your hands,” replied his Grace. “He has served his turn. Léon, you will await me in the hall.” He stretched out his hand to pick up Hugh’s cards. “Dear me!” He laid them down again, and watched the play in silence for a while.
At the end of the round Lavoulère spoke to him.
“Where is your brother, Alastair? The so charming youth! He is quite, quite mad!”
“Lamentably so. Rupert, for all I know, is either languishing in an English sponging house, or living upon my hapless brother-in-law’s bounty.”
“That is Miladi Fanny’s husband, yes? Edward Marling, n’est-ce pas? You have only the one brother and sister?”
“They more than suffice me,” said his Grace.
Lavoulère laughed.

===Chinese===

他又倒了一杯，双眼紧盯着莱昂的面庞。察觉到那道不偏不倚的注视，莱昂抬起头，坦坦荡荡地迎上了圣维尔的目光。伯爵悬着酒瓶，整整一分钟之久未再倾倒半滴酒液。
“你叫什么名字，孩子？”
“莱昂，先生。”
圣维尔笑了笑。
“没别的内容了？”
那顶红卷发的脑袋摇了摇。
“其余的我一概不知了，先生（Je ne sais plus rien, m’sieur）。”
“当真一无所知？”圣维尔继续倒酒。端起最后一只酒杯时，他又开口了。“依我看，你跟随公爵大人的时日并不长吧？”
“是的，先生。正如先生所言。”莱昂站直身子，朝对面的达弗南特望去。“先生？”
“没事了，莱昂，多谢你。”
“看来你总算替他找着差使了，休？我把他带出来岂不是英明之至？愿效犬马之劳，拉武莱尔。”
那低柔的嗓音猛地吓了圣维尔一跳，他的手不由得微微一抖，杯中的酒液洒出了些许。阿翁正立在他身侧，单柄眼镜已然高高举起。
“真是一位侍童中的王公王子呢，”拉武莱尔打趣地笑道。“今晚手气如何，贾斯汀？”
“烦闷透顶，”公爵叹了口气。“整整一周了，想输一场都难于登天。瞧瞧休脸上那副失魂落魄的模样，我猜他那头的光景怕是恰恰相反吧。”他踱步走到休的椅后立定，将一只手搭在休的肩头。“说不定，我亲爱的休，我能给你带来几分转运的好手气呢。”
“我还从未见过你带来过半点好运呢，”达弗南特反唇相讥。他搁下饮尽的酒杯。“咱们再开一局吗？”
“那是自然，”圣维尔点头赞同。“你我如今可真是同病相怜、处境凄惨啊，达弗南特。”
“待会儿怕是要惨上加惨呢，”休一边洗牌一边冷嘲热讽道。“拉武莱尔，改天提醒我一句，往后我只跟你搭档打牌。”他依次分发牌底，一边发牌，一边用英语低声对公爵说道：“把那孩子打发到楼下去，阿拉斯泰尔。你用不着他在跟前。”
“在你手里我向来任凭摆布，”公爵大人答道。“他也算派完用场了。莱昂，去门厅里候着我。”他伸手翻看了一下休抓到的底牌。“哎呀呀！”他又把牌搁了回去，默不作声地旁观了一阵出牌。
这一局将尽时，拉武莱尔转头同他搭讪。
“你那个兄弟去哪儿了，阿拉斯泰尔？那个招人喜爱的小伙子！他可真是疯得彻头彻尾啊！”
“当真可悲之至。鲁珀特（Rupert）眼下究竟在哪儿我也说不上来，要么是在英国的债务拘留所里苟延残喘，要么就是在靠我那倒霉的妹夫接济过活呢。”
“就是范妮米莱狄（Miladi Fanny）的夫君，对吧？爱德华·马林（Edward Marling），不是吗？你膝下就只有这一个弟弟和一个妹妹吗？”
“有这两个就够我受的了，”公爵大人说道。
拉武莱尔哈哈大笑起来。

===Original===

“Voyons, it amuses me, your family! Is there no love between you at all?”
“Very little.”
“And yet I have heard that you reared them, those two!”
“I have no recollection of it,” said Justin.
“Come now, Justin, when your mother died you kept a hand on the reins!” expostulated Davenant.
“But lightly, my dear. Enough only to make both a little afraid of me; no more.”
“Lady Fanny is very fond of you.”
“Yes, I believe she is occasionally,” agreed Justin calmly.
“Ah, Miladi Fanny!” Lavoulère kissed his fingertips. “Behold! How she is *ravissante*!”
“Also behold that Hugh wins,” drawled his Grace. “My compliments, Davenant.” He shifted his position slightly, so that he faced Saint-Vire. “Pray how is Madame, your charming wife, dear Comte?”
“Madame is well, I thank you, m’sieur.”
“And the Vicomte, your so enchanting son?”
“Also.”
“Not here tonight, I think?” Avon raised his glass, and through it surveyed the room. “I am desolated. No doubt you deem him too young for these delights? He is but nineteen, I believe?”
Saint-Vire laid his cards face downwards on the table, and looked angrily up at that handsome, enigmatic countenance.
“You are most interested in my son, M. le Duc!”
The hazel eyes widened and narrowed again.
“But how could it be otherwise?” asked the Duke politely.
Saint-Vire picked up his cards again.
“He is at Versailles, with his mother,” he said curtly. “My play, Lavoulère?”

===Chinese===

“得了吧（Voyons），你们这一家人可真叫人忍俊不禁！你们彼此之间难道就没有半点骨肉亲情吗？”
“少得可怜。”
“可我听说当年是你一手把他们两个拉扯大的呀！”
“我半点也记不得了，”贾斯汀说道。
“行了吧，贾斯汀，当年令堂过世时，可全凭你一人执掌大局呢！”达弗南特抗辩道。
“不过是轻描淡写地管管罢了，我亲爱的。无非教他们两个都对我敬畏三分，仅此而已。”
“范妮夫人可是极疼惜你的。”
“是啊，我想她偶尔确实如此，”贾斯汀泰然应允道。
“啊，范妮米莱狄！”拉武莱尔亲吻了一下自个儿的指尖。“瞧啊！她是何等的千娇百媚、颠倒众生（ravissante）啊！”
“你倒也瞧瞧休赢牌了，”公爵大人慢条斯理地拖着长音说道。“恭喜你了，达弗南特。”他微微挪动了一下站姿，正面朝向圣维尔。“请问令夫人近来可好，我亲爱的伯爵？”
“内人一切安好，多谢挂念，先生。”
“那令郎子爵（Vicomte）大人呢，那个招人喜爱的小生灵？”
“也一切安好。”
“今晚似乎没瞧见他的身影？”阿翁举起单柄眼镜，透过镜片巡视着整个大厅。“真是叫人好生遗憾。想必您觉得他年纪尚轻，不宜涉足此等销魂荡魄之地吧？我想他今年才不过十九岁（19）吧？”
圣维尔把手中的纸牌反扣在桌面上，怒气冲冲地抬头仰望着那张英俊而高深莫测的面孔。
“公爵大人对犬子可真是关怀备至啊！”
那双浅褐色的眼眸微微睁大，随即又眯成了一条缝。
“这又何足为奇呢？”公爵彬彬有礼地反问道。
圣维尔重新抓起手牌。
“他正同他母亲一道待在凡尔赛（Versailles）呢，”他冷硬地说道。“该我出牌了吗，拉武莱尔？”

## IX Léon and Léonie / 莱昂与莱昂妮
===Original===

Early in the next week Davenant left Paris for Lyons. On the same day Avon summoned his maître d’hôtel, Walker, to his presence, and informed him that he was leaving France on the morrow. Well accustomed to his master’s sudden decisions, Walker felt no surprise. He was a discreet personage with an unyielding countenance. For many years he had been in the Avon employ, and as he had proved himself to be scrupulously honest and trustworthy, the Duke had placed him in charge of his Paris establishment. As his Grace owned another establishment in St. James’s Square, London, and kept both open and staffed with servants, this post was one of considerable importance. It was Walker’s duty to keep the Hôtel Avon in such strictness and order that it should always be ready for the Duke or for his brother.
When Walker left the library he went below-stairs to inform Gaston, the valet, Meekin, the groom, and Léon, the page, that they must hold themselves in readiness to depart from Paris tomorrow morning. He found Léon seated on the table in the housekeeper’s room, swinging his legs and munching a slice of cake. Madame Dubois was sitting in a large chair before the fire, dolefully regarding him. She welcomed Walker with a coy smile, for she was a comely woman, but Léon, having cast one glance towards the prim figure in the doorway, tilted his head a little, and went on eating.
“*Eh bien, m’sieur!*” Madame smoothed her gown, smiling upon the maître d’hôtel.
“I crave pardon thus to have disturbed you, madame.” Walker bowed. “I came but to find Léon.”
Léon wriggled round to face him.
“You perceive me, Walker,” he said.
A slight spasm contracted Walker’s features. Alone amongst the staff Léon never gave him a prefix to his name.
“His Grace sent for me a few moments back to tell me that he is leaving for London tomorrow. I come to warn you, Léon, that you must be ready to accompany him.”
“Bah! He had told me that this morning,” said Léon scornfully.
Madame nodded.
“Yes, and he comes to eat a last cake with me, *le petit*.” She sighed gustily. “Indeed, my heart is heavy to think I must lose thee, Léon. But thou﻿—thou art glad, little ingrate!”
“I have never been to England, you see,” apologised Léon. “I am so excited, ma mère.”
“*Ah, c’est cela!* So excited that you will forget fat old Madame Dubois.”
“No, I swear I will not! Walker, will you have some of Madame’s cake?”

===Chinese===

下周初，达弗南特动身离开巴黎前往里昂。就在同一天，阿翁把掌礼管家沃克召到跟前，告知他自己翌日便要启程离开法国。沃克早对主人这种说风就是雨的脾性习以为常，因而丝毫不感到意外。他是个谨小慎微、不苟言笑的人。他在阿翁家效力多年，且已证明自己为人极其廉洁忠厚，公爵便委派他全权打理巴黎的这处府邸。鉴于公爵大人在伦敦圣詹姆斯广场（St. James’s Square, London）还坐拥另一处宅邸，且两处府邸始终保持开张、仆从齐备，这门管家差事自然非同小可。沃克的职责就是把阿翁府邸（Hôtel Avon）治理得井井有条、规矩森严，以便随时迎候公爵或其胞弟的大驾。
沃克离开书房后，下楼来到仆役房，知会贴身男仆加斯东（Gaston）、马夫米金（Meekin）以及侍童莱昂，叫他们明早做好离开巴黎的准备。他走进女管家房时，瞧见莱昂正坐在桌子上晃荡着双腿，嘴里嚼着一片蛋糕。杜布瓦夫人（Madame Dubois）坐在壁炉前一张宽大的扶手椅里，正满腹惆怅地瞧着他。她带着含羞带怯的微笑迎候沃克，毕竟她也是个颇有姿色的妇人；莱昂却只是朝门口那个刻板的身影扫了一眼，微微扬起下巴，继续若无其事地吃着蛋糕。
“哎呀，先生（Eh bien, m’sieur）！”夫人抚平裙摆，冲着掌礼管家展颜微笑。
“这般冒昧打扰夫人，万乞恕罪，”沃克欠身致意，“我不过是来找莱昂的。”
莱昂扭过身子正对着他。
“你瞧见我了，沃克，”他说。
沃克的脸颊肌肉不由自主地微微抽动了一下。在全体仆役当中，唯独莱昂从不给他加任何敬称头衔。
“公爵大人方才召见我，吩咐说他明早启程前往伦敦。我特来知会你一声，莱昂，你必须收拾停当随公爵大人出行。”
“呸！他今儿早晨早就同我讲过了，”莱昂不屑一顾地说道。
夫人连连点头。
“是啊，他特意跑来同我吃最后一块蛋糕呢，小家伙（le petit）。”她深深地叹了口气，“一想到要同你作别，莱昂，我这心口就沉甸甸的。可你呢——你倒高兴得很呢，小没良心的！”
“您瞧，我打小就没去过英国嘛，”莱昂赔笑道，“我心里可兴奋啦，大妈（ma mère）。”
“啊，可不就是嘛（Ah, c’est cela）！兴奋得连又胖又老的杜布瓦大妈都要抛到九霄云外去啦。”
“不会的，我发誓绝不会！沃克，你要尝尝大妈做的蛋糕吗？”

===Original===

Walker drew himself up.
“No, I thank you.”
“*Voyons*, he insults your skill, *ma mère*!” chuckled Léon.
“I assure you, madame, it’s no such thing.” Walker bowed to her and withdrew.
“He is like a camel,” remarked the page placidly.
He repeated this observation to the Duke next day, as they sat in the coach, bound for Calais.
“A camel?” said his Grace. “Why?”
“We‑ll﻿ ﻿…” Léon wrinkled his nose. “I saw one once, a long time ago, and I remember it walked along with its head very high, and a smile on its face, just like Walker. It was so full of dignity, Monseigneur. You see?”
“Perfectly,” yawned his Grace, leaning farther back into the corner.
“Do you think that I shall like England, Monseigneur?” asked Léon presently.
“It is to be hoped that you will, my infant.”
“And﻿—and do you think that I shall feel sick upon the ship?”
“I trust not.”
“So do I,” said Léon devoutly.
As it chanced the journey was quite uneventful. They spent one night on the road to Calais, and embarked next day on a night boat. Much to Léon’s disgust, the Duke sent him into his cabin, with orders to remain there. For perhaps the first time in all his Channel crossings Avon remained on deck. Once he went down to the tiny cabin, and finding Léon fast asleep in a chair, lifted him, and put him gently into a bunk, covering him with a fur rug. Then he went out again to pace the deck until morning.
When Léon appeared on deck next morning he was shocked to find that his master had remained there all night, and said so. Avon pulled one of his curls, and, having breakfasted, went below to sleep until Dover was reached. Then he emerged, and with becoming languor went ashore, Léon at his heels. Gaston had disembarked one of the first, and by the time the Duke arrived at the inn on the quay had roused the landlord to activity. A private parlour awaited them, with lunch set out on the table.
Léon eyed the meal with some disapproval and not a little surprise. A sirloin of English beef stood at one end of the table, flanked by a ham and some capons. A fat duck was at the other end, with pasties and puddings. There was also a flagon of burgundy, and a jug of foaming ale.
“Well, my Léon?”
Léon turned. His Grace had entered the room, and stood behind him, fanning himself. Léon looked sternly at the fan, and seeing the condemnation in his eyes Avon smiled.
“The fan does not find favour with you, infant?”

===Chinese===

沃克挺直了腰板。
“不必了，多谢好意。”
“得了吧（Voyons），他这是看不起您的手艺呢，大妈！”莱昂咯咯轻笑起来。
“我向您保证，夫人，绝无此事。”沃克向她躬身行礼，退了出去。
“他活像一只骆驼，”侍童平心静气地评价道。
翌日坐在前往加来（Calais）的马车上时，他又把这番评语讲给了公爵听。
“骆驼？”公爵大人说道，“为何这么说？”
“哎呀……”莱昂皱起鼻子，“很久以前我见过一回骆驼，我记得它迈着大步往前走，脑袋高高昂起，脸上还挂着一丝冷笑，同沃克简直一模一样。满脸都是傲慢的尊严呢，大人。您能想象吗？”
“完全能想象，”公爵大人打了个哈欠，身子进一步陷进车厢的角落里。
“大人，您觉得我会喜欢英国吗？”过了一会儿莱昂又问道。
“但愿你能喜欢吧，我的小家伙。”
“还有——还有您觉得我上了船会晕船呕吐吗？”
“我想不至于吧。”
“但愿承您吉言，”莱昂虔诚地说道。
说来也巧，这趟旅程一路上风平浪静。他们在通往加来的驿道上歇宿了一宿，次日便登上了夜航客船。公爵将莱昂打发到客舱里，勒令他待在里面不许乱跑，这教莱昂老大不高兴。在以往横渡英吉利海峡的所有航程中，阿翁大概还是头一遭整夜留在甲板上。其间他曾下到底舱那间狭窄的客舱里一次，见莱昂蜷缩在椅子里睡得正香，便把他抱起来，轻柔地安放在铺位上，拉过一张皮毛毯子替他掖好。随后他又回到甲板上踱步，直至东方既白。
翌日清晨莱昂走上甲板，惊骇地发现主人居然在甲板上吹了整整一夜的海风，便忍不住埋怨起来。阿翁轻轻揪了揪他的一缕鬈发，用过早膳后，便下舱补觉，直到客船抵达多佛（Dover）。公爵这才现身，带着恰到好处的倦怠仪态缓步登岸，莱昂形影不离地跟在他脚边。加斯东是最早离船登岸的随从之一，待公爵抵达码头边的客栈时，他早已把掌柜支使起来忙前忙后了。一间雅间正候着他们，餐桌上已然摆满了午膳。
莱昂打量着眼前的饭食，眉宇间流露出几分嫌弃和掩饰不住的诧异。餐桌的一头摆着一大块英式牛里脊，两旁簇拥着火腿和几只阉鸡。另一头是一只肥得流油的烤鸭，周围摆满了肉馅饼和布丁。此外还有一整壶勃艮第葡萄酒，以及一罐泛着泡沫的浓啤酒。
“如何，我的莱昂？”
莱昂回过身来。公爵大人已然走进屋内，正立在他身后摇着折扇。莱昂神情严肃地瞪着那柄折扇，阿翁瞧出他眼底的指责，不由得笑了。
“看来这折扇不合你的心意啊，小家伙？”

===Original===

“I do not like it at all, Monseigneur.”
“You distress me. What think you of our English meats?”
Léon shook his head.
“Terrible, Monseigneur. It is﻿—it is *barbare*!”
The Duke laughed, and came to the table. At once Léon went to him, intending to stand behind his chair.
“Child, you will observe that two places are laid. Seat yourself.” He shook out his napkin, and picked up the carving-knife and fork. “Will you essay the duck?”
Léon sat down shyly.
“Yes, please, Monseigneur.” He was served, and began to eat, rather nervously, but daintily, as Avon saw.
“So﻿—so this is Dover,” remarked Léon presently, in a politely conversational tone.
“You are right, infant,” replied his Grace. “This is Dover. You are pleased to approve?”
“Yes, Monseigneur. It is queer to see everything English, but I like it. I should not like it if you were not here, of course.”
Avon poured some burgundy into his glass.
“I fear you are a flatterer,” he said severely.
Léon smiled.
“No, Monseigneur. Did you remark the landlord?”
“I know him well. What of him?”
“He is so little, and so fat, with such a bright, bright nose! When he bowed to you, Monseigneur, I thought he would burst! It looked so droll!” His eyes twinkled.
“A horrible thought, my child. You would appear to have a slightly gruesome sense of humour.”
Léon gave a delighted chuckle.
“Do you know, Monseigneur,” he said, wrestling with a stubborn joint, “I had never seen the sea until yesterday! It is very wonderful, but just for a little while it made the inside of me go up and down. Like that.” He described the motion with his hand.
“My dear Léon! Really, I cannot have that topic discussed at meal time. You make me feel quite ill.”
“Well, it made me feel ill, Monseigneur. But I was not sick. I shut my mouth very tightly﻿—”
Avon picked up his fan and dealt Léon a smart rap with it across the knuckles.
“Continue to keep it shut, infant, I beg of you.”
Léon rubbed his hand, looking at the Duke in aggrieved wonderment.
“Yes, Monseigneur, but﻿—”
“And do not argue.”
“No, Monseigneur. I was not going to argue. I only﻿—”
“My dear Léon, you are arguing now. I find you most wearisome.”
“I was trying to explain, Monseigneur,” said Léon, with great dignity.
“Then please do not. Confine your energy to the duck.”
“Yes, Monseigneur.” Léon continued eating in silence for perhaps three minutes. Then he looked up again.

===Chinese===

“我一点也不喜欢，大人。”
“你真叫我伤心。你觉得咱们英国的肉食如何？”
莱昂摇了摇头。
“糟糕透顶，大人。这——这简直野蛮至极（barbare）！”
公爵笑出声来，走到桌旁。莱昂立刻迎上前去，打算侍立在他的椅后。
“孩子，你且瞧瞧桌上摆了两个位子。坐下吧。”公爵抖开餐巾，抄起切肉刀和叉子。“可要尝尝这烤鸭？”
莱昂羞怯怯地坐了下来。
“好的，多谢大人。”公爵替他布了菜，他便有些局促不安地吃了起来，但吃相却甚是秀气端庄，阿翁全看在眼里。
“这么说——这么说这就是多佛了，”过了一会儿，莱昂以一种客气闲聊的口吻说道。
“你说得一点没错，小家伙，”公爵大人答道，“这便是多佛。你可还看得过眼？”
“看得过眼，大人。看着满目皆是英国风物，感觉甚是古怪，但我挺喜欢的。当然，要是没有您在身边，我可绝不会喜欢。”
阿翁将勃艮第红酒斟入杯中。
“我瞧你这是在阿谀奉承吧，”他故作威严地说道。
莱昂展颜一笑。
“绝无虚言，大人。您留意到那位掌柜了吗？”
“我认得他很久了。他怎么了？”
“他生得又矮又胖，还长着一个红彤彤、亮堂堂的鼻子！刚才他向您躬身行礼时，我都生怕他肚子会炸开呢！瞧着可滑稽了！”他的一双眸子闪烁着光芒。
“当真可怖的念头，我的孩子。看来你这幽默感未免有点阴森恐怖呢。”
莱昂欢快地咯咯轻笑起来。
“您可知道，大人，”他一边费力地对付着一块坚韧的骨肉，一边说道，“直到昨天我才头一回见到大海呢！真是一桩奇观，可有那么一阵子，我的五脏六腑简直被颠得翻江倒海。就跟这样似的。”他用手在空中比画着上下颠簸的动作。
“我亲爱的莱昂！说真的，我绝不容许在用膳时谈论此等污秽话题。你直教我倒尽了胃口。”
“可那当真教我难受极了呀，大人。不过我并没吐出来。我把嘴巴闭得紧紧的——”
阿翁顺手抄起折扇，朝他的指节上狠狠敲了一下。
“我求你最好继续闭紧嘴巴，小家伙。”
莱昂揉着手背，满腹委屈又惊奇地望着公爵。
“遵命，大人，可是——”
“休要顶嘴。”
“是，大人。我本没打算顶嘴的。我只是——”
“我亲爱的莱昂，你眼下分明就是在顶嘴。你真叫我烦透了。”
“我方才不过是想同您分辩清楚罢了，大人，”莱昂一本正经、极为庄重地说道。
“那就劳烦你别分辩了。把你那份心思全用在这只烤鸭上吧。”
“遵命，大人。”莱昂默不作声地继续埋头吃肉，大概维持了整整三分钟（3）。随后他又抬起头来。

===Original===

“When do we begin to go to London, Monseigneur?”
“What an original way of putting it!” remarked his Grace. “We begin in about an hour’s time.”
“Then when I have finished my *déjeuner* may I go for a walk?”
“I am desolated to have to refuse my permission. I want to talk to you.”
“To talk to me?” echoed Léon.
“Madness, you think? I have something of import to say. What is the matter now?”
Léon was examining a black pudding with an expression akin to loathing on his face.
“Monseigneur, this”﻿—he pointed disdainfully at the pudding﻿–﻿“this is not for *people* to eat! Bah!”
“Is aught amiss with it?” inquired his Grace.
“Everything!” said Léon crushingly. “First I am made to feel sick upon that ship, and then I am made to feel sick again by an evil﻿—pudding, you call it? *Voyons*, it is a good name! Pig-pudding! Monseigneur, you must not eat it! It will make you﻿—”
“Pray do not describe my probable symptoms as well as your own, infant. You have certainly been prodigiously ill-used, but endeavour to forget it! Eat one of those sweetmeats.”
Léon selected one of the little cakes, and started to nibble it.
“Do you always eat these things in England, Monseigneur?” he asked, pointing to the beef and the puddings.
“Invariably, my infant.”
“I think it would be better if we did not stay very long here,” said Léon firmly. “I have finished now.”
“Then come here.” His Grace had moved to the fire, and was sitting on the oaken settle. Léon sat beside him obediently.
“Yes, Monseigneur?”
Avon started to play with his fan, and his mouth was rather grim. He was frowning slightly, and Léon racked his brains to think how he could have offended his master. Suddenly Avon laid his hand on Léon’s and held it in a cool strong clasp.
“My infant, it has become necessary for me to put an end to the little comedy you and I have been playing.” He paused, and saw the big eyes grow apprehensive. “I am very fond of Léon, my child, but it is time he was Léonie.”
The little hand in his quivered.
“Monseigneur!”
“Yes, my child. You see, I have known from the very first.”
Léonie sat rigid, staring up into his face with the look of a stricken creature in her eyes. Avon put up his free hand to pat her white cheek.
“It is no such great matter after all, infant,” he said gently.
“You﻿—you won’t send me﻿—away?”
“I will not. Have I not bought you?”
“I﻿—I may still be your page?”
“Not my page, child.

“咱们什么时候开始动身去伦敦，大人？”
“这说法可真够别出心裁的！”公爵大人评论道。“大概一个钟头后咱们就‘开始动身’。”
“那么等我吃完午膳（déjeuner），我能出去溜达溜达吗？”
“很遗憾我不得不回绝你的请求。我有话要同你说。”
“同我说？”莱昂附和道。
“觉得不可思议？我有要事同你交代。这又是怎么了？”
莱昂正低头审视着一块黑布丁，脸上写满了近乎厌恶的神情。
“大人，这东西”——他轻蔑地指了指那盘布丁——“这东西哪是给人吃的呀！呸！”
“这东西有何不妥吗？”公爵大人探问道。
“浑身上下没一处妥当的！”莱昂斩钉截铁地斥道。“先是那条倒霉的船教我恶心反胃，接着又是这种恶心人的——你们管这叫布丁？得了吧（Voyons），这名字起得倒真贴切！猪食布丁！大人，您可千万碰不得！这会教您——”
“求你千万别把我的下场同你自个儿相提并论，小家伙。你这趟旅途确实受尽了委屈，但你还是尽力抛诸脑后吧！尝尝那盘点心。”
莱昂挑了一块小点心，开始一小口一小口地啃着。
“在英国你们平日里就总吃这些玩意儿吗，大人？”他指着牛肉和布丁问道。
“向来如此，我的小家伙。”
“依我看咱们最好别在这儿逗留太久，”莱昂语气笃定地说道。“我吃饱了。”
“那就到这儿来。”公爵大人已然移步到了壁炉旁，正端坐在橡木长靠椅上。莱昂顺从地在他身旁坐下。
“大人有何吩咐？”
阿翁开始漫不经心地把玩手中的折扇，嘴角绷得略显严厉。他微微蹙着眉头，莱昂绞尽脑汁也想不出自己究竟在何处触怒了主人。冷不丁，阿翁将手覆在莱昂的手背上，清凉而有力地紧紧握住。
“我的小家伙，你我演的这出小把戏，眼下不得不收场了。”他顿了顿，瞧见侍童那双大眼睛里泛起了忐忑不安的惊惧。“我很喜爱莱昂这个孩子，可现在是时候让他变回莱昂妮（Léonie）了。”
他掌心里的那只小手不由得狠狠颤抖了一下。
“大人！”
“是的，我的孩子。你瞧，我打一开始便心知肚明。”
莱昂妮浑身僵硬地僵坐在那儿，眼底流露出受伤幼兽般的绝望，怔怔地仰望着公爵的面孔。阿翁抬起空着的那只手，轻轻拍了拍她煞白的面颊。
“这终究算不上什么了不得的大事，小家伙，”他轻声细语地说道。
“您——您不会把我——赶走吧？”
“我绝不会赶你走。难道我没把你买下来吗？”
“那我——我还能继续当您身边的侍童吗？”
“侍童是当不成了，孩子。

===Original===

I am sorry, but it is not possible.”
All the rigidity went out of the slight frame. Léonie gave one great sob, and buried her face in his coat sleeve.
“Oh please! Oh please!”
“Infant, sit up! Come I object to having my coat ruined. You have not heard all yet.”
“I won’t, I won’t!” came the muffled voice. “Let me be Léon! Please let me be Léon!”
His Grace lifted her.
“Instead of my page you shall be my ward. My daughter. Is it so terrible?”
“I do not want to be a girl! Oh please, Monseigneur, please.” Léonie slipped from the settle to the floor, and knelt at his feet, gripping his hand. “Say yes, Monseigneur! Say yes!”
“No, my babe. Dry your tears and listen to me. Don’t tell me you have lost your handkerchief.”
Léonie drew it from her pocket, and mopped her eyes.
“I don’t w-want to be﻿—a girl!”
“Nonsense, my dear. It will be far more pleasant to be my ward than my page.”
“No!”
“You forget yourself,” said his Grace sternly. “I will not be contradicted.”
Léonie gulped down another sob.
“I﻿—I am sorry, Monseigneur.”
“It’s very well. As soon as we have come to London I am going to take you to my sister﻿—no, do not speak﻿—my sister, Lady Fanny Marling. You see, infant, you cannot live with me until I have found some lady to act as﻿—ah﻿—duenna.”
“I will not! I will not!”
“You will do as I say, my good child. My sister with clothe you as befits your new position, and teach you to be﻿—a girl. You will learn these things﻿—”
“I will not! Never, never!”
“﻿–﻿because I command it. Then, when you are ready, you shall come back to me, and I will present you to Society.”
Léonie tugged at his hand.
“I won’t go to your sister! I will be just Léon! You cannot make me do as you say, Monseigneur; I will *not*!”
His Grace looked down at her in some exasperation.
“If you were still my page I should know how to deal with you,” he said.
“Yes, yes! Beat me, if you like, and let me still be your page! Ah, please, Monseigneur!”
“Unhappily it is impossible. Recollect, my infant, that you are mine, and must do as I say.”
Léonie promptly collapsed into a crumpled heap beside the settle, and sobbed into the hand she held. Avon allowed her to weep unrestrainedly for perhaps three minutes. Then he drew his hand away.
“You want me to send you away altogether?”
“Oh!” Léonie started up. “Monseigneur, you would not! You﻿—oh no, no!”
“Then you will obey me. It is understood?”

我很抱歉，但这万万不能。”

“噢，求求您！求求您！”
“小家伙，坐端正！快起来，我可不乐意我的大衣被你糟践了。你还没听我把话说完呢。”
“我不听，我不听！”衣料下传来闷声闷气的哭喊。“让我当莱昂吧！求求您让我继续当莱昂吧！”
公爵大人一把将她扶坐起来。
“往后你不必当侍童了，你将是我的受监护人。我的养女。这难道很可怕吗？”
“我不想当什么姑娘家！噢，求求您了，大人，求求您了。”莱昂妮从长椅上滑跪到地上，紧紧抓着他的手，跪倒在他的脚边。“答应我吧，大人！答应我吧！”
“不行，我的宝贝。擦干你的眼泪，听我把话说完。可别同我说你把帕子弄丢了。”
莱昂妮从口袋里摸出手帕，抹了抹眼角。
“我不想——不想当——姑娘家！”
“胡闹，我亲爱的。当我的受监护人，可比当个任人支使的侍童风光惬意得多呢。”
“我不干！”
“你未免太放肆了，”公爵大人严厉地喝道，“容不得你同我顶撞。”
莱昂妮硬生生把下一记抽噎咽回肚里。
“我——我很抱歉，大人。”
“这就对了。一抵达伦敦，我便要把你交给我妹妹照料——别插嘴——我妹妹，范妮·马林夫人（Lady Fanny Marling）。你瞧，小家伙，在我物色到某位贵妇出任你的——呃——年长女伴之前，你绝不能同我同住在一个屋檐下。”
“我才不去！我死也不去！”
“你必须照我的吩咐办，我的乖孩子。我妹妹会替你置办一身配得上你全新身份的行头，教导你如何举手投足像个——真正的闺秀。你必须学好这些体统规矩——”
“我不学！我死也不学！”
“——因为这是我的严令。等到你学有所成的那一天，你便能回到我身边，由我亲自引荐你涉足上流社会。”
莱昂妮死死扯着他的手。
“我绝不去你妹妹那儿！我就要当莱昂！大人，您强迫不了我照您的话做；我横竖是不从！”
公爵大人低头审视着她，神情间带上了几分无可奈何的恼意。
“倘若你依旧是我的侍童，我倒有的是法子收拾你，”他说。
“打吧，打吧！您爱抽我鞭子就抽我鞭子，只要容我继续当您的侍童！啊，求求您了，大人！”
“遗憾的是，这绝无可能。好好记清楚，我的小家伙，你如今是属于我的人，必须一切照我的吩咐行事。”
莱昂妮当即颓然瘫软在长椅旁，缩成一团，把脸埋在她攥着的那只公爵的手掌心里号啕大哭。阿翁任由她肆无忌惮地痛哭了大概三分钟（3）。随后他抽回了手。
“你当真巴不得我把你彻底打发走吗？”
“噢！”莱昂妮猛地惊跳起来。“大人，您绝不会那么狠心的！您——噢不，绝不！”
“既然如此，你便唯有听命于我。可听明白了？”

There was a long pause. Léonie stared hopelessly into the cold hazel eyes. Her lip trembled, and a large tear rolled down her cheek.
“Yes, Monseigneur,” she whispered, and drooped her curly head.
Avon leaned forward, and put his arm about the childish figure, drawing it close.
“A very good infant,” he said lightly. “You will learn to be a girl to please me, Léonie.”
She clung to him, her curls tickling his chin.
“Will﻿—will it please you, Monseigneur?”
“Above all things, child.”
“Then﻿—I’ll try,” said Léonie, a heartbroken catch in her voice. “You won’t l-leave me with y-your sister for l-long, will you?”
“Only until I can find someone to take care of you. Then you shall go to my house in the country, and learn to curtsy, to flirt with your fan, to simper, to have the vapours﻿—”
“I﻿—won’t!”
“I hope not,” said his Grace, smiling faintly. “My dear child, there is no need for such misery.”
“I have been Léon for so﻿—so long! It will be so very, very hard!”
“I think it will,” said Avon, and took the crumpled handkerchief from her. “But you will try to learn all that you are taught, that I may be proud of my ward.”
“Could you be, Monseigneur? Of﻿—of *me*?”
“It is quite possible, my infant.”
“I should like that,” said Léonie, more happily. “I will be very good.”
The Duke’s fine lips twitched.
“So you may be worthy of me? I wish Hugh could hear.”
“Does﻿—does he know?”
“It transpired, my child, that he always knew. Allow me to suggest that you rise from your knees. So. Sit down.”
Léonie resumed her place on the settle, and gave a doleful sniff.
“I must wear petticoats, and not say bad words, and always be with a woman. It is very hard, Monseigneur. I do not like women. I wish to be with you.”
“And I wonder what Fanny will say to you?” remarked his Grace. “My sister, Léonie, is all a woman.”
“Is she like you?” asked Léonie.
“Now, how am I to take that?” inquired his Grace. “She is not like me, infant. She is golden-haired and blue-eyed. I beg your pardon?”
“I said Bah!”
“You seem partial to that observation. It is not at all ladylike, my dear. You will obey Lady Fanny, and you will not flout and scorn her because of her golden hair.”
“Of course I shall not. She is your sister, Monseigneur,” answered Léonie. “Will she like me, do you think?” She looked up at him with a troubled gleam in her eyes.
“Why not?” said his Grace flippantly.

屋里陷入了长久的死寂。莱昂妮绝望地凝视着那双冰冷的浅褐色眼眸。她的嘴唇止不住地颤抖，一滴硕大的泪珠顺着面颊滚落下来。
“听明白了，大人，”她低声抽泣着喃喃道，垂下了那一头红卷发。
阿翁俯下身，伸臂揽住那个稚嫩单薄的身躯，将她拉近怀中。
“这才是个极乖巧的小家伙，”他轻描淡写地说道。“你学着做个知书达理的姑娘家，也是为了讨我欢心呀，莱昂妮。”
她依偎在他怀里，柔软的卷发拂过他的下巴。
“这——这当真能讨大人欢心吗？”
“比天底下任何事都更能讨我欢心，孩子。”
“既然如此——那我便试上一试吧，”莱昂妮的声音里带着心碎的哽咽。“您可别把我扔在您妹妹那儿太久，成吗？”
“只消等到我替你物色到妥当的人照料你为止。到时候你便能搬去我乡间的庄园，学着行屈膝礼、摇弄折扇调情、浅笑弄姿、娇嗔装晕——”
“我——我才不学呢！”
“但愿你别学那一套，”公爵大人嘴角掠过一丝淡淡的笑意。“我亲爱的孩子，何必这般万念俱灰呢。”
“我都当了那么——那么久的莱昂了！这该有多难、多难熬啊！”
“我想确实难为你了，”阿翁一边说着，一边从她手里抽走了那方皱巴巴的手帕。“但你必须尽力把教给你的体统全都学好，好教我以我的受监护人为荣。”
“大人当真能——能以我为荣吗？”
“完全有可能，我的小家伙。”
“那我倒愿意学了，”莱昂妮的心情开朗了些许。“我一定会表现得极乖巧的。”
公爵那两片线条优美的薄唇微微一挑。
“好教你配得上我？我真巴不得休能亲耳听听这话。”
“他——他也知晓内情吗？”
“说来倒奇了，我的孩子，闹了半天他从一开始便心知肚明。容我奉劝你从地上起来吧。这就对了。坐下吧。”
莱昂妮重新在长椅上坐好，抽抽搭搭地吸了吸鼻子。
“我非得穿衬裙不可，还不能骂粗话，成天都得跟妇人扎在一堆。这日子可太难熬了，大人。我可不喜欢女人。我只想待在您身边。”
“我倒真纳闷范妮见到你时会说出什么话来呢，”公爵大人评论道。“我那个妹妹啊，莱昂妮，可是个彻头彻尾的妇人家。”
“她生得同您相像吗？”莱昂妮打听道。
“这话我可该怎么接呢？”公爵大人探问道。“她同我可半点不像，小家伙。她生着一头金发，长着一双碧眼。你说什么来着？”
“我说‘呸’！”
“你对这个字眼可真是情有独钟啊。这可半点算不上大家闺秀的教养，我亲爱的。你往后必须顺从范妮夫人，绝不许因为人家生着一头金发就嗤之以鼻、百般讥诮。”
“我自然不会啦。她毕竟是您老人家的胞妹嘛，大人，”莱昂妮答道。“您觉得她会喜欢我吗？”她抬头凝望着他，眼底闪烁着忐忑不安的光芒。
“有何不可呢？”公爵大人漫不经心地随口说道。

===Original===

A little smile flitted across Léonie’s mouth.
“Oh﻿—oh, I do not know, Monseigneur!”
“She will be kind to you for my sake.”
“Thank you,” said Léonie meekly, and with eyes downcast. Then, as Avon said nothing, she peeped up, and the roguish dimple appeared. Seeing it Avon ruffled her curls as though she still had been a boy.
“You are refreshing,” he said. “Fanny will try and make you like the rest of her sex. I believe that I do not want that.”
“No, Monseigneur. I will be just myself.” She kissed his hand, and her lip trembled. She controlled it, and smiled through her tears. “You have taken my handkerchief, Monseigneur.”

## XXV Léonie Curtsies to the Polite World / 莱昂妮向上流社会行屈膝礼
===Original===

Lady Fanny stepped back to obtain a better view of her handiwork.
“I cannot make up my mind,” she said. “Shall I put a ribbon in your hair, or﻿—no, I have it!﻿—a single white rose!” She picked one up from the table at her side. “You can well spare it from your corsage, my dear. Where is the little buckle Justin gave you?”
Léonie, seated before the mirror, held out the pearl-and-diamond ornament. My lady proceeded to fasten the rose with it above Léonie’s left ear, so that it nestled amongst the powdered curls that were skilfully arranged to resemble a coiffure. The *friseur* had worked wonders. The curls clustered thickly about the queenly little head, and just one had been coaxed to fall to the shoulder.
“It could not be better!” said my lady. “Give me the haresfoot, wench!”
Léonie’s maid handed it to her, and stood ready with the various pots.
“Just a touch of rouge, I think,” said Fanny. “The veriest suspicion﻿—so! The lipstick, girl!﻿ ﻿… Keep still, my love, I must not overdo it. There! Powder, girl!” The haresfoot fluttered over Léonie’s face. My lady studied the effect intently. “It’s very well. Now for the patches! Two, I think. Don’t wriggle, child!” Expert fingers pressed the patches on: one below the dimple, one above the cheekbone. “Famous!” cried my lady. “Mercy, look at the time! I must hurry! Stand up, Léonie, and you, girl, hand me the dress!”
Léonie stood up in her underdress of lace, ruffle upon ruffle of it falling over a great hoop to her ankles, and watched my lady shake out the folds of soft white brocade. Fanny flung it deftly over her head, so that not a hair was disturbed, pulled it over the hoop, twitched it into place, and told the maid to lace it up. Léonie’s feet peeped from beneath the lace petticoat in shoes of white satin with heels that were studded with tiny diamonds. Buckles flashed on them﻿—yet another present from Avon. Léonie pointed her toe, and regarded the effect gravely.
Fanny came to arrange a lace fichu about Léonie’s shoulders. Out of the lace they rose, sloping and very white. Fanny shook out the ruffles, tied the ribbons, and fastened the two other roses into place over the knot with a pearl pin.
“Why, madame, what is that?” asked Léonie quickly. “It is not mine, I know!”
Fanny kissed her lightly.
“Oh, it is naught but a trifle, my love, that I had a mind to give you! I beg you will not heed it!”
Léonie flushed.

===Chinese===

范妮夫人退后两步，以便更端详自己精心打扮的杰作。
“我实在拿不定主意，”她说，“我是该往你头发里插根缎带呢，还是——不，我有着落了！——插一朵白玫瑰！”她从身侧的桌上拈起一朵白玫瑰。“从你的胸花上匀出一朵来倒也无伤大雅，我亲爱的。贾斯汀送你的那枚小带扣搁在哪儿了？”
坐在妆镜前的莱昂妮递过那枚珍珠镶钻的发饰。夫人随即用它把白玫瑰固定在莱昂妮的左耳上方，使其恰到好处地掩映在精心梳理成高耸发髻的扑粉鬈发丛中。那位美发理发师（friseur）当真手艺通神。鬈发浓密地簇拥在那颗宛若女王般高贵的头颅四周，唯独有一缕发丝被巧妙地梳拢垂落在香肩上。
“再完美不过了！”夫人赞叹道，“把野兔脚胭脂刷拿过来，丫头！”
莱昂妮的贴身侍女把胭脂刷递上前去，捧着各色妆盒垂手侍立一旁。
“我想，只消略施粉黛即可，”范妮说道，“只要那么若有若无的一抹——对，就这样！拿口红来，丫头！……别乱动，我的宝贝，可不能涂得太浓重了。好啦！拿香粉来，丫头！”野兔脚胭脂刷在莱昂妮的面庞上轻盈拂过。夫人全神贯注地审视着妆效。“极好。现在该贴面靥花钿了！我想贴两张（2）便够了。别乱晃，孩子！”熟稔的手指迅速将面靥按压服帖：一枚贴在酒窝下方，一枚贴在颧骨上方。“妙极了！”夫人失声惊呼，“天哪，瞧瞧都什么时辰了！我得抓紧了！站起身来，莱昂妮；还有你这丫头，把那件礼服递给我！”
莱昂妮身着蕾丝衬裙站起身来，层层叠叠的蕾丝花边覆在巨大的裙撑之上，一直垂落到脚踝，她眼睁睁看着夫人抖开那袭柔软白锦缎礼服的褶皱。范妮手法利落地将裙摆从她头顶罩下，未曾碰乱半根青丝，继而将长裙套过裙撑，稍加拉扯抚平，吩咐侍女系紧后背的束带。莱昂妮的一双玉足从蕾丝衬裙下若隐若现，脚踩一双缀着细碎钻石的白色缎面高跟鞋。鞋面上闪耀着带扣的光芒——这又是阿翁赠予的另一件厚礼。莱昂妮绷直脚尖，神情严肃地端详着这身装束的华彩。
范妮走上前来，替莱昂妮肩头整理好一条蕾丝披肩。那两抹圆润削瘦、莹白胜雪的香肩从蕾丝中半露出来。范妮抖开褶边，系好丝带，用一枚珍珠别针将另外两朵白玫瑰固定在结扣上方。
“咦，夫人，这是何物？”莱昂妮敏锐地问道，“我知道这可不是我的东西！”
范妮在她面颊上轻轻印下一吻。
“哎呀，这不过是一件不值钱的小玩意儿罢了，我的心肝，是我特意想送你的！我求你可别往心里去！”
莱昂妮羞红了脸。

===Original===

“Madame, you are *very* good to me! Thank you!”
Someone scratched on the door; the abigail went to open it, and came back into the room with a small silver tray, on which were two packages, and white roses in a silver holder.
“For mademoiselle,” smiled the maid.
Léonie ran forward.
“For me? Who sent them?” She bent over the tray to read the cards. “Rupert﻿—M. Marling﻿—M. Davenant! But how they are kind! Why do you all give me presents, madame?”
“My sweet, ’tis your first appearance. I suspect Hugh asked Justin what flowers he should send.” She picked up the bouquet. “See, child, the holder is so cunningly wrought! What says the card?”
Léonie held it between her fingers.
“ ‘To Léon, from Hugh Davenant.’ *Voyons*, I am not Léon tonight, but Mademoiselle de Bonnard! What can this be?﻿—from M. Marling﻿—oh, the little ring! Madame, look!” She slipped the wrappings from the last package, and disclosed a fan of delicately painted chicken-skin mounted on ivory sticks. “Oh, this clever Rupert! Madame, how did he know I wanted a fan?”
Fanny shook her head mysteriously.
“La, child, don’t ask me! Stop skipping round the room, stupid! Where are Justin’s pearls?”
“Oh, the pearls!” Léonie ran to the dressing-table, and extracted the long, milky string from one of the boxes there.
Fanny twisted it twice round her neck, cast another distracted glance at the clock, sprinkled scent on to a handkerchief, and over Léonie, gave a last twitch to the brocade gown, and hurried to the door.
“You will be so late!” Léonie cried. “All because you dressed me. I will wait for you, madame, shall I?”
“Yes, child, of course! I want to be there when Jus﻿—when they see you. Come and sit with me while I finish my toilette.”
But Léonie was in no mood to sit still. She paraded in front of the mirror, curtsied to herself, fluttered her fan, and sniffed at her roses.
Rachel worked swiftly tonight, and soon my lady stood up in a gown of rose silk, with a petticoat of silver lace, and the most enormous hoop Léonie had ever seen. My lady whisked the haresfoot across her face again, slipped bracelets on to her arms, and fixed nodding feathers into her marvellous coiffure.
“Oh, madame, it is very fine, I think!” said Léonie, pausing in her perambulations to and fro.
My lady pulled a face at her own reflection.
“It matters naught what I look like tonight,” she said. “Do you like the silver lace, child?

===Chinese===

“夫人，您对我实在太好了！多谢您！”
门外传来一阵轻轻的抓门声；贴身女仆过去开门，端着一只小银托盘折返回屋，托盘里摆着两个包裹，以及一只插在银花插里的白玫瑰花束。
“这是送给小姐的，”侍女含笑说道。
莱昂妮快步迎上前去。
“送给我的？是谁送来的？”她俯身看向托盘，辨认着附带的卡片。“鲁珀特——马林先生——达弗南特先生！他们可真是体贴入微啊！夫人，你们为何全都要送我厚礼呢？”
“我心爱的小宝贝，今晚可是你头一回公开露面啊。我琢磨着休八成事先向贾斯汀打探过该送什么花束。”她拿起捧花。“瞧瞧，孩子，这花插雕琢得何等精巧绝伦！卡片上写了什么？”
莱昂妮用指尖捏着卡片。
“‘送给莱昂，休·达弗南特敬赠。’得了吧（Voyons），今晚我可不是莱昂，而是德·博纳尔小姐！这又是什么东西？——马林先生敬赠——噢，一枚精致的小戒指！夫人，您快瞧瞧！”她剥开最后一个包裹的外皮，露出一柄在细羊皮上精工细绘、配着象牙扇骨的折扇。“噢，聪慧过人的鲁珀特！夫人，他怎么会知道我正巴不得要一把折扇呢？”
范妮神神秘秘地摇了摇头。
“哎呀，孩子，可别问我！别在屋里上蹿下跳了，傻丫头！贾斯汀送你的珍珠项链搁在哪儿了？”
“噢，珍珠！”莱昂妮飞奔到梳妆台前，从那里的首饰匣里取出一串长长的乳白色珍珠项链。
范妮将项链在她颈间优雅地缠绕了两圈，又神色慌张地朝座钟瞥了一眼，往手帕上以及莱昂妮周身洒了些香水，最后扯平了那袭锦缎长裙的下摆，急匆匆地朝房门走去。
“您待会儿要迟到了！”莱昂妮失声喊道，“全是因为替我梳妆打扮耽搁了。我在这儿候着您，夫人，成吗？”
“成啊，孩子，那是自然！我想在贾斯——在他们初见你的那一刻在场。来陪我一道坐着，等我把自个儿的行头收拾停当。”
然而莱昂妮此刻哪里坐得住。她在妆镜前迈着台步走来走去，冲着镜中的自己款款行屈膝礼，轻轻摇曳折扇，低头轻嗅白玫瑰的芬芳。
蕾切尔（Rachel）今晚的手脚麻利极了，不多时，夫人便穿戴妥当亭亭玉立：身着一袭玫瑰色丝绸长裙，下衬银色蕾丝衬裙，裙摆撑着莱昂妮平生所见最为庞大的裙撑。夫人飞快地用野兔脚刷在脸上补了补粉，把手镯套在皓腕上，又将颤巍巍的羽毛插进她那令人惊叹的高耸发髻里。
“噢，夫人，依我看这可真够华贵气派的！”莱昂妮停下踱来踱去的步子由衷赞道。
夫人冲着镜中自己的倒影做了个鬼脸。
“今晚我生得是美是丑根本无关紧要，”她说。“你可中意这银色蕾丝，孩子？

===Original===

And the shoes?” She lifted her skirts and showed a pretty ankle.
“Yes, madame. I like it﻿—oh, much! Now let us go downstairs and show Monseigneur!”
“I am with you in a moment, my sweet life. Rachel, my fan and gloves! Léonie, hold your bouquet in the other hand, and slip the ribbon of your fan over your wrist. Yes, that is excellent. Now I am ready.”
“I am so excited I feel as though I should burst!” said Léonie.
“Child! Remember you are to put a guard on your tongue! Let me hear no ‘bursts’ or ‘pig-persons’ on your lips tonight, as you love me.”
“No, madame, I will remember. And not ‘breeches’ either!”
“Certainly not!” tittered Fanny, and sailed out to the staircase. At the head of it she paused, and stood aside. “Go before me, child. Slowly, slowly! Oh dear, you will break hearts, I know!” But this she said to herself.
Léonie went sedately down the broad stairway that was brilliantly lit tonight with branches of tall candles set in the niches of the wall. Below, in the hall, gathered about the fire, the gentlemen were waiting, his Grace with orders glittering on a coat of purple satin; Lord Rupert in pale blue, with much rich lacing, and an elegant flowered waistcoat; Marling in puce; and Davenant in maroon. Léonie paused halfway down the stairs and unfurled her fan.
“But look at me!” she said reprovingly.
They turned quickly at the sound of her voice, and saw her with candles on either side, a little figure, all white, from the ordered curls to the jewelled heels: white brocade cut low across the shoulders, white lace to form a petticoat, white roses at her breast and in her hand. Only her eyes were deep, sparkling blue, and her parted lips like cherries, her cheeks faintly flushed.
“You beauty!” gasped Rupert. “By﻿—Gad, you beauty!”
His Grace went forward to the foot of the stairs, and held out his hands.
“Come, *ma belle*!”
She ran down to him. He bowed low over her hand, whereat she blushed, and curtsied a little way.
“I am nice, Monseigneur, do you not think? Lady Fanny did it all, and see, Monseigneur, she gave me this pin, and Rupert gave me the flow﻿—no, the fan. It was M. Davenant gave me the flowers, and M. Marling this pretty ring!” She danced over to where they stood, just staring at her. “Thank you very much, all of you! Rupert, you are very grand tonight! I have never seen you so﻿—so tidy, and *tout à fait beau*!”

===Chinese===

还有这双鞋？”她提起裙摆，露出一截秀美的脚踝。
“中意，夫人。我喜欢得——噢，不得了！现在咱们下楼去见公爵大人吧！”
“我片刻就随你下去，我的心肝小宝贝。蕾切尔，拿我的扇子和手套来！莱昂妮，把花束换到另一只手上，把折扇的缎带套在手腕上。对，这样就无可挑剔了。我现在准备妥当了。”
“我心里激动得简直感觉自己整个人都要炸开了！”莱昂妮说道。
“孩子！记着管住你的舌头！看在我们情同母女的份上，今晚在贵客面前可别让我听见半句‘炸开’或者‘猪狗之辈’之类的话。”
“不会的，夫人，我一定牢牢记着。也绝不说‘马裤’！”
“那是自然！”范妮噗嗤一声笑出声来，仪态万方地朝楼梯口款款走去。走到楼梯顶端时，她驻足侧身让开。“走在我前面，孩子。步子放慢点，慢一点！哎呀天哪，我敢发誓你今晚非把客人们的心全揉碎不可！”不过这最后一句话，她只是暗自在心底喃喃自语。
莱昂妮端庄娴雅地迈步走下宽阔的楼梯，今晚这道楼梯在嵌于墙壁壁龛里一排排高烛台的映照下灯火通明、璀璨夺目。楼下的门厅里，几位绅士正围聚在壁炉旁静候，公爵大人身着一袭紫罗兰色缎面外套，胸前的勋章熠熠生辉；鲁珀特勋爵身着一袭浅蓝色华服，缀满了繁复华丽的金银花边，内衬一件雅致的刺绣印花背心；马林一袭暗紫褐红，达弗南特则是一身栗色华服。莱昂妮走在楼梯半途驻足停步，唰地展开手中的折扇。
“你们倒是瞧瞧我呀！”她带着娇嗔责备的口吻说道。
听到她的嗓音，众人飞快地转过身来，只见两旁烛光掩映之下，一个通体雪白的娇小身影亭亭玉立，从整齐的发卷到镶钻的高跟鞋皆是一尘不染：白锦缎长裙在削肩处低低开领，下覆白蕾丝衬裙，胸前与纤手中皆捧着白玫瑰。唯有那一双眼眸宛若深邃而闪烁的蓝宝石，微启的朱唇宛如熟透的樱桃，双颊泛着淡淡的红晕。
“你这倾国倾城的尤物！”鲁珀特倒吸了一口凉气。“老天作证，你这尤物！”
公爵大人迈步走上前，迎在楼梯脚下，伸出双手。
“下来吧，我的绝代佳人（ma belle）！”
她一路小跑下楼奔向他。公爵执起她的柔荑深深躬身亲吻，这教她羞红了双颊，微微躬身行了个屈膝礼。
“我瞧着挺标致的吧，大人，您不这么觉得吗？全凭范妮夫人一手操办的，您瞧，大人，她送了我这枚别针，鲁珀特送了我这束花——不对，是这把扇子。花是达弗南特先生送的，马林先生送了这枚精巧的戒指！”她像一只欢快的小鹿般轻盈地跃向他们站立之处，引得众人目不转睛地凝视着她。“万分感激你们大家！鲁珀特，你今晚可真是仪表堂堂！我还从没见过你打扮得这么——这么齐整体面、潇洒倜傥呢（tout à fait beau）！”

===Original===

Lady Fanny came down the stairs.
“Well, Justin? Have I succeeded?”
“My dear, you have surpassed yourself.” His eyes ran over her. “Your own toilette leaves nothing to be desired.”
“Oh!” she shrugged her shoulders. “I am naught tonight.”
“You are *très grande dame*, my dear,” he said.
“That, perhaps,” she nodded. “It was my intention.”
Rupert lifted his quizzing glass.
“You always look a beauty, Fan; I’ll say that for you.”
The lackeys about the great doorway suddenly sprang to attention.
“La, are they arriving already?” cried my lady. “Come, child!” she led the way into the big ballroom, that ran the length of the house. Léonie looked about her appreciatively.
“*Voyons*, this pleases me!” she said, and went up to one of the great baskets of flowers to inspect the frail blooms. “We are all very grand, and so is the house. Monseigneur, Rupert is beautiful, is he not?”
Avon surveyed his tall, rakish young brother.
“Would you call him beautiful?” he drawled.
“Devil take you, Justin!” spluttered his lordship.
A footman stood in the wide doorway, and rolled forth names. Rupert effaced himself, and Lady Fanny went forward.
An hour later it seemed to Léonie that the whole house was full of gaily dressed ladies and gentlemen. She had curtsied a hundred times; she still could hear my lady’s voice saying: “I have the honour to present to you Mademoiselle de Bonnard, madame, my brother’s ward.”
Very early in the evening Avon had come to her with a young man beside him: a young man dressed in the height of fashion, with orders on his breast, and a marvellous wig upon his head. Avon had said:
“My ward, Prince. Léonie, M. le Prince de Condé desires an introduction.”
She curtsied very low; Condé bent over her hand.
“But mademoiselle is *ravissante*!” he murmured.
Léonie rose from her curtsy, and smiled shyly. M. le Prince laid a hand over his heart.
“Mademoiselle will honour me for this first dance?” he said.
She thought him a charming boy, no more. She put her hand on his arm, and smiled sunnily up at him.
“Yes, please, m’sieur. It is my very own ball! Is it not exciting?”
Condé, accustomed to débutantes who were properly bored, was enchanted with this frank enjoyment. The fiddlers struck up, the couples took their places behind him and Léonie.
“Must we go first?” she asked confidentially.
“But yes, mademoiselle, surely!” he smiled. “You lead your very own ball.”

===Chinese===

范妮夫人款款走下楼梯。
“如何，贾斯汀？我可算大功告成了？”
“亲爱的，你简直超越了你自己。”他的目光在她身上流转巡视。“你自个儿的这身梳妆也无可挑剔。”
“噢！”她耸了耸香肩。“今晚我不过是个陪衬罢了。”
“你俨然是一位气派非凡的贵胄贵妇了（très grande dame），我亲爱的，”他说。
“那倒也是，”她颔首应承道，“这正是我的本意。”
鲁珀特举起单柄眼镜。
“你向来都是个大美人，范妮；这一点我不得不承认。”
大门两旁的仆役们突然个个挺胸肃立。
“哎呀，宾客们这么早就到了吗？”夫人失声惊呼，“快来，孩子！”她领头步入那间贯穿整座府邸进深的大舞厅。莱昂妮饶有兴味地环视四周。
“得了吧（Voyons），这地方真合我的心意！”她一边说着，一边走向一只巨大的花篮，俯身端详那些娇嫩欲滴的花朵。“咱们个个都穿戴得气派极了，这宅子也是如此。大人，鲁珀特生得挺俊俏的，不是吗？”
阿翁上下打量着他那身材修长、放荡不羁的胞弟。
“你管他叫俊俏？”他慢条斯理地拖长了调子。
“见鬼去吧，贾斯汀！”勋爵气急败坏地啐道。
一名男仆肃立在宽阔的大门口，高声唱报着宾客的名号。鲁珀特悄然隐退到一旁，范妮夫人迎上前去接客。
一个钟头后，在莱昂妮眼中，整座宅邸似乎已然挤满了衣着华丽的贵妇和绅士。她足足行了一百次（100）屈膝礼；耳畔至今仍回响着夫人一遍又一遍的介绍声：“夫人，我有幸向您引见德·博纳尔小姐，家兄的受监护人。”
就在晚宴伊始之际，阿翁便引着一名青年走到她跟前：那青年衣着入时奢华，胸前佩戴着显赫的勋章，头顶戴着一顶巧夺天工的高档假发。阿翁引见道：
“亲王殿下，这是我的受监护人。莱昂妮，孔代亲王殿下（M. le Prince de Condé）渴望与你结识。”
她深深地行了一记屈膝礼；孔代亲王俯身亲吻了她的纤手。
“小姐当真千娇百媚、令人销魂（ravissante）！”他低声喃喃道。
莱昂妮直起身来，羞怯地抿嘴微笑。亲王殿下把一只手抚在心口。
“不知小姐可肯赏光，与我共跳这开场的第一支舞？”他说道。
在她眼里，他不过是个招人喜爱的大男孩罢了。她把手搭在他的臂弯上，仰起脸冲他灿烂地一笑。
“好的，多谢先生。这可是专属于我自个儿的舞会呢！这难道不教人兴奋吗？”
孔代亲王平日里见惯了那些故作慵懒厌倦的初入社交界的贵媛，此刻被这份坦率真挚的欢快深深吸引住了。琴师们拉响了提琴，一对对舞伴纷纷在亲王与莱昂妮身后排好队列。
“咱们非得排在第一个吗？”她凑近他轻声打探道。
“那是自然，小姐，毫无疑问！”他微笑着说道，“你在为你自个儿的舞会领舞呢。”

===Original===

Lady Fanny, standing by the door, touched Rupert’s arm.
“Who has the child got for partner? It should be a prince of the Blood at least, by the orders! Who is it?”
“Young Condé,” Rupert answered. “You wouldn’t know him, Fan. He’s only twenty or so.”
“La, how did Justin get him here so early?” gasped my lady. “He to lead her out! She’s made for life! Look, he’s laughing! Oh, she has captivated him, never fret!” She turned her head to find Avon behind her. “Justin, *how* did you contrive to get Condé here so early? You’re a wizard, I vow.”
“Yes, it was well thought of, was it not?” said his Grace. “You will present her next to De Brionne. He is just come. Who is that child with the silver roses on her gown?”
“My dear, I don’t know! There are so many new faces I protest I cannot remember to whom they all belong! Justin, Condé is enchanted! There’s not a man in the room will not hasten to Léonie’s side having seen him so enraptured! Ah, madame!” She rustled away to greet a latecomer.
“I think I’ll go to the cardroom and take charge there,” said Rupert ingenuously, and prepared to depart.
“Quite unnecessary, my child,” said his Grace, barring the way. “Hugh has it well in hand. You, boy, will lead out Mademoiselle de Vauvallon.”
“Oh, lud!” groaned Rupert, but he moved away to where Mademoiselle was seated.
When next Fanny had leisure to observe Léonie she saw her seated on a couch in an alcove, drinking negus with her partner. The two seemed to be enjoying themselves hugely. Fanny watched, well pleased, and presently, evading the group of young men who were one and all clamouring for an introduction, she took the Comte de Brionne over to the alcove, and presented him. Condé rose, and made a leg.
“Oh, mademoiselle, you must save one little minute for me later!” he said. “When may it be?”
“We will meet somewhere,” said Léonie. “I know! Under the big palm over there, at﻿—at ten minutes past eleven!” She twinkled. “That is like an adventure!”
“Mademoiselle, I shall be there!” Condé promised, laughing.
Fanny stepped forward.
“My brother’s ward, m’sieur. M. de Brionne, Léonie.”
Léonie set down her glass, rose, and curtsied. Her brow was wrinkled. Inexorably Fanny bore Condé away.
“Mademoiselle looks worried?” De Brionne gave her her glass again.
She turned to him, and smiled engagingly.
“M’sieur, I am very stupid. I cannot remember who you are!”

===Chinese===

立在门旁的范妮夫人轻轻碰了碰鲁珀特的胳膊。
“这孩子抓到的舞伴是哪位？单看那满胸膛的勋章，起码也是位王室血统的亲王吧！究竟是哪一位？”
“年轻的孔代亲王，”鲁珀特答道。“你怕是不认得他，范妮。他不过二十岁（20）光景。”
“哎呀，贾斯汀究竟施了什么法术，竟能教他这么早就大驾光临？”夫人倒吸了一口凉气。“由他领着她跳开场舞！她这一辈子的荣华富贵可算稳如泰山了！瞧啊，他在笑呢！噢，她把他的心彻底勾走了，半点不用愁了！”她回过头，猛然发现阿翁正立在她身后。“贾斯汀，你究竟是怎么把孔代这么早就弄到这儿来的？我敢发誓你简直是个巫师。”
“是啊，这安排谋划得甚是周密，不是吗？”公爵大人说道。“待会儿你再把她引见给布里奥纳伯爵（De Brionne）。他方才刚到。礼服上缀着银玫瑰的那个女童是谁？”
“我亲爱的，我上哪儿知道去！新面孔多得数不胜数，我敢发誓我根本记不得她们全都是谁家的千金！贾斯汀，孔代整个人都神魂颠倒了！满大厅的男人们瞧见他这副如痴如醉的模样，谁还会不争先恐后地往莱昂妮身边凑啊！啊，夫人！”她裙裾窸窣地快步迎向一位迟到的贵客。
“我想我还是去牌室主持大局吧，”鲁珀特天真坦率地提议道，抬脚便打算开溜。
“大可不必，我的孩子，”公爵大人横身挡住去路。“休在那里料理得井井有条。你这小子，去请德·沃瓦隆小姐（Mademoiselle de Vauvallon）跳舞。”
“噢，老天啊！”鲁珀特哀嚎一声，却也只得悻悻地朝那位小姐坐着的角落踱步过去。
待到范妮下一次得空打量莱昂妮时，只见她正坐在偏厅壁龛的一张长榻上，同舞伴一道啜饮着热甜葡萄酒（negus）。两人似乎谈笑风生、惬意极了。范妮满心欢喜地旁观着，随后巧妙地避开了一群群争相吵着要引见结识的青年才俊，领着布里奥纳伯爵走向壁龛，向两人引见。孔代亲王站起身来，优雅地迈腿躬身行礼。
“噢，小姐，待会儿您务必为我匀出一丁点闲暇来！”他说道。“咱们何时能再叙？”
“咱们在某个地方碰头吧，”莱昂妮说。“我想好了！就在那头那株巨大的棕榈树底下，在——在十一点十分（11:10）！”她的双眸闪烁着狡黠的光彩。“这多像是一场探险啊！”
“小姐，我定会准时赴约！”孔代哈哈大笑着许诺道。
范妮跨步上前。
“先生，这位是家兄的受监护人。莱昂妮，这位是布里奥纳伯爵。”
莱昂妮搁下酒杯，起立行屈膝礼。她微微蹙起娥眉。范妮不由分说地将孔代亲王引开了。
“小姐瞧着似乎心事重重？”布里奥纳重新将酒杯递到她手中。
她转过身来，展颜露出一抹惹人怜爱的微笑。
“先生，我这个人笨极了。我实在记不得您究竟是哪一位了！”

===Original===

De Brionne was taken aback for a moment. It was not thus that young ladies were wont to address the son of Louis de Lorraine. But he could not resist the fascination of Léonie’s eyes. Moreover, where Condé had been pleased De Brionne would certainly not be affronted. He returned the smile.
“You are new come to Paris, mademoiselle?”
She nodded.
“Yes, m’sieur. Now let me think. *I* know! You are the son of the Comte d’Armagnac﻿—M. le Grand!”
The Comte was much amused. It was probable that he had never before met a lady who pondered thus naively over his genealogy. He settled down to enjoy himself, and found that he was required to name most of the people who passed, for Léonie’s edification.
“*Voyons, m’sieur*, you know everybody!” she said presently. “You are being very useful to me. Now tell me who it is dancing with Monseigneur?”
“Monseigneur?”
“Yes, the Duc﻿—my﻿—my guardian.”
“Oh﻿—! That is Madame du Deffand.”
“Truly?” Léonie regarded the lady intently. “She amuses him, I think.”
“She is a very amusing lady,” said De Brionne gravely. “Did Condé point our notables out to you?”
“No﻿—no.” Léonie dimpled. “We found such a lot of other things to talk about, m’sieur. He told me about duels, and what it is like to be a royal prince.”
De Brionne began to laugh.
“Did you ask him, mademoiselle?”
“Yes, m’sieur,” said Léonie innocently.
In the doorway Fanny was curtsying low to the Duc de Penthièvre, who had just arrived. He kissed her hand with pretty gallantry.
“My dear Lady Fanny! One was *bouleversé* when one learned of the return of the so charming Lady Fanny!”
“Ah, m’sieur!” She smiled, and spread out her fan.
Avon came up with Madame du Deffand on his arm.
“My dear Penthièvre, I am rejoiced to see you.”
“*Mon cher Duc! Madame, votre serviteur!*” He swept a bow. “Tell me, Alastair, where is this ward one hears tell of?”
“My ward﻿ ﻿… let me see, she was with De Brionne a moment ago. No, she is dancing now with my brother. In white, with the rose in her hair.”
De Penthièvre looked across the room to where Léonie was circling gracefully round Rupert. Their hands were held high, her foot was pointed, and she was laughing.
“So!” said de Penthièvre. “Our débutantes will tear their powdered locks, Duc!”
The rooms grew more crowded.

===Chinese===

布里奥纳伯爵一时之间怔住了。以往可从未有哪位年轻贵媛敢这般同路易·德·洛林的爱子说话。然而他实在无法抵挡莱昂妮眼底的那抹迷人魅力。况且，既然连孔代亲王都心满意足，布里奥纳自然也断无动怒生隙的道理。他也回以一抹微笑。
“小姐是初来巴黎吗？”
她点了点头。
“是的，先生。现在容我想想。我知道了！您是大总管大人——阿马尼亚克伯爵（Comte d’Armagnac - M. le Grand）的令郎！”
伯爵被逗得开怀大笑。很可能他平生还从未遇见过哪位名门闺秀会这般天真无邪地当面推敲揣摩他的家世谱系。他索性安下心来享受这番攀谈，很快发现自己被拉来当了差使，必须挨个儿为莱昂妮指认介绍那些经过眼前的达官显贵。
“得了吧，先生（Voyons, m’sieur），您简直无所不知啊！”过了一会儿她说道。“您对我可真是派上大用场了。现在快同我说说，那位正陪着公爵大人跳舞的贵妇是谁？”
“公爵大人？”
“是啊，那位公爵——我——我的监护人。”
“噢——！那是杜·德芳夫人（Madame du Deffand）。”
“当真？”莱昂妮目不转睛地审视着那位夫人。“依我看，她把公爵逗得挺开心的。”
“她确实是一位妙趣横生的贵妇，”布里奥纳伯爵神情严肃地答道。“孔代方才没替您指点介绍咱们这儿的名流显贵吗？”
“没——没有。”莱昂妮唇边浮现出小酒窝。“先生，我们俩找着了好多别的话题聊呢。他同我讲起了决斗的事，还有当个王室亲王究竟是何等滋味。”
布里奥纳忍不住失声大笑起来。
“是小姐主动问他的吗？”
“是啊，先生，”莱昂妮天真烂漫地答道。
大门口处，范妮正向刚刚莅临的庞蒂埃夫勒公爵（Duc de Penthièvre）深深行屈膝礼。公爵以极为考究的风度亲吻了她的纤手。
“我亲爱的范妮夫人！得知魅力四射的范妮夫人重返巴黎，当真教人欣喜若狂（bouleversé）啊！”
“啊，公爵大人！”她嫣然一笑，刷地展开折扇。
阿翁挽着杜·德芳夫人的臂弯踱步上前。
“我亲爱的庞蒂埃夫勒，见到你真叫人欣慰。”
“我亲爱的公爵！夫人，愿效犬马之劳（Mon cher Duc! Madame, votre serviteur）！”他深深作了一揖。“快同我说说，阿拉斯泰尔，大家交口称赞的那位受监护人眼下身在何处？”
“我的受监护人……容我瞧瞧，片刻前她还同布里奥纳待在一道呢。不，她眼下正同舍弟翩翩起舞呢。穿一身白裙、发间插着玫瑰的那位便是。”
庞蒂埃夫勒公爵隔着大厅望向莱昂妮，只见她正仪态万方地围绕着鲁珀特轻盈旋舞。两人双手高高牵起，她的足尖优雅地绷直点地，银铃般的笑声不绝于耳。
“好啊！”庞蒂埃夫勒公爵叹道，“公爵，咱们全巴黎的初入社交界的闺秀们怕是要把扑了香粉的鬈发全扯秃了！”
大厅里的宾客愈发摩肩接踵。

===Original===

Some time later Lady Fanny, proceeding to the refreshment room, met her husband in the hall, and said radiantly:
“My dearest love, what a success! Have you seen the child? De Penthièvre has danced with her, and Condé! Where’s Justin?”
“Gone into the little salon. You’re satisfied, sweet?”
“Satisfied! Paris will talk of naught but this ball and Léonie for weeks to come! I shall keep them talking, I promise you!” She hurried away to the refreshment room, found it crowded, with Léonie the centre of a delighted and admiring group. Fanny took a forlorn lady under her wing, and bore her off in search of a cavalier.
In the cardroom they discussed the Duke’s latest whim.
“*Mon Dieu*, Davenant, but what a beauty! What colouring! What wonderful eyes!” cried Lavoulère. “Who is she?”
The Chevalier d’Anvau cut in before Hugh could reply.
“Ah, he is proud of her, is Satanas! One sees it clearly.”
“He has reason,” remarked Marrignard, toying with a dice-box. “She has not only beauty, but also espièglerie! I was amongst the fortunate who obtained her hand. Condé is greatly *épris*.”
The Chevalier looked at Hugh.
“She is like someone. I cannot think who it may be. I have racked my brains, but it eludes me.”
“Yes, it is true,” nodded Lavoulère. “When I set eyes on her it came to me in a flash that I had met her before. Is it possible that I have done so, Davenant?”
“Quite impossible,” Hugh said fervently. “She has but just come from England.”
Madame de Marguéry, playing at lansquenet at an adjacent table, looked up.
“But she is French, surely? Who were her parents?”
“I do not know, madame,” said Hugh with truth. “As you know, Justin is never communicative.”
“Oh!” Madame cried. “He loves to make a mystery! It is to intrigue us all! The child is quite charming, and wellborn, of course. That naive innocence should make her success assured. I would my daughters had it.”
Meanwhile Lady Fanny had sent Rupert to extricate Léonie from the refreshment room. She came back on my lord’s arm, and chuckled gleefully.
“Madame, M. le Prince says I have eyes like stars, and another man said that a shaft from my eyes had slain him, and﻿—”
“Fie, child!” said my lady. “Never tell me all that here! I am going to present you to Madame de la Roque. Come!”
But at midnight Léonie escaped from the ballroom, and wandered into the hall. Condé, coming from one of the other salons, met her there.

===Chinese===

过了好一阵子，范妮夫人前往冷餐间时，在门厅撞见了丈夫，满面红光地说道：
“我最心爱的夫君，何等轰动的大获全胜啊！你瞧见那孩子了吗？连庞蒂埃夫勒公爵都同她共舞了，还有孔代亲王！贾斯汀去哪儿了？”
“去那间小沙龙了。你可算心满意足了吧，亲爱的？”
“何止是心满意足！往后几周全巴黎除了这场舞会和莱昂妮之外，休想再聊别的任何话题！我向你打包票，我定要让他们念叨个没完！”她急匆匆赶往冷餐间，只见里面挤满了人，莱昂妮俨然成了一群满脸倾慕欢喜的贵客们众星辟月的核心。范妮顺手把一位形单影只的落寞贵妇拢到羽翼之下，领着她前去物色护花使者。
而在牌室里，赌客们正交口议论着公爵大人的这一最新怪癖。
“天哪（Mon Dieu），达弗南特，简直是个倾国倾城的大美人！何等明艳的肤色！何等摄人心魄的美眸啊！”拉武莱尔失声赞叹道。“她究竟是何方神圣？”
不等休开口，丹沃骑士便抢白道：
“啊，撒旦对她可是引以为傲得很呢！任谁都瞧得一清二楚。”
“他自然有骄傲的由头，”马里尼亚尔一边漫不经心地摆弄着手里的骰盅，一边评论道。“她不仅生得沉鱼落雁，更兼有一副顽皮机灵的灵气（espièglerie）！我有幸讨得了与她共舞的一曲。孔代亲王更是彻底神魂颠倒（épris）了。”
骑士扭头看着休。
“她生得很像某个人。我实在想不起究竟像谁。我绞尽脑汁，却始终抓不住端倪。”
“是啊，确实如此，”拉武莱尔连连点头附和。“方才我一打量她，脑海里电光石火般闪过一个念头，我以前绝对在哪儿见过她。达弗南特，我当真可能在哪儿见过她吗？”
“绝无可能，”休斩钉截铁地说道，“她方才刚从英国抵埠呢。”
邻桌正玩着朗斯克内牌的德·马盖里夫人（Madame de Marguéry）抬起头来。
“可她分明是个法国姑娘呀？她父母究竟是何许人也？”
“我毫不知情，夫人，”休据实相告。“您也是知道的，贾斯汀向来嘴严得很。”
“噢！”夫人失声叹道，“他就爱故弄玄虚！纯粹是为了吊咱们大家的胃口！这孩子当真惹人怜爱，出身自然也是名门望族无疑。那份不谙世事的天真烂漫，注定教她无往不利。我倒巴不得我自个儿的几个闺女也能有这份灵气呢。”
与此同时，范妮夫人打发鲁珀特去冷餐间把莱昂妮解救出来。莱昂妮挽着勋爵的胳膊折返回来，得意洋洋地咯咯轻笑。
“夫人，亲王殿下说我的双眸宛若璀璨繁星，还有一位绅士说他已被我眼底射出的丘比特之箭一箭穿心了，而且——”
“羞死人了，孩子！”夫人嗔怪道，“可千万别当着外人的面说这些！我正打算引见你结识德·拉罗克夫人呢。快来！”
然而待到午夜十二点（12:00），莱昂妮悄悄溜出了大舞厅，在门厅里漫无目的地信步游荡。正从另一间沙龙走出来的孔代亲王恰好迎面撞见了她。

===Original===

“The little butterfly! I went to look for you, mademoiselle, and could not find you.”
Léonie smiled upon him.
“Please, have you seen Monseigneur, m’sieur?”
“A dozen monseigneurs, little butterfly! Which one do you want?”
“My own Monseigneur,” said Léonie. “The Duc of Avon, of course.”
“Oh, he is in the farthest salon, mademoiselle, but shall not I do as well?”
She shook her head.
“But no, m’sieur. I want him.”
Condé took her hand, and smiled down at her.
“You are unkind, Fairy Princess! I thought you liked me just a little?”
“Yes, I do. I like you very much,” Léonie assured him. “But now I want Monseigneur.”
“Then I’ll fetch him for you at once,” Condé said gallantly.
“But no! I will go to him, m’sieur. You take me!”
Condé presented his arm promptly.
“Now you are a little kinder, mademoiselle! Is this monseigneur going to bring you to Versailles, I wonder?”
“Yes, I think so. Will you be there? Please do, m’sieur!”
“Of a certainty I shall be there. Then, at Madame de Longchamps’ rout I shall meet you, surely?”
“I do not know,” she said. “I think I am going to a great many routs, but Monseigneur has not told me which ones yet. Oh, there he is!” She released Condé’s arm, and ran forward to where his Grace was standing. “Monseigneur, I have been looking for you. The Prince brought me. Thank you very much, m’sieur!” She held out a friendly hand. “Now you will go and dance with﻿—with﻿—oh, with somebody! I do not know the names!”
Condé kissed the small hand.
“You will bring her to court, Duc?”
“To the levée next week,” said his Grace.
“Then I am satisfied,” Condé said, bowed, and left them.
The Duke looked down at his ward in some amusement.
“You dismiss royalty very summarily, Babe.”
“Oh, Monseigneur, he is quite young, and very like Rupert! He did not mind, do you think?”
“He did not appear to mind,” said the Duke. “What do you want with me, infant?”
“Nothing, Monseigneur. But I thought I would come to find you.”
“You are tired, infant.” He led her to a couch. “You shall sit quietly with me awhile.”
“Yes, please, Monseigneur. It is a very nice dance, I think. I have danced with a great many grand people, and they were all very kind to me indeed.”
“I am glad to hear it, child,” he said gravely. “How does your Prince please you?”
“Oh, he is *fort amusant*! He told me ever so many things about court, Monseigneur, and he explained who the people were﻿—oh no!

===Chinese===

“这只翩翩起舞的小蝴蝶！方才我四下里寻你，小姐，却怎么也找不着你的踪影。”
莱昂妮冲着他展颜微笑。
“烦请问一声，先生，您瞧见公爵大人了吗？”
“大厅里足有一打公爵大人呢，我的小蝴蝶！你找的是哪一位？”
“自然是我自个儿的那位公爵大人，”莱昂妮答道，“阿翁公爵大人，这还用问吗。”
“噢，他在最里头的那间沙龙里呢，小姐，不过由我陪着你难道就不成吗？”
她摇了摇头。
“那可不行，先生。我偏要找他。”
孔代执起她的纤手，含笑低头凝视着她。
“你可真狠心啊，童话里的小仙子公主！我还以为你心里多多少少总有那么一丁点喜欢我呢？”
“是啊，我确实喜欢。我喜欢你得很呢，”莱昂妮向他保证道。“可我现在非要见公爵大人不可。”
“那我这就立刻替你把他请过来，”孔代彬彬有礼地说道。
“那怎么行！我要亲自去找他，先生。你领我过去吧！”
孔代立刻殷勤地递出自己的臂弯。
“这下你可体恤宽厚多了，小姐！不知这位公爵大人是否打算带你去凡尔赛宫呢？”
“是的，我想会的。届时你也会在场吗？务必请你在场啊，先生！”
“那是自然，我必定在场。那么，在德·隆尚夫人（Madame de Longchamps）家的晚宴聚会上，想必咱们也能碰面吧？”
“我不知道，”她说。“我想我大概要去参加许许多多的晚宴，可大人迄今还没同我挑明究竟是哪些呢。噢，他在那儿呢！”她抽回挽着孔代臂弯的手，飞奔向公爵大人伫立的地方。“大人，我一直在寻您呢。是亲王领我过来的。多谢你了，先生！”她友好地伸出一只小手。“现在你可以去同——同——噢，同某位小姐跳舞去啦！那些大家闺秀的名号我可全叫不上来！”
孔代亲吻了那只小巧的柔荑。
“公爵，你届时会带她入宫觐见吗？”
“下周的朝觐早会（levée），”公爵大人答道。
“那我便心满意足了，”孔代说道，躬身行礼退下。
公爵带着几分忍俊不禁的神情低头审视着自己的受监护人。
“你打发王室王孙倒是利索得很啊，宝贝。”
“噢，大人，他年纪轻轻的，脾性同鲁珀特活像一个模子刻出来的！您觉得他会介意吗？”
“瞧着倒不像介意的模样，”公爵说。“你找我有何贵干，小家伙？”
“没什么要紧事，大人。可我心里琢磨着该来找找您。”
“你累了，小家伙。”他引着她走向一张长榻。“且随我静静坐上一会儿。”
“好的，多谢大人。依我看，这舞会真棒极了。我已经同好多位达官贵人跳过舞了，他们待我都极为客气体贴呢。”
“听到这话我倒挺欣慰的，孩子，”他神情严肃地说道。“你那位亲王殿下可还教你中意？”
“噢，他可真逗乐（fort amusant）！他同我讲了好多宫廷里的趣事，大人，还替我解释那些宾客究竟是何许人也——噢不对！

===Original===

It was M. de Brionne who did that. I said ‘Bah’ to the Prince, I am afraid, but he liked it, and he laughed. And I danced with Rupert﻿—and oh, Monseigneur, with M. d’Anvau! He said he was sure he had met me before!” Her eyes danced. “I wanted to say, ‘But yes, m’sieur. I brought you wine at Vassaud’s one night!’ ”
“I sincerely trust you did not, infant?”
“Oh no, I was very discreet, Monseigneur. I said ‘*Tiens!* Me, I do not think I have met m’sieur before.’ It was not at all true, was it?”
“Never mind, child, it was a very proper reply. And now I am going to present you to a very old friend of mine who desires speech with you. Come, infant!”
“*Qui est-ce?*” she asked.
He walked slowly with her through the salons to the hall.
“It is M. de Richelieu, my child. You will be very polite to him.”
“Yes, Monseigneur,” she said docilely, and nodded her head to a young exquisite who was smiling at her and trying to catch her eye. “I have been very polite to everyone tonight. Except Rupert, of course.”
“That goes without saying,” said his Grace, and took her back into the ballroom.
A middle-aged exquisite was standing by the fire at one end, holding animated converse with a plump lady of some beauty. Avon waited until others had gathered about his lady, and then he went forward.
Richelieu saw him, and came to meet him.
“Ah, Justin, the promised introduction! Your beautiful ward!”
Léonie took her hand from Avon’s arm, and curtsied. Richelieu bowed to her, and took her hand, and patted it.
“Child, I envy Justin. Justin, go away! I shall look after mademoiselle very well without you.”
“I don’t doubt it,” said his Grace, and went away to find Lady Fanny.
Armand de Saint-Vire pounced on him as he crossed the hall.
“My friend, who is that girl?” he demanded. “I craved an introduction. Miladi Fanny was good enough to present me. I talked with the sprite﻿—*mon Dieu, qu’elle est jolie!*﻿—and all the time I asked myself: Who is she? Who is she?”
“And did you obtain an answer from yourself?” inquired his Grace.
“No, Justin, I did not! Therefore I ask you: Who is she?”
“She is my ward, dear Armand,” smiled his Grace, and passed on as Mademoiselle de la Vogue came up.
Fanny was in the refreshment room, with Davenant. She waved to Justin as he entered.
“I have earned a moment’s repose!” she said gaily.

===Chinese===

那是布里奥纳伯爵讲给我听的。至于那位亲王嘛，我生怕自个儿方才还冲他啐了声‘呸’呢，可他倒挺受用的，还哈哈大笑。我还同鲁珀特跳了舞——噢，大人，还同丹沃先生跳过呢！他说他敢打包票以前肯定在哪儿见过我！”她的眼眸闪烁着俏皮的光芒。“我当时真巴不得脱口而出：‘那是自然，先生。有一回晚上在瓦索沙龙里，还是我端酒伺候您的呢！’”
“但愿你万万没把这话说出口吧，小家伙？”
“噢没有，我谨慎得很呢，大人。我说：‘哎呀（Tiens）！至于我嘛，我可不记得以前在哪儿见过先生呢。’这可全然是一句瞎话，不是吗？”
“无伤大雅，孩子，这话回得甚是大方得体。现在我打算引见你结识我的一位渴望与你叙话的昔日故交。来吧，小家伙！”
“是谁呀（Qui est-ce）？”她打听道。
他挽着她穿过各间沙龙，缓步走向门厅。
“是黎塞留公爵大人（M. de Richelieu），我的孩子。在他面前你务必表现得彬彬有礼。”
“遵命，大人，”她温顺恭谨地应道，顺便冲着一名正对她展颜微笑、试图引起她注意的花花公子微微颔首示意。“今晚我对每一个人都彬彬有礼极了。当然，除了鲁珀特之外。”
“那是自然之理，”公爵大人说道，领着她折返回大舞厅。
一位中年花花公子正立在舞厅尽头的壁炉旁，同迎面一位略有姿色、体态丰腴的贵妇谈笑风生。阿翁静候其他人聚拢到那位贵妇身旁后，方才迈步上前。
黎塞留瞧见了他，迎上前来。
“啊，贾斯汀，说好的引见！你那绝代风华的受监护人！”
莱昂妮从阿翁的臂弯中抽回纤手，敛衽行屈膝礼。黎塞留向她欠身还礼，握起她的玉手轻轻拍了拍。
“孩子，我可真嫉妒贾斯汀啊。贾斯汀，走开走开！没有你，我自会把小姐照料得无微不至。”
“我毫不怀疑，”公爵大人说道，转身去寻范妮夫人。
穿过门厅时，阿芒·德·圣维尔（Armand de Saint-Vire）猛地扑向了他。
“我的朋友，那姑娘究竟是何许人也？”他迫不及待地盘问道。“我渴望结识她。承蒙范妮米莱狄好心引见。我同那个小精灵攀谈了一阵——天哪，她是何等楚楚动人啊（mon Dieu, qu’elle est jolie!）——可整个过程中我心头始终在打鼓：她究竟是谁？她究竟是谁？”
“那你自个儿可曾寻出答案来了？”公爵大人探问道。
“没有，贾斯汀，我毫无头绪！正因如此我才来问你：她究竟是何方神圣？”
“她是我的受监护人，我亲爱的阿芒，”公爵大人展颜一笑，趁着德·拉沃格小姐（Mademoiselle de la Vogue）走上前来之际抽身走开了。
范妮正同达弗南特待在冷餐间里。见贾斯汀走进来，她欢快地挥手致意。
“我总算挣得了一刻清闲！”她兴高采烈地说道。

===Original===

“Lud, Justin, I’ve presented a score of children to each other and never caught one of their names! Where’s Léonie?”
“With Richelieu,” he said. “No, Fanny, you need not to be alarmed. He is under oath to be discreet. Hugh, you have been a godsend to me this night.”
My lady began to fan herself.
“We have all of us worked a little,” she said. “My poor Edward is with the dowagers, playing at ombre, and Rupert has scarce been inside the cardroom.”
“You have worked the hardest of us all,” said Hugh.
“Oh, but I have enjoyed myself so prodigiously!” she said. “Justin, I don’t know how many young beaux have not been making love to Léonie! Condé is ravished, he tells me. Do I not make a famous chaperon? When I present Léonie I feel fifty﻿—yes, Hugh, positively I do!﻿—but when I meet Raoul de Fontanges again﻿—ah, then I am back in my teens!” She cast up her eyes.
But presently people began to take their leave, and at last they were alone again in the hall, tired but triumphant.
Rupert yawned prodigiously.
“Lord, what an evening! Burgundy, Hugh?” He poured out several glasses. “Fan, you’ve torn your lace.”
Fanny sank into a chair.
“My dear, I do not care if ’tis in ribbons. Léonie, my pet, you look worn out! Oh, my poor Edward, you did nobly with the dowagers!”
“Ah yes!” said his Grace. “I have to thank you, Edward. You were quite untiring. Infant, can you still hold your eyes open?”
“Yes, Monseigneur. Oh, madame, M. le Prince said that my dress was ravishing!”
“Ay﻿—” Rupert shook his head at her. “I’d give something to know what you’ve been at this night, rogue! Did old Richelieu make love to you?”
“Oh no!” Léonie was surprised. “Why he is quite an old man!”
“Alas, poor Armand!” said his Grace. “Don’t tell him so, infant, I implore you.”
“Nor anyone, my love,” said her ladyship. “It would fly round Paris! He would be so chagrined!”
“Well, who did make love to you?” asked Rupert. “Besides Condé.”
“He didn’t, Rupert! No one did.” Léonie looked round innocently. “He only said I was a Fairy Princess. Yes, and he said that about my eyes.”
“If that’s not making﻿—” Rupert encountered a glance from his brother, and broke off. “Oh ay! I’m dumb, never fear!”
“Monseigneur,” Léonie said. “I kept thinking it was a dream! If they knew I had been a page I do not think they would have been so kind to me. They would have thought I was not enough respectable!”

===Chinese===

“天哪，贾斯汀，我今晚足足替二十来个（20）少男少女互相引见，可他们的名号我愣是一个都没记住！莱昂妮人呢？”
“同黎塞留待在一块儿呢，”他说。“不必大惊小怪，范妮。他发过重誓绝不越雷池半步。休，今晚你可真是我的救苦救难大恩人啊。”
夫人轻轻摇起折扇。
“咱们大家都出了一份力呢，”她说。“可怜的爱德华正陪着那些老封君们玩奥姆伯牌（ombre）呢，鲁珀特今晚也几乎没怎么跨进过牌室半步。”
“出力最多的当数你自个儿，”休说道。
“噢，可我自个儿也痛快尽兴得不得了！”她说。“贾斯汀，我简直数不清今晚究竟有多少花花公子排着队向莱昂妮大献殷勤！孔代亲王同我说他整个人都被勾走了魂。我这个女伴监护人当得难道不称职吗？引见莱昂妮时，我直觉得自己足有五十岁（50）高龄了——千真万确，休，我真这么觉得！——可当我再次重逢拉乌尔·德·丰唐热（Raoul de Fontanges）时——啊，我顿时又变回那个豆蔻年华的少女了！”她仰起面庞满脸神往。
不多时，宾客们开始陆续告辞散席，到最后，门厅里终于只剩下了他们一家人，虽然精疲力竭，却个个满怀大功告成的凯旋喜悦。
鲁珀特夸张地打了个哈欠。
“天哪，何等翻天覆地的一夜啊！来点勃艮第葡萄酒吗，休？”他斟满了几个酒杯。“范妮，你的蕾丝撕破了。”
范妮软绵绵地陷进扶手椅里。
“我亲爱的，哪怕撕成碎布条我也浑不在意了。莱昂妮，我的心肝小宝贝，你瞧着累坏了吧！噢，我可怜的爱德华，你今晚在那些老封君堆里干得可真够英勇绝伦的！”
“啊，正是！”公爵大人说道。“我非得好好谢谢你不可，爱德华。你当真不知疲倦。小家伙，你那双眼睛还睁得开吗？”
“睁得开，大人。噢，夫人，亲王殿下夸赞我的裙裳美得令人销魂呢！”
“是啊——”鲁珀特冲她摇了摇头。“哪怕搭上点身家，我也巴不得知道你今晚究竟耍了些什么花招呢，小滑头！黎塞留那个老家伙有没有向你求爱献殷勤？”
“噢才没有呢！”莱昂妮诧异万分。“哎呀，他分明就是个一把年纪的老头子了嘛！”
“唉，可怜的阿芒啊！”公爵大人叹道。“我求求你可千万别当着他的面讲这话，小家伙。”
“也绝不能同任何人讲，我的心肝，”夫人叮嘱道，“这会在全巴黎传得沸沸扬扬的！那非把他气得七窍生烟不可！”
“好吧，那究竟有谁向你献殷勤了？”鲁珀特追问道。“除了孔代亲王之外。”
“他也没献殷勤，鲁珀特！谁都没献殷勤。”莱昂妮一脸无辜地环顾众人。“他不过是说我是童话里的仙子公主罢了。对，他还说了关于我眼睛的那番话。”
“这要是都不算求爱献——”鲁珀特迎上兄长扫过来的凌厉目光，顿时把后半截话咽了回去。“噢，得得得！我闭嘴便是，只管放宽心！”
“大人，”莱昂妮喃喃道，“我总觉得这一切就像是一场美梦！要是他们得知我先前曾是个伺候人的侍童，我想他们绝不会对我这般和颜悦色。他们定会觉得我压根不够体面尊贵！”

## X Lady Fanny’s Virtue Is Outraged / 范妮夫人的贞淑大受冒犯
===Original===

Lady Fanny Marling, reposing on a settee, found life monotonous. She pushed away the book of poems, over which she had been yawning, and started to play with one golden curl that had strayed over her shoulder and lay glistening on the lace of her wrapper. She was en déshabillé, her fair hair unpowdered, and loosely dressed beneath a Mechlin cap whose blue ribbons were tied under her chin in a coquettish bow. She wore a blue taffeta gown, with a broad fichu about her perfect shoulders and as the room in which she sat was furnished in gold and blue and white she had reason to be pleased with herself and her setting. She was pleased, but she would have liked it better had there been someone with her to share the aesthetic pleasure. So when she heard the clang of her front-door bell her china-blue eyes brightened, and she stretched out her hand for her mirror.
In a few minutes her black page tapped upon the door. She put the mirror down, and turned her head to look at him.
Pompey grinned and bobbed his woolly head.
“Genelman to see ma’am!”
“His name?” she asked.
A soft voice spoke from behind the page.
“His name, my dear Fanny, is Avon. I am fortunate to find you at home.”
Fanny shrieked, clapped her hands, and flew up to greet him.
“Justin! You! Oh, how prodigiously delightful!” She would not permit him to kiss her fingertips, but flung her arms about his neck, and embraced him. “I declare, ’tis an age since I have seen you! The cook you sent is a marvel! Edward will be so pleased to see you! Such dishes! And a sauce at my last party which I positively cannot describe!”
The Duke disengaged himself, shaking out his ruffles.
“Edward and the cook would appear to have become entangled,” he remarked. “I trust I find you well, Fanny?”
“Yes, oh yes! And you? Justin, you cannot imagine how glad I am that you have come back! I vow I have missed you quite too dreadfully! Why what is this?” Her eyes had alighted on Léonie, wrapped in a long cloak, her tricorne in one hand, a fold of the Duke’s coat in the other.
His Grace loosened the tight hold on his garment, and allowed Léonie to clutch his hand.
“This, my dear, was, until yesterday, my page. It is now my ward.”
Fanny gasped, and fell back a pace.
“Your﻿—your *ward*! This boy? Justin, have you taken leave of your senses?”
“No, my dear, I have not. I solicit your kindness for Mademoiselle Léonie de Bonnard.”

===Chinese===

斜倚在长榻上的范妮·马林夫人只觉得日子单调乏味得紧。她随手推开那本方才看得哈欠连天的诗集，开始漫不经心地拨弄起一缕散落香肩的金色鬈发，那一缕青丝在晨袍的蕾丝花边上闪烁着耀眼的光泽。她身上只穿了件居家晨袍（en déshabillé），一头浅金色的秀发未曾施扑香粉，随意地绾在一顶梅克林蕾丝软帽（Mechlin cap）之下，软帽的蓝色缎带在下颌处打了个娇俏的蝴蝶结。她身着一袭蓝色塔夫绸长裙，完美的香肩上披着一条宽大的蕾丝披肩，鉴于她所置身的起居室通体以金色、蓝色与白色为基调装潢布置，她自然有足够的由头对自个儿以及周遭的陈设感到心满意足。她确实挺满意的，但倘若眼下能有个人陪在身侧一同分享这份赏心悦目的美感，她定会觉得更为受用。因此，当听到前门门铃清脆的当啷声骤然响起时，她那一双瓷蓝色的眸子顿时亮了起来，伸手便去摸桌上的妆镜。
不出数分钟，她的黑人小僮便轻轻敲响了房门。她放下妆镜，转过头去瞧他。
小僮庞培（Pompey）咧嘴傻笑，晃荡着一头卷发频频点头。
“外头有位大爷要见太太！”
“他尊姓大名？”她问道。
小僮身后传来一道低柔的嗓音。
“他的大名嘛，我亲爱的范妮，唤作阿翁。能在府上撞见你，当真三生有幸。”
范妮失声尖叫起来，欢快地拍着双手，像只小鸟般扑上前去迎候。
“贾斯汀！居然是你！噢，简直太教人喜出望外了！”她哪里由得他亲吻指尖，伸出双臂死死搂住他的脖颈，一把将他拥入怀中。“我敢发誓，自打上回见你，简直隔了整整一个世纪呢！你差人送来的那位大厨简直是个奇才！爱德华见着你定会高兴坏的！做出的菜肴何等绝妙！还有我上一回宴席上的那道调味汁，我简直找不出辞藻来形容它有多美妙！”
公爵好不容易从她的纠缠中挣脱开来，轻轻抖了抖衣袖上的花边。
“爱德华同那个厨子瞧着倒像是搅和在一块儿了，”他评论道。“我想你一向一切安好吧，范妮？”
“好，噢，好得很！你呢？贾斯汀，你根本想象不出得知你归来我有多高兴！我发誓我都快想死你了！咦，这又是哪一位？”她的视线猛然落在了莱昂妮身上，只见她通体裹着一件宽大的长斗篷，一手抓着三角帽，另一只手死死攥着公爵外套的一角衣摆。
公爵大人轻轻掰开她紧紧抓着衣角的小手，由着莱昂妮一把攥住自己的手掌。
“这位嘛，我亲爱的，直到昨天为止还是我身边的侍童。如今则是我的受监护人了。”
范妮倒吸了一口凉气，不由自主地连连后退了一步。
“你的——你的受监护人！这个男童？贾斯汀，你难道疯了不成？”
“没有，我亲爱的，我心智清醒得很。我特来恳请你宽仁体恤，善待莱昂妮·德·博纳尔小姐。”

===Original===

Fanny’s cheeks grew crimson. She drew her small figure up, and her eyes became haughtily indignant.
“Indeed, sir? May I ask why you bring your﻿—your ward here?”
Léonie shrank a little, but spoke never a word. Very silky became Avon’s voice.
“I bring her to you, Fanny, because she is my ward, and because I have no duenna for her. She will be glad of you, I think.”
Fanny’s delicate nostrils quivered.
“You think so? Justin, how dare you! How dare you bring her here!” She stamped her foot at him. “You have spoiled everything now! I hate you!”
“You will perhaps accord me a few minutes’ private conversation?” said his Grace. “My infant, you will await me in this room.” He went to one end of the room and opened a door, disclosing an antechamber. “Come, child.”
Léonie looked up at him suspiciously.
“You’ll not go?”
“I will not.”
“Promise! Please, you must promise!”
“This passion for oaths and promises!” sighed Avon. “I promise, my infant.”
Léonie released his hand then, and went into the adjoining room. Avon shut the door behind her, and turned to face his wrathful sister. From his pocket he drew his fan, and spread it open.
“You are really very foolish, my dear,” he said, and came to the fire.
“I am at least respectable! I think it very unkind and insulting of you to bring your﻿—your﻿—”
“Yes, Fanny? My﻿—?”
“Oh, your *ward*! It’s not decent! Edward will be very, very angry, and I hate you!”
“Now that you have unburdened yourself of that sentiment no doubt you will allow me to explain.” His Grace’s eyes were nearly shut, and his thin lips sneered.
“I do not want an explanation! I want you to take that creature away!”
“When I have told my story, and if you still wish it, I will take her away. Sit down, Fanny. The expression of outraged virtue is entirely wasted on me.”
She flounced into a chair.
“I think you are very unkind! If Edward comes in he will be furious.”
“Then let us hope that he will not come in. Your profile is enchanting, my dear, but I would sooner see both your eyes.”
“Oh, Justin!” She clasped her hands, anger forgotten. “You think it enchanting still? I vow, I thought I looked a positive fright when I looked in the mirror this morning! ’Tis age, I suppose. Oh, I am forgetting to be angry with you! Indeed, I am so thankful to see you again I cannot be cross! But you must explain, Justin.”
“I will start mine explanation, Fanny, with an announcement.

===Chinese===

范妮的双颊瞬间涨得通红。她猛地挺直了娇小的身躯，眼眸里迸射出居高临下的羞恼与愤慨。
“当真如此，先生？请问你为何要把你的——你的受监护人带到我这儿来？”
莱昂妮有些退缩地瑟缩了一下，却自始至终未曾吐露半个字。阿翁的嗓音变得愈发轻柔顺滑如丝。
“我之所以把她带到你这儿来，范妮，全因她是我的受监护人，而我手头又缺一位替她打理体统的年长女伴。我想，她定会乐意得你庇护的。”
范妮娇嫩的鼻翼微微颤动着。
“你觉得她乐意？贾斯汀，你好大的胆子！你怎么敢把她领到我这儿来！”她气急败坏地冲他跺了跺脚。“你把一切全给毁了！我恨死你了！”
“不知你是否能赏脸，同我借一步私下叙上几句？”公爵大人说道。“我的小家伙，你且在这间屋里候着我。”他踱步走到房间尽头推开一扇门，露出一间前厅小室。“来吧，孩子。”
莱昂妮满腹疑窦地抬头凝望着他。
“您不会走吧？”
“我绝不会走。”
“发誓！求求您，您必须发誓！”
“动不动就要人起誓许诺，真是服了你了！”阿翁叹了口气。“我发誓，我的小家伙。”
莱昂妮这才松开他的手，乖乖走进了隔壁的房间。阿翁在她身后将房门合拢，转过身来面对怒不可遏的妹妹。他从口袋里摸出折扇，刷地展开。
“你可真够糊涂透顶的，我亲爱的，”他说着，信步踱到了壁炉前。
“我起码还是个正经端庄的体面妇人！你把你的——你的——领到这儿来，未免太狠心、太折辱人了吧！”
“怎么了，范妮？我的什么——？”
“噢，你那个所谓的‘受监护人’！成何体统！要是爱德华撞见了，非发好大的雷霆不可，而且我恨死你了！”
“既然你已经把满腹牢骚吐了个痛快，想必眼下总该由得我好生解释一番了吧。”公爵大人的双眼几乎眯成了一条缝，薄薄的嘴角泛起一抹冷嘲热讽的笑意。
“我才不要听你什么狡辩解释呢！我只要你立刻把那个狐媚妖精弄走！”
“等我把原委道来，倘若届时你依旧坚持，我自会把她带走。坐下吧，范妮。在我面前摆出这副贞淑受辱的架势，纯粹是对牛弹琴、白费力气。”
她一甩身重重地陷进扶手椅里。
“我觉得你实在太刻薄无情了！要是爱德华撞进来，他非气疯不可。”
“那咱们便但愿他别撞进来吧。你的侧颜当真千娇百媚、颠倒众生，我亲爱的，但我更情愿同时瞧见你的那双明眸。”
“噢，贾斯汀！”她双手紧扣在心口，先前的怒火顿时抛到了九霄云外。“你当真觉得如今瞧着依然倾国倾城？我敢发誓，今儿早晨照镜子时，我还觉得自己简直丑得像个青面獠牙的怪物呢！想必是上了岁数的缘故吧。哎呀，我怎么倒忘了自个儿还在冲你动怒呢！说实在的，能重逢你我心里高兴得不得了，哪里还生得起气来！可你非得同我解释清楚不可，贾斯汀。”
“在开诚布公向你道出原委之前，范妮，我得先向你郑重宣告一桩事实。

===Original===

I am not in love with Léonie. If you will believe that it will make matters more simple.” He tossed the fan on the couch, and drew out his snuffbox.
“But﻿—but if you are not in love with her, why﻿—what﻿—Justin, I don’t understand! You are most provoking!”
“Pray accept my most humble apologies. I have a reason for adopting the child.”
“Is she French? Where did she learn to speak English? I wish you would explain!”
“I am endeavouring to do so, my dear. Allow me to say that you give me very little opportunity.”
She pouted.
“Now you are cross. Well, start, Justin! The child is pretty enough, I grant you.”
“Thank you. I found her in Paris one evening, clad as a boy, and fleeing from her unpleasant﻿—er﻿—brother. It transpired that this brother and his inestimable wife had made the child masquerade as a boy ever since her twelfth year. She was thus of more use to them. They kept a low tavern, you see.”
Fanny cast up her eyes.
“A tavern-wench!” She shuddered, and raised her scented handkerchief to her nose.
“Precisely. In a fit of﻿—let us say﻿—quixotic madness, I bought Léonie, or Léon, as she called herself, and took her home with me. She became my page. I assure you she created no little interest in polite circles. It pleased me to keep her a boy for a time. She imagined that I was in ignorance of her sex. I became a hero to her. Yes, is it not amusing?”
“It is horrid! Of course the girl hopes to intrigue you. La, Justin, how can you be such a fool?”
“My dear Fanny, when you know Léonie a little better you will not accuse her of having designs upon me. She is in very truth the infant I call her. A gay, impertinent, and trusting infant. I have a notion that she regards me in the light of a grandparent. To resume: as soon as we arrived at Dover I told her that I knew her secret. It may surprise you to hear, Fanny, that the task was damnably hard.”
“It does,” said Fanny frankly.
“I was sure it would. However, I did it. She neither shrank from me nor tried to coquette. You can have no idea how refreshing I found it.”
“Oh, I make no doubt you found it so!” retorted Fanny.
“I am glad that we understand one another so well,” bowed his Grace. “For reasons of mine own I am adopting Léonie, and because I will have no breath of scandal concerning her I bring her to you.”
“You overwhelm me, Justin.”
“Oh, I trust not!

===Chinese===

我并没爱上莱昂妮。倘若你肯深信这一点，接下来的许多事便容易想通得多了。”他将折扇随手丢在长榻上，摸出了他的鼻烟盒。
“可——可倘若你并未爱上她，那为何——究竟是何缘故——贾斯汀，我简直一头雾水！你真够磨人烦人的！”
“烦请笑纳我最谦卑由衷的歉意。我之所以收养这个孩子，自有我的深意。”
“她是个法国人吗？她上哪儿学会说一口英语的？我真巴不得你能解释个明白！”
“我这不是正竭尽所能向你分说嘛，我亲爱的。容我冒昧进一言，你压根没给我半点开口的缝隙。”
她噘起了嘴。
“这会儿你倒生起气来了。行了，快说吧，贾斯汀！这孩子模样生得倒挺标致的，这一点我承认。”
“多谢夸奖。那天傍晚我在巴黎街头撞见了她，一身男童装束，正仓皇逃避她那位面目可憎的——呃——哥哥的毒手。后来查实，自打她十二岁（12）起，那个哥哥和他的贤内助便强逼着这孩子女扮男装冒充男童。如此一来对他们开店干活便更能派上用场。您瞧，他们开着一间下等小客栈。”
范妮仰面翻了个白眼。
“一个小客栈的粗使丫头！”她浑身打了个寒战，把浸了香水的手帕掩在鼻端。
“一点没错。在一时——咱们且说是——唐吉诃德式的疯癫驱使之下，我出资买下了莱昂妮，或者说买下了她自称的莱昂，把她领回了我府上。她便成了我身旁的侍童。我向你保证，她在上流交际圈里当真掀起了不小的轰动呢。有那么一阵子，由着她继续扮作男童颇能博我一乐。她还以为我对她的女儿身一无所知呢。在她心目中，我俨然成了顶天立地的大英雄。是啊，这难道不滑稽引人发笑吗？”
“恶心透顶！那丫头分明是存心巴望着勾引你入港呢。哎呀，贾斯汀，你怎么会犯这种糊涂呢？”
“我亲爱的范妮，只要你对莱昂妮多了解几分，便绝不会指责她对我存有什么非分之想了。她骨子里当真就是我口口声声唤着的那个毛孩子。一个快活、放肆又满心依赖信任的毛孩子。我甚至琢磨着，在她眼里我活像是个祖父辈的长辈似的。言归正传：咱们一抵达多佛，我便同她挑明了我早就知晓她的底细。听闻此言你八成会大吃一惊，范妮，要挑明这桩真相，过程当真该死地艰难。”
“确实教人吃惊，”范妮坦率地说道。
“我早就料到你会这么想。无论如何，我终究是挑明了。她既未曾退避畏缩，也未曾卖弄风情。你根本想象不出那份纯真直率有多么教人如沐春风。”
“噢，我毫不怀疑你自是觉得如沐春风了！”范妮反唇相讥。
“见到你我能这般心照不宣，我甚感欣慰，”公爵大人欠身致意。“出于我自个儿的考量，我决意收养莱昂妮，而为了不使她的名声蒙受半点闲言碎语的玷污，我这才将她送到了你这里。”
“你真教我受宠若惊啊，贾斯汀。”
“噢，但愿不至于如此吧！

===Original===

I believe you told me some months ago that our cousin by marriage, the unspeakable Field, had died?”
“What has that to do with it?”
“It follows, my dear, that our respected cousin, his wife, whose name I forget, is free. I have a mind to make her Léonie’s chaperon.”
“Lud!”
“And as soon as may be I will send her and Léonie down to Avon. The infant must learn to be a girl again. Poor infant!”
“That is all very well, Justin, but you cannot expect me to house the girl! I vow ’tis preposterous! Think of Edward!”
“Pray hold me excused. I never think of Edward unless I can help it.”
“Justin, if you are minded to be disagreeable﻿—”
“Not at all, my dear.” The smile faded from his lips. Fanny saw that his eyes were unwontedly stern. “We will be serious for once, Fanny. Your conviction that I had brought my mistress to your house﻿—”
“Justin!”
“I am sure you will forgive my plain speaking. That conviction, I say, was pure folly. It has never been my custom to compromise others in my numerous affairs, and you should know that I am sufficiently strict where you are concerned.” There was peculiar meaning in his voice, and Fanny, who had once been famed for her indiscretions, dabbed at her eyes.
“How c-can you be *s-so* unkind! I do not think you are at all nice today!”
“But I trust I have made myself plain? You realise that the child I have brought you is but a child?﻿—an innocent child?”
“I am sorry for her if she is!” said her ladyship spitefully.
“You need not be sorry. For once I mean no harm.”
“If you mean her no harm how can you think to adopt her?” Fanny tittered angrily. “What do you suppose the world will say?”
“It will be surprised, no doubt, but when it sees that my ward is presented by the Lady Fanny Marling its tongue will cease to wag.”
Fanny stared at him.
“I present her? You’re raving! Why should I?”
“Because, my dear, you have a kindness for me. You will do as I ask. Also, though you are thoughtless, and occasionally exceedingly tiresome, I never found you cruel. ’Twere cruelty to turn my infant away. She is a very lonely, frightened infant, you see.”
Fanny rose, twisting her handkerchief between her hands. She glanced undecidedly at her brother.
“A girl from the back streets of Paris, of low birth﻿—”
“No, my dear. More I cannot say, but she is not born of the canaille. You have but to look at her to see that.”

===Chinese===

我记得几个月前你曾同我提起过，咱们那位因姻亲关系结识的表亲、那位不可救药的菲尔德（Field）过世了？”
“这同眼下这桩事有何相干？”
“这便意味着，我亲爱的，咱们那位备受敬重的表姐，也就是他的遗孀——我一时记不得她的名字了——如今已然恢复自由身了。我正打算聘请她出任莱昂妮的女伴监护人。”
“老天啊！”
“只要一安排妥当，我便会把她和莱昂妮打发到阿翁领地的乡间庄园去。这孩子必须重新学着做个真正的闺秀。可怜的毛孩子！”
“这全都在理，贾斯汀，可你总不能指望我把这姑娘收留在自个儿家里吧！我发誓这简直荒谬绝伦！你想想爱德华！”
“烦请免开尊口。除非万不得已，我从来懒得去想爱德华。”
“贾斯汀，要是你非要存心找茬惹人厌烦——”
“绝无此意，我亲爱的。”他嘴角的笑意悄然敛去。范妮察觉到他的目光泛起了一抹异乎寻常的冷峻严厉。“咱们就推心置腹地认真谈一回吧，范妮。你先前一口咬定是我把情妇领进了你的府门——”
“贾斯汀！”
“我相信你定会宽宥我的直言不讳。我必须说，那番断定纯粹是荒唐透顶的蠢念头。在我过往数不胜数的风流韵事当中，我向来绝无连累牵扯旁人的做派，而且你也该清楚，只要牵涉到你的清誉名节，我一向管束得有多森严。”他的嗓音里带着某种非同寻常的意味，曾以放浪轻佻闻名遐迩的范妮不由得用手帕轻轻擦拭起眼角来。
“你怎么能这么刻薄狠心！我觉得你今天简直半点都不可亲！”
“但我相信我已经把话说得够明白了吧？你总该认清，我领到你跟前来的这个孩子，不过是个货真价实的孩子吧？——一个天真无邪的孩子？”
“倘若她当真天真无邪，我倒替她感到可怜呢！”夫人恶狠狠地啐道。
“你大可不必可怜她。破天荒头一回，我绝无半点加害歹意。”
“倘若你对她全无歹意，你怎会动念头去收养她呢？”范妮气急败坏地讥笑道。“你以为外头的世人会如何议论？”
“外人自然免不了大吃一惊，但当大家瞧见我的受监护人是由范妮·马林夫人亲自引荐出阁的，闲话的长舌自然就会消停下来。”
范妮目瞪口呆地瞪着他。
“由我引见她？你简直在发疯说胡话！我凭什么要这么做？”
“全因，我亲爱的，你心里对我尚有一份兄妹深情。你自会照我的嘱托去办。况且，尽管你平日里轻浮任性、偶尔也叫人头疼烦闷得紧，但我还从未见你心肠歹毒过。将我的小家伙拒之门外，未免太残忍了。你瞧，她不过是个形单影只、受尽惊吓的孤苦孩子罢了。”
范妮站起身来，在指间把手帕绞来绞去。她举棋不定地打量着兄长。
“一个从巴黎背街陋巷里爬出来的姑娘，出身卑贱下流——”
“并非如此，我亲爱的。更多底细我眼下无可奉告，但她绝非贩夫走卒贱民（canaille）之辈。只要你拿眼瞧瞧她，便自能一清二楚。”

===Original===

“Well, a girl of whom I know naught﻿—foisted on me! I declare ’tis monstrous! I could not possibly do it! What would Edward say?”
“I am confident that you could, if you would, cajole the worthy Edward.”
Fanny smiled.
“Yes, I could, but I do not want the girl.”
“She will not tease you, my dear. I wish you to keep her close, to dress her as befits my ward, and to be gentle with her. Is it so much to ask?”
“How do I know that she will not ogle Edward, this innocent maid?”
“She is too much the boy. Of course, if you are uncertain of Edward﻿—”
She tossed her head.
“Indeed, ’tis no such thing! ’Tis merely that I’ve no wish to house a pert, redheaded girl.”
His Grace bent to pick up his fan.
“I crave your pardon, Fanny. I’ll take the child elsewhere.”
Fanny ran to him, penitent all at once.
“Indeed and you shall not! Oh, Justin, I am sorry to be so disobliging!”
“You’ll take her?”
“I﻿—yes, I’ll take her. But I don’t believe all you say of her. I’ll wager my best necklet she’s not so artless as she would have you think.”
“You would lose, my dear.” His Grace moved to the door into the antechamber, and opened it. “Infant, come forth!”
Léonie came, her cloak over her arm. At sight of her boy’s raiment Fanny closed her eyes as though in acute pain.
Avon patted Léonie’s cheek.
“My sister has promised to care for you until I can take you myself,” he said. “Remember, you will do as she bids you.”
Léonie looked shyly across at Fanny, who stood with primly set lips and head held high. The big eyes noted the unyielding pose, and fluttered up to Avon’s face.
“Monseigneur﻿—please do not﻿—leave me!” It was a despairing whisper, and it amazed Fanny.
“I shall come to see you very soon, my babe. You are quite safe with Lady Fanny.”
“I don’t﻿—want you to go away! Monseigneur, you﻿—you do not understand!”
“Infant, I do understand. Have no fear; I shall come back again!” He turned to Fanny, and bowed over her hand. “I have to thank you, my dear. Pray convey my greetings to the excellent Edward. Léonie, how often have I forbidden you to clutch the skirts of my coat?”
“I﻿—I am sorry, Monseigneur.”
“You always say that. Be a good child, and strive to bear with your petticoats.” He held out his hand, and Léonie dropped on one knee to kiss it. Something sparkling fell on to those white fingers, but Léonie turned her head away, surreptitiously wiping her eyes.

===Chinese===

“好吧，一个我底细一无所知的黄毛丫头——硬塞到我头上！我发誓这简直不可理喻！我横竖是万难从命！爱德华会怎么说？”
“我深信只要你肯移驾开恩，自有一万种法子把那个可敬的爱德华哄得团团转。”
范妮扑哧一笑。
“是啊，我确实哄得住他，可我实在不想要这丫头。”
“她绝不会给你惹半点麻烦，我亲爱的。我只消你把她好生留在内宅，替她置办一身配得上我受监护人身份的行头，待她和颜悦色些。这要求难道算得上过分吗？”
“我上哪儿知道这天真无邪的小蹄子会不会背地里冲爱德华抛媚眼？”
“她身上男童习气太重，哪里懂得那一套。当然，倘若你连爱德华都信不过的话——”
她一扬下巴。
“呸，压根没那回事！纯粹是我不想在屋里收留一个放肆无礼的红发黄毛丫头罢了。”
公爵大人弯腰捡起折扇。
“万乞恕罪，范妮。我这便带这孩子另寻投奔之处。”
范妮立刻飞奔向他，刹那间满脸尽是悔愧之色。
“别介啊，你万万使不得！噢，贾斯汀，真抱歉我方才这般不通情理！”
“你肯收留她了？”
“我——是啊，我收留她便是了。可你说的那些鬼话我一个字也不信。我敢拿我顶好的项链打赌，她绝没有你口口声声夸赞的那么天真纯良。”
“你非输得倾家荡产不可，我亲爱的。”公爵大人踱步走到通往前厅的小门前，一把将门推开。“小家伙，出来吧！”
莱昂妮走了出来，斗篷挽在臂弯上。一打量她那一身男童装束，范妮痛苦地闭紧了双眼，仿佛正忍受着剧烈的折磨。
阿翁轻轻抚摸着莱昂妮的面颊。
“舍妹已经应允好生照料你了，直到我能亲自接你为止，”他说。“记清楚了，凡事遵从她的吩咐。”
莱昂妮羞怯怯地望向对面的范妮，只见对方双唇紧抿、下巴高高扬起，神情刻板端庄。那双大眼睛察觉到了这份不容通融的冷硬姿态，不由得扑闪着重新仰望向阿翁的面庞。
“大人——求求您别——扔下我一个人！”那是一声绝望透顶的低语呢喃，直教范妮也惊得目瞪口呆。
“我很快便会来探望你的，我的宝贝。留在范妮夫人身边，你大可安安稳稳。”
“我不想——不想让您走！大人，您——您不明白！”
“小家伙，我全都明白。休要害怕；我定会回来的！”他转过身面对范妮，执起她的柔荑躬身亲吻。“全仗你多费心了，我亲爱的。烦请替我向卓绝出众的爱德华致意。莱昂妮，我数不清告诫过你多少回了，不许老扯着我的外套衣摆？”
“我——我很抱歉，大人。”
“你嘴里向来只有这句。做个乖巧的孩子，尽力去讨好舍妹吧。”公爵欠身致意，快步走向门口，拉开房门，转瞬便退了出去。

===Original===

“F-Farewell, Mon-monseigneur.”
“Farewell, my infant. Fanny, your devoted servant!” He made a profound leg, and went out, shutting the door behind him.
Left alone with the small but forbidding Lady Fanny, Léonie stood as though rooted to the ground, looking hopelessly towards the shut door, and twisting her hat in her hands.
“Mademoiselle,” said Fanny coldly, “if you will follow me I will show you your apartment. Have the goodness to wrap your cloak about you.”
“Yes, madame.” Léonie’s lip trembled. “I am﻿—very sorry, madame,” she said brokenly. A tiny sob escaped her, valiantly suppressed, and suddenly the icy dignity fell from Fanny. She ran forward, her skirts rustling prodigiously, and put her arms about her visitor.
“Oh, my dear, I am a shrew!” she said. “Never fret, child! Indeed, I am ashamed of myself! There, there!” She led Léonie to the sofa, and made her sit down, petting and soothing until the choked sobs died away.
“You see, madame,” Léonie explained, rubbing her eyes with her handkerchief. “I felt so﻿—very lonely. I did not mean to cry, but when﻿—Monseigneur﻿—went away﻿—it was so very dreadful!”
“I wish I understood!” sighed Fanny. “Are you fond of my brother, child?”
“I would die for Monseigneur,” said Léonie simply. “I am here only because he wished it.”
“Oh, my goodness gracious me!” said Fanny. “Here’s a pretty coil! My dear, be warned by me, who knows him! Have naught to do with Avon: he was not called Satanas for no reason.”
“He is not a devil to me. And I do not care.”
Fanny cast up her eyes.
