掌柜鼓起了腮帮子。
“这儿由我当家做主，”他豪气干云地说道。“我说你们安全，便断无差池。埃克托这就骑马去勒阿弗尔请外科大夫，至于什么公爵不公爵的浑话嘛！”他宽容地摇了摇头，转头差使一名惊得目瞪口呆的端茶女仆去唤女掌柜，顺便拿些干净的亚麻布来。
女掌柜雷厉风行地疾步赶来，身形同她丈夫一般敦实富态，眉宇间却颇有几分风韵姿色。女掌柜朝鲁珀特勋爵只扫了一眼，当即连珠炮似地下达了一连串指令，动手麻利地撕扯起亚麻布条。在将鲁珀特勋爵的伤口严严实实包扎妥当之前，女掌柜哪里容得旁人插半句嘴。
“哎呀，生得好一位俊俏公子哥啊（Hé, le beau）！”她说。“何等丧尽天良的歹毒勾当！这下好多了。”她将一根胖乎乎的手指抵在唇边，单手叉腰，宽大的身躯像座肉山般起伏着。“必须把他的衣服全脱了，”她拍板定案道。“让（Jean），去翻件干净睡袍来。”
“玛尔特（Marthe），”她丈夫插话道，“这个侍童其实是个名门闺秀呢！”
“天哪，成何体统（Quel horreur）！”女掌柜平心静气地念叨了一句。“是啊，咱们最好赶紧把他的衣裳全换下来，可怜的人儿（le pauvre）！”她转过身，把探头探脑的女仆连同莱昂妮一道从屋里轰了出去，反手当着两人的面重重合上了房门。
莱昂妮失魂落魄地走下楼梯，来到了院落里。埃克托早已策马奔赴勒阿弗尔的大道上去了；四周空无一人，莱昂妮精疲力竭地跌坐在厨房窗户旁的一条长凳上，终于忍不住号啕大哭起来。
“哎，呸！”她恶狠狠地冲着自己破口痛骂道。“蠢货！笨蛋！胆小鬼（Bête! Imbécile! Lâche!）！”
然而眼泪却决堤般止不住地往下淌。待到女掌柜仪态万方地踱步迈进院子时，映入眼帘的便是这么一个浑身湿漉漉、无精打采的娇小身影。
女掌柜从丈夫嘴里听闻了这桩惊世骇俗的离奇经历，本已憋了一肚子的惊骇与怒火。她双手叉腰，神情威严地开腔道：
“这简直是大逆不道的罪孽，小姐！我得教你知道，咱们开门做生意的——”说到一半她戛然而止，快步迎上前去。“哎呀不，不，我的小乖乖（ma petite）！这有什么好哭鼻子的。别哭了，我的心肝小宝贝（Tais toi, mon chou）！一切都会好起来的，包在玛尔特大妈（Maman Marthe）身上！”她张开宽阔温暖的臂膀，一把将莱昂妮深深搂入怀中，不出数分钟，怀里便传来一道闷声闷气、带着沙哑抽噎的抗议声：
“我——我才没哭呢！”
女掌柜笑得浑身肥肉乱颤。
“我当真没哭！”莱昂妮倔强地直起身子。

===Original===

“But oh, I think I am very miserable, and I wish Monseigneur were here, for that man will surely find us, and Rupert is like one dead!”
“It is true then that there is a Duc?” Madame asked.
“Of course it is true!” said Léonie indignantly. “I do not tell lies!”
“An English Duc, *alors*? Ah, but they are of a wildness, these English! But thou﻿—thou art French, little cabbage!”
“Yes,” said Léonie. “I am so tired I cannot tell you all now.”
“It is I who am a fool!” Madame cried. “Thou shalt to bed, mon ange, with some hot bouillon, and the wing of a fowl. That goes well, *hein*?”
“Yes, please,” Léonie answered. “But there is Milor’ Rupert, and I fear that he will die!”
“Little foolish one!” Madame scolded. “I tell thee﻿—*moi qui te parle*﻿—that it is well with him. It is naught. A little blood lost; much weakness﻿—and that is all. It is thou who art nigh dead with fatigue. Now thou shalt come with me.”
So Léonie, worn out with the terrors and exertions of the past two days, was tucked up between cool sheets, fed, crooned over, and presently left alone to sleep.
When she awoke the morning sun streamed in at the window, and sounds of bustle came from the street below. Madame was smiling at her from the doorway.
She sat up and rubbed her eyes.
“Why﻿—why, it is morning!” she said. “Have I slept so long?”
“Nine of the clock, little sluggard. It is better now?”
“Oh, I am very well today!” Léonie said, and threw back the blankets. “But Rupert﻿—the doctor﻿—?”
“*Doucement, doucement*, said I not that it was naught? The doctor came when thou were asleep, my cabbage, and in a little minute the bullet was out, and no harm done, by the grace of the good God. Milor’ lies on his pillows, and calls for food, and for thee.” Madame chuckled. “And when I bring him good broth he snatches the wig from his head, and demands red beef, as they have it in England. *Dépêches toi, mon enfant.*”
Twenty minutes later Léonie went dancing into Rupert’s chamber, and found that wounded hero propped up by pillows, rather pale, but otherwise himself. He was disgustedly spooning Madame’s broth, but his face brightened at the sight of Léonie.
“Hey, you madcap! Where in thunder are we now?”
Léonie shook her head.
“That I do not know,” she confessed. “But these people are kind, *n’est-ce pas*?”
“Deuced kind,” Rupert agreed, then scowled. “That fat woman won’t bring me food, and I’m devilish hungry.

===Chinese===

“可噢，我觉得我真是可怜透顶了，我真巴不得公爵大人眼下就在身边，因为那个恶人迟早会搜捕过来的，而鲁珀特眼下又同一具尸体没什么两样！”
“这么说，当真有一位大名鼎鼎的公爵大人了？”女掌柜探询道。
“那是自然无疑的！”莱昂妮愤愤不平地反驳道，“我从不扯谎！”
“这么说，是一位英国公爵了（alors）？啊，这帮英国佬可真够放浪野蛮的！可你呢——你分明是个地地道道的法国小宝贝呀！”
“是啊，”莱昂妮说。“我实在太累了，眼下没法把原委一股脑讲给你听。”
“我才是老糊涂了呢！”女掌柜惊叫道。“你非得上床安歇不可，我的小天使，喝上一碗热气腾腾的肉汤（bouillon），再啃上一只鸡翅膀。这下妥当了吧，嗯（hein）？”
“好的，多谢您，”莱昂妮应承道。“可鲁珀特勋爵还在楼上呢，我生怕他挺不过来会断气！”
“傻丫头！”女掌柜宠溺地嗔怪道。“我同你讲——我这把年纪的人亲口向你打包票——他身子骨硬朗着呢。算不得什么大碍。不过是失了点血，身子虚弱乏力罢了——仅此而已。真正快累散架的反倒是你自个儿呢。现在且随我来。”
于是，在饱受了过去两天（2）的惊魂动魄与疲于奔命之后，莱昂妮终于被塞进了清凉柔软的床单里，饱餐了一顿，被老妇人温声细语地轻哄了一阵，不多时便独自沉沉睡去。
待她醒转过来时，清晨的阳光正穿透窗棂洒满一室，楼下街巷里传来了喧闹嘈杂的叫卖劳作声。女掌柜正立在门口，冲着她和蔼可亲地展颜微笑。
她坐起身来，揉了揉惺忪的双眼。
“咦——咦，天亮了！”她说。“我竟睡了这般久吗？”
“都九点钟（9:00）了，贪睡的小懒虫。眼下可觉得爽利些了？”
“噢，我今儿好得不得了！”莱昂妮掀开毛毯翻身而起。“可鲁珀特呢——大夫怎么说——？”
“慢着点，慢着点（Doucement, doucement），我昨儿不是早就同你讲过算不得什么大碍嘛？大夫趁你熟睡时便来诊治过了，我的小宝贝，只消转瞬之间便把弹头给取出来了，承蒙仁慈天主的眷顾，半点要害都没伤着。勋爵大人眼下正舒舒服服地倚在软枕上，吵吵嚷嚷着要吃要喝，还一个劲儿地嚷嚷着要见你呢。”女掌柜咯咯轻笑起来。“我端了上好的浓肉汤给他，他倒一把扯下头顶的假发，非要吃半生不熟的血淋淋红牛肉不可，嚷嚷着说英国都是这么吃的。手脚麻利点梳洗吧，我的乖孩子（Dépêches toi, mon enfant）。”
二十分钟（20）后，莱昂妮迈着轻快欢愉的碎步跳进了鲁珀特的寝房，只见这位挂彩的英雄正倚靠在一大堆软枕上，脸色略显苍白，神情却已然恢复了往昔的神气活现。他正满脸嫌弃地一勺一勺舀着女掌柜端来的肉汤，可一瞥见莱昂妮的身影，整张面孔顿时神采奕奕地亮了起来。
“嘿，你这冒失鬼！见鬼，咱们眼下究竟身在何方？”
莱昂妮摇了摇头。
“这我上哪儿知道去，”她据实坦白道。“不过这间客栈的店家夫妇待人极好，不是吗（n’est-ce pas）？”
“好得邪门，”鲁珀特赞同道，随即又恶狠狠地拉长了脸。“那个胖婆娘死活不肯给我端正经吃食来，我都快饿成孤魂野鬼了。

===Original===

I could eat an ox, and this is what she gives me!”
“Eat it!” Léonie commanded. “It is very good, and an ox is not good at all. Oh, Rupert, I feared you were dead!”
“Devil a bit!” said Rupert cheerfully. “But I’m as weak as a rat, confound it. Stap me if I know what we’re at, the pair of us! What happened to you? And why by all that’s queer did Saint-Vire run off with you?”
“I do not know. He gave me an evil drug, and I slept for hours and hours. He is a pig-person. I hate him. I am glad that I bit him, and threw the coffee over him.”
“Did you, b’gad? Blister me if I ever met such a lass! I’ll have Saint-Vire’s blood for this, see if I don’t!” He wagged his head solemnly, and applied himself to the broth. “Here am I chasing you to God knows where, with never a sou in my pocket, nor a sword at my side, and the landlord’s hat on my head! And what they’ll be thinking at home the Lord knows! I don’t!”
Léonie curled herself up on the bed, and was requested not to sit on his lordship’s feet. She shifted her position a little, and related her adventures. That done, she demanded to know what had befallen Rupert.
“Blessed if I know!” said Rupert. “I went haring after you as far as the village, and learned the way you went. So I got me a horse, and set off for Portsmouth. But the luck was against me, so it was! You’d set sail an hour since, and the only boat leaving the harbour was a greasy old tub﻿—well, well! What did I do then? ’Pon my soul I almost forget! No, I have it! I went off to sell the horse. Twenty meagrely guineas was all he fetched, but a worse﻿—”
“Sold one of Monseigneur’s horses?” exclaimed Léonie.
“No, no, ’twas a brute I got at the blacksmith’s, owned by﻿—burn it, what’s the fellow’s name﻿—Manvers!”
“Oh, I see!” said Léonie, relieved. “Go on. You did very well, Rupert!”
“Not so bad, was it?” said Rupert modestly. “Well, I bought a passage on the old tub, and we got in at Le Havre at one, or thereabouts.”
“We did not leave Le Havre until two! He thought you would not follow, and he said that he was safe enough now!”
“Safe, eh? I’ll show him!” Rupert shook his fist. “Where was I?”
“At Le Havre,” Léonie prompted.
“Oh, ay, that’s it! Well, by the time I’d paid this fee and that my guineas were all gone, so off I went to sell my diamond pin.”
“Oh! It was such a pretty pin!”
“Never mind that.

===Chinese===

我连整头牛都吞得下去，她偏就端这玩意儿打发我！”
“给我咽下去！”莱昂妮俨然发号施令道。“这东西滋补得很，而且整头牛一点也不好吃。噢，鲁珀特，我先前真生怕你挺不过来死掉了呢！”
“哪能呢，连半点影儿都没有！”鲁珀特爽朗快活地答道。“不过老子眼下浑身软得跟只落水耗子似的，真他娘的憋屈。要是搞得清咱们这一对活宝眼下究竟在唱哪出大戏，老子当场被雷劈死都甘心！你究竟遭了什么变故？还有圣维尔那个天杀的究竟犯了什么疯病，非把你拐跑不可？”
“我不知道。他给我灌了极厉害的蒙汗药，我昏睡了整整好多个时辰。他就是个猪狗般的恶棍。我恨死他了。好在我当时狠狠咬了他一口，还把整杯咖啡全泼在了他身上。”
“老天在上，你当真干了这等壮举？打死我平生也没遇见过你这般烈性的小蹄子！我发誓非放干圣维尔的血不可，瞧我不弄死他！”他一本正经地摇晃着脑袋，继续埋头对付起肉汤来。“你瞧瞧我如今这副德行，身无分文，腰间连把防身的宝剑都没有，头顶还顶着客栈掌柜的破毡帽，一路追着你赶到了这鸟不拉屎的鬼地方！至于家里那帮人眼下会作何感想，天晓得！反正我是管不着了！”
莱昂妮蜷着身子坐在床榻上，被勋爵大人抗议说压着了他的双脚。她稍微挪动了一下身子，将自个儿的惊险奇遇原原本本地讲了一遍。道完之后，她便迫不及待地盘问起鲁珀特的经历来。
“鬼才知道呢！”鲁珀特说。“我像只疯兔子似的追着你一路赶到了村子，打探到了你们逃窜的路径。我索性顺手牵了一匹马，快马加鞭直奔朴茨茅斯。可老天爷偏不作美！你们乘的船已经起航了一个钟头（1）了，海港里唯一剩下要出海的只有一艘油腻腻的破烂平底帆船——唉，罢了，罢了！接下来我干了些什么来着？凭良心讲我都快忘光了！不对，我想起来了！我跑去把那匹马给变卖了。区区二十个几尼（20）的微薄银子，可那匹劣马——”
“你把公爵大人的宝马给卖了？”莱昂妮失声惊呼道。
“哪能呢，绝不是，那是我从铁匠铺里顺手牵来的劣马，原主是——见鬼，那厮叫什么名号来着——曼弗斯（Manvers）！”
“噢，这还差不多！”莱昂妮如释重负地松了一口气。“继续说。你干得漂亮极了，鲁珀特！”
“倒也不算太寒碜，不是吗？”鲁珀特颇为自谦地表功道。“行吧，我花银子在那艘破烂平底船上买了张船票，咱们在一点钟（1:00）前后抵埠勒阿弗尔。”
“我们直到两点（2:00）才离开勒阿弗尔呢！他料定你绝不敢一路追杀过来，口口声声说眼下已然万无一失了！”
“万无一失，嗯？看老子怎么收拾他！”鲁珀特恶狠狠地挥了挥拳头。

===Original===

The trouble I had to get rid of the damned thing you’d scarce believe. ’Pon my soul, I believe they thought I’d stolen it!”
“But did you sell it?”
“Ay, for less than half its worth, rot it! Then I skipped off to the inn to inquire of you, and to get me something to eat. Thunder and turf, but I was hungry!”
“So was I!” sighed Léonie. “And that pig-person ate and ate!”
“You put me out,” said Rupert severely. “Where was I? Oh yes! Well, the landlord told me that Saint-Vire was gone off by coach to Rouen at two o’clock, so the next thing I had to do was to hire a horse to be after you again. That’s all there is to it, and devilish good sport it was! But where we are now, or what we’re to do, beats me!”
“The Comte will come, do you not think?” Léonie asked anxiously.
“I don’t know. He can’t very well snatch you when I’m here. I wish I knew what the plague he wants with you. Y’know, this is mighty difficult, for we haven’t either of us a notion what the game is we’re playing.” He frowned, thinking. “Of course, Saint-Vire may come to steal you again. He’ll have ridden back to Le Havre first, depend on’t, and when he finds we’ve not been there he may scour the countryside, for he knows he hit me and it’s likely we’d be hiding somewhere near.”
“What are we to do?” asked Léonie, with pale cheeks.
“What, not afraid, are you? Damn it, he can’t walk off with you under my very nose!”
“Oh, he can, Rupert, he can! You are so weak you cannot help me!”
Rupert made an effort to hoist himself up, and failed dismally. He lay fuming.
“Well, damme, I can fire!”
“But we have no gun!” objected Léonie. “At any moment he may come, and these people will never be able to keep him out.”
“Pistol, child, pistol! Lord, what will you say next? Of course we have one! D’ye take me for a fool? Feel in the pockets of my coat.”
Léonie jumped down from the bed, and dragged my lord’s coat from the chair. She produced Mr. Fletcher’s unwieldy pistol from one of its pockets, and brandished it gleefully.
“Rupert, you are very clever! Now we can kill that pig-person!”
“Hi, put it down!” commanded Rupert in some alarm. “You know naught of pistols, and we’ll have an accident if you fiddle with it! The thing’s loaded and cocked!”
“I *do* know about pistols!” said Léonie indignantly. “You point it, so! And pull this thing.”
“For God’s sake, put it down!” cried Rupert.

===Chinese===

要不是路上走漏了风声，我早在那间客栈里就一把掐死他了！后来听闻那辆该死的马车半路坏了车轮，我便顺着大路一路狂追。”
“后来你便撞见我了，”莱昂妮微笑着接话道。
“可不是嘛，你这冒失鬼！偏巧迎面挨了他一记黑枪！真见鬼，贾斯汀如今身在何处？他若是得知你遭了毒手，不知会闹出何等惊涛骇浪来呢。”
“大人不知道，”莱昂妮满腹惆怅地说道。“我被掳走时，大人还没赶回府邸呢。鲁珀特，咱们眼下该作何打算？”
“老子怎么知道。有我在这儿守着，他休想轻而易举把你抢走。我真巴不得知晓他究竟图你什么。你懂的，这事难办得要命，因为咱们俩谁也摸不清眼下这局棋究竟在玩什么把戏。”他蹙起眉头，苦苦思索起来。“当然，圣维尔极有可能折返回来再次掳走你。他必定会先策马赶回勒阿弗尔查探，我敢打包票，等他发现咱们压根没去过勒阿弗尔，极可能会四下里大肆搜山，毕竟他心知打中了我，咱们极有可能在附近某个角落藏匿避难。”
“那咱们该如何是好呢？”莱昂妮面色煞白地探问道。
“怎么，你难道害怕了不成？见鬼，有老子在这儿立着，他休想当着我的面大摇大摆把你掳走！”
“噢，他办得到的，鲁珀特，他绝对办得到！你眼下这般虚弱，根本护不住我！”
鲁珀特试图撑起身子坐直，却狼狈不堪地颓然跌了回去。他满腔怒火地躺在床上生闷气。
“哼，见鬼，老子好歹还能开枪！”
“可咱们手里连杆火枪都没有呀！”莱昂妮抗辩道。“他随时都有可能杀将过来，这间小客栈里的人根本挡不住他。”
“是手枪，傻孩子，是手枪！天哪，你接下来不知还要冒出什么疯话来呢？咱们手里怎么可能没有！你当老子是个不长脑子的蠢汉不成？去摸摸我外套的大口袋。”
莱昂妮翻身跳下床榻，一把抓过搭在椅子上的勋爵外套。她从其中一只口袋里摸出了弗莱彻先生（Fletcher）的那柄沉甸甸笨拙的手枪，得意洋洋地在空中挥舞起来。
“鲁珀特，你可真聪明绝顶！这下咱们能亲手宰了那个猪狗恶棍了！”
“喂，快放下！”鲁珀特心惊肉跳地厉声喝令道。“你对火器一窍不通，要是胡乱摆弄非走火出人命不可！那玩意儿可是填满了火药、而且扳机击锤早就张开了的！”
“我才不是一窍不通呢！”莱昂妮愤愤不平地反驳道。“只消这么平平一指，对，就这样！然后再扣动这个机关。”
“看在老天爷的份上，快把它放下来！”鲁珀特失声惊呼道。

===Original===

“You’re levelling the damned thing at me, silly chit! Put it on the table beside me, and find my purse. It’s in my breeches pocket.”
Léonie laid the pistol down reluctantly, and rummaged anew for the purse.
“How much have we?” Rupert asked.
Léonie emptied the guineas on to the bed. Three rolled on to the floor, and one dropped into Rupert’s broth with a splash.
“ ’Pon my soul, you are a careless minx!” said Rupert, fishing for the coin in his bowl. “There’s another gone now, under the bed!”
Léonie dived after the errant guineas, retrieved them, and sat down on the bed to count them.
“One, two, four, six, and a louis﻿—oh, and another guinea, and three sous, and﻿—”
“That’s not the way! Here, give ’em to me! There’s another gone under the bed, burn it!”
Léonie was grovelling under the bed in search of the coin when they heard the clatter of wheels outside.
“What’s that?” said Rupert sharply. “Quick! To the window!”
Léonie extricated herself with difficulty, and ran to the window.
“Rupert, ’tis he! *Mon Dieu, mon Dieu*, what are we to do?”
“Can you see him?” Rupert demanded.
“No, but there is a coach, and the horses are steaming! Oh listen, Rupert!”
Voices were heard below, expostulating. Evidently Madame was guarding the staircase.
“Saint-Vire, I’ll bet a monkey!” said Rupert. “Where’s that pistol? Plague take this broth!” He threw the bowl and the rest of its contents on to the floor, settled his wig straight, and reached out a hand for the pistol, a very grim look on his drawn young face.
Léonie darted forward and seized the weapon.
“You are not enough strong!” she said urgently. “See, you have exhausted yourself already! Leave me! I will shoot him dead!”
“Here, no, I say!” expostulated Rupert. “You’ll blow him to smithereens! Give it to me! Fiend seize it, do as I say!”
The commotion below had subsided a little, and footsteps could be heard mounting the stairs.
“Give that pistol to me, and get you to the other side of the bed,” ordered Rupert. “By Gad, we’ll see some sport now! Come *here*!”
Léonie had backed to the window, and stood with the pistol levelled at the door, her finger crooked about the trigger. Her mouth was shut hard, and her eyes blazed. Rupert struggled impatiently to rise.
“For God’s sake, give it to me! We don’t want to kill the fellow!”
“Yes, we do,” said Léonie. “He gave me an evil drug.”
The door opened.

===Chinese===

“你拿那该死的玩意儿直愣愣指着我呢，小笨蛋！把它搁在身旁的桌上，顺便把我的钱袋翻出来。就在我马裤的口袋里。”
莱昂妮心不甘情不愿地搁下手枪，转头重新翻找起钱袋来。
“咱们手头还剩多少银两？”鲁珀特打听道。
莱昂妮将钱袋里的金币哗啦啦倾倒在床单上。其中三枚（3）滚落到了地板上，还有一枚（1）噗通一声溅进了鲁珀特的肉汤碗里，激起一片油花。
“凭良心讲，你可真算得上是个丢三落四的野丫头！”鲁珀特一边在碗里捞着金币，一边咒骂道。“瞧瞧，又有一枚滚到床底下去了！”
莱昂妮一头扎到床底下钻来钻去，把那些滚落的几尼金币一一摸索寻了回来，一屁股坐在床沿上数起铜板来。
“一（1）、二（2）、四（4）、六（6），还有一枚路易金币……噢，又是一枚几尼，还有三个苏（3），还有——”
“有你这么数钱的嘛！拿来，全给我！见鬼，又有一枚掉床底下去了！”
莱昂妮正趴在地上撅着屁股在床底摸索硬币的当口，窗外冷不丁传来了急促刺耳的车轮声。
“那是什么声响？”鲁珀特厉声喝问道。“快！去窗口瞧瞧！”
莱昂妮手忙脚乱地从床底下爬了出来，快步飞奔到窗前。
“鲁珀特，是他！天哪，天哪（Mon Dieu, mon Dieu），咱们该如何是好？”
“你能瞧见他人吗？”鲁珀特盘问道。
“瞧不见，但外头停了一辆马车，拉车的辕马周身直冒热气呢！噢快听，鲁珀特！”
楼下传来了嘈杂纷乱的争执分辩声。显而易见，女掌柜正横身挡在楼梯口不肯放行。
“准是圣维尔，我敢押五百磅（bet a monkey）赌咒！”鲁珀特说。“手枪搁在哪儿了？去他娘的肉汤！”他一把将汤碗连同残羹剩饭恶狠狠砸翻在地板上，手忙脚乱地扶正假发，伸手便去抓手枪，那张消瘦年轻的面庞上浮现出一抹极其冷酷决绝的杀气。
莱昂妮如离弦之箭般扑上前去，抢先一把夺过了火器。
“你身子骨根本不够硬朗！”她十万火急地嚷道。“你瞧，你方才稍微一动弹便已然精疲力竭了！撒手！我亲自开枪取他狗命！”
“喂，别胡闹，听我说！”鲁珀特急得大声抗辩道。“你非把他轰成肉泥不可！快给我！真见鬼，听老子的话！”
楼下的嘈杂喧嚣声渐渐平息了下来，紧接着楼梯上传来了稳健沉着的噔噔脚步声。
“把枪给我，你给我老老实实躲到床那头去，”鲁珀特厉声喝令道。“老天作证，待会儿可有好戏看了！到这儿来！”
莱昂妮已然退到了窗户旁，双手稳稳端着手枪直指房门，食指死死扣在扳机之上。她紧咬牙关，一双大眼睛里迸射出熊熊怒火。鲁珀特急不可耐地拼命挣扎着想要爬起身来。
“看在老天爷的份上，快把枪给我！咱们可没打算真要了那家伙的命啊！”
“偏要要他的命，”莱昂妮语气森冷地说道。“他强灌了我恶毒的蒙汗药。”
房门被一把推开。

===Original===

“If you come one step into the room I will shoot you dead!” said Léonie clearly.
“And I thought that you would be pleased to see me, *ma fille*,” said a soft, drawling voice. “I beg you will not shoot me dead.” Great-coated, booted and spurred, not a hair of his elegant wig out of place, his Grace of Avon stood upon the threshold, quizzing glass raised, a faint smile curling his thin lips.
Rupert gave a shout of laughter, and collapsed on to his pillows.
“Thunder and turf, but I never thought I’d live to be thankful for the sight of you, Justin!” he gasped. “Stap me if I did!”

===Chinese===

“你若敢迈进屋里半步，我立刻开枪取你性命！”莱昂妮清脆响亮地喝道。
“我还以为你见着我定会喜出望外呢，我的闺女（ma fille），”一道慵懒柔和的慢板嗓音悠悠响起。“我恳请你万万莫要开枪取了我的性命。”身着厚呢大氅，足蹬皮靴马刺，头顶那顶考究华丽的假发连半根发丝都未曾碰乱，阿翁公爵大人正施施然伫立在门槛之上，手中高高举着单柄眼镜，薄薄的双唇微微勾起一抹若有若无的浅笑。
鲁珀特喉头猛地爆出一阵惊天动地的大笑，整个人颓然瘫倒在身后的软枕上。
“老天作证，我做梦也没想到自个儿这辈子居然还会有盼着见你大驾光临的一天，贾斯汀！”他喘着粗气失声欢呼道。“要是撒谎，教我当场给雷劈死！”

## XXIII Mr. Marling Allows Himself to Be Persuaded / 马林先生甘愿被说服
===Original===

Lady Fanny partook of breakfast in bed next morning, and was sipping her hot chocolate when Léonie scratched on the door. My lady put up her hands to her pretty nightcap and patted her golden curls before she called “Come in!”
“Oh, ’tis you, child! Mercy, are you riding out so early?”
Léonie was in riding dress, with polished boots, and leathern gauntlets, tasselled, and a big black beaver on her head with a long feather that swept her shoulder.
“Yes, madame, but only if you do not need me. Monseigneur said that I must ask you.”
Lady Fanny nibbled at a sweet biscuit and regarded the bedpost with rapt interest.
“No, child, no. Why should I need you? Lud, what roses you have; I’d give my best necklet for your complexion. To be sure, I had it once. Go, my love. Don’t keep Justin waiting. Is Rupert up?”
“His valet dresses him, madame.”
“I’ll bear him company in the parlour,” said her ladyship, and pushed her cup and saucer away. “Away with you, child! Stay! Send Rachel to me, my love, if you will be so good.”
Léonie went with alacrity. Half an hour later my lady, having bustled exceedingly, came tripping into the parlour dressed in a flowered muslin, and her fair hair unpowdered beneath a becoming cap. Rupert looked up as she entered, and put down the book over which he had been yawning.
“Lord, you’re up early, Fan!”
“I came to bear you company,” she cooed, and went to sit by him, at the window.
“Wonders’ll never cease,” Rupert said. He felt that this amiability on Fanny’s part ought not to go unrewarded. “You look twenty this morning, Fan, ’pon my soul you do!” he said handsomely.
“Dear Rupert! Do you really think so?”
“Ay﻿—that’ll do, though! Léonie has gone riding with his Grace.”
“Rupert,” said my lady.
“Ay, what?”
Fanny looked up.
“I have made up my mind to it Justin shall marry that child.”
Rupert was unperturbed.
“Will he, do you think?”
“My dear boy, he’s head over ears in love with her!”
“I know that﻿—I’m not blind, Fan. But he’s been in love before.”
“You are most provoking, Rupert! Pray what has that to do with it?”
“He’s not married any of ’em,” said my lord.
Fanny affected to be shocked.
“Rupert!”
“Don’t be prudish, Fanny! That’s Edward’s doing, I know.”
“Rupert, if you are minded to be unkind about dear Edward﻿—”
“Devil take Edward!” said Rupert cheerfully.
Fanny eyed him for a moment in silence, and suddenly smiled.

===Chinese===

次日清晨，范妮夫人照例在床榻上用早膳，正小口啜饮着热巧克力的当口，门外传来了莱昂妮轻轻抓门的声音。夫人连忙伸手摸了摸俏丽的睡帽，抚平鬓角的金色鬈发，方才扬声唤道：“进来吧！”
“哎呀，是你呀，孩子！天哪，你一大清早就要出去骑马兜风吗？”
莱昂妮一身骑装穿戴齐整，足蹬锃亮的皮靴，戴着缀有流苏的皮手套，头顶扣着一顶宽大的黑色海狸皮帽，帽上一根修长的羽毛低垂下来轻抚着香肩。
“是的，夫人，但前提是您手头不需要我伺候。公爵大人吩咐说我务必先来请示您的意向。”
范妮夫人轻咬了一口甜饼干，目不转睛、满含神往地打量着床柱。
“不用，孩子，不用。我哪用得着你伺候呢？天哪，你这脸蛋红扑扑的多水灵啊；哪怕搭上我顶好的项链，我也真巴不得能换得你这身好气色呢。说实在的，从前我也曾这般水灵过。去吧，我的心肝。别让贾斯汀久等了。鲁珀特起身了吗？”
“贴身男仆正伺候他穿衣呢，夫人。”
“那我去客厅陪他解闷吧，”夫人一边说着，一边推开茶杯与茶托。“去吧，孩子！等等！劳驾行个好，顺便替我把蕾切尔唤到屋里来，我的心肝。”
莱昂妮欢快利落地应命而去。半小时（0.5）后，夫人经过一番手忙脚乱的折腾，换上了一袭印花细棉布衣裙，未施香粉的浅金秀发掩映在娇俏别致的软帽之下，迈着轻盈的碎步走进了客厅。鲁珀特见她进门，抬起头来，放下了手中那本方才看得直打哈欠的闲书。
“天哪，你今儿可真够早起的，范妮！”
“我特意来陪你说说话解闷呀，”她柔声细语地说道，顺势走到他身侧的窗前坐下。
“怪事年年有，今儿特别多，”鲁珀特说。他觉得范妮这般破天荒的和颜悦色合该得到几句好言好语的犒劳。“今儿早晨你瞧着才二十岁（20）呢，范妮，凭良心讲当真如此！”他颇为大度地恭维道。
“亲爱的鲁珀特！你当真这么觉得吗？”
“行了行了——奉承话说到这儿便够了！莱昂妮已经随公爵大人策马出门去了。”
“鲁珀特，”夫人开口道。
“嗯，怎么了？”
范妮抬起头来。
“我心里已然打定主意了，贾斯汀非明媒正娶把那孩子娶进门不可。”
鲁珀特神色泰然、全无波动。
“依你看，他肯依吗？”
“我亲爱的弟弟，他整个人早就爱她爱得无法自拔、神魂颠倒了！”
“这我心里有数——我又不瞎，范妮。可他以往又不是没坠入过爱河。”
“你真够讨厌磨人的，鲁珀特！请问过往的风流韵事同眼下这桩有何相干？”
“以往的那些相好，他愣是一个都没娶过门，”勋爵说道。
范妮故作惊骇。
“鲁珀特！”
“别在那儿假正经了，范妮！准是受了爱德华的熏陶，我一清二楚。”
“鲁珀特，要是你非要存心编排亲爱的爱德华的坏话——”
“去他娘的爱德华！”鲁珀特爽朗快活地笑道。
范妮默不作声地端详了他片刻，冷不丁扑哧一笑。

===Original===

“I am not come to quarrel with you, horrid boy. Justin would not take Léonie as his mistress.”
“No, damme, I believe you’re right. He’s turned so strict you’d scarce know him. But marriage﻿—! He’d not be so easily trapped.”
“Trapped?” cried my lady. “It’s no such thing! The child has no notion of wedding him. And that is why he will want her to wife, mark my words!”
“He might,” Rupert said dubiously. “But﻿—Lord, Fanny, he’s turned forty, and she’s a babe!”
“She is twenty, my dear, or near it. ’Twould be charming! She will always think him wonderful, and she’ll not mind his morals, for she’s none herself; and he﻿—oh, he will be the strictest husband in town, and the most delightful! She will always be his infant, I dare swear, and he ‘Monseigneur.’ I am determined he shall wed her. Now what do you say?”
“I? I’d be pleased enough, but﻿—egad, Fanny, we don’t know who she is! Bonnard? I’ve never met the name, and it hath a plaguey bourgeois ring to it, damme, so it has! And Justin﻿—well, y’know, he’s Alastair of Avon, and it won’t do for him to marry a nobody.”
“Pooh!” said my lady. “I’ll wager my reputation she does not come of common stock. There’s some mystery, Rupert.”
“Any fool could tell that,” Rupert said frankly. “And if you asked me, Fan, I’d say she was related to Saint-Vire.” He leaned back in his chair and looked for surprise in his sister. It did not come.
“Where would be my wits if I’d not seen that?” demanded Fanny. “As soon as I heard that ’twas Saint-Vire who carried her off I felt positive she was a baseborn child of his.”
Rupert spluttered.
“Gad, would you have Justin marry any such?”
“I should not mind at all,” said my lady.
“He won’t do it,” Rupert said with conviction. “He’s a rake, but he knows what’s due to the family, I’ll say that for him.”
“Pho!” My lady snapped her fingers. “If he loves her he’ll not trouble his head over the family. Why, what did I care for the family when I married Edward?”
“Steady, steady! Marling has his faults, I’m not saying he hasn’t, but there’s no bad blood in his family, and you can trace him back to﻿—”
“Stupid creature, could I not have had Fonteroy for the lifting of a finger? Ay, or my Lord Blackwater, or his Grace of Cumming? Yet I chose Edward, who beside them was a nobody.”
“Damn it, he’s not baseborn!”
“I would not have cared, I give you my word!”
Rupert shook his head.

===Chinese===

“我可不是来同你吵嘴的，讨厌鬼。贾斯汀绝不会收莱昂妮做情妇的。”
“是啊，见鬼，我想你是对的。他如今管束得森严极了，严得你简直快认不出他来了。可谈婚论嫁——！他可绝不至于轻易跌进逼婚的圈套里去。”
“圈套？”夫人失声嚷道。“哪有什么圈套！那孩子心里压根没有半点要嫁他为妻的念头。而这恰恰就是他之所以非要明媒正娶她的由头，记牢我的话吧！”
“说不定真如你所言，”鲁珀特半信半疑地说道。“可——老天啊，范妮，他都年过四十（40）了，而她分明还是个毛娃娃呢！”
“她今年二十岁（20）了，我亲爱的，或者说差不离了。这亲事该有多美满绝伦啊！在她眼里，贾斯汀永远是那个无所不能的天神，而且她也绝不会挑剔他的风流品行，毕竟她自个儿也全无俗套体统；至于他嘛——噢，他会是全伦敦城最严厉的夫君，同时也是最教人倾心的良人！我敢发誓，在她面前他永远是‘大人’，而她也永远是他的‘小家伙’。我打定主意非促成他们成婚不可。现在你觉得如何？”
“我？我自然乐见其成，可——天哪，范妮，咱们压根摸不清她究竟是何方神圣！博纳尔（Bonnard）？我平生还从未听闻过这个名号，而且听着满是该死的小市民市侩味道，见鬼，千真万确！而贾斯汀嘛——哎，你懂的，他可是阿翁领地的阿拉斯泰尔，他堂堂公爵明媒正娶一个无名下贱之辈，可万万不成体统。”
“呸！”夫人啐道。“我敢拿我的名誉打赌，她绝非什么寻常草莽贱民的血统。这其中大有玄机，鲁珀特。”
“哪怕是个傻子也瞧得出其中有古怪，”鲁珀特坦率地说道。“倘若你问我的话，范妮，我敢说她八成同圣维尔脱不了干系。”他身子向后靠在椅背上，静候姐姐脸上浮现出惊诧的神色。然而惊诧并未如期而至。
“要是我连这都瞧不出来，我脑子岂不是白长了？”范妮质问道。“自打听闻是圣维尔把她强掳走的那一刻起，我便百分之百笃定她准是圣维尔在外头风流生下的私生女。”
鲁珀特忍不住嗤嗤笑出声来。
“老天，你居然真想让贾斯汀迎娶这么个私生女？”
“我横竖是半点不介意，”夫人说道。
“他绝不会答应的，”鲁珀特语气笃定地说道。“他固然是个浪荡浪子，可他也深知自个儿背负的家族荣耀门楣，这一点我不得不替他讲句公道话。”
“得了吧！”夫人打了个响指。“只要他深爱着她，哪里还会为劳什子家族名头烦心。哎，当年我下嫁爱德华时，何曾把家族体统放在眼里过？”
“慢着点，慢着点！马林固然有他的缺点，我并不是说他十全十美，可人家家底清清白白，半点不招人非议！可若是个私生女——！贾斯汀爱怎么放肆是他的自由，但我敢同你押五百磅（lay a monkey）赌咒，他绝不会明媒正娶一个私生女。”
“一言为定！”夫人爽快地击掌应道。“噢，鲁珀特！上周在牌桌上我把那颗大翡翠给输掉了！我都快把眼珠子哭瞎了，而爱德华嘴里翻来覆去就只有一句这权当给我的教训！”
“这活脱脱就是爱德华的做派，”鲁珀特连连点头。“我难道还不清楚嘛！”
“不，你压根不清楚，讨厌鬼！他迟早会再给我买一颗新的大翡翠的。”她飞快地眨巴着双眼。“说实在的，他平日里待我体贴极了。不知他会不会一路追到这儿来呢？我发誓要是他不来，我非难过得要命不可！”
鲁珀特正极目远眺着窗外的街景。
“得，他已经来了，而且来得当真太是时候了。”
“什么！当真是他吗，鲁珀特？你没在同我开玩笑吧？”
“千真万确是他，瞧那副脸色，分明憋着好大的雷霆之怒呢。”
范妮夫人如痴如醉地长长叹了一口气。
“心爱的爱德华！我敢担保他非冲我发好大一通脾气不可。”
马林大步流星地跨进门来。他风尘仆仆，双眼因缺觉而布满了血丝，双唇决绝刻板地紧绷着。他默不作声地上下审视着自己美艳的娇妻。
“这下人总算全聚齐了，”鲁珀特爽朗快活地打趣道。“全家老小一个不落，感谢老天爷！早安啊，爱德华！”
范妮夫人站起身来，伸出一只纤手。
“爱德华，我不得不说你这趟赶来未免太糊涂犯傻了。”
他全然无视了她递上来的柔荑。
“你今天非随我打道回府不可，范妮。我决不容忍你这般放肆顶撞。”
“好家伙！”鲁珀特在喉头暗暗吹了声口哨。“接招吧——开战啦！”
范妮夫人噗嗤笑出声来。
“哎呀，先生，你可真够全无绅士风度的！请问你进门前可曾照过镜子？你满身泥泞、衣衫不整地跑到我跟前！而我向来最喜欢男人穿戴得一丝不苟、无可挑剔了！”
“烦请把我的仪容撇在一旁。范妮，你的任性胡闹我已经容忍得够久了。你今天必须同我回英国去。”
“当真如此吗，先生，你当真以为我会乖乖就范吗？”夫人的眼眸里已然燃起了战斗的熊熊烈火。
“夫人，你是我的明媒正娶的妻子。”
“可我绝不是你名下的私有财产，先生。劳驾把脸上的怒容收起来！我可不吃这一套。”
“正是，收起来吧！”鲁珀特在一旁插科打诨道。“你把我表姐一个人撇下在家里可安顿好了，马林？”
“是啊，先生，你为何把可怜亲爱的哈丽雅特一个人扔在老家？这可半点不厚道，爱德华。”
“范妮，你胡闹够了没有？我警告你，我眼下可没心思由着你耍这些小把戏！”
“留神啊，范妮，小心点！”鲁珀特在一旁看热闹不嫌事大、痛快极了。“他非休了你不可，绝对会休了你！”
马林猛地扭过身来怒目直视着他。

===Original===

“It’s lax, Fanny, ’fore Gad, it’s lax. I don’t like it.”
My lady pulled a face at him.
“Oh, tell Justin you do not like it, my dear! Tell him﻿—”
“I’m not meddling in Justin’s affairs, I thank you. He’ll do as he likes, but I’ll lay you a monkey he weds no bastard.”
“Done!” said my lady. “Oh, Rupert! I lost my big emerald at play last week! I could have cried my eyes out, and Edward could only say that it must be a lesson to me!”
“That’s Edward all over,” nodded Rupert. “Don’t I know it!”
“No, you do not, tiresome boy! He will give me another emerald.” She blinked rapidly. “Indeed, he is very good to me. I wonder if he will come here? I vow I shall be miserable if he does not!”
Rupert’s eyes were on the street.
“Well, he has come, and mighty apropos, too.”
“What! Is it really he, Rupert? You’re not teasing me?”
“No, it is he, right enough, and in a thundering rage, by the look of him.”
Lady Fanny sighed ecstatically.
“Darling Edward! He will be very angry with me, I am sure.”
Marling came quickly in. He was travel-stained, and heavy-eyed from lack of sleep, and his mouth was set in an uncompromising fashion. He looked his pretty wife over in silence.
“That’s the last of us,” said Rupert jovially. “We’ve all the family now, glory be! Give you good morrow, Edward!”
Lady Fanny rose, and held out her hand.
“Edward, I protest this is foolish of you.”
He ignored the outstretched hand.
“You’ll return with me today, Fanny. I don’t brook your defiance.”
“Whew!” spoke Rupert under his breath. “Sa-sa﻿—Have at you!”
Lady Fanny tittered.
“Oh, sir, you are ungallant! Pray have you looked at yourself in the mirror? You come to me muddied and in disorder! And I who so love a man to be point-device!”
“We’ll leave my appearance out of it, if you please. I’ve borne enough of your whims, Fanny. You’ll return with me to England.”
“Indeed, sir, do you think I shall?” The light of battle was in my lady’s eyes.
“You are my wife, madam.”
“But not your chattel, sir. Pray take that frown from your face! It likes me not.”
“Ay, do!” Rupert put in. “How did you leave my cousin, Marling?”
“Yes, sir, and why did you leave poor dear Harriet? It was not well done of you, Edward.”
“Fanny, have you done? I warn you, I am in no mood for these tricks!”
“Now, careful, Fan, careful!” said Rupert, enjoying himself hugely. “He’ll disown you, so he will!”
Marling swung round to face him.

===Chinese===

“你的打趣太不合时宜了，阿拉斯泰尔。我想要是你能行行好退下，咱们能把话说得更敞亮些。”
“你好大的胆子，爱德华！这个可怜的孩子才刚刚大病初愈从病榻上爬起来，肩头的枪伤离肺腑不过差了区区一英寸（1）呢！”
“我对鲁珀特的伤势毫无兴趣，”马林冷酷刻薄地回敬道。“没有我的虚情假意，他也死不了。”
“是啊，可他娘的见鬼，要是我还得老对着你这张哭丧般的死人脸，我非当场伤势复发不可！”鲁珀特反唇相讥。“看在老天爷的份上，笑一笑吧，老兄！”
“噢对啊，爱德华，笑一笑嘛！”夫人哀求道。“瞧着你老拧着眉毛，我头都开始疼了。”
“范妮，由我同你单独谈上五分钟（5）。”
“不行，先生，我绝不答应。你摆出这副臭脾气同我说话，简直尖酸刻薄到了极点，我发誓我再也不想听了。”
“听见了吧，马林！”鲁珀特附和道。“快去张罗点早膳填填肚子吧。吃饱了肚皮包管你能舒坦些，我发誓！肚里空空如也才教人肝火旺盛呢：这种滋味我再清楚不过了。眼下来块火腿，就着肉馅饼，再拿热咖啡往下一顺，包管教你脱胎换骨变成个新人，要是骗你老子当场天打雷劈！”
范妮夫人咯咯笑个不停。马林的脸色愈发铁青阴鸷，双眸冷若寒霜。
“你会为今天的胡闹后悔的，夫人。你对我肆意妄为的次数未免太多了些。”
“哎呀，先生，我眼下可没兴致听你在这儿慷慨陈词！劳驾把这套大义凛然留给哈丽雅特去吧！她八成对这一套受用得很呢！”
“不如留着拿去对付贾斯汀吧，”鲁珀特建议道。“他这就到了，还带着莱昂妮呢。天哪，何等其乐融融的大团圆啊！”
“最后问你一次，范妮——我绝不会再问第二遍——你究竟肯不肯匀出片刻闲暇单独同我分说？”
“单独？”鲁珀特阴阳怪气地怪叫道。“那是自然，她当然乐意，你爱单独多久就单独多久！独处才是人间正道啊，千真万确！独处，再外加一大块肥美流油的火腿——”
“我亲爱的马林，但愿你一向一切安好？”阿翁公爵大人悄然踱步迈进门来。
马林抄起了自个儿的帽子。
“托福，我身子骨硬朗得很，多谢挂念，阿翁。”
“可他的精神头嘛！”鲁珀特在一旁补刀道。“哎呀呀，老天啊！”
“我承认，”马林语气平稳沉着地说道，“我的心神确实被——伤着了那么一丁点。”
“可千万别这么说！”鲁珀特故作惊诧道。“爱德华，你这趟横渡海峡必定遭了罪，肝气郁结上火啦。”
阿翁转过身来。
“你的阔论向来这般振聋发聩、引人入胜啊，鲁珀特。不过我想眼下咱们大可免了这番高谈阔论。”
鲁珀特当即灰溜溜地缩了回去。夫人傲慢地一扬下巴。阿翁踱步走到边桌旁，倒满了一杯勃艮第葡萄酒，客客气气地递向马林，马林却挥手冷冷挡开了。
“先生，我此番前来纯粹是为了接内人回府。既然她执意不肯同行，那便无话可说了。在下这就告辞。”

===Original===

“Your pleasantries are ill-timed, Alastair. I believe we shall do better if you leave us.”
“How dare you, Edward? And the poor boy just out of his bed, with a wound in his shoulder that only escaped the lung by a bare inch!”
“I am not concerned with Rupert’s hurts,” said Marling cuttingly. “He will survive without my sympathy.”
“Ay, but damme, I shall suffer a relapse if I have to look on your gloomy countenance much longer!” retorted Rupert. “For God’s sake, smile, man!”
“Oh yes, Edward, do smile!” begged her ladyship. “It gives me a headache to see you frowning so.”
“Fanny, you will give me five minutes in private.”
“No, sir, I shall not. You are prodigious ill-natured to talk to me in this vein, and I protest I want no more of it.”
“There’s for you, Marling!” Rupert said. “Go and bespeak some breakfast. You’ll be better for it, I swear! ’Tis the emptiness of you makes you feel jaundiced: I know the feeling well. A ham, now, and some pasties, with coffee to wash it down will make a new man of you, stap me if it won’t!”
Lady Fanny giggled. Marling’s brow grew blacker, his eyes harder.
“You’ll regret this, madam. You’ve trifled with me once too often.”
“Oh, sir, I’m in no mood for your heroics! Pray keep them for Harriet! She has the taste for them, no doubt!”
“Try ’em on Justin,” suggested Rupert. “Here he is, with Léonie. Lord, what a happy gathering!”
“For the last time, Fanny﻿—I shall not ask again﻿—will you accord me a few minutes alone?”
“Alone?” echoed Rupert. “Ay, of course she will, as many as you like! Solitude’s the thing, so it is! Solitude, and a fat ham﻿—”
“My dear Marling, I hope I see you well?” His Grace had come quietly in.
Marling picked up his hat.
“I am in excellent health, I thank you, Avon.”
“But his spirits!” said Rupert. “Oh, lud!”
“I confess,” Marling said steadily, “my spirits are a little﻿—bruised.”
“Never say so!” Rupert feigned astonishment. “You’ve had a bad crossing, Edward, and your liver’s upside down.”
Avon turned.
“Your conversation is always so edifying, Rupert. Yet I believe we can dispense with it.”
Rupert collapsed promptly. My lady tossed her head. Avon went to the side-table, and poured out a glass of burgundy, and offered it to Marling, who waved it aside.
“I came, sir, to fetch my wife home. As she declines to accompany me there is no more to be said. I’ll take my leave of you.”

===Chinese===

阿翁举起单柄眼镜，透过镜片意味深长地审视着夫人。
“是的，贾斯汀。我横竖是不走。我要同你一道去巴黎。”
“听到你这般厚爱，我自然受宠若惊，”公爵大人说道。“即便如此，我亲爱的，你终究还是要随你夫君回去的。”
“多谢好意！”马林冷笑一声。“倘若她是奉了你的法旨才肯回心转意，我倒断断不肯领受！她必须遵从我的吩咐。”
“我才不会奉任何人的法旨行事呢！”范妮夫人的整张面孔抽搐纠结在一起，活像个快要哭出声来的毛孩子。“你们个个都狠心刻薄极了！”
马林默不作声。她用手帕抹着眼角。
“你一来就——盛气凌人、横眉冷对——我才不要同你回去呢！我恨死你了，爱德华！”
“闹到这一步，总算全说透了，”马林冷硬地说道，转身便朝大门走去。
屋里顿时响起一阵急促窸窣的丝绸响动，只见夫人像只受惊的飞鸟般飞奔穿过客厅。
“噢，爱德华，我方才说的全是气话，你明明知道我不是存心的！”
他伸手将她推离身前，隔着臂展之遥凝视着她。
“你当真肯随我回去？”
她犹豫不决，随后仰起脸望着他的面孔。两滴硕大的泪珠顺着面颊滚落下来。马林握住她的双手，用力紧紧握了握。
“说老实话，”他嗓音温柔了下来，“我实在受不得瞧你落泪，我心爱的宝贝。随贾斯汀去吧。”
听到这话，她猛地一头扎进他的怀抱里，号啕大哭起来。
“噢爱德华，我随你回去！我当真随你回去！你务必得宽恕我！”
“我亲爱的心肝！”他一把将她紧紧搂入怀中。
“我显然成了碍手碍脚的局外人（de trop），”公爵大人慢条斯理地评论道，顺手又给自己倒了一杯勃艮第葡萄酒。
“我随你回去，爱德华，可我心里——噢，我当真好想去巴黎呀！”
“那就去吧，我的心肝。我怎舍得剥夺你的快活呢。”
“可我万万舍不得离开你呀！”范妮抽抽搭搭地哭喊道。
“不知是否容我冒昧进一言？”公爵大人缓步走上前去。“实在犯不上这般痛断肝肠。这桩事体处置起来简单至极。”他向马林仪态万方地行了个潇洒大方的屈膝礼。“烦请随我们一同启程前往巴黎吧，我亲爱的爱德华。”
“噢，承蒙厚爱，但——”
“是啊，我心知肚明，”阿翁懒洋洋地说道。“你向来不屑踏进我那声名狼藉的不洁府门半步。”
马林涨红了脸。
“我发誓——”
“大可不必分辩，相信我。若非眼下我急需范妮相助，我也断不会提出这般惹你厌烦的倡议来。”
“我实在想不通你为何非要她相助不可，阿翁。”
公爵大人满脸不可置信的神情。
“我最亲爱的爱德华，我本以为凭你那恪守体统的刻板做派，其中的缘由理该在脑海里电光石火般一清二楚才是啊。”
“莱昂妮！我居然给忘了。”马林神情举棋不定地伫立在那儿。“难道你就寻不出旁的名门贵妇替她充当女伴监护人了吗？”
“我闭着眼睛都能寻出一百位（100）来，但我需要一位名正言顺的女主人执掌中馈。”
“既然如此，范妮便留在你身边吧。我自个儿打道回英国。”

===Original===

Avon put up his quizzing glass, and through it regarded my lady.
“Yes, Justin. I do. I am coming to Paris with you.”
“I am gratified, of course,” said his Grace. “Nevertheless, my dear, you will go with your husband.”
“I thank you!” Marling laughed harshly. “I do not take her an she comes at your bidding! She must come at mine.”
“I w-won’t go at anyone’s b-bidding!” Lady Fanny’s face puckered like that of a child about to cry. “You are very unkind!”
Marling said nothing. She dabbed at her eyes.
“You come﻿—bullying, and﻿—and scowling﻿—I won’t go with you! I hate you, Edward!”
“It needed only that,” said Marling, and turned to the door.
There was a rustle of silks as my lady fled across the room.
“Oh, Edward, I didn’t meant it, you know I didn’t!”
He held her away from him.
“You will return with me?”
She hesitated, then looked up into his face. Two large tears stole down her cheeks. Marling took her hands, and pressed them.
“In truth,” he said gently, “I cannot bear to see you weep, love. Go with Justin.”
At that she cast herself into his arms, and sobbed.
“Oh Edward, I will come! I truly will! You must f-forgive me!”
“My dear!” He caught her to him.
“I am decidedly de trop,” remarked his Grace, and poured out another glass of burgundy.
“I’ll come, Edward, but I do﻿—oh, I do want to go to Paris!”
“Then go, sweetheart. I’d not deny you your pleasure.”
“But I c-can’t bear to leave you!” sobbed Fanny.
“May I be allowed to make a suggestion?” His Grace came slowly forward. “There is really no occasion for these heartburnings. The matter is very simple.” He swept Marling a magnificent leg. “Pray come with us to Paris, my dear Edward.”
“Oh, I thank you, but﻿—”
“Yes, I know,” said Avon languidly. “You would prefer not to enter the unhallowed portals of my abode.”
Marling flushed.
“I protest﻿—”
“It is quite unnecessary, believe me. I would not propose such a distasteful plan were it not for the fact that I have need of Fanny.”
“I don’t understand why you should need her, Avon.”
His Grace was incredulous.
“My very dear Edward, I should have thought that with your strict sense of propriety the reason must positively leap to your understanding.”
“Léonie! I had forgot.” Marling stood irresolute. “Can you find no other lady to chaperon her?”
“I could doubtless find an hundred, but I require a hostess.”
“Then Fanny had best stay with you. I will go back to England.”

===Chinese===

范妮幽幽叹了口气。
“爱德华，要是你死活不肯去巴黎，那我唯有随你回老家去了。可我真巴不得你能一同前往呀！”
就在此刻，莱昂妮悄然现身，一瞥见马林的身影，当即开心地拍起巴掌来。
“天哪（Parbleu），是马林先生！早安，先生（Bonjour, m’sieur）！”
马林展颜一笑，执起她的纤手躬身亲吻。
“不知小姐近来可好？瞧你这一脸娇艳的气色，我便自有了答案。”
“我这小家伙看来在这双铁面无私的冷峻眼眸里颇为讨喜呢，”公爵大人低声喃喃道。“小家伙，我正竭尽所能劝说马林先生赏光移驾寒舍呢。烦请你也同我一道苦苦哀求他一番吧。”
“当真？”莱昂妮在两人脸上来回打量。“先生，求求您一同前往好吗？我这就去央求公爵大人把达弗南特先生也一并接来呢。”
阿翁忍不住莞尔一笑。
“当真绝妙的主意，我的闺女（ma fille）。”
“哎呀，孩子，我想我怕是去不得，”马林说。“你们把夫人带去便是，由着我回老家去吧。”
“哎，呸！”莱昂妮啐道。“全因你不喜欢公爵大人，不是吗？”
“我这小家伙向来快人快语、直言不讳，”阿翁评论道。“一语切中要害，孩子。”
“你觉得他不够正派体面。可他眼下规矩正派极了，我向你担保（je vous assure）！”
鲁珀特喉头猛地爆出一阵呛咳声；夫人的双肩剧烈耸动，马林更是彻底破功，忍不住前仰后合地放声大笑起来。莱昂妮满脸厌弃嫌鄙地瞪视着这三个笑得直不起腰的人，扭头望向公爵。
“大人，他们这是怎么了？究竟在傻笑什么？”
“我毫不知情，小家伙，”阿翁神情严肃地答道。
“依我看他们简直蠢透了。蠢不可及。”
然而这番放声大笑到底驱散了满屋的阴霾火药味。马林望向公爵，嗓音略带颤抖地开口道：
“我承认——正是公爵大人身上那份缺乏——缺乏正派体统的名声，一直像鱼刺般哽在我的喉头！”
“我相信定是如此，”公爵大人说道。“不过到时自有达弗南特在身旁陪着你。他定会欣喜若狂地同你一道为我早已丧尽的天良痛哭流涕的。”
“这番前景听着倒真诱人得很，”马林说道。他略带疑虑地扫了自己的妻子一眼。“可我实在觉得自个儿不大适合掺和进这场荒唐的冒险之中。”
“我亲爱的爱德华，难道我就很适合掺和其中吗？”公爵大人故作痛心疾首地反问道。“我全指望着你能移驾同往，好替咱们全家上下增添一抹持重沉稳的正气呢。”
马林神情古怪地打量着公爵大人身上那袭深绯红色的天鹅绒华贵外套。
“增添沉稳正气我倒勉为其难，可你呢，阿翁？依我看，你自个儿单枪匹马便足以撑起所有的奢靡排场了。”
“过奖过奖，”阿翁欠身致意。“这么说，我便算作你应允同行了？”
“答应吧，爱德华，快答应吧！噢求求你了！”
“得了吧，这准会好玩极了，先生（fort amusant, m’sieur）。您必须同往。”
鲁珀特也趁机大声起哄道。
“是啊，一道来吧，马林。

===Original===

Fanny sighed.
“Edward, if you will not come to Paris I must return with you. But I do wish that you would come!”
At that moment Léonie appeared, and clapped her hands at sight of Marling.
“*Parbleu*, it is M. Marling! *Bonjour, m’sieur!*”
He smiled and kissed her hand.
“I hope I see you well, child? Your pretty colour answers me.”
“My infant finds favour in the austere eyes,” murmured his Grace. “Infant, I am trying to prevail upon Mr. Marling to honour my poor house with his presence. Pray add your entreaties to mine.”
“Yes?” Léonie looked from one to the other. “Please will you come, m’sieur? I shall ask Monseigneur to invite M. Davenant also.”
In spite of himself Avon smiled.
“A happy thought, *ma fille*.”
“Why, child, I believe I must not,” Marling said. “You shall take her ladyship, and let me go home.”
“Ah, bah!” said Léonie. “It is because you do not like Monseigneur, is it not?”
“My infant is nothing if not outspoken,” remarked Avon. “That is the matter in a nutshell, child.”
“You do not think he is enough respectable. But indeed he is very respectable now, *je vous assure*!”
A choking sound came from Rupert; my lady’s shoulders shook, and Marling collapsed into helpless laughter. Léonie looked at the convulsed trio in disgust, and turned to the Duke.
“What is the matter with them, Monseigneur? Why do they laugh?”
“I have no idea, infant,” replied Avon gravely.
“They are silly, I think. Very silly.”
But the laughter cleared the air. Marling looked at the Duke, and said unsteadily:
“I confess﻿—it’s your lack of﻿—of respectability that sticks﻿—somewhat in my gullet!”
“I am sure it must,” said his Grace. “But you shall have Davenant to support you. He will be delighted to join you in mourning over my departed morals.”
“The prospect is most alluring,” Marling said. He glanced uncertainly at his wife. “But I do not think I fit well in this mad venture.”
“My dear Edward, do I fit well in it?” asked his Grace, pained. “I count upon you to aid me in lending a note of sobriety to the party.”
Marling regarded his Grace’s coat of dull crimson velvet quizzically.
“I might lend sobriety, but you, Avon? You supply the magnificence, I think.”
“You flatter me,” Avon bowed. “I am to understand that you will join us?”
“Yes, Edward, yes! Oh please!”
“Voyons, it will be *fort amusant, m’sieur*. You must come.”
Rupert ventured to uplift his voice.
“Ay, join us, Marling.

人多才热闹嘛。”
“面对这般盛情难却的苦苦挽留，我还能多说什么呢？”马林握住了妻子的纤手。“多谢你了，阿翁。我答应一同前往。”
“既然如此，最好打发加斯东立刻动身返回伦敦替你取换洗行装，”公爵大人说道。
莱昂妮在一旁咯咯轻笑起来。
“他非累死不可，大人。我敢断言。”
“正如你所见，”公爵大人转头冲着马林评论道，“死亡与横祸向来是我这小家伙取之不尽的开心乐子。”
马林伸手轻抚着莱昂妮的头顶。
“这孩子是个狡黠的小坏蛋，阿翁，不是吗？不过是个挺标致惹人怜爱的小坏蛋。”
莱昂妮睁大了双眼。
“当真（Vraiment）？大人，我生得标致吗？您也这么觉得吗？”
“还算过得去吧，我的小家伙，勉强过得去。”
她那张小脸顿时垮了下来。
“我先前就生怕您不这么觉得呢，大人。”
阿翁宠溺地捏了捏她的下巴。
“孩子，难道我平日里没唤过你‘我的美人（ma belle）’吗？”
莱昂妮一把抓起他的手背贴在唇边。
“多谢大人（Merci, Monseigneur）！您真教我心满意足极了（enfin）！”
马林冷不丁转头望向自己的妻子。她嫣然一笑，娇羞地垂下了眼帘。马林随即转头冲着鲁珀特说道：
“我想我打算采纳你方才那条高见——虽说来得不合时宜——我的好弟弟。”
鲁珀特咧嘴直笑。
“怎么，吃火腿吗？是啊，那绝对是条金玉良言，天打雷劈也是金玉良言！但我也不瞒你，我方才那番话纯粹是为了存心激怒你，爱德华。”
“你确实得手了，小混球。阿翁，我不劳烦你差使加斯东折返英国了。我自个儿回伦敦收拾停当，下周直接去巴黎同你们汇合便是。”
“我亲爱的爱德华，由着加斯东奔波一番对他大有裨益。他近来日益发福慵懒了。吩咐他在巴黎同咱们碰头便是。”
“你真是太体贴了，”马林欠身致谢。
“这可向来不是世人对我的评语，”公爵大人说道，抬手拉响了门铃。

===Original===

The more the merrier.”
“In face of such kind entreaties what can I say?” Marling took his wife’s hand. “I thank you, Avon. I will come.”
“Gaston, then, had best return to London for your baggage,” said his Grace.
Léonie chuckled.
“He will die, Monseigneur. I know it.”
“As you observe,” remarked his Grace to Marling, “death and disaster are a source of never-failing amusement to my infant.”
Marling laid his hand on Léonie’s head.
“She is a rogue, Avon, is she not? But a pretty rogue.”
Léonie opened wide her eyes.
“*Vraiment?* Am I pretty, Monseigneur? Do you think so?”
“Passable, my infant, passable.”
Her face fell.
“I was afraid you would not think so, Monseigneur.”
Avon pinched her chin.
“Child, do I not call you ‘*ma belle*’?”
Léonie caught his hand to her lips.
“*Merci, Monseigneur!* You make me very happy, *enfin*!”
Marling looked suddenly at his wife. She smiled, and cast down her eyes. Marling turned to Rupert.
“I think I’ll take your excellent﻿—though ill-timed﻿—advice, my boy.”
Rupert grinned.
“What, the ham? Ay, ’twas good advice, stap me it was! But I’ll not deny ’twas said to enrage you, Edward.”
“It succeeded in doing so, scamp. Avon, I’ll not ask you to send Gaston back to England. I can return there myself, and join you in Paris next week.”
“My dear Edward, it is good for Gaston to bestir himself. He grows fat and lazy. He shall meet us in Paris.”
“You are very good,” Marling bowed.
“That is not my reputation,” said his Grace, and rang the bell.
---
On the following morning the whole party set out for Paris. Lady Fanny was flustered, Marling amused, Rupert flippant, Léonie excited and the Duke leisurely and placid as ever. The entire population of Le Dennier turned out to see the passing of this cavalcade, and marvelled at the chaise piled high with baggage, at the great berline with his Grace’s arms blazoned on the door, and at the two smaller coaches that followed it.
The Marlings occupied one of these, while Avon, Léonie and Rupert travelled in the berline. Rupert was propped up with cushions to alleviate the discomfort of the jolting, and whiled away the time by playing cards with Léonie. His Grace lay back in his corner and watched them in some amusement.

===Chinese===

翌日清晨，一行人浩浩荡荡启程直奔巴黎。范妮夫人手忙脚乱，马林忍俊不禁，鲁珀特轻佻放浪，莱昂妮兴奋雀跃，而公爵大人则一如既往从容不迫、气定神闲。勒德尼耶（Le Dennier）全村的老少妇孺几乎倾巢而出，夹道围观这支气派非凡的车队隆隆驶过，无不惊叹于那辆堆积如山的双轮轻便行李车，惊叹于那辆车门上赫然绘着公爵家族显赫纹章的豪华柏林大马车（berline），以及紧随其后的两辆稍小的随从厢车。
马林夫妇端坐在其中一辆小厢车内，阿翁公爵、莱昂妮与鲁珀特则同乘那辆柏林大马车。鲁珀特周身垫满了软枕，以减轻车身颠簸带来的伤口剧痛，一路同莱昂妮摇骰子玩牌消磨漫漫长途。公爵大人则气定神闲地仰靠在车厢角落里，神情间带着几分忍俊不禁的笑意，安然注视着这一对欢喜冤家。

## XI Mr. Marling’s Heart Is Won / 马林先生的心被俘获了
===Original===

Lady Fanny found her husband in the library, standing before the fire and warming his hands. He was a medium-sized man, with regular features and steady grey eyes. He turned as she entered the room, and held out his arms to her. Lady Fanny tripped towards him.
“Pray have a care for my gown, Edward. ’Tis new come from Cerisette. Is it not elegant?”
“Prodigious elegant,” agreed Marling. “But if it means that I must not kiss you I shall think it hideous.”
She raised china-blue eyes to his face.
“Just one then, Edward. Oh, you are greedy, sir! No, Edward, I’ll not be held. I’ve a monstrous exciting thing to tell you.” She shot him a sidelong glance, wondering how he would take her news. “Do you remember, my love, that I was so *ennuyée* today that I could almost have cried?”
“Do I not!” smiled Marling. “You were very cruel to me, sweet.”
“Oh no, Edward! I was not cruel! ’Twas you who were so very provoking. And then you went away, and I was so dull! But now it is all over, and I have something wonderful to do!”
Edward slipped an arm about her trim waist.
“Faith, what is it?”
“ ’Tis a girl,” she answered. “The most beautiful girl, Edward!”
“A girl?” he repeated. “What new whim is this? What do you want with a girl, my dear?”
“Oh, I didn’t want her! I never thought about her at all. How could I, when I’d not set eyes on her? Justin brought her.”
The clasp about her waist slackened.
“Justin?” said Marling. “Oh!” His voice was polite, but not enthusiastic. “I thought he was in Paris.”
“So he was, until a day or two ago, and if you are minded to be disagreeable, Edward, I shall cry. I am very *fond* of Justin!”
“Ay, dear. Go on with your tale. What has the girl, whoever she is, to do with Avon?”
“That is just the astonishing part of it!” said Fanny, her brow clearing as if by magic. “She is Justin’s adopted daughter! Is it not interesting, Edward?”
“*What?*” Marling’s arm fell away from her. “Justin’s what?”
“Adopted daughter,” she answered airily. “The sweetest child, my dear, and so devoted to him! I declare I quite love her already, although she is so lovely, and﻿—oh, Edward, don’t be cross!”
Edward took her by the shoulders, and made her look up at him.
“Fanny, do you mean to tell me that Alastair had the effrontery to bring the girl here? And you were mad enough to take her in?”
“Indeed, sir, and why not?” she demanded.

===Chinese===

范妮夫人走进藏书室时，只见丈夫正立在壁炉前烘烤着双手。他是个中等身材的男子，五官端正匀称，生着一双沉稳笃定的灰眼睛。听闻她踏进房门，他转过身来，张开双臂迎向她。范妮夫人迈着轻盈的碎步迎上前去。
“爱德华，留神些我的礼服。这可是打塞丽塞特（Cerisette）那儿新裁制得来的呢。难道不够华贵雅致吗？”
“华贵雅致到了极点，”马林附和道，“但倘若这便意味着我碰不得你的芳唇，那我倒宁可觉得它丑陋不堪了。”
她抬起一双瓷蓝色的明眸凝望着他的面孔。
“那就只许亲一下，爱德华。噢，先生，你可真够贪得无厌的！不行，爱德华，不许老搂着我。我有桩惊天动地、激动人心的大事要同你分说呢。”她斜睨了他一眼，心中暗暗揣度着他听闻消息后会作何反应。“我亲爱的心肝，你可还记得今儿早晨我无聊沉闷（ennuyée）得简直快要哭出声来了吗？”
“我难道还不记得嘛！”马林展颜微笑道，“你方才待我可狠心刻薄极了，我的心肝。”
“噢才没有呢，爱德华！我哪有狠心！分明是你自个儿太招人头疼磨人了。后来你抽身走开了，我心里冷清乏味极了！可眼下一切全翻篇了，我手里正有桩绝妙的差事要做呢！”
爱德华伸手揽住她纤细的腰肢。
“凭良心讲，究竟是何等差事？”
“是个姑娘家，”她答道。“天底下生得顶绝色标致的姑娘，爱德华！”
“姑娘家？”他重复了一遍。“这又是哪出新冒出来的一时兴起？我亲爱的，你凭白要个姑娘家作甚？”
“噢，才不是我自个儿想要的呢！我原先压根连想都没想过这一节。面都未曾谋过，我上哪儿想得着呢？是贾斯汀把她送来的。”
环在她腰际的那只手骤然松弛了下来。
“贾斯汀？”马林说道。“噢！”他的语气客气得体，却听不出半点热情。“我还以为他眼下正待在巴黎呢。”
“直到一两天（1-2）前他确实还在巴黎呢，要是你非存心在这儿使小性子惹人不快，爱德华，我可真要哭鼻子了。我可是打心眼里疼惜贾斯汀的！”
“是是是，我的好夫人。继续说你的奇遇吧。那个姑娘——管她是何许人也——同阿翁究竟有何干系？”
“这也正是整件事里最教人拍案惊奇的一节呢！”范妮说道，两道娥眉刹那间舒展开来，满脸笑逐颜开。“她是贾斯汀收养的义女！爱德华，这难道不教人拍案叫绝吗？”
“什么？”马林揽着她的手彻底滑落了下来。“贾斯汀的什么？”
“收养的义女，”她轻描淡写地随口答道。“天底下顶招人疼爱的孩子，我亲爱的，而且待他那叫一个死心塌地！我发誓我已经快爱死她了，虽说她生得未免太娇美了些，而且——噢，爱德华，别板着脸动怒嘛！”
爱德华双手按住她的香肩，迫使她抬头与自己正视。
“范妮，难道你是想同我说，阿拉斯泰尔竟然有这般泼天的胆量把那姑娘领到了这儿？而你居然也当真疯疯癫癫地把她收留下来了？”
“千真万确，先生，这有何不可呢？”她理直气壮地质问道。

===Original===

“A pretty thing ’twould be an I turned away my brother’s ward!”
“Ward!” Marling almost snorted.
“Yes, sir, his ward. Oh, I’ll not deny I thought the same as you when first I saw her, but Justin swore ’twas not so. And Edward, you know how strict Justin is with me. You can’t be cross! Why, ’tis but a child, and half a boy at that!”
“Half a boy, Fanny? What mean you?”
“She has been a boy for seven years,” said Fanny triumphantly. Then, as the lines about his mouth hardened, she stamped her foot angrily. “You’re very unkind, Edward! How dare you suppose that darling Justin would bring his light o’ love to my house? ’Tis the stupidest notion I ever heard! He wants me to chaperon the child until he can prevail upon Madam Field to come. What if she has been a boy? Pray what has that to say to anything?”
Marling smiled unwillingly.
“You must admit that for Justin to adopt a girl﻿—”
“Edward, I truly believe that he means no ill! Léonie has been his page﻿—Oh, now you are shocked again!”
“Well, but﻿—”
“I won’t hear a word!” Fanny put up her hands to his mouth. “Edward, you’ll not be angry, and hard?” she coaxed. “There’s some mystery about Léonie, I feel sure, but﻿—oh, my dear, you have only to look in her eyes! Now listen to me, dear Edward!”
He imprisoned her hands in his, drawing her to the couch.
“Very well, my dear, I’ll listen.”
Fanny seated herself.
“Dearest Edward! I knew you’d be kind! You see, Justin came here today with Léonie dressed as a boy. I was so enchanted! I never imagined that Justin was in England! Oh, and he has a fan! You cannot conceive anything so absurd, dear! Though indeed I believe they are become quite the most fashion﻿—”
“Ay, Fanny, but you were to explain about this girl﻿—Léonie.”
“I was explaining,” she protested, pouting. “Well, he sent Léonie into another room﻿—my dear, I think she positively worships him, poor child﻿—and he begged me to keep her with me for a few days because he does not want there to be a shadow of scandal attached to her. And I am to clothe her, and oh, Edward, will it not be entertaining? She has red hair, and black eyebrows, and I have given her my green silk. You cannot imagine how quite too tiresomely lovely she is, though perhaps she would look better in white.”
“Never mind that, Fanny. Go on with your story.”
“To be sure.

===Chinese===

“难道要把家兄的受监护人拒之门外不成，那成何体统！”
“受监护人！”马林气得几乎从鼻孔里哼出声来。
“正是，先生，他的受监护人。噢，我也不瞒你，刚瞧见她那一刻我也同你一个心思，可贾斯汀赌咒发誓绝无不轨勾当。而且爱德华，你是最清楚贾斯汀在我跟前管束得有多森严的。你横竖是生不起气来的！哎呀，那分明还是个毛孩子，而且大半截还是个男童习气呢！”
“大半截是个男童，范妮？你这话究竟是何意？”
“她当男孩子整整当了七年（7）呢，”范妮得意洋洋地宣布道。紧接着，瞥见丈夫嘴角紧绷的冷硬线条，她气急败坏地一跺脚。“你真是不近人情，爱德华！你怎么敢胡乱猜疑亲爱的贾斯汀会把他的风月情妇领到我的府门上来？这简直是我平生听闻过最愚不可及的荒唐念头！他不过是央求我在菲尔德夫人抵埠之前，替这孩子充当几日女伴监护人罢了。她即便曾当过男孩子又如何？请问这干卿何事？”
马林不由自主地掠过一丝苦笑。
“你总得承认，由贾斯汀收养一个姑娘家——”
“爱德华，我当真坚信他绝无半点加害歹意！莱昂妮先前一直充任他身旁的侍童——噢，这会儿你又大惊小怪起来了！”
“可话虽如此——”
“我不听我不听！”范妮伸手一把捂住他的嘴。“爱德华，你可不许动怒发作，也不许狠心冷酷！”她温言软语地娇嗔央求道。“我敢断言这孩子身上必定大有身世玄机，可——噢，我亲爱的，你只消拿眼瞧瞧她的那双眸子便自知晓了！现在且老老实实听我说，亲爱的爱德华！”
他将她的双手拢在掌心，拉着她在长榻上坐下。
“好好好，我亲爱的，我洗耳恭听便是。”
范妮在长榻上款款落座。
“最亲爱的爱德华！我就知道你最通情达理了！你瞧，今儿贾斯汀领着一身男童打扮的莱昂妮登门。我都高兴坏了！我做梦也没想到贾斯汀居然已经回英国了！噢，而且他手头还摇着一把折扇呢！你根本想象不出那副模样有多荒唐滑稽，亲爱的！虽说眼下这东西好像成了最入时的风尚——”
“行了，范妮，你原本是要解释那个姑娘——莱昂妮的来历的。”
“我这不是正解释着嘛，”她噘着嘴娇嗔地抗议道。“行吧，他把莱昂妮打发到偏厅去候着——我亲爱的，我想那可怜的孩子简直对他顶礼膜拜、奉若神明呢——他苦苦央求我把她收留在身边住上几日，因为他绝不容许她的名节蒙受半点闲言碎语的玷污。而且全由我替她置办一身名媛闺秀的行头，噢，爱德华，这难道不是好玩透顶的差事吗？她长着一头红鬈发，生着两道浓黑的柳叶眉，我已经把我那件绿丝绸礼服赏赐给她了。你根本想象不出她生得有多令人头疼地千娇百媚，虽说兴许穿白裙裳还要更为衬她呢。”
“别扯远了，范妮。接着往下说。”
“那是自然。

===Original===

It seems that Justin found her in Paris﻿—only then he thought she was a boy﻿—and she was being ill-treated by some tavern-keeper. So Justin bought her and made her his page. And he says that he has a fondness for her, and will make her his ward. And oh, Edward, I have just thought how wonderfully romantic ’twould be an he married her! But she is only a child, and dreadfully boyish. Only fancy!﻿—she insisted on keeping her breeches! Now, Edward, say that you will be nice to her, and that I may keep her! Say it, Edward, say it!”
“I suppose you must keep her,” he said reluctantly. “I cannot turn her out. But I do not like it.”
Fanny embraced him.
“It doesn’t signify in the least, Edward. You will fall in love with her, and I shall be jealous.”
“There’s no fear of that, you little rogue,” he said, and gave her hand a quick squeeze.
“No, and I am so glad. And now go and put on that new puce coat. ’Tis prodigious modish, and I want you to look very nice tonight.”
“Are we not dining out?” he asked. “I thought﻿—”
“Dining out! Good gracious, Edward, and that child a visitor, and only just arrived! No indeed!” With that she rustled out of the room, full of a new importance.
An hour later, when Marling sat in the withdrawing-room awaiting his wife, the door was flung open, and Fanny sailed in. Behind her came Léonie, hesitantly. Edward rose quickly, staring.
“My love,” said Fanny, “this is my husband, Mr. Marling. Edward, Mademoiselle de Bonnard.”
Marling bowed; so also did Léonie, but paused in the act of doing so.
“I must curtsy, is it not so? Bah, what skirts!” She smiled shyly up at Edward. “Please pardon me, m’sieur. I have not learned to curtsy yet.”
“Give him your hand, child,” commanded Fanny.
The small hand was extended.
“Please, why?” asked Léonie.
Marling kissed her fingertips punctiliously, and released them. Léonie’s cheeks were tinged with colour, and she looked doubtfully up at him.
“*Mais, m’sieur*”﻿—she began.
“Mademoiselle?” In spite of himself Marling smiled.
“*C’est peu convenable*,” explained Léonie.
“No such thing,” said Fanny briskly. “Gentlemen do always kiss the lady’s hands. Remember that, my love. And now my husband will give you his arm to the dining-room. Lay but the tips of your fingers on it, like that. What ails you now, child?”
“It is nothing, madame. Only that I am not at all myself. I think that I look very strange.”

===Chinese===

原委似乎是贾斯汀在巴黎街头撞见了她——只是当时他还当她是个男童呢——而且她正惨遭某个客栈掌柜的毒打虐待。于是贾斯汀出资把她买了下来，带在身边做侍童。他说他心底对这孩子生出了一份怜爱，打算收她作受监护人。噢，爱德华，我方才冷不丁琢磨着，倘若贾斯汀有朝一日明媒正娶了她，那该是一出何等浪漫绝伦的神仙姻缘啊！不过她眼下终究还是个毛孩子，浑身上下满是男孩子的鲁莽野性。你敢信吗！——她方才还死活吵嚷着非留着那条马裤不可呢！好了，爱德华，快表个态答应你会待她和颜悦色，准许我把她留在府里！快说呀，爱德华，快表个态！”
“我想你也只能收留她了，”他无可奈何地勉强说道。“我总不能狠心把她撵到大马路上去。但我终究不怎么乐意。”
范妮一把搂住他的脖颈亲吻起来。
“这根本无关紧要，爱德华。你待会儿非深深迷上她不可，到时候我可要吃大醋呢。”
“那可断断用不着担心，你这磨人的小淘气，”他说道，顺势用力捏了捏她的小手。
“那就好，这我就放心了。现在快去换上那身新裁的暗紫褐色外套吧。入时气派极了，今晚我非要你打扮得英俊得体不可。”
“今晚咱们不是要去外头赴宴吗？”他探问道。“我原以为——”
“去外头赴宴！我的老天爷呀，爱德华，府里添了这么一位贵客，而且才刚刚抵埠！断断使不得！”言罢，她满腹雷厉风行的全新派头，裙裾窸窣地快步跨出了房门。
一小时（1）后，当马林端坐在会客雅间里静候妻子时，房门被猛地推开，范妮仪态万方地飘然迈入。在她身后，莱昂妮脚步迟疑地怯生生跟了进来。爱德华迅速站起身，目不转睛地审视着眼前之人。
“我亲爱的，”范妮引见道，“这位是拙夫马林先生。爱德华，这位是德·博纳尔小姐。”
马林躬身作揖；莱昂妮也依样画葫芦地躬身还礼，却在动作做到一半时猛地僵住了。
“我必须行屈膝礼，不是吗？呸，这什么该死的裙子！”她羞怯怯地仰起脸冲着爱德华展颜一笑。“请宽恕我，先生。我还没学会该怎么行屈膝礼呢。”
“把手递给他，孩子，”范妮发号施令道。
那只纤细白皙的小手怯生生地递了过来。
“请问这是为何呀？”莱昂妮打听道。
马林一丝不苟、彬彬有礼地亲吻了她的指尖，随即将手放开。莱昂妮的双颊泛起了一抹娇艳的绯红，满腹疑窦地抬头端详着他。
“可是，先生（Mais, m’sieur）——”她欲言又止。
“小姐有何指教？”马林不由自主地莞尔一笑。
“这可大不成体统（C’est peu convenable），”莱昂妮解释道。
“哪有这回事，”范妮雷厉风行地纠偏道。“绅士向来都是要亲吻贵妇的纤手的。牢牢记清楚了，我的心肝。现在由拙夫奉上臂弯领你移步去膳厅。只消把指尖虚搭在他胳膊上即可，对，就这样。你这又是怎么了，孩子？”
“没什么，夫人。唯独觉得自个儿浑身上下都不自在了。我想我瞧着必定古怪滑稽透顶了。”

===Original===

“Tell the silly child that it is not so, Edward,” sighed her ladyship.
Edward found that he was patting Léonie’s hand.
“My dear, ’tis as my lady says. You look very proper and charming.”
“Ah bah!” said Léonie.

===Chinese===

“爱德华，快同这傻孩子讲明白绝无此事，”夫人幽幽叹道。
爱德华猛然发现自己正不由自主地轻拍着莱昂妮的小手。
“我亲爱的孩子，正如内人所言。你瞧着端庄体面极了，而且楚楚动人。”
“哎，呸！”莱昂妮脱口啐道。

## XVII Of a Capture, a Chase, and Confusion / 劫持、追捕与混乱
===Original===

“*Oh, parbleu!*” Léonie said in disgust. “This Rupert he is always late, the *vaurien*!”
“My dearest love,” Madam Field reproved her. “That expression! Indeed, it is not becoming in a young lady! I must beg of you﻿—”
“Today I am not a lady at all,” said Léonie flatly. “I want Monseigneur to come.”
“My dear, it is hardly proper in you to﻿—”
“Ah, bah!” said Léonie, and walked away.
She went to her own apartment, and sat disconsolately down at the window.
“It is two weeks since Monseigneur wrote,” she reflected. “And then he said, I come soon now. *Voyons*, this is no way to keep that promise! And Rupert is late again.” A sparkle came into her eyes. She jumped up. “I will have a game with Rupert,” she said.
With this intention she pulled her boy’s raiment out of the cupboard, and struggled out of her skirts. Her hair had grown, but it was not yet long enough to be confined in the nape of her neck by a ribbon. It clustered about her head still in a myriad of soft curls. She brushed it back from her forehead, dressed herself in shirt and breeches and coat, and catching up her tricorne, swaggered downstairs. Luckily Madam Field was nowhere to be seen, so she escaped without let or hindrance into the garden. It was the first time she had ventured out of doors in her boy’s gear, and since it was an illicit pleasure her eyes twinkled naughtily. Rupert, with all his laxity, had in him a quaint streak of prudery, as she knew.
He would of a certainty be shocked to see her parading the grounds thus clad, and as this was precisely what she wanted she set out in the hope of meeting him, making for the woods that ran down towards the road.
Halfway across the big meadow that separated her from the woodland she espied Rupert coming from the stables, carrying his hat under his arm, and whistling jauntily. Léonie cupped her hands about her mouth.
“Ohé, Rupert!” she called gleefully.
Rupert saw her, stood still for a moment, and then came striding towards her.
“Fiend seize it, what will you be at next?” he shouted. “ ’Pon my soul, it’s scandalous, stap me if it’s not! Home with you, you hoyden!”
“I shall not, Milor’ Rupert!” she cried tauntingly, and danced away. “You cannot make me!”
“Can I not, then?” called Rupert, and, dropping his hat, broke into a run.

===Chinese===

“噢，见鬼（Oh, parbleu）！”莱昂妮满脸厌恶鄙夷地嘟囔道。“这个鲁珀特老是磨磨蹭蹭迟到，真是个无赖游手好闲之徒（vaurien）！”
“我最心爱的小宝贝，”菲尔德夫人在一旁训诫道。“姑娘家怎能把这等粗俗字眼挂在嘴边！这可半点不合大家闺秀的体统！我求求你——”
“今儿我压根不想当什么闺秀名媛，”莱昂妮生硬地顶撞道。“我只要公爵大人回来。”
“我亲爱的，你这般做派可实在不成体统——”
“哎，呸！”莱昂妮啐道，转身大步走开了。
她径直走回自个儿的闺房，满腹惆怅地在窗台前坐了下来。
“离公爵大人来信已经隔了整整两周（2）了，”她在心底暗暗琢磨着。“他信里分明讲了，过不了多久便会赶回来。得了吧（Voyons），这算哪门子信守诺言嘛！而且鲁珀特今儿又迟到了。”一抹狡黠促狭的光芒刹那间跃入了她的眼帘。她霍地一跃而起。“我非跟鲁珀特好好玩上一局不可，”她自言自语道。
打定主意之后，她一把从衣橱深处拽出那套久违的男童行头，七手八脚地挣脱了身上的长裙。她的头发虽说长长了些许，却还不足以在脑后用缎带扎成发辫。一簇簇柔软的鬈发依旧像繁星般蓬松地簇拥在她那颗小脑袋四周。她把碎发整齐地往后梳拢露出光洁的额头，换上衬衫、马裤与外套，抄起那顶三角帽，大摇大摆地晃荡着下了楼。好在四下里并无菲尔德夫人的身影，她便顺顺当当、毫无阻拦地一头扎进了花园里。这还是她头一回大着胆子一身男装走到户外，鉴于这是一桩背德违禁的刺激乐子，她的一双眸子里闪烁着顽皮得意的光芒。她深知，鲁珀特平日里虽说放荡不羁，骨子里却偏偏生着一根古怪刻板的假正经筋骨。
瞧见她这副大摇大摆漫步庄园的男儿身打扮，鲁珀特必定会大惊失色、骇破胆的，而这恰恰就是她巴不得瞧见的乐子，于是她怀着撞见他的期许，径直奔向了那片一直蔓延到大路边沿的幽深树林。
穿过将树林与宅邸隔开的那片开阔草地半途，她一眼瞥见鲁珀特正从马厩那头晃晃悠悠踱步走来，帽子夹在腋下，嘴里还吊儿郎当地吹着轻快的口哨。莱昂妮连忙拢起双手合成喇叭状凑在嘴边。
“喂——噢，鲁珀特！”她欢天喜地地高声大喊道。
鲁珀特瞧见了她，猛地僵立在原地愣了片刻，随即大步流星地朝她狂奔而来。
“见鬼，你这小蹄子接下来不知又要胡闹出什么名堂来！”他扯着嗓子大吼道。“凭良心讲，这简直伤风败俗到了极点，老天打雷劈也是伤风败俗！快给我滚回屋里去，你这个野丫头！”
“我偏不回，鲁珀特勋爵大人！”她满脸挑衅讥讽地大喊大叫着，像只灵巧的小鹿般轻盈旋舞着闪避开来。“你拿我没辙！”
“老子拿你没辙是吧？”鲁珀特嚷嚷道，顺手把帽子往地上一扔，拔腿便狂奔直追上来。

===Original===

Léonie straightway dived into the wood, and fled as for her life, for she knew very well that if he caught her Rupert would have no hesitation in picking her up and carrying her back to the house.
“Wait till I catch you!” threatened Rupert, crashing through the undergrowth. “Damme, I’ve torn my ruffle, and the lace cost me fifteen guineas! Plague take it, where are you!”
Léonie sent a mocking cry echoing through the wood, and ran on, listening to Rupert’s blundering progress behind her. She led him in and out of the trees, through bushes, round in circles, and over the stream, always keeping just out of sight, until she found herself coming out into the road. She would have turned, and doubled back, had she not chanced to see a light travelling coach standing near by. She was surprised, and tiptoed to peep at it over a low thorn-bush. In the distance she heard Rupert’s voice, half-exasperated, half-laughing. She threw back her head to call to him, and as she did so, saw to her amazement the Comte de Saint-Vire, walking quickly up on one of the paths that led through the wood. He was frowning, and his heavy mouth pouted. He looked up, and as his glance fell upon her the frown went from his face, and he came hurrying towards her.
“I give you good morrow, Léon the Page,” he said, and the words bit. “I had hardly hoped that I should find you thus soon. The luck is with me this round, I think.”
Léonie retreated a little. Avon’s warning was in her mind.
“*Bon jour, m’sieur*,” she said, and wondered what he was doing in the Duke’s grounds, or why he was in England at all. “Did you go to see Monseigneur?” she asked, with wrinkled brow. “He is not here.”
“I am desolated,” said Saint-Vire sarcastically, and came right up to her. She shrank, and in a fit of inexplicable panic, called to Rupert.
“Rupert, Rupert, *à moi*!”
Even as she cried Saint-Vire’s hand was over her mouth and his other arm about her waist. Struggling madly she was swept from the ground and borne at a run to where the coach stood waiting. Without compunction she bit deeply into the hand over her mouth. There was a muttered oath, the hand flinched a little, and she jerked her head away to shriek again.
“Rupert, Rupert, *on m’enporte*! *À moi, à moi, à moi!*”
His voice came to her, nearer at hand.
“Who﻿—what﻿—? What the devil﻿—?”

===Chinese===

莱昂妮当即一头钻进了树林深处，像逃命般拔腿飞奔，因为她心里再清楚不过，一旦被鲁珀特逮个正着，他定会毫不客气地一把将她打横扛起强行扛回宅邸去。
“有种给我站住，看老子逮着你怎么收拾你！”鲁珀特在身后一路劈波斩浪般踏碎灌木枯枝恶狠狠地威胁道。“真他娘的该死，老子的袖口花边都被挂烂了，那蕾丝花边可是花了我整整十五个几尼（15）的银子呢！瘟神缠身了，你这小狐狸究竟躲到哪儿去了！”
莱昂妮冲着林子里丢下一声嘲弄的清脆笑声，娇小的身形在树影间飞掠，耳畔听着鲁珀特在身后笨拙狂暴的盲目追赶。她领着他在密林深处兜兜转转，穿灌木，绕圈子，越溪流，始终恰到好处地将身形隐匿在视线之外，直到一不留神冷不丁一头钻出了树林，来到了大路边沿。她本打算掉头折返回林子里，偏巧无意间瞥见大路旁正静悄悄停泊着一辆轻便旅行马车。她大感诧异，蹑手蹑脚地凑近一丛矮荆棘丛，探头朝外偷瞧。远处林子里隐隐传来了鲁珀特半是气恼半是发笑的呼喊声。她正要仰起脑袋回应他，就在这一瞬间，她惊骇欲绝地瞧见圣维尔伯爵正沿着穿林小径的一头快步朝这边走来。他面色阴沉，厚厚的嘴唇紧闭着。他一抬眼，当视线冷不丁撞在莱昂妮身上那一刻，脸上的阴霾刹那间烟消云散，快步朝她迎面扑来。
“早安啊，侍童莱昂，”他语气森冷如刀，字字透着刺骨的寒意。“我真没料到自个儿竟能这般快便撞见你。这一局看来是老天眷顾于我了。”
莱昂妮本能地连连后退。阿翁当年的严厉告诫在脑海里电光石火般炸响。
“日安，先生（Bon jour, m’sieur），”她说，心里百思不得其解他为何会出现在公爵家的领地上，又为何会大驾光临英国。“您是来拜会公爵大人的吗？”她蹙起娥眉打听道。“他眼下不在此处。”
“那可真教人遗憾万分啊，”圣维尔皮笑肉不笑地讥诮道，步步紧逼径直走到她跟前。她吓得浑身瑟缩，在一阵毫无由头的恐慌驱使下，扯开嗓子拼命向鲁珀特呼救：
“鲁珀特，鲁珀特，快救我（Rupert, Rupert, à moi）！”
不等她把呼喊吐尽，圣维尔一只铁钳般的大手已然狠狠捂住了她的嘴巴，另一条臂膀如铁箍般死死勒住了她的细腰。伴随着她拼命挣扎扑打，整个人被硬生生凌空提离地面，一路疾奔被强行拖向等候在旁的马车。莱昂妮毫不留情，一口狠狠咬在那只捂着嘴的大手上。手背传来一声沉闷的咒骂，铁掌不由得吃痛松脱了那么一丁点，莱昂妮拼命甩开脑袋，再度爆出一声凄厉的尖叫：
“鲁珀特，鲁珀特，有人要把我劫走了（on m’enporte）！快来救我，快来救我，快来救我啊（À moi, à moi, à moi）！”
鲁珀特焦急的嗓音从不远处近在咫尺的林子里轰然炸响：
“是谁——怎么了——？到底出了什么鬼动静——？”

===Original===

She was flung then into the coach, sprang up like a small fury, but was thrust roughly back again. She heard Saint-Vire give an order to the coachman; then he jumped in beside her, and the coach lurched forward.
Rupert came plunging out into the road, hot and dishevelled, just in time to see the coach disappear round the bend in the road, in the direction of the village.
He had suspected at first that Léonie was only teasing him, but her second cry had held a note of genuine alarm, while now there was no sign of her. With characteristic impetuosity he went headlong down the road in pursuit of the coach, never stopping to consider the wisdom of returning to the stables for his horse. Full-tilt he went, hatless, with torn ruffles, and wig askew. The coach was out of sight, but he ran on until he was blown. Then he dropped into a walk. When he had got his breath back he ran again, and had a grin for the comic figure he knew he must be cutting. He had no idea who had seized Léonie, or why, but he felt certain that she was in that coach. His fighting spirit was aroused, and, incidentally, his love of adventure: he determined to catch the coach if it cost him his life. So, alternately running and walking, he came at last to the straggling village, three miles distant, and seeing the first cottage, broke once more into a weary jog-trot.
The blacksmith was working in his yard, and looked up in astonishment as Rupert’s well-known figure approached.
“Hey, there!” Rupert panted. “A coach﻿—passed this way. Where went﻿—it?”
The smithy rose and touched his forelock.
“Yes, my lord.”
“Devil take you! The coach!”
“Yes, my lord, yes,” said the puzzled smith.
“Did﻿—it﻿—pass here?” demanded Rupert in stentorian tones.
Light broke upon the smith.
“Why, yes, your lordship, and stopped at the Arms. ’Tis gone this twenty minutes.”
“Curse it! Whither?”
The smith shook his head.
“Beg pardon, your lordship, but I was not watching.”
“You’re a fool,” said Rupert, and plodded on.
The landlord of the Avon Arms was more communicative. He came bustling out to meet his young lordship, and threw up his hands at sight of him.
“My lord! Why, your lordship has lost his hat! Your coat, sir﻿—”
“Never mind my coat,” said Rupert. “Where went that coach?”
“The French gentleman’s coach, sir?”
Rupert had collapsed on to the settle, but he sat bolt upright now.
“French? *French?* So that’s it, is it?

===Chinese===

紧接着她被粗暴地狠狠扔进了车厢，莱昂妮像只被激怒的小母豹般一跃窜起反抗，却被无情地一把推搡跌回座位上。她听见圣维尔冲着车夫厉声下达了启程号令；随即伯爵翻身跃入车厢紧挨着她坐定，整辆马车猛然一颠，飞速向前疾驰而去。
鲁珀特气喘吁吁、衣衫不整地从密林深处一头撞出大路，恰好目睹那辆马车拐过路面弯道，一路绝尘朝着村庄的方向疾驰而去。
起初他尚且疑心莱昂妮不过是在同他闹着玩恶作剧，可她那第二声凄厉的求救声里分明带着货真价实的惊恐绝望，而眼下四周更是连她半点人影都找不着了。凭着他那风风火火、不计后果的混不吝脾性，他拔腿便沿着大路直追马车而去，压根连折回马厩骑马的念头都未曾转过。他光着脑袋连帽子都没戴，袖口蕾丝碎成布条，假发歪歪斜斜，一路全速狂奔。马车早已消失在视线尽头，他却依旧拼命狂奔直到上气不接下气、两肺快要炸开。他这才被迫放慢脚步快步疾走。待到喘匀了粗气，他又拔腿狂奔起来，嘴角甚至还忍不住自嘲地咧嘴一笑，深知自个儿眼下这副狼狈模样必定滑稽透顶。他压根摸不清究竟是何方神圣强掳了莱昂妮，又究竟是图什么，但他百分之百笃定那孩子就在那辆马车里。他骨子里的血性与好斗精神被彻底激荡了起来，外加他对冒险天生的痴迷：他暗暗发誓哪怕豁出这条命，也非把那辆马车截停不可。就这样，一路跑一阵走一阵，他终于赶到了三英里（3）开外那座稀稀落落的村庄，一瞥见第一间农舍茅屋，他再度拖着疲惫不堪的步子小跑了起来。
铁匠正在铺子院落里打铁劳作，一抬眼瞧见鲁珀特那道熟稔的身影仓皇逼近，不由得大感惊诧。
“喂，那边的！”鲁珀特喘得上气不接下气。“刚才是不是有一辆马车——打这儿疾驰过去了。究竟往哪儿——跑了？”
铁匠站起身，恭谨地抚了抚额前的乱发。
“回勋爵大人的话，有的。”
“见鬼去吧！那辆马车呢！”
“是，勋爵大人，是，”一头雾水的铁匠唯唯诺诺道。
“究竟——是不是——打这儿过去的？”鲁珀特声如洪钟地厉声喝问道。
铁匠这才恍然大悟。
“哎呀，回勋爵大人的话，确实过去了，还在阿翁军械客栈（Arms）门前停了一遭呢。已经过去整整二十分钟（20）了。”
“真该死！究竟奔哪儿去了？”
铁匠连连摇头。
“万乞恕罪，勋爵大人，小的方才只顾着干活没留神看。”
“你真是个蠢蛋，”鲁珀特骂了一句，拖着沉重的步伐继续向前赶去。
阿翁军械客栈的掌柜可就要健谈得多。一瞧见自个儿年轻的勋爵大驾光临，连忙一路小跑迎出门来，一打量他的尊容当即失声惊呼着举起了双手。
“勋爵大人！哎呀天哪，大人的帽子去哪儿了？您这身衣裳，先生——”
“少他娘的管我的衣裳，”鲁珀特粗暴地打断道。“刚才那辆马车往哪儿开去了？”
“大人指的是那位法国老爷的马车吗，先生？”
鲁珀特原本已然筋疲力竭地瘫坐在长椅上，闻言猛地像弹簧般直挺挺弹坐了起来。
“法国人？法国佬？闹了半天竟然是这么回事吗？

===Original===

Oho, M. le Comte! But what the deuce does he want with Léonie?”
The landlord looked at him sympathetically, and waited for him to explain.
“Ale!” said Rupert, sinking back again. “And a horse, and a pistol.”
The landlord was more perplexed than ever, but he went off to fetch ale in a large tankard. Rupert disposed of it speedily, and drew a deep breath.
“Did the coach stop here?” he demanded. “Did you see my brother’s ward in it?”
“Mistress Léonie, my lord? No, indeed! The French gentleman did not alight. He was in a mighty hurry, sir, seemingly.”
“Scoundrel!” Rupert shook his fist, scowling.
Mr. Fletcher retreated a pace.
“Not you, fool,” said Rupert. “What did the coach stop for?”
“Why, sir, the reckoning was not paid, and the moossoo had left his valise. The servant jumps off the box, comes running in here to settle the reckoning with me, snatches up the valise, and was out of the place before I’d time to fetch my breath. They’re queer people, these Frenchies, my lord, for there was me never dreaming the gentleman proposed to leave today. Driving hell for leather, they was, too, and as good a team of horses as ever I see.”
“Rot his black soul!” fumed Rupert. “The devil’s in it now, and no mistake. A horse, Fletcher, a horse!”
“Horse, sir?”
“Burn it, would I want a cow? Horse, man, and quickly!”
“But, my lord﻿—”
“Be hanged to your buts! Go find me a horse and a pistol!”
“But, my lord, I’ve no riding horses here! Farmer Giles hath a cob, but﻿—”
“No horse? Damme, it’s disgraceful! Go and fetch the animal the smith’s shoeing now! Away with you!”
“But, my lord, that is Mr. Manvers’ horse, and﻿—”
“Devil take Mr. Manvers! Here, I’ll go myself! No, stay! A pistol, man.”
The landlord was upset.
“My lord, it’s a touch of the sun must have got into your head!”
“Sun at this time of the year?” roared Rupert, thoroughly exasperated. “Go find me a pistol, sirrah!”
“Yes, my lord, yes!” said Fletcher, and retreated in haste.
Rupert set off down the road to the blacksmith’s, and found him whistling to himself as he worked.
“Coggin! Coggin, I say!”
The blacksmith paused.
“Yes, my lord?”
“Hurry with that shoe, my man! I want the horse.”
Coggin stared, open-mouthed.
“But﻿—but ’tis not one of his Grace’s horses, sir﻿—”
“Tare an’ ’ouns, would his Grace own such a brute? Do ye take me for a fool?”
“But ’tis Mr. Manvers’ roan, your lordship!”

===Chinese===

哈哈，伯爵大人（M. le Comte）！可他娘的他掳走莱昂妮究竟安的什么心？”
掌柜满脸同情地打量着他，静候他的下文分说。
“拿麦芽浓啤酒来！”鲁珀特重新跌回椅背上命令道。“还要一匹快马，外加一柄手枪。”
掌柜听得愈发云里雾里、满腹疑窦，却也只得手忙脚乱地端来一大银罐啤酒。鲁珀特仰起脖子一饮而尽，长长地倒吸了一口粗气。
“刚才那辆马车当真在府上停过？”他厉声盘问道。“你可曾瞧见家兄的受监护人坐在车里？”
“莱昂妮小姐，我的大人？没有啊，当真未曾得见！那位法国老爷压根就没下车。瞧着像是火烧眉毛似的心急如焚呢，先生。”
“该死的恶贼！”鲁珀特脸色铁青，恶狠狠地挥了挥拳头。
弗莱彻先生（Mr. Fletcher）吓得慌忙后退了一步。
“没骂你，蠢材，”鲁珀特啐道。“那马车凭白停在这儿作甚？”
“哎呀，先生，全因那位法国老爷还没结清房钱账目，而且把随身的旅行皮箱给落在店里了。车夫座上的仆人跳下车来，一路飞奔进店同我结清了账目，一把抓起皮箱夺门而出，快得我连喘口气的工夫都没有。这帮法国佬可真够古怪的，我的大人，亏我先前做梦也没想到那位老爷今儿就要拔营动身呢。赶车赶得那叫一个玩命狂奔，而且那四匹辕马拉车的牲口，也是小的平生所见顶神骏的良驹呢。”
“老天非烂了他的黑心不可！”鲁珀特暴跳如雷地大骂道。“真是撞了邪了，半点没错。快备马，弗莱彻，快备马！”
“备马，先生？”
“见鬼，难道老子要一头母牛不成？备马，蠢汉，手脚麻利点！”
“可是，勋爵大人——”
“去你娘的可是！立刻去给老子弄一匹马来，还有手枪！”
“可是，勋爵大人，小店里哪有什么供人骑乘的快马呀！农夫吉尔斯（Farmer Giles）家倒有一匹矮种马，可是——”
“连匹马都没有？见鬼，真是不成体统到了极点！快去把铁匠铺里正在挂掌的那头牲口给老子牵过来！快滚去！”
“可是，勋爵大人，那是曼弗斯先生（Mr. Manvers）的坐骑呀，而且——”
“去他娘的曼弗斯先生！算了，老子亲自去牵！等等，别走！手枪，快拿手枪来。”
掌柜急得直擦冷汗。
“勋爵大人，您八成是头顶挨了日头晒着着魔了吧！”
“大冷天的这节骨眼哪来的日头暴晒？”鲁珀特被彻底激得七窍生烟，暴跳如雷地咆哮道。“立刻去给老子找把手枪来，狗奴才！”
“是，勋爵大人，小的这就去！”弗莱彻吓得屁滚尿流，慌忙忙不迭地退下。
鲁珀特大步流星折返大路赶到铁匠铺，只见铁匠正一边干活一边自得其乐地吹着口哨。
“科金（Coggin）！老子叫你呢，科金！”
铁匠停下了手里的铁锤。
“在呢，勋爵大人？”
“手脚麻利点把马掌挂好，伙计！这马老子要使。”
科金目瞪口呆，嘴巴张得老大。
“可——可这并非公爵大人名下的宝马良驹呀，先生——”
“真是见鬼了，公爵大人怎会瞧得上这等劣马牲口？你当老子是个不长眼的瞎子不成？”
“可这当真是曼弗斯先生名下的铁青马呀，勋爵大人！”

===Original===

“I don’t care if ’tis the devil’s own chestnut!” cried Rupert. “I want it, and that’s enough! How long before you have that shoe on?”
“Why, sir, twenty minutes, or maybe longer.”
“A guinea for you if you hasten!” Rupert searched in his pockets and produced two crowns. “And ask it of Fletcher,” he added, stowing the crowns away again. “Don’t sit staring at me, man! Hammer that shoe on, or I’ll take the hammer to knock sense into your head withal! Stap me if I won’t!”
Thus adjured, the smith set to with a will.
“The groom’s walked on to Fawley Farm, my lord,” he ventured presently. “What will your honour have me say to him when he comes back?”
“Tell him to present Lord Rupert Alastair’s compliments to Mr. Manvers﻿—who the devil is Mr. Manvers?﻿—and thank him for the loan of his horse.” Rupert, walked round the animal, inspecting its points. “Horse, is it? Cow-hocked bag of bones! A man’s no right to own a scarecrow like this! You hear me, Coggin?”
“Yes, my lord. Certainly, sir!”
“Hurry with that shoe, then, and fetch the animal up to the Arms.” Away went Rupert up the road again to the inn, where he found Fletcher awaiting him with a large pistol.
“ ’Tis loaded, sir,” Fletcher warned him. “Indeed, my lord, and are you sure your lordship is well?”
“Never mind! Which way did the coach go?”
“Making for Portsmouth, sir, as I judge. But surely to goodness your lordship isn’t of a mind to chase it?”
“What else, fool? I want a hat. Produce me one.”
Fletcher resigned himself to the inevitable.
“If your lordship would condescend to take my Sunday beaver﻿—”
“Ay, ’twill suffice. Make out the reckoning and I’ll pay﻿—er﻿—when I return. Damn that fellow Coggin! Will he be all night at his work? They’ve nigh on an hour’s start of me already!”
But Coggin came presently, leading the roan. Rupert stowed his pistol away in the saddle holster, tightened the girths, and sprang into the saddle. The smith gave vent to a last appeal.
“My lord, Mr. Manvers is a testy gentleman, and indeed﻿—”
“To hell with Mr. Manvers, I’m sick of the fellow!” said Rupert, and rode off at a canter.
The borrowed horse was no fiery charger, as Rupert soon discovered. It cherished its own ideas as to a suitable pace to maintain, and managed to do so for the most part, to its own satisfaction and Rupert’s disgust.

===Chinese===

“老子才不管它是不是魔鬼自个儿骑的栗色马呢！”鲁珀特嚷道。“老子要使它，这便足够了！你还要多久才能把马掌挂好？”
“哎呀，先生，大概二十分钟（20），兴许还要久些。”
“要是你手脚麻利点，赏你一个几尼（1）！”鲁珀特伸手在口袋里摸索了一番，掏出了两枚克朗银币（2）。“拿着这去跟弗莱彻讨赏银，”他补充道，顺手把两枚克朗重新塞回口袋里。“少在那儿直愣愣瞪着老子！快拿铁锤把马掌钉牢，否则老子亲自动手拿锤子往你脑袋上敲出点长进心来！要是骗你老子当场天打雷劈！”
听到这番恫吓，铁匠只得咬紧牙关拼命挥锤猛干起来。
“曼弗斯先生的马夫去福利农场（Fawley Farm）那边办事去了，勋爵大人，”过了一会儿他大着胆子探问道。“等他折返回来，不知小的合该如何向他交代？”
“同他讲，就说鲁珀特·阿拉斯泰尔勋爵向曼弗斯先生致意——曼弗斯究竟是哪路该死的毛神？——顺便感谢他借马的大恩大德。”鲁珀特绕着那匹马转了一圈，审视着它的身架骨相。“这也配叫马？牛蹄拐弯、瘦骨嶙峋的皮包骨架子！天底下哪有人好意思骑这种稻草人劣马的！听见老子的话没有，科金？”
“听见了，勋爵大人。遵命，先生！”
“那就抓紧把掌钉好，牵到阿翁军械客栈来。”言罢，鲁珀特一甩身大步沿着大路重返客栈，只见弗莱彻正捧着一柄沉甸甸的大口径手枪心惊胆战地等候着。
“枪里压满了弹药，先生，”弗莱彻提醒道。“说实在的，勋爵大人，您当真笃定自个儿身子骨无碍吗？”
“少废话！那辆马车究竟奔哪条道去了？”
“照小的估摸，八成是奔朴茨茅斯（Portsmouth）去了，先生。可看在老天爷的份上，大人您总不至于当真要单枪匹马追杀过去吧？”
“不追难道干看着，蠢货？老子还要顶帽子。给老子弄一顶来。”
弗莱彻无可奈何，只得认命。
“倘若大人不嫌弃肯戴小的这顶礼拜天戴的黑海狸皮毡帽——”
“成，能遮顶就行。把茶水账目记下，等老子——呃——折返回来时一并结清。该死的科金那个混账！难道他打算钉马掌钉到天黑不成？他们眼下抢先了将近一个钟头（1）的程途呢！”
好在不多时科金便牵着那匹铁青马气喘吁吁地赶了过来。鲁珀特将手枪稳稳塞进马鞍旁的枪套里，勒紧肚带，纵身一跃稳稳跨上马鞍。铁匠忍不住发出了最后的哀求：
“勋爵大人，曼弗斯先生脾气暴躁得很，而且当真——”
“让他去见鬼吧，老子早听腻那厮的名号了！”鲁珀特抛下一句粗话，双腿一夹马腹，策马一路小跑疾驰而去。
借来的这匹牲口绝非什么神勇烈马，鲁珀特很快便领教到了这一点。它对于该保持何等慢吞吞的步态自有一套执拗的主张，而且一路上大部分时间都我行我素地按着自个儿的节奏晃悠，牲口自个儿倒是心满意足惬意得很，却直教鲁珀特气得咬牙切齿。

===Original===

Thus it was close on four in the afternoon when he came at last into Portsmouth, and both he and his mount were very weary.
He rode at once to the quay, and learned that the private schooner anchored there for the past three days had set sail not an hour ago. Rupert dashed Mr. Fletcher’s hat on the ground.
“Blister me, I’m too late!”
The harbour-master eyed him in polite surprise, and picked up the hat.
“Tell me now,” said Rupert, dismounting. “Was it a French scoundrel embarking?”
“Ay, sir, ’twas a foreign gentleman with red hair, and his son.”
“Son?” ejaculated Rupert.
“Ay, sir, a sick lad it was. The moossoo said he was suffering from a fever. He carried him on board like one dead, all muffled up in a great cloak. I said to Jim here, ‘Jim,’ I said, ‘it’s a shame to take the boy on board, ill as he is, that it is.’ ”
“Drugged, by Gad!” exclaimed Rupert. “I’ll have his blood for this! Taken her to France, has he! Now, what in thunder does he want with her? Hi, you! When does the next packet sail for Le Havre?”
“Why, sir, there’s no boat for the likes of you till Wednesday,” said the harbour-master. Rupert’s ruffles might be torn, and his coat muddied, but the harbour-master knew a gentleman when he saw one.
Rupert glanced ruefully down his person.
“The likes of me, eh? Well, well!” He pointed with his whip to a ramshackle vessel laden with bales of cloth. “Where is she bound for?”
“For Le Havre, sir, but ’tis only a trading ship, as your honour sees.”
“When does she sail?”
“Tonight, sir. She’s lain here two days too long already, waiting for the wind to turn, but she’ll be away with the tide soon after six.”
“That’s the ship for me,” said Rupert briskly. “Where’s her master?”
The harbour-master was perturbed.
“ ’Tis but a dirty old boat, sir, and never a﻿—”
“Dirty? So am I dirty, damn it!” said Rupert. “Go find me the master, and tell him I want a passage to France this night.”
So off went the harbour-master, to return anon with a burly individual in homespun, with a great black beard. This gentleman eyed Rupert stolidly, and, removing the long clay pipe from his mouth, rumbled forth two words.
“Twenty guineas.”
“What’s that?” said Rupert. “Not a farthing more than ten, you rogue!”
The bearded gentleman spat deliberately into the sea, but vouchsafed no word. A dangerous light came into Rupert’s eyes. He tapped the man on the shoulder with his riding-whip.

===Chinese===

就这样，待他终于跌跌撞撞赶抵朴茨茅斯时，天色已然将近下午四点钟（4:00）了，他和坐下的牲口早已双双累得骨头快要散架。
他当即策马直奔码头，打探得知过去三天（3）一直停泊在此处的一艘私人双桅纵帆船，在不到一个小时（1）前刚刚扬帆起航。鲁珀特狠狠将弗莱彻先生的那顶毡帽砸在地上。
“该死的天打雷劈，老子到底迟了一步！”
海港总管满脸客气疑窦地打量着他，俯身捡起了帽子。
“快同我讲讲，”鲁珀特翻身下马追问道。“刚才登船起航的可是个法国恶棍？”
“回先生的话，正是，是位生着一头红发的外国老爷，身边还带着他的令郎呢。”
“令郎？”鲁珀特失声叫嚷道。
“正是，先生，是个染了重病的孩子。那位法国老爷说是患了伤寒热病。整个人用宽大的厚斗篷裹得严严实实，像具死尸般被一路抱上了船。小的方才还同身旁的吉姆（Jim）念叨呢，‘吉姆啊，’小的说，‘孩子病得这般奄奄一息，还要硬弄上船出海，当真造孽啊，一点没错。’”
“被下了蒙汗药，老天作证！”鲁珀特失声痛骂道。“老子非放干他的血不可！居然把她硬掳到法国去了！见鬼，他掳走她究竟图什么？喂，你！下一班开往勒阿弗尔（Le Havre）的客船何时起航？”
“哎呀，先生，像您这等体面身份的贵客，直到周三之前可都没有体面的客船呢，”海港总管答道。鲁珀特领口的蕾丝固然撕成了布条，外套上也沾满了泥浆，但总管终究是一眼便能瞧出名门绅士气度的行家里手。
鲁珀特满腹沮丧地低头审视着自个儿周身的狼狈行头。
“像我这等体面身份，嗯？罢了，罢了！”他手中的马鞭凌空一指，指向远处一艘堆满了布匹货包、破破烂烂的货船。“那艘破船奔哪儿去？”
“回先生的话，开往勒阿弗尔，可那不过是一艘寻常的运货商船，如大人所见。”
“何时起锚出海？”
“就今晚，先生。因为死等风向掉头，已经在海港里白白延误了两天（2）了，不过六点（6:00）刚过趁着涨潮必定扬帆出海。”
“那便正合老子的意，”鲁珀特利落地拍板道。“船长在哪儿？”
海港总管面露难色、满腹踌躇。
“那不过是一艘肮脏不堪的腌臜旧船罢了，先生，而且上头连个——”
“肮脏？老子浑身上下也干净不到哪儿去，去他娘的！”鲁珀特啐道。“快去替我把船长找来，告诉他老子今晚非搭他的船去法国不可。”
海港总管只得领命而去，不多时便领回了一位身着粗褐麻布短衫、满脸黑胡子拉碴的魁梧大汉。这位船长神色木然冷漠地上下打量着鲁珀特，缓缓从嘴里拔出长长的陶土烟斗，喉头瓮声瓮气地挤出了两个词：
“二十个几尼（20）。”
“你说什么？”鲁珀特嚷道。“顶多十个几尼（10），多半个便士都没有，你这贪得无厌的混账！”
黑胡子船长慢条斯理地朝海面啐了一口浓痰，却自始至终懒得多吐露半个字。鲁珀特的眼眸里泛起了一抹凶狠凌厉的杀气。他拿起马鞭在那大汉的肩膀上重重戳了戳。

===Original===

“Fellow, I am Lord Rupert Alastair. You shall have ten guineas off me and for the rest I’ll see you damned.”
The harbour-master pricked up his ears.
“I was hearing, my lord, that his Grace has the *Silver Queen* anchored in Southampton Water.”
“The devil fly away with Justin!” exclaimed Rupert wrathfully. “He was always wont to have her here!”
“Maybe, sir, if you was to ride to Southampton﻿—”
“Ride to hell! I’d find them painting her, like as not. Come now, fellow, ten guineas!”
The harbour-master took his colleague aside and whispered urgently. Presently he turned, and addressed Rupert.
“I am saying, my lord, as how fifteen guineas is a fair price.”
“Fifteen guineas it is!” said Rupert promptly, thinking of the two crowns in his pocket. “I shall have to sell the horse.”
“Six o’clock we sets sail, and don’t wait for nobbut,” growled the captain, and walked off.
Rupert rode into the town, and by good fortune was able to sell Mr. Manvers’ roan for the sum of twenty guineas. The sale being accomplished he went to the inn on the quayside, and refreshed himself with a wash, and a bowl of punch. Thus fortified he boarded the sailing vessel, and sat him down on a coil of rope, thoroughly enjoying the adventure, and not a little amused.
“ ’Fore Gad, I never was in such a mad chase!” he remarked to the sky. “Here’s Léonie spirited off by Saint-Vire, the Lord knows why, or where, for that matter﻿—and myself hot on the scent with five crowns in my pocket, and the landlord’s hat on my head. And what am I going to do when I find the chit?” He pondered deeply. “It’s a plaguey queer business, so it is,” he decided. “Justin’s at the back of it, I’ll be bound. And where the devil is Justin?” Suddenly he flung back his head and laughed. “Damme, I’d give something to see old cousin Harriet’s face when she finds me gone off with Léonie! Hey, hey, here’s a pretty coil, to be sure, for, faith, I don’t know where I am, and I don’t know where Léonie is, nor she where I am, and at Avon they don’t where any of us are!”

===Chinese===

“狗东西，老子可是堂堂鲁珀特·阿拉斯泰尔勋爵。你从老子手里顶多捞得着十个几尼（10），至于剩下的，老子宁可让你去见阎王。”
海港总管在一旁猛地竖起了耳朵。
“小的方才还听闻呢，勋爵大人，公爵大人名下的‘银后号’（Silver Queen）眼下正停泊在南安普敦港湾（Southampton Water）呢。”
“让魔鬼把贾斯汀捉走吧！”鲁珀特愤愤不平地叫骂道。“他以往分明向来把船停在这边的！”
“说不定，先生，倘若大人肯策马赶去南安普敦——”
“赶去见鬼吧！等老子赶过去，保不齐正撞见他们慢悠悠给船刷油漆呢。少废话，伙计，十个几尼！”
海港总管连忙把船长拉到一旁，凑在耳畔十万火急地耳语规劝了一番。片刻后总管转过身来，满脸堆笑地向鲁珀特打圆场道：
“小的同他讲妥了，大人，十五个几尼（15）算是个公道价钱。”
“十五个几尼便十五个几尼！”鲁珀特盘算着兜里那两枚克朗（2），当机立断应承道。“老子非得把那匹马卖了换银子不可。”
“六点钟（6:00）准时起锚出海，过时不候，”船长粗声粗气地哼了一句，扭头便走。
鲁珀特随即策马返回城里，说来当真走运，他竟然顺顺当当地把曼弗斯先生的那匹铁青马脱手卖出了整整二十个几尼（20）的高价。银子一落袋，他便信步走进码头边的小客栈，痛痛快快洗了把脸净了身，又灌下了一整碗热气腾腾的潘趣酒。这番好生犒劳之后，他大步踏上了那艘货船，在一卷粗缆绳上一屁股坐定，对于这场突如其来的冒险享用得不得了，心里更是乐开了花。
“老天作证，老子平生还从未经历过这般荒唐绝伦的疯狂追逐呢！”他仰头冲着苍天失声赞叹道。“莱昂妮被圣维尔这混账神不知鬼不觉拐跑了，天晓得究竟图什么，也天晓得究竟被拐去了哪儿——而老子兜里揣着五个克朗（5），头顶扣着客栈掌柜的破毡帽，一路顺着味儿死死咬住不放。等老子当真找着了那丫头，接下来又该如何收场呢？”他陷入了深思。“这桩勾当当真邪门古怪到了骨子里，千真万确，”他暗自定论道。“幕后必定有贾斯汀在作祟，老子敢打包票。可贾斯汀这该死的天杀的如今究竟身在何处呢？”冷不丁他仰天哈哈狂笑起来。“见鬼，要是让老哈丽雅特表姐得知我居然跟莱昂妮一道‘私奔’逃之夭夭了，我真巴不得能瞧瞧她脸上会是何等精彩绝伦的表情呢！嘿，嘿，这可真是一出天衣无缝的绝妙大乱局啊，老天作证，老子自个儿压根不知道身在何方，更摸不清莱昂妮身在何处，她也不知道老子人在哪里，而阿翁领地的那帮蠢货更是对咱们大家伙儿的死活下落一无所知！”

## VII Satan and Priest at One / 撒旦与神甫不谋而合
===Original===

The village of Bassincourt, which lay some six or seven miles to the west of Saumur, in Anjou, was a neat and compact place whose white houses were for the most part gathered about its hub, a square marketplace paved by cobblestones as large as a man’s fist. On the north the square was flanked by various houses of the more well-to-do inhabitants; on the west by smaller cottages, and by a lane that led into the square at right angles to this side, and which stretched out into the open country, winding this way and that to touch each of the three farms that lay to the west of Bassincourt. On the south side was the small grey church, within whose square tower a cracked bell was wont to ring out its summons to the villagers. The church stood back from the marketplace with its burial ground all about it and beyond, on one side, the Curé’s modest house, squatting in its own garden, and seeming to smile across the square in gentle rulership.
The east side of the square was close-packed by shops, a blacksmith’s yard, and a white inn, over whose open door hung a gay green shield, with a painting of the Rising Sun thereon. The sign swung to and fro with every wind that blew, creaking a little if the gale were fierce, but more often sighing only on its rusted chains.
On this particular day of November the square was a-hum with voices, and echoing occasionally to a child’s shrill laugh, or to the stamp of a horse’s hoofs on the cobbles. Old Farmer Mauvoisin had driven into Bassincourt with three pigs for sale in his cart, and had drawn up at the inn to exchange the time of day with the landlord, and to quaff a tankard of thin French ale while his pigs grunted and snuffled behind him. Close by, gathered about a stall where La Mère Gognard was selling vegetables, was a group of women, alternately haggling and conversing. Several girls in stiff gowns kilted high above their ankles, their feet in clumsy wooden sabots, stood chattering beside the ancient porch which led into the graveyard; in the centre of the square, near to the fountain, some sheep were herded, while a party of possible buyers picked their way amongst them, prodding and inspecting at will. From the blacksmith’s yard came the ring of hammer on anvil, mingled with spasmodic snatches of song.
Into this busy, contented scene rode his Grace of Avon, upon a hired horse.

===Chinese===

巴桑库尔（Bassincourt）村坐落在安茹（Anjou）境内的索米尔（Saumur）以西约六七英里（6-7）处，是一处齐整紧凑的村落，其间粉刷得雪白的农舍大半环聚在村落的核心地带——那是一处铺着男子拳头般大小鹅卵石的开阔集市广场。集市广场北侧错落着村里几户殷实人家的深宅大院；西侧则是几间稍矮的茅舍，以及一条自直角方向切入广场的乡间小道，该道一直延伸向开阔的郊野，蜿蜒曲折地串联起巴桑库尔以西的三座农庄（3）。南侧是一座灰色的小教堂，其方塔之内的残破古钟平日里总爱向村民们敲出祈祷的召唤。教堂矗立在集市广场后方，四周环绕着墓地，再往旁侧稍远些，则是本堂神甫（Curé）那座掩映在独立小花园里的质朴宅院，宛若一位慈祥的长者隔着广场颔首微笑，施展着温和的庇护与管辖。
集市广场的东侧则密密匝匝地挤满了各色店铺、一座铁匠铺的场院，以及一间白色的客栈，客栈敞开的大门上方悬着一面绘着一轮旭日的翠绿盾形招牌。那招牌迎着每一阵掠过的清风前后摇曳，狂风呼啸时便发出轻微的吱呀声，但更多时候不过是在锈迹斑斑的铁链上幽幽叹息罢了。
在十一月（11）的这寻常一天，广场上人声鼎沸、语声嘈杂，时不时回荡起孩童清脆的啼笑声，或是马蹄踏在鹅卵石上的得得声响。老农夫莫瓦桑（Mauvoisin）驾着大车拉了三头猪（3）赶到巴桑库尔来兜售，此刻正把车停在客栈门前同掌柜寒暄致意，一边仰脖灌着大口清淡的法国淡啤酒，大车里的几头猪则在他身后哼哼唧唧、四处拱嗅。不远处，戈尼亚尔大妈（La Mère Gognard）的蔬菜摊子前围拢了一群妇人，一边讨价还价一边家长里短地闲聊。几个身着硬挺长裙、裙摆高高系起露出脚踝、脚踩沉重木屐（sabots）的村姑，正聚在通往墓地的古老门廊前叽叽喳喳说笑；在广场正中央的喷泉旁聚集着一小群绵羊，几位潜在的买主穿梭其间，随意地戳戳按按、评头品足。打铁匠铺的场院里，不绝于耳地传出铁锤击打铁砧的铿锵脆响，间或夹杂着几声断断续续的粗犷歌声。
正是在这一片忙碌而祥和的市井图景之中，阿翁公爵大人骑着一匹租来的坐骑，缓步信马由缰地踏入了视线。

===Original===

He came trotting into the marketplace from the eastern road that led to Saumur, dressed all in sombre black, with lacing of gold. As soon as his horse’s hoofs struck the uneven cobblestones he reined in, and sitting gracefully at ease in the saddle, one gloved hand resting lightly on his hip, cast a languid glance round.
He attracted no little attention. The villagers stared at him from his point-edged hat to his spurred boots, and back again. One tittering girl, remarking those cold eyes and the thin, curling lips, whispered that it was the devil himself come amongst them. Although her companion scoffed at her for a foolish maid, she crossed herself surreptitiously, and drew back into the shelter of the porch.
The Duke’s glance swept all round the square, and came to rest at last on a small boy, who watched him with goggling eyes, and his thumb in his mouth. One hand in its embroidered gauntlet beckoned imperiously, and the small boy took a hesitating step forward in answer to the Duke’s summons.
His Grace looked down at him, faintly smiling. He pointed to the house beside the church.
“Am I right in thinking that that is the abode of your Curé?”
The boy nodded.
“Yes, milor’.”
“Do you think that I shall find him within?”
