我的老天爷，当年他们离开香槟省时，我怎么就没打听他们搬去了哪里呢！”
“你当时……你当时觉得不知道反而更保险，”夫人结结巴巴地说，“可那个男人究竟是从哪儿找着我的小宝贝的？他怎么会知道……”
“他就是撒旦转世。我相信这世上就没有他不知道的事。但只要我能把那丫头从他手心里抢过来，他就无计可施了。我敢肯定他根本没有确凿证据。”
夫人开始在房间里来回踱步，两手死死绞在一起。
“一想到她落在他的掌控之中，我就心如刀绞！”她惊呼道，“天晓得他会对她做出什么事来？她还那么年轻，生得那么娇美……”
“她对阿翁倒是死心塌地得很，”圣维尔短促地冷笑了一声，“而且她自保的本事大着呢，那个小泼妇！”
夫人停下了脚步，脸上浮现出一线希望。
“亨利，要是阿翁没有证据，他又怎么能断定莱昂妮是我的孩子呢？他难道不会以为她真像外头传的那样……是那个身份吗？难道就没有这种可能？”
“有这个可能，”圣维尔承认道，“然而从他对我说过的只言片语来看，我敢肯定他已经猜出了十之八九。”
“那阿尔芒呢！”她惊叫起来，“难道他就猜不出来吗？天哪，天哪，我们该怎么办？这一切值得吗，亨利？噢，难道仅仅为了报复阿尔芒，就值得这么做吗？”
“我从不后悔！”圣维尔厉声喝道，“做过的事就是做过了，既然覆水难收，我就绝不浪费时间去纠结到底值不值得！夫人，请你照常出门见客。我可不想给阿翁提供更多起疑的借口。”
“可他究竟想干什么？”夫人追问道，“他为什么这么按兵不动？他葫芦里到底卖的什么药？”
“该死，夫人，我要是知道，你以为我还会这样坐以待毙吗？”
“她……依你看，她自己知道吗？”
“不知道，我敢拿名誉担保她蒙在鼓里。”
夫人凄厉地笑了起来。
“你的名誉！你的名誉！天老爷，你居然还有脸提这几个字？”
他怒气冲冲地朝她逼近一步；她的手指已经搭在了门把手上。
“当年你逼我抛弃亲骨肉的时候，名誉早就荡然无存了！”她哭喊道，“你迟早会眼睁睁看着自己的名声被拖进烂泥里！还有我！还有我！天哪，难道你就束手无策了吗？”
“住嘴，夫人！”他压低嗓门恶狠狠地嘶声道，“你想让外头的仆人都听见吗？”
她浑身一颤，警惕而心虚地环顾四周。
“事情一旦败露……我想我必死无疑，”她语气出奇地平静下来说完，便退了出去。
圣维尔颓然陷进椅子里，眉头紧锁。不多时，一名男仆走了进来。
“什么事？”圣维尔没好气地喝问。
“先生，有位女士求见。”

===Original===

“A lady?” Saint-Vire was surprised. “Who?”
“Monsieur, I do not know. She awaits you in the smaller salon, and she says that she will see you.”
“Of what like is she?”
“Monsieur, she is veiled.”
“An intrigue, *enfin*!” Saint-Vire rose. “In the smaller salon?”
“Yes, monsieur.”
Saint-Vire went out, and crossed the hall to the little withdrawing-room. A lady was standing by the window, enveloped in a cloak, and with a veil hanging down over her face. She turned as Saint-Vire came in, and put back the veil with a small, resolute hand. Saint-Vire looked into his daughter’s dark eyes.
“Oho!” he said softly, and looked for the key to the door.
“I have it,” Léonie said calmly. “And I will tell you, m’sieur, that my maid waits for me in the street. If I do not come to her in half an hour she will go at once to Monseigneur and tell him that I am here.”
“Very clever,” Saint-Vire said smoothly. “What is it that you want of me? Are you not afraid to put yourself in my power?”
“Bah!” said Léonie, and let him see her little gold-mouthed pistol.
Saint-Vire came further into the room.
“A pretty toy,” he sneered, “but I know what women are with such playthings.”
“*Quant à ca*,” said Léonie frankly. “I should like very much to kill you, because you gave me an evil drink, but I won’t kill you unless you touch me.”
“Oh, I thank you, mademoiselle! To what am I indebted for this visit?”
Léonie fixed her eyes on his face.
“Monsieur, you shall tell me now if it is true that you are my father.”
Saint-Vire said nothing, but stood very still, waiting.
“Speak you!” Léonie said fiercely. “Are you my father?”
“My child﻿—” Saint-Vire spoke softly. “Why do you ask me that?”
“Because they are saying that I am your baseborn daughter. Tell me, is it true?” She stamped her foot at him.
“My poor child!” Saint-Vire approached, but was confronted by the nozzle of the pistol. “You need not fear, *petite*. It has never been mine intention to harm you.”
“Pig-person!” Léonie said. “I am not afraid of anything, but if you come near me I shall be sick. Is it true what they say?”
“Yes, my child,” he said, and achieved a sigh.
“*How* I hate you!” she said with fervour.
“Will you not be seated?” he asked. “It grieves me to hear you say that you hate me, but indeed I understand what you must feel. I am very sorry for you, *petite*.”

===Chinese===

“一位女士？”圣维尔有些惊讶，“是谁？”
“先生，小的不知。她在小会客厅等候，执意要见您。”
“她生得什么模样？”
“先生，她蒙着面纱。”
“终于有桩风流韵事找上门了！”圣维尔站起身，“在小会客厅？”
“是的，先生。”
圣维尔走出门，穿过前厅来到那间小休息室。窗前伫立着一位裹在斗篷里的女子，面纱垂落遮住了脸庞。听到圣维尔进门，她转过身来，伸出一只纤小而果决的手撩开了面纱。圣维尔望进了一双黑黝黝的眼眸——那是他亲生女儿的眼睛。
“噢嚯！”他低呼了一声，下意识地去摸门上的钥匙。
“钥匙在我这儿，”莱昂妮平静地说，“而且先生，不妨告诉你，我的侍女就在街上候着我。如果我半个钟头（30分钟）内没回去找她，她就会立刻跑去报告公爵大人，告诉他我在这里。”
“聪明显赫，”圣维尔从容自若地说，“你找我有何贵干？难道你不怕羊入虎口，落入我的掌心吗？”
“啐！”莱昂妮啐了一声，亮出了她那把镀金枪口的小手枪。
圣维尔往屋里踱了几步。
“挺漂亮的小玩意儿，”他冷笑道，“不过我深知女人拿着这类玩具往往顶不上什么用场。”
“至于这个嘛，”莱昂妮坦白地说道，“我倒是巴不得一枪结果了你，因为你上次给我灌了迷药；但只要你不碰我，我就不会开枪。”
“噢，那我多谢您啦，小姐！不知今日哪阵风把您吹来了？”
莱昂妮的目光死死钉在他脸上。
“先生，你现在就给我句实话：你到底是不是我亲生父亲？”
圣维尔默不作声，只是静静站着，以静制动。
“说话呀！”莱昂妮厉声喝道，“你到底是不是我父亲？”
“我的孩子……”圣维尔语气轻柔，“你为何突然问这个？”
“因为人人都在传我是你的私生女。告诉我，这是真的吗？”她冲他狠狠一跺脚。
“我可怜的孩子！”圣维尔走上前去，却立刻迎上了黑洞洞的枪口。“你不必害怕，小家伙。我从来没有存心伤害你的意图。”
“猪猡！”莱昂妮骂道，“我什么都不怕，但你要是再敢靠近我一步，我保准吐出来。外头传的到底是不是真的？”
“是真的，我的孩子，”他说着，挤出了一声叹息。
“我有多恨你！”她咬牙切齿地说。
“何不坐下说话？”他问，“听你说恨我真叫我痛心，但我确实能理解你此刻的心情。我真替你难过，小家伙。”

===Original===

“I will not be seated,” Léonie said flatly, “and it makes me feel worse when you call me *petite*, and say you are sorry for me. More than ever I want to kill you.”
Saint-Vire was rather shocked.
“I am your father, child!”
“I do not care at all,” she replied. “You are an evil person, and if it is true that I am your daughter you are more evil than even I thought.”
“You do not understand the ways of the world we live in,” he sighed. “A youthful indiscretion﻿—you must not think too hardly of me, child. I will do all in my power to provide for you, and indeed I am greatly exercised over your welfare. I believed you to be in the charge of some worthy people once in mine employ. You may judge of my feelings when I found you in the Duc of Avon’s clutches.” Before the look on Léonie’s face he recoiled a little.
“If you speak one word against Monseigneur I will shoot you dead,” said Léonie softly.
“I do not speak against him, child. Why should I? He is no worse than any of us, but it grieves me to see you in his toils. I cannot but take an interest in you, and I fear for you when it becomes common knowledge that you are my daughter.”
She said nothing. After a moment he continued.
“In our world, child, we dislike open scandal. That is why I tried to rescue you from Avon a while back. I wish that I had told you then why I carried you off, but I thought to spare you that unpleasant knowledge.”
“How you are kind!” marvelled Léonie. “Of a truth it is a great thing to be the daughter of M. de Saint-Vire!”
He flushed.
“You thought me brutal, I know, but I acted for the best. You outwitted me, and I saw that it would have been wiser to have told you of your birth. The secret cannot be kept, for you resemble me too greatly. We are like to be plunged in a scandal now that will hurt us all.”
“It seems that most people know who I am,” Léonie answered, “but I am very well received, *je vous assure*.”
“At the moment you are, but when I openly acknowledge you﻿—what then?”
“*Tiens!*” Léonie stared at him. “Why should you do that?”
“I have no cause to love your﻿—guardian,” Saint-Vire said, and kept a wary eye on the pistol. “And I do not think that he would be pleased if the world knew he had adopted a baseborn child of mine. His pride would be humbled, I think.”
“What if he knows already?” Léonie asked. “If others know so must he.”
“Do you think he does?” Saint-Vire said.

===Chinese===

“我绝不坐，”莱昂妮断然拒绝道，“你叫我小家伙、假惺惺地说替我难过，只会让我觉得更恶心。我现在比刚才更想杀了你。”
圣维尔有些错愕。
“我可是你的生身父亲啊，孩子！”
“我压根不在乎，”她反驳道，“你是个十恶不赦的坏蛋，要是事实证明我真是你女儿，那你比我想象的还要歹毒千百倍。”
“你不谙世事，不懂我们这个世界的规矩，”他叹了口气，“青年时代的一时失足罢了……你不该把我想得太坏，孩子。我会尽我所能为你提供优渥的生活，而且我当真十分挂念你的福祉。我原以为你一直由我从前雇佣的忠实仆役代为抚养。当我在阿翁公爵的魔爪中发现你时，你能体会我当时的心情吗？”面对莱昂妮脸上的神色，他不由得退缩了半步。
“你要是敢说公爵大人半句坏话，我就一枪崩了你，”莱昂妮语气平缓而森然地说。
“我并没有诋毁他，孩子。我干嘛要诋毁他？他和我们这些人比起来也没坏到哪儿去，但我看到你深陷他的罗网，实在痛心疾首。我不能不对你关照有加，一想到外人一旦全都知道你是我女儿后的局面，我就替你提心吊胆。”
她一言不发。过了一会儿，他继续说道：
“在我们这个阶层，孩子，大家最忌讳的就是公开丑闻。正因如此，前些日子我才设法想把你从阿翁手里解救出来。我真希望当时就把拐走你的真相原原本本告诉你，可我那时一心只想让你免受这种难堪真相的折磨。”
“您可真是慈悲心肠啊！”莱昂妮讥讽地赞叹道，“当圣维尔先生的女儿可真是一桩无上光荣的大喜事呢！”
他涨红了脸。
“我知道你觉得我手段粗暴，但我一切都是为了你好。你当时棋高一着逃了脱去，我现在才明白，早知道把你的身世和盘托出才更明智。这个秘密根本守不住，因为你和我生得实在太像了。我们如今都快被卷入一场损害所有人的丑闻中了。”
“看来大多数人都知道我是谁，”莱昂妮回答，“但我处处受人热烈欢迎，我向你保证。”
“眼下是这样，可要是我公开认下你……到那时又该如何？”
“哎呀！”莱昂妮瞪着他，“你干嘛要那么做？”
“我可没理由喜欢你的……监护人，”圣维尔说，目光始终戒备地盯着手枪，“而且我想，要是天下人都知道他收养的是我见不得光的私生女，他绝不会感到痛快。他的骄傲怕是要被彻底踩在脚下了吧。”
“要是他早就心知肚明呢？”莱昂妮问，“既然旁人都知道，他也一定知道。”
“你觉得他知情吗？”圣维尔追问道。

===Original===

She was silent.
“He might suspect,” he went on. “Perhaps he does; I do not know. Yet I think if he had done so he would hardly have brought you to Paris. He would not like Society to laugh at him as Society will laugh when it learns who you are, I can harm him greatly in this matter.”
“How can you harm him, you﻿—you pig-person?”
Saint-Vire smiled.
“Were you not his page, *ma fille*? It is not *convenable* for young girls to masquerade as boys in the house of an Alastair. Think of the scandal when I tell that tale! Be very sure that I shall take care to set Paris about M. le Duc’s ears. His morals are well known, and I do not think that Paris will believe in his innocence, or yours.”
Léonie curled her lip.
“Voyons, am I a fool? Paris would not care that Monseigneur had made a bastard his mistress.”
“No, child, but would not Paris care that Avon had had the audacity to take his baseborn mistress into Society? You have queened it right royally, and I hear that you even have Condé in your toils. That will not make Paris more lenient. You have been too great a success, my dear. You are a masquerader, and Avon has cheated Society with you. Do you think Society will forgive that? I think we shall not see M. le Duc in France again, and it is possible that scandal might spread to London. His reputation would not aid him to kill the scandal, I assure you.”
“I wonder if it would be better that I kill you now?” Léonie said slowly. “You shall not harm Monseigneur, pig-person. That I swear!”
“I have no great wish to harm him,” Saint-Vire said indifferently. “But I cannot see my child in his care. Some paternal feeling you will allow me. Put yourself in my hands, and Avon has nothing to fear from me. All my wish is to see you safely disposed in life. There need be no scandal if you disappear from Society, but if you remain under Avon’s roof scandal must come. And since I am like to be involved in it, I prefer to head the cry.”
“And if I go you will say nothing?”
“Not a word. Why should I? Let me make provision for you. I can find a home for you. I will send you money. And perhaps you will﻿—”
“I do not put myself in the hands of a pig-person,” Léonie said crushingly. “I will disappear, *bien entendu*, but I will go to one who loves me, not to you, who are without doubt a villain.” She swallowed hard, and her hand clutched on the pistol.

===Chinese===

她沉默了。
“他或许有所怀疑，”他接着说，“兴许他当真起疑了，我也不得而知。然而我想他若当真知晓，绝不会带你来巴黎。他绝不想成为上流社会的笑柄，而一旦上流社会得知你的真实身世，势必会哄堂大笑。在这件事上，我完全能让他身败名裂。”
“你能怎么害他，你这个……你这头猪猡？”
圣维尔冷笑了一声。
“你从前难道不是他的侍童吗，我的好女儿？在阿拉斯泰尔家族的宅邸里，年轻姑娘女扮男装冒充侍童，可一点都不合体统。想想看，要是我把这桩往事抖落出来，会掀起多大的轩然大波！你大可以放心，我定会煽动整个巴黎把公爵大人的耳朵吵翻天。他的名声人尽皆知，我不信巴黎会相信他的清白，抑或是你的清白。”
莱昂妮撇了撇嘴。
“得了吧，难道我是傻子吗？公爵大人就算把一个私生女纳为情妇，巴黎也根本不会在乎。”
“没错，孩子，但公爵居然胆大包天，将自己的私生女情妇大摇大摆带进贵族上流社会，巴黎难道还会无动于衷吗？你这些天宛如女王般大出风头，我听说甚至连孔代亲王都拜倒在你的石榴裙下。这绝不会让巴黎对你网开一面。你实在太引人瞩目了，亲爱的。你是个冒牌货，而阿翁用你把整个上流社会戏耍了一通。你以为上流社会会宽恕这种羞辱吗？我想公爵大人此生都别想再踏进法国半步了，而且这场丑闻极可能会一路蔓延到伦敦。我可以向你保证，他以往的名声绝不足以替他平息这场风暴。”
“我在琢磨，现在就一枪崩了你是不是更利落？”莱昂妮语气缓慢地说道，“你休想伤害公爵大人，猪猡。我发誓！”
“我其实并无意害他，”圣维尔满不在乎地说，“但我绝不能眼睁睁看着自己的骨肉落在他的照管之下。你总该容许我保留几分做父亲的舐犊之情吧。把自己交到我手里，阿翁便无需对我心存顾忌。我唯一的愿望就是妥善安顿你的一生。只要你从社交界彻底消失，便不会掀起任何丑闻；但要是你继续留在阿翁的屋檐下，风暴势不可挡。而既然我势必会被牵连其中，我情愿亲自打头阵发难。”
“要是我离开，你就什么都不说？”
“绝口不提。我何苦自找麻烦？让我来供养你。我可以给你安置一个住所，按月给你寄钱。兴许以后你还会……”
“我绝不会把自己交到一头猪猡的手里，”莱昂妮斩钉截铁地打断他，“从社交界消失，这毋庸置疑，但我会去投奔一个真心爱我的人，而不是投奔你这个不折不扣的恶棍。”她狠狠咽了一口唾沫，紧紧握着手中的手枪。

===Original===

“I give you my word that I will disappear.”
He held out his hand.
“Poor child, this is a sad day for you. There is nothing I can say, but that I am sorry. It is for the best, as you will see. Where do you go?”
She held her head high.
“I do not tell you or anyone that,” she said. “I make just one prayer to the good God that I may never see you again.” Words choked in her throat; she made a gesture of loathing, and went to the door. There she turned. “I forget. You will swear to me that you will say nothing that may harm Monseigneur. Swear it on the Bible!”
“I swear,” he said. “But there is no need. Once you are gone there will be no occasion for me to speak. I want no scandal.”
“*Bon!*” she said. “I do not trust your oath, but I think you are a great coward, and you would not like to make a scandal. I hope you will be punished one day.” She flung the door-key down on the floor, and went quickly out.
Saint-Vire passed his handkerchief across his brow.
“*Mon Dieu*,” he whispered. “She showed me how to play my ace! Now, Satanas, we shall see who wins!”

===Chinese===

“我向你保证我会彻底消失。”
他伸出手来。
“可怜的孩子，今天对你来说真难熬。除了替你难过，我实在无话可说。不过长远来看这都是为你好，你日后自会明白。你打算去哪儿？”
她昂起头颅。
“我绝不会告诉你，也不会告诉任何人，”她说，“我只向上苍祈求一件事，那就是此生再也不要见到你。”话语在她喉间哽住了；她做了一个厌恶至极的动作，径直走向门口。到了门边，她回过头来。“我差点忘了。你必须向我起誓，绝不吐露半句中伤公爵大人的话。对着圣经起誓！”
“我起誓，”他说，“但其实大可不必。只要你一离开，我就压根没有开口的必要。我可不想要什么丑闻。”
“很好！”她说，“我信不过你的誓言，但我知道你是个十足的懦夫，你也害怕惹出丑闻。我希望你总有一天会遭报应。”她把门钥匙重重摔在地上，快步走了出去。
圣维尔掏出手帕拭了拭额头的冷汗。
“天哪，”他喃喃低语，“她居然亲手教我怎么打出这张王牌！现在，撒旦，且看鹿死谁手吧！”

## XXIX The Disappearance of Léonie / 莱昂妮失踪
===Original===

Lord Rupert yawned mightily, and heaved himself up in his chair.
“What do we do tonight?” he asked. “ ’Pon my soul, I’ve never been to so many balls in my life! It’s no wonder I’m worn out.”
“Oh, my dear Rupert, I am nigh dead with fatigue!” Fanny cried. “At least we have this one evening quiet! Tomorrow there is Madame du Deffand’s soirée.” She nodded to Léonie. “You will enjoy that, my love, I assure you. A few poems to be read, discussion, all the wit of Paris present﻿—oh, ’twill be a most amusing evening, I vow! There is no one who will not be there.”
“What, so we have respite today, have we?” said Rupert. “Now, what shall I do?”
“I thought you said you were worn out?” Marling remarked.
“So I am, but I can’t sit at home all the evening. What do you do?”
“Hugh and I are bound for de Châtelet’s, to visit Merivale. Will you accompany us?”
Rupert considered for a while.
“No, I believe I’ll go to this new gaming-house I hear tell of.”
Avon put up his glass.
“Oh? What, and where, is the novelty?”
“In the Rue Chambéry. It’s like to kill Vassaud’s if what they say is true. I’m surprised you’d not heard of it.”
“Yes, it is not in keeping with the part,” Avon said. “I believe I will go with you there this evening, child. It will not do for Paris to think I did not know of it.”
“What, will you all be out?” Fanny asked. “And I had promised to dine with my dear Julie! Léonie, I am sure that she will be pleased if you come with me.”
“Oh madame, I am so tired!” Léonie protested. “I would like to go to bed early tonight.”
Rupert stretched his long legs out before him.
“Tired at last!” he said. “Faith, I thought you’d never be wearied out!”
“My dearest life, I will tell the servants to take a tray to your room,” Fanny said. “You must not be tired tomorrow, for I am determined you shall come to Madame du Deffand’s soirée! Why, Condé is sure to be there!”
Léonie smiled rather wanly, and encountered Avon’s scrutiny.
“My infant, what has happened to trouble you?” he asked.
She opened wide her eyes.
“But nothing, Monseigneur! It is just that I have a touch of the *migraine*.”
“To be sure I am not surprised.” My lady shook her head wisely. “We have been abroad late every night this week. It is I who am at fault to have permitted it.”
“Oh, but madame, it has been *fort amusant*!” Léonie said. “I have enjoyed myself so much!”
“Egad, and so have I!” Rupert remarked.

===Chinese===

鲁珀特勋爵大大打了个哈欠，在扶手椅里费力地挪了挪身子。
“我们今晚干什么去？”他问，“凭良心发誓，我这辈子都没去过这么多场舞会！难怪我整个人都快累散架了。”
“哎呀，我亲爱的鲁珀特，我都快累瘫了！”范妮叫道，“好歹今晚我们能落个清静！明天还有杜·德芳夫人的晚会呢。”她朝莱昂妮点点头，“你肯定会喜欢的，心肝儿，我向你担保。晚会上要朗诵几首小诗，进行讨论，全巴黎的名流雅士全都会到场——噢，我敢发誓，那准是个有趣透顶的夜晚！绝不会有人缺席的。”
“怎么，这么说我们今天能喘口气啦？”鲁珀特说，“那我该干点什么好呢？”
“我刚才还听你说累散架了呢？”马林挖苦道。
“我是累啊，可我总不能整个晚上都闷在家里吧。你们俩去哪儿？”
“我和休要去德·沙特莱公馆拜访梅里维尔。你要一起去吗？”
鲁珀特琢磨了一会儿。
“不了，我想去听说刚开的那家新赌场转转。”
阿翁举起单片眼镜。
“哦？有什么新鲜花样，在什么地方？”
“在尚贝里街。据传言说要是真像他们吹的那样，瓦索赌场怕是要关门大吉了。真奇怪你居然还没听说过。”
“是啊，这倒与我的做派不符了，”阿翁说，“我看今晚我和你一块儿去瞧瞧吧，孩子。要是让巴黎觉得我竟对此一无所知，那可说不过去。”
“怎么，你们全都要出门吗？”范妮问，“我还答应了去跟我亲爱的朱莉共进晚餐呢！莱昂妮，我相信你要是能跟我同去，她一定会高兴坏的。”
“噢，夫人，我累坏了！”莱昂妮推辞道，“今晚我想早点歇息。”
鲁珀特在身前伸展着大长腿。
“终于也知道累啦！”他说，“老天，我还以为你永远不知道疲倦呢！”
“我最甜美的心肝儿，我吩咐仆人把饭菜端到你房里去，”范妮说，“明天你可千万不能没精神，因为我铁了心要带你出席杜·德芳夫人的晚会！天哪，孔代亲王准会在场呢！”
莱昂妮有些勉强地笑了笑，迎上了阿翁审视的目光。
“我的小家伙，出什么事惹你烦心了吗？”他问。
她睁大了双眼。
“哪有什么事呀，公爵大人！不过是有点偏头痛罢了。”
“这倒一点也不奇怪。”夫人煞有介事地摇了摇头，“这周每天晚上我们都在外头玩到后半夜。都怪我，不该由着性子由你们胡闹。”
“噢，可是夫人，这周过得好玩极了！”莱昂妮说，“我开心得很呢！”
“天哪，我也是！”鲁珀特附和道。

===Original===

“It has been a mad two months, and I scarce know whether I am standing on my head or my heels. Are you off already, Hugh?”
“We are dining with de Châtelet at four,” Hugh explained. “I’ll say good night, Léonie. You’ll be abed when we return.”
She gave him her hand; her eyes were downcast. Both he and Marling kissed the slender fingers. Hugh made some joke to Rupert, and they went out.
“Do you dine at home, Justin?” asked my lady. “I must go change my gown, and order the light chaise to take me to Julie.”
“I will bear my infant company at dinner,” said Avon. “And then she shall go to bed. Rupert?”
“No, I’m off at once,” said Rupert. “I’ve a little matter to talk over with d’Anvau. Come, Fan!”
They went out together. Avon crossed over to the couch where Léonie sat, and tweaked one of her curls.
“Child, you are strangely silent.”
“I was thinking,” she said gravely.
“Of what, *ma mie*?”
“Oh, I shall not tell you that, Monseigneur!” she said, and smiled. “Let us﻿—let us play at piquet until it is time for dinner!”
So they played at piquet, and presently Lady Fanny came in to say good night, and was gone again in a minute, having adjured Léonie to be sure and retire to bed immediately after dinner. She kissed Léonie, and was surprised to receive a quick hug from her. Rupert went away with Fanny, and Léonie was left alone with the Duke.
“They are gone,” she said in a curious voice.
“Yes, child. What of it?” His Grace dealt the cards with an expert hand.
“Nothing, Monseigneur. I am stupid tonight.”
They played on until dinner was served, and then went into the big dining-room, and sat down together at the table. Avon soon sent the lackeys away, whereat Léonie gave a sigh of relief.
“That is nice,” she remarked. “I like to be alone again. I wonder whether Rupert will lose much money tonight?”
“We will hope not, infant. You will know by his expression tomorrow.”
She did not reply, but began to eat a sweetmeat, and did not look at his Grace.
“You eat too many sweetmeats, *ma fille*,” he said. “It’s no wonder you are growing pale.”
“You see, Monseigneur, I had never eaten any until you bought me from Jean,” she explained.
“I know, child.”
“So now I eat too many,” she added. “Monseigneur, I am very glad that we are alone together tonight, like this.”
“You flatter me,” he bowed.
“No.

===Chinese===

“这两个月（2个月）简直太疯狂了，我都快分不清头顶和脚跟了。休，你们这就动身了吗？”
“我们四点钟（4点）在德·沙特莱公馆用膳，”休解释道，“那我就先道晚安了，莱昂妮。等我们回来时你准睡下了。”
她把手递给他，垂着眼帘。休和马林都吻了吻她纤细的手指。休朝鲁珀特开了句玩笑，两人便一道出门了。
“贾斯汀，你留在家里用晚膳吗？”夫人问，“我得去换条裙子，叫人备一辆轻便双轮马车送我去朱莉那儿。”
“我陪我家小家伙吃完晚膳，”阿翁说，“然后催她上床歇息。鲁珀特，你呢？”
“不了，我马上就走，”鲁珀特说，“我还有点私事要跟丹沃谈谈。走吧，范！”
两人一同出了门。阿翁走到莱昂妮坐着的睡榻旁，轻轻扯了扯她的一绺鬈发。
“孩子，你今晚出奇地沉默。”
“我在琢磨事情呢，”她神色凝重地说。
“在琢磨什么，我的心肝儿？”
“噢，我才不告诉你呢，公爵大人！”她说，微微一笑，“咱们……咱们在开饭前玩一会儿皮克牌吧！”
于是两人玩起了皮克牌。不多时范妮夫人进来道晚安，嘱咐莱昂妮吃完晚膳务必立刻上床歇息，随即转眼便离去了。她亲了亲莱昂妮，诧异地得到莱昂妮紧紧的一个拥抱。鲁珀特跟着范妮一道出了门，屋里只剩下莱昂妮和公爵独处。
“他们都走了，”她语气异样地轻声说。
“是啊，孩子。怎么啦？”公爵大人以熟练的手法洗牌发牌。
“没什么，公爵大人。我今晚有点心不在焉。”
他们继续玩牌，直到晚膳备妥，两人移步宽敞的餐厅，在餐桌旁相对而坐。阿翁很快打发走了男仆，莱昂妮见状长舒了一口气。
“这样真好，”她低语道，“我真喜欢像现在这样又只剩我们俩。不知鲁珀特今晚会不会输惨？”
“但愿不会吧，小家伙。明天看他的脸色你就能一清二楚了。”
她没有接话，剥了颗糖果含在嘴里，没有抬眼看公爵大人。
“你吃太多甜食了，我的女儿，”他说，“难怪脸色越来越苍白。”
“公爵大人，要知道，在您从让手里买下我之前，我从没尝过糖果是什么滋味，”她解释道。
“我知道，孩子。”
“所以如今我总忍不住多吃，”她补了一句，“公爵大人，今晚能像这样只有我们两个人在一起，我真高兴。”
“你过奖了，”他欠身道。
“不是过奖。

===Original===

Since we came back to Paris we have hardly ever been alone, and I have wanted﻿—oh, many times!﻿—to thank you for being so very kind to me.”
He frowned down at the walnut he was cracking.
“I pleased myself, infant. I believe I told you once before that I am no hero.”
“Did it please you to make me your ward?” she asked.
“Evidently, *ma fille*, else I had not done so.”
“I have been very happy, Monseigneur.”
“If that is so it is very well,” he said.
She rose, and put down her napkin.
“I am growing more and more tired,” she said. “I hope Rupert wins tonight. And you.”
“I always win, child.” He opened the door for her, and went with her to the foot of the stairs. “I wish you a good night’s rest, *ma belle*.”
She dropped suddenly on one knee, and pressed his hand to her lips and held it there a moment.
“*Merci, Monseigneur. Bonne nuit!*” she said huskily. Then she rose again, and ran up the stairs to her chamber.
Her maid was there, agog with excitement. Léonie shut the door carefully, brushed past the girl, and flung herself on to the bed, and cried as though her heart would break. The abigail hovered over her, soothing and caressing.
“Oh, mademoiselle, why will you run away like this? Must we go tonight indeed?”
Downstairs the great front door shut; Léonie clasped her hands over her eyes.
“Gone! Gone! Ah, Monseigneur, Monseigneur!” She lay battling with her sobs, and presently rose, quiet and resolute, and turned to her maid. “The travelling-coach, Marie?”
“Yes, mademoiselle, I hired one this morning, and ’tis to await us at the corner of the road in an hour’s time. But it has cost you the best part of six hundred francs, mademoiselle, and the man did not like to start so late. We shall not reach farther than Chartres tonight, he says.”
“It’s no matter. I have enough money left to pay for everything. Bring me paper now, and ink. Are you sure﻿—are you *sure* that you wish to come with me?”
“But yes, mademoiselle!” the girl averred. “M. le Duc would be wroth with me an I let you go alone.”
Léonie looked at her drearily.
“I tell you we shall never, never see him again.”
Marie shook her head sceptically, but merely said that she had quite made up her mind to go with mademoiselle. Then she fetched ink and paper, and Léonie sat down to write her farewell.
---
Upon her return Lady Fanny peeped into Léonie’s room to see whether she slept.

===Chinese===

自从回到巴黎，我们几乎从没单独相处过，而我心里一直想……噢，想过很多次了！……好好谢谢您待我这般体贴宽厚。”
他低头打量着正在剥壳的核桃，微微蹙眉。
“我不过图自己高兴罢了，小家伙。我想我从前对你说过，我绝非什么大善人。”
“收我做受监护人，也是图您自己高兴吗？”她问。
“显然如此，我的女儿，否则我何苦这么做。”
“我一直过得开心极了，公爵大人。”
“果真如此，那便再好不过了，”他说。
她站起身，放下餐巾。
“我越发觉得困倦了，”她说，“但愿鲁珀特今晚能赢钱。您也能赢。”
“我向来只赢不输，孩子。”他替她拉开房门，陪她走到楼梯口。“祝你好梦，我的美人儿。”
她冷不防单膝跪倒在地，双手捧起他的手贴在唇上，久久不愿松开。
“多谢公爵大人。晚安！”她嗓音嘶哑地说。随即她站起身，飞快奔上楼梯跑回自己的卧房。
侍女早就在房里候着了，兴奋得满脸通红。莱昂妮小心翼翼地带上房门，径直越过侍女，一头扑在床上，撕心裂肺地痛哭起来。侍女守在一旁，柔声宽慰抚摸。
“哎呀，小姐，您干嘛非要这样逃走呢？今晚我们当真非走不可吗？”
楼下传来沉重的前门关闭声；莱昂妮双手捂住了双眼。
“走了！他走了！啊，公爵大人，公爵大人！”她趴在床上极力克制住抽泣，片刻后坐起身来，神色已恢复了平静与决绝，转向侍女。“旅行马车备好了吗，玛丽？”
“是的，小姐，我今早雇下的，按吩咐一个小时后（1小时后）在路口等候。可足足花掉了将近六百法郎（600法郎）呢，小姐，而且车夫不大乐意这么晚动身。他说今晚我们最远只能赶到沙特尔。”
“无所谓。我剩下的盘缠足够应付一切开销了。现在去拿纸笔来。你确定……你当真确定愿意跟我走吗？”
“那当然了，小姐！”女孩言之凿凿，“要是小的放任您一人孤身在外，公爵大人非得扒了我的皮不可。”
莱昂妮凄然地看了她一眼。
“我告诉你，我们此生再也、再也见不到他了。”
玛丽将信将疑地摇了摇头，不过嘴上只说自己铁了心要陪小姐同去。接着她取来墨水和信纸，莱昂妮坐下来提笔写起绝别信。
---
范妮夫人归来后，悄悄推开莱昂妮的房门，想瞧瞧她睡下了没有。

===Original===

She held her candle high so that the light fell on the bed, and saw that it was empty. Something white lay upon the coverlet; she darted forward, and with a trembling hand held two sealed notes to the candlelight. One was addressed to herself; the other to Avon.
Lady Fanny felt suddenly faint, and sank down into a chair, staring numbly at the folded papers. Then she set her candle down upon the table, and tore open the note that was for her.

> **My Dear Madame**, (*she read*)﻿—
> I write this to say Fare Well, and Because I want to Thank you for your Kindness to me. I have told Monseigneur why I must go. You have been so very Good to me, and I Love you, and indeed, indeed I am sorry thatt I can only write to you. I shall never forget you.
> Léonie

Lady Fanny flew up out of her chair.
“Oh, good God!” she cried. “Léonie! Justin! Rupert! Oh, is *no* one here? Heavens, what shall I do?” Down the stairs she ran, and seeing a lackey by the door, hurried up to him. “Where’s mademoiselle? When did she go out? Answer me, dolt!”
“Madame? Mademoiselle is abed.”
“Fool! Imbecile! Where’s her maid?”
“Why, madame, she went out just before six, with﻿—Rachel, I think it was.”
“Rachel is in my chambers!” snapped her ladyship. “Oh, what in God’s name shall I do? Is his Grace returned?”
“No, Madame, not yet.”
“Send him to me in the library as soon as he comes in!” Lady Fanny commanded, and went there herself, and read Léonie’s note again.
Twenty minutes later his Grace entered.
“Fanny? What’s to do?”
“Oh, Justin, Justin!” she said on a sob. “Why did we leave her? She’s gone! Gone, I tell you!”
His Grace strode forward.
“Léonie?” he said sharply.
“Who else?” demanded my lady. “Poor, poor child! She left this for me, and one for you. Take it!”
His Grace broke the seal of his note, and spread out the thin sheet. Lady Fanny watched him while he read, and saw his mouth set hard.
“Well?” she said. “What does she write to you? For heaven’s sake tell me!”
The Duke handed the note to her, and went to the fire, and stared down into it.

===Chinese===

她高高举起烛台，烛光照向床榻，却发现床上空无一人。被面上隐约有一团白晃晃的东西；她快步上前，颤抖着手把那两封封好的信凑近烛光。一封是写给她的；另一封写给阿翁。
范妮夫人顿时感到一阵眩晕，跌坐在椅子里，目光发直地瞪着那两封折好的信笺。随后她把蜡烛放在桌上，颤手撕开了写给自己的那一封。

> 亲爱的夫人（她读道）：
> 我写这封信向您道别，也想借此感谢您待我的体贴仁爱。我已经告诉公爵大人我为何必须离开。您待我实在太好了，我爱您；当真、当真万分遗憾只能以这种方式与您告别。我永远不会忘记您。
> 莱昂妮

范妮夫人从椅子里猛地弹跳起来。
“噢，我的老天爷啊！”她惊叫道，“莱昂妮！贾斯汀！鲁珀特！天哪，难道一个人都没有吗？老天，我该怎么办才好？”她飞奔下楼，见到门边站着一名男仆，急忙冲上前去。“小姐人呢？她什么时候出去的？快回答我，蠢材！”
“夫人？小姐早就睡下了呀。”
“蠢货！饭桶！她的侍女呢？”
“哎呀，夫人，她在六点差一点（6点前）就出去了，好像是跟……跟蕾切尔一起走的。”
“蕾切尔一直在我的卧房里！”夫人厉声呵斥道，“天哪，看在老天的份上，我该怎么办？公爵大人回来了没有？”
“回夫人，还没有。”
“等他一回来，立刻叫他到藏书室来找我！”范妮夫人吩咐道，自己快步赶去藏书室，把莱昂妮的字条又翻来覆去读了一遍。
二十分钟后（20分钟后），公爵大人走了进来。
“范妮？出什么事了？”
“噢，贾斯汀，贾斯汀！”她泣不成声，“我们干嘛要把她一个人丢下？她走了！她跑掉了，我告诉你！”
公爵大人大步跨上前。
“莱昂妮？”他声音陡然一沉。
“还能有谁？”夫人哭道，“可怜的孩子！她留了这封信给我，还有一封是给你的。拿着！”
公爵大人拆开信封火漆，展开那张薄薄的信纸。范妮夫人注视着他阅读，只见他的嘴角骤然紧绷成一道冷硬的线条。
“怎么样？”她说，“她到底给你写了什么？看在老天的份上快告诉我呀！”
公爵把信递给她，自己踱到壁炉旁，目光死死盯在炉火中。

===Original===

> Monseigneur﻿—
> I have run away from you because I have discovered thatt I am not what you Think me. I told you a Lie when I said that Madame de Verchoureux had not Spoken to me the other Night. She told me thatt Every One knows I am a Baseborn daughter of Saint-Vire. It is Quite True, Monseigneur, for on Thursday I slipped out with my Maid, and went to his House, and asked him if it were indeed so. Monseigneur, it is not *convenable* thatt I stay with you. I cannot bear thatt I should bring Scandal to you, and I know thatt I must do this if I stay with you, for M. de Saint-Vire will say thatt I am his Bastard, and your Mistress. I do not want to go, Monseigneur, but it is best thatt I should. I tried to Thank you Tonight, but you would not let me. Please, you must not be anxious for me. I wanted at first to Kill myself, but then I saw thatt thatt is Cowardly. I am Quite Safe, and I am going very far away to Some One who will be good to me, I know. I have left all my Things, except the Money you gave me, which I must take to pay my journey, and the Sapphire Chain which you gave me when I was your Page. I thought you would not Mind if I took thatt, because it is the only thing I have kept which you gave me. Marie goes with me, and Please you must not be Angry with the lackeys for letting me go, for they thought I was Rachel. I leave for Rupert, and M. Davenant, and M. Marling, and Milor’ Merivale my so Great Love for them. And for you, Monseigneur. I cannot write it. I am Glad thatt we were Alone tonight.
> A Dieu

**Infant**

===Chinese===

> 公爵大人：
> 我从您身边逃走了，因为我发现自己并非您所想象的那样。那晚我对您撒了谎，说德·韦尔舒勒夫人没跟我说过话。其实她告诉我，人人都知道我是圣维尔的私生女。这是千真万确的，公爵大人，因为星期四那天我和侍女悄悄溜出去去了他府上，当面问他是否当真如此。公爵大人，我继续留在您身边是不合体统的。我绝不能容忍自己给您抹黑、带来丑闻，而我知道只要我留下来就必定会如此，因为圣维尔先生会到处宣扬我是他的杂种，也是您的情妇。我不想走，公爵大人，但我非走不可。今晚我原本想当面感谢您，可您不肯让我说出口。请您千万不要为我悬心。我起初甚至想一死了之，可后来明白自寻短见是懦夫的行径。我现在十分安全，正前往一个极远的地方，投奔一个真心待我好的人。我把所有的东西都留下了，除了您给我的银钱——我必须用它们支付路费，还有那条蓝宝石链子，那是当年我做您侍童时您赏给我的。我想您不会怪我带走它，因为这是我身边唯一留下的您赠予我的念想。玛丽跟着我一块儿走，请您千万别为放我们走而责怪男仆们，因为他们误以为那是蕾切尔。请代我转达对鲁珀特、达弗南特先生、马林先生以及梅里维尔勋爵的大爱。还有对您，公爵大人。我无法用言语表达。今晚能与您单独共进晚餐，我感到无比欣慰。
> 别了
> 
> 小家伙

===Original===

Lady Fanny’s face worked for a minute, then she whisked out her handkerchief and cried into it, regardless of paint and powder. His Grace picked up the note, and read it through again.
“Poor little infant!” he said softly.
“Oh Justin, we must find her!” sniffed her ladyship.
“We shall find her,” he answered. “I think I know where she has gone.”
“Where? Can you go after her? Now? She is such a babe, and she has only a foolish abigail with her.”
“I believe that she has gone to﻿—Anjou.” His Grace folded the note and put it into his pocket. “She has left me because she fears to endanger my﻿—reputation. It is somewhat ironic, is it not?”
Lady Fanny blew her nose vigorously, and gave yet another watery sniff.
“She loves you, Justin.”
He was silent.
“Oh Justin, do you not care? I felt so certain that you loved her!”
“I love her﻿—too well to marry her, my dear,” said his Grace.
“Why?” Lady Fanny put away her handkerchief.
“There are so many reasons,” sighed his Grace. “I am too old for her!”
“Oh, fiddle!” said my lady. “I thought that maybe ’twas her birth you cavilled at.”
“Her birth, Fanny, is as good as yours. She is Saint-Vire’s legitimate daughter.”
Lady Fanny gaped at him.
“In her place he has put that clod you know as de Valmé. His name is Bonnard. I have waited too long, but I strike now.” He picked up a handbell, and rang it. To the lackey who came he said: “You will go at once to the Hôtel de Châtelet, and request M. Marling and M. Davenant to return at once. Ask Milor’ Merivale to accompany them. You may go.” He turned again to his sister. “What did the child write to you?”
“Only farewell!” Lady Fanny bit her lip. “And I wondered why she kissed me so sweetly tonight! Oh dear, oh dear!”
“She kissed my hand,” Avon said. “We have all been fools this day. Do not distress yourself, Fanny. I shall bring her back if I have to search the world for her. And when she comes she will come as Mademoiselle de Saint-Vire.”
“But I don’t understand how﻿—oh, here is Rupert! Yes, Rupert, I have been crying, and I do not care. Tell him, Justin.”
Avon showed his young brother Léonie’s letter. Rupert read it, exclaiming at intervals. When he came to the end he snatched his wig from his head, threw it upon the floor, and stamped on it, saying various things beneath his breath that made Lady Fanny clap her hands over her ears.

===Chinese===

范妮夫人的面部肌肉抽搐了片刻，随即抽出手帕捂脸痛哭起来，连脸上的脂粉也顾不上了。公爵大人拾起信笺，从头到尾又细细读了一遍。
“可怜的小家伙！”他轻柔地低语道。
“噢贾斯汀，我们必须把她找回来！”夫人吸着鼻子抽泣道。
“我们会找到她的，”他回答，“我想我知道她去了哪里。”
“去了哪儿？你能去追她吗？现在就去？她年纪那么小，身边又只带了个少不更事的笨丫头。”
“我相信她是去了……安茹。”公爵大人折好信纸收进衣袋。“她离开我是因为害怕败坏我的……名誉。这可真够讽刺的，不是吗？”
范妮夫人用力擤了擤鼻子，又湿漉漉地抽噎了一声。
“她深爱着你，贾斯汀。”
他默不作声。
“噢贾斯汀，难道你一点都不动心吗？我原本那么笃定你是爱她的！”
“我爱她……爱得太深，因而绝不能娶她，亲爱的，”公爵大人说。
“为什么？”范妮夫人收起了手帕。
“理由太多了，”公爵大人叹道，“我对她来说太老了！”
“哎呀，瞎扯！”夫人说，“我还以为你是嫌弃她的出身呢。”
“她的出身，范妮，与你相比毫不逊色。她是圣维尔明媒正娶的婚生女儿。”
范妮夫人目瞪口呆地望着他。
“圣维尔把那个被你们称作德·瓦尔梅的粗胚顶替了她的位置。那个冒牌货其实姓博纳尔。我隐忍按捺得太久了，但现在我该出手了。”他拾起桌上的手摇铃摇了摇。对闻声赶来的男仆吩咐道：“你立刻去一趟德·沙特莱公馆，请马林先生和达弗南特先生即刻赶回来。顺便请梅里维尔勋爵一同过来。去吧。”他重新转向妹妹。“那孩子给你写了些什么？”
“只有告别的话！”范妮夫人咬着下唇，“我当时还在纳闷，她今晚临走前干嘛亲得我那么依依不舍！哎呀，天哪，天哪！”
“她亲了我的手，”阿翁说，“我们今天全成了糊涂蛋。别难过了，范妮。哪怕走遍天涯海角，我也一定要把她接回来。而等她回来时，她将堂堂正正以圣维尔小姐的身份归来。”
“可我怎么也想不明白……噢，鲁珀特来了！没错，鲁珀特，我刚才哭了，我才不在乎呢。贾斯汀，你告诉他吧。”
阿翁把莱昂妮的信递给弟弟。鲁珀特读着信，不时发出愤怒的惊叫。读到信末，他一把扯下头上的假发狠狠摔在地上，死死踩在脚下，嘴里低声咒骂着污言秽语，吓得范妮夫人赶忙捂住了耳朵。

===Original===

“If you don’t have his blood for this, Justin, I shall!” he said at last, picked up his wig, and put it on his head again. “May he rot in hell for a black scoundrel! Is she his bastard?”
“She is not,” said Avon. “She is his legitimate daughter. I have sent for Hugh and Marling. It is time that you all knew my infant’s story.”
“Left her love for me, bless her!” choked Rupert. “Where is she? Are we to set off at once? Only give the word, Justin, and I’m ready!”
“I do not doubt it, child, but we do not start today. I believe I know whither she has gone; she will be safe enough. Before I bring her back she shall be righted in the eyes of the world.”
Rupert glanced down at the letter in his hand.
“*I cannot bear thatt I should bring Scandal to you*,” he read. “Burn it, your life’s one long scandal! And she﻿—Devil take it, I could cry like a woman, so I could!” He gave the letter back to the Duke. “She’s made a cursed idol of you, Justin, and you’re not fit to kiss her little feet!” he said.
Avon looked at him.
“That I know,” he said. “My part ends when I bring her back to Paris. It is better so.”
“So you do love her.” Rupert nodded to his sister.
“I have loved her for a long time. And you, my son?”
“No, no, I’m no suitor of hers, I thank you! She’s a darling, but I’d have none of her to wife. It’s you she wants, and it’s you she’ll have, mark my words!”
“I am ‘Monseigneur,’ ” Avon replied with a crooked smile. “There is glamour attached to me, but I am too old for her.”
Then the others came in in a state of liveliest curiosity.
“What’s to do, Justin?” asked Hugh. “Has there been a death in the house?”
“No, my dear. Not a death.”
Lady Fanny sprang up.
“Justin﻿—she﻿—she would not have killed herself, and﻿—and said that in her letter so that you should not guess her intention? I never thought of that! Oh, Edward, Edward, I am so unhappy!”
“She?” Marling put an arm about Fanny. “Do you mean﻿—Léonie?”
“She has not killed herself, Fanny. You forget that she has her maid with her,” Avon said reassuringly.
Davenant shook him by the arm.
“Speak out, man, for God’s sake! What has happened to the child?”
“She has left me,” Avon said, and put Léonie’s note in his hand.
With one accord Merivale and Marling went to look over Hugh’s shoulder.
“God’s truth!” exploded Merivale, and clapped a hand to his sword-hilt as he read: “Oh, what a villain!

===Chinese===

“贾斯汀，要是你不去取下他的狗命，我就亲自去！”他终于喘着粗气说道，捡起地上的假发重新扣回头上，“愿那个下流恶棍在下地狱里烂掉！难道她真是他的私生女吗？”
“她不是，”阿翁说，“她是他的婚生女儿。我已经派人去召休和马林了。是时候让你们都知道我家小家伙的身世真相了。”
“她在信里还留下对我的爱，保佑她！”鲁珀特哽咽道，“她人在哪儿？我们现在就动身去追吗？只要你一声令下，贾斯汀，我随时出发！”
“我毫不怀疑你的心意，孩子，但我们今天不动身。我相信我知道她去了哪里；她在那儿足够安全。在我把她接回来之前，必须先在世人眼前为她正名。”
鲁珀特低头扫了一眼手中的信。
“‘我绝不能容忍自己给您抹黑、带来丑闻’，”他念道，“见鬼，你这辈子不就是一场接一场的丑闻吗！而她……该死，我都快像女人一样哭出来了，真的！”他把信递还给公爵。“她简直把你当成了该死的神明供奉，贾斯汀，而你根本不配亲吻她的小脚！”他说。
阿翁抬眼看着他。
“这一点我心知肚明，”他说，“等把她接回巴黎，我的职责便告一段落了。这样对大家都好。”
“这么说你确实爱她。”鲁珀特朝妹妹点了点头。
“我爱她很久了。你呢，我的好弟弟？”
“得了吧，我可没福分做她的追求者，多谢抬举！她固然讨人喜欢，但我绝不娶她做妻子。她心里装的全是你，注定要做你的人，走着瞧吧！”
“在她眼里我是‘公爵大人’，”阿翁苦笑了一下，“我身上附带着一层虚幻的光环，但我对她而言太老了。”
这时，其他人满怀着极大的好奇快步走了进来。
“贾斯汀，到底出什么事了？”休问，“府里有人过世了吗？”
“没有，我的挚友。没人过世。”
范妮夫人霍然起身。
“贾斯汀……她……她该不会寻了短见，然后在信里故意那么说，免得你猜出她的企图吧？我刚才怎么没想到这层！噢，爱德华，爱德华，我伤心死了！”
“她？”马林一把搂住范妮的肩膀，“你是说……莱昂妮吗？”
“她没有寻短见，范妮。你忘了她身边还跟着侍女呢，”阿翁出言安抚道。
达弗南特抓住他的臂膀使劲晃了晃。
“看在老天的份上，老兄，有话快直说！那孩子到底怎么了？”
“她离开我了，”阿翁说着，把莱昂妮的信递到了他手里。
梅里维尔和马林立刻不约而同凑上前，从休的肩膀上方俯身读信。
“苍天在上！”梅里维尔破口大骂，手按剑柄怒斥道，“好个卑鄙无耻的恶棍！

===Original===

*Now*, Justin, you shall have at him, and I’m with you to the death!”
“But﻿—” Marling looked up with puckered brows. “Poor, poor child, is it true?”
Hugh came to the end, and said huskily:
“Little Léon! ’Fore God, it’s pathetic!”
Rupert, at this juncture, relieved his feelings by throwing his snuffbox at the opposite wall.
“Oh, we’ll send him to hell between us, never fear!” he stormed. “Cur! Dastardly cur! Here, give me some burgundy, Fan! I’m in such a heat﻿—Swords are too good for the rogue, damme they are!”
“Much too good,” agreed his Grace.
“Swords!” Merivale exclaimed. “It’s too quick. You or I, Justin, could kill him in less than three minutes.”
“Too quick, and too clumsy. There is more poetry in the vengeance I take.”
Hugh looked up.
“But explain?” he begged. “Where is the child? What are you talking about? You have found a way to pay your debt in full, I suppose, but how have you found it?”
“Curiously enough,” said his Grace, “I had forgotten that old quarrel. You remind me most opportunely. The scales weigh heavily against M. de Saint-Vire. Give me your attention for one minute, and you shall know Léonie’s story.” Briefly, and with none of his accustomed suavity, he told them the truth. They listened in thunderstruck silence, and for some time after he finished, could find no words to speak. It was Marling who broke the silence.
“If that is true the man is the biggest scoundrel unhung!” he said. “Are you sure, Avon?”
“Perfectly, my friend.”
Rupert shook his fist, and muttered darkly.
“Good God, do we live in the Dark Ages?” cried Hugh. “It’s almost incredible!”
“But the proof!” Fanny cut in. “What can you do, Justin?”
“I can stake everything on the last round, Fanny. I am going to do that. And I think﻿—yes, I really think that I shall win.” He smiled unpleasantly. “For the present my infant is safe, and I believe I may put my hand on her when I wish.”
“What do you intend to do?” shouted Rupert.
“Oh yes, Justin, please tell us!” besought my lady. “It is so dreadful to know nothing. To have to sit idle!”
“I know, Fanny, but once more I must ask you all to be patient. I play my games best alone. One thing I may promise you: You shall be in at the death.”
“But when will it be?” Rupert poured out another glass of burgundy. “You’re too devilish tricky for me, Justin. I want a hand in the affair.”
“No.” Hugh shook his head.

===Chinese===

现在，贾斯汀，你该去跟他算总账了，我誓死奉陪！”
“可是……”马林眉头紧锁着抬起头，“可怜的孩子，这信上说的都是真的吗？”
休读完了信，嗓音沙哑地感叹道：
“可怜的小莱昂！老天作证，太令人心碎了！”
鲁珀特在这当口再也按捺不住情绪，一把将鼻烟盒砸向对面的墙壁。
“噢，我们大伙合力非把他送进地狱不可，走着瞧吧！”他暴跳如雷，“杂种！卑鄙透顶的杂种！快，范，给我倒点勃艮第红葡萄酒！我胸口快气炸了……拔剑砍了他都算便宜了这个恶贼，该死的！”
“确实太便宜他了，”公爵大人表示赞同。
“用剑？”梅里维尔惊呼道，“那太便宜他了。贾斯汀，换作你我，不到三分钟（3分钟）就能取他性命。”
“太快了，也太笨拙了。我准备的复仇方式要艺术得多。”
休抬起头来。
“到底怎么回事，快解释清楚啊！”他恳求道，“孩子究竟在哪儿？你到底在打什么算盘？我想你终于找到彻底清算旧账的办法了，可你到底是怎么查出来的？”
“说来真巧，”公爵大人说，“我原本都快把那桩陈年旧怨给忘了。多亏你提醒得正是时候。如今圣维尔先生犯下的恶行可远远不止这一桩。请诸位给我一分钟（1分钟）时间，听我将莱昂妮的身世和盘托出。”简短地，没有了往日那种从容优雅的腔调，他将残酷的真相原原本本讲给他们听。众人听得目瞪口呆，陷入死一般的沉寂，在他讲完后的良久时间里，谁也找不出一句话来打破沉默。最终是马林打破了僵局。
“如果当真如此，那家伙简直是世上最该千刀万剐的头号恶棍！”他说，“阿翁，你当真确定？”
“千真万确，我的朋友。”
鲁珀特挥舞着拳头，嘴里咬牙切齿地暗暗发咒。
“天哪，难道我们生活在黑暗的中世纪吗？”休失声叫道，“简直匪夷所思！”
“可证据呢！”范妮插嘴道，“贾斯汀，你打算怎么做？”
“我可以把一切赌注都押在最后一轮博弈上，范妮。我正是打算这么做。而且我想……是的，我真切地认为我赢定了。”他嘴角浮现出一抹森冷的微笑，“眼下我的小家伙安然无恙，我相信只要我愿意，随时都能找到她。”
“你究竟打算怎么动手？”鲁珀特嚷道。
“是啊，贾斯汀，求求你告诉我们吧！”夫人央求道，“一无所知的滋味太折磨人了。干坐在这一动不动简直要逼死人！”
“我理解，范妮，但我必须再次请各位沉住气。我的棋局，我向来习惯独自下到底。有一点我可以向大家保证：在给予他致命一击时，你们全都会在场见证。”
“可到底要等到什么时候？”鲁珀特又给自己斟了一杯勃艮第红葡萄酒，“贾斯汀，你这套神神秘秘的做派太折磨我了。这事我必须插一手。”
“不行。”休摇了摇头。

===Original===

“Let Avon play his game to a close. There are too many of us to join with him, and there’s a proverb that says ‘too many cooks spoil the broth.’ I’m not usually bloodthirsty, but I do not want Saint-Vire’s broth to be spoiled.”
“I want to see him crushed,” said Merivale. “And that soon!”
“You shall, my dear Anthony. But for the present we will behave as ever. If any ask for Léonie she is indisposed. Fanny, did you say that Madame du Deffand gives a soirée tomorrow?”
“Yes, but I’ve not the heart to go,” sighed my lady. “It will be so brilliant too, and I did want Léonie to be there!”
“Nevertheless, my dear, you will go, with us all. Calm yourself, Rupert. Your part was played, and played well, at Le Havre. Now it is my turn. Fanny, you are tired out. Go to bed now; you cannot do anything yet.”
“I must go back to de Châtelet,” said Merivale. He gripped Avon’s hand. “Act up to your name now, Satanas, if ever you did! We are all with you.”
“Even I,” said Marling with a smile. “You may be as devilish as you please, for Saint-Vire is the worst kind of villain I have had the ill-luck to meet.”
Rupert, hearing, choked in the act of drinking his third glass of burgundy.
“Damme, I boil with rage when I think of him!” he swore. “Léonie called him pig-person, but ’fore Gad he’s worse than that! He’s﻿—!”
Fanny fled incontinently from the room.

===Chinese===

“让阿翁把这场棋局下完吧。我们这么多人要是全掺和进去，俗话不是说‘厨子多了烧坏汤’嘛。我平日里虽不嗜血，但我绝不想看到圣维尔这锅肉汤被搞砸。”
“我想亲眼看着他被碾得粉碎，”梅里维尔说，“而且越快越好！”
“如你所愿，我亲爱的安东尼。但眼下我们必须一切照旧。要是外人问起莱昂妮，就说她偶感微恙。范妮，你方才说杜·德芳夫人明天要办晚会？”
“是的，但我现在哪有心思去呀，”夫人叹气道，“晚会场面肯定璀璨得很，我原本一心盼着带莱昂妮一起去的！”
“尽管如此，亲爱的，你还是得去，我们大家一起去。鲁珀特，放平心态。你在勒阿弗尔的那一出戏演得很出色。现在该轮到我登场了。范妮，你累坏了。现在快去歇息吧；眼下你什么忙也帮不上。”
“我得回德·沙特莱公馆去了，”梅里维尔说。他紧紧握住阿翁的手，“现在，撒旦，拿出你名号该有的真本事来吧！我们所有人都站在你这边。”
“哪怕我也毫不犹豫，”马林微笑着说，“你爱怎么狠毒就怎么狠毒吧，因为圣维尔是我有幸碰见的最卑劣下流的人渣。”
鲁珀特听闻此言，刚喝下第三杯（3杯）勃艮第红葡萄酒，猛地呛了一下。
“该死，我一想到他就气得热血沸腾！”他恶狠狠地发誓，“莱昂妮骂他是猪猡，可老天作证，他比猪猡还要下作百倍！他简直就是个……！”
范妮听不下去，急匆匆地逃出了房间。

## XXI The Discomfiture of the Comte de Saint-Vire / 圣维尔伯爵狼狈受挫
===Original===

Mine host and hostess of the Black Bull at Le Dennier had never before entertained such quality at their humble inn. Madame sent a serving-man running hotfoot to her neighbour, Madame Tournoise, and presently that lady came hurrying in with her daughter to aid Madame in her preparations. When she heard that no less a personage than an English Duke, with his entourage, had arrived at the inn, she was round-eyed in wonderment, and when his Grace came slowly down the stairs clad in a coat of palest lavender, with lacing of silver, and a silver waistcoat, amethysts in his lace, and on his fingers, she stood staring open-mouthed.
His Grace went to the little parlour, and sent for writing materials. Mine host came bustling with the inkhorn, and desired to know whether Monseigneur would take any refreshment. His Grace bespoke a bottle of canary wine, and three glasses, and sat him down to write to his cousin. A faint smile hovered about his lips.

> My Very Dear Cousin﻿—
> I Trust that by the Time you Receive this Missive you will have recovered from the Sad Indisposition which had overtaken you when I had the Pleasure of seeing you, three Days since. I am Desolat’d to be Oblig’d to put you to Added Inconvenience, but I believe I must Request you to Join me here as soon as may be. Gaston, who brings this letter, will Escort you. Pray pack your Trunks for a long stay, for I have some notion of Proceeding in due Course to Paris. My Ward, you will be Reliev’d to hear, is with me in this charming Village, in Company with my Lord Rupert.
> I have the Honour, my dear Cousin, to be

Yr most devot’d, humble, and obedient servant

**Avon**

===Chinese===

勒德尼耶“黑牛客栈”的掌柜夫妇，在这座简陋的小客栈里还从未接待过身份如此尊贵的贵客。老板娘打发一名男仆飞奔去邻居图尔努瓦斯太太家求援，不多时那位太太便带着女儿匆匆赶来，协助老板娘打点张罗。当她听说下榻客栈的贵宾竟然是一位英国公爵及其随从时，她惊讶得目瞪口呆；而当公爵大人身着一袭镶着银色滚边的极浅淡紫色外套、银色马甲，蕾丝领巾与手指上缀着紫水晶，迈着从容优雅的步伐缓步下楼时，她更是张大嘴巴站在那里，看得发痴。
公爵大人踱进小会客室，吩咐备好文房用具。掌柜殷勤地送来墨水瓶，恭敬地询问公爵大人是否需要用些茶点酒水。公爵大人吩咐送上一瓶加那利葡萄酒和三只酒杯（3只酒杯），便坐下来给他的表姐修书一封。一抹若有若无的微笑浮现在他的嘴角。

> 我最亲爱的表姐：
> 相信待你收到这封信函时，三天前（3天前）我有幸与你相见时你所患的那场令人揪心的微恙，已然痊愈。不得不再次叨扰令你受累，我深感遗憾，但我以为必须请你尽快赶来此地与我汇合。替我捎带此信的加斯东将负责护送你。请务必收拾妥当长住所需的行李箱笼，因我打算适时携众人前往巴黎。得知我那受监护人眼下正与鲁珀特勋爵一同随我滞留在此幽静的小村落，想必足以令你宽心。
> 我深感荣幸，亲爱的表姐，谨上。

你最忠诚、谦卑且顺从的仆人
阿翁

===Original===

His Grace signed his name with a flourish, still smiling. The door opened, and Léonie came in, all in foaming white muslin, with a blue sash about her waist, and a blue ribbon in her hair.
“Monseigneur, is it not kind of Lady Fanny to send me this pretty dress? I look nice, do you not think?”
The Duke put up his glass.
“My child, you look charming. Lady Fanny’s taste is unimpeachable.” He rose, and picked up a flat velvet case from the table. “I beg you will accept this trifling mark of my affection for you, infant.”
Léonie skipped up to him.
“*Another* present, Monseigneur? I think you are very kind to me! What is it, I wonder?”
His Grace opened the case. Léonie’s lips formed a soundless Oh!
“Monseigneur!”
The Duke lifted the pearls from their bed of velvet, and clasped them about her neck.
“Oh, Monseigneur, thank you!” she said in a gasp, and held the long string between her fingers. “They are beautiful! I love them, oh, much! Would you like me to curtsy to you, or may I just kiss your hand?”
His Grace smiled.
“You need do neither, infant.”
“I will do both,” said Léonie, and sank down with skirts outspread and one little foot peeping from beneath the muslin flounces. Then she kissed the Duke’s hand, and rose. Lastly she inspected his Grace’s clothes.
“That is a nice dress, I think,” she said.
Avon bowed.
“I like it,” Léonie said. “Monseigneur, I feel very brave now. What will you do to this pig-person when he comes?”
“I shall have the honour of presenting you, my dear,” Avon answered. “Let him have your haughtiest curtsy. It is a little game we play.”
“Yes? But I do not want to curtsy to him. I want to make him sorry.”
“Believe me, he will be very sorry, but the time is not yet. Bear in mind, *ma fille*, that you have not till now set eyes on my dear friend.”
“Ah, bah, what is this?” she demanded. “I know him well, and he knows me!”
“Strive to cultivate a little imagination,” sighed his Grace. “The so dear Comte stole my page, Léon. You are my ward, Mademoiselle de Bonnard.”
“Oh!” said Léonie doubtfully. “I must be polite, *enfin*?”
“Very polite, child. And remember, you and I are here for our health. We know naught of abductions, or evil drinks, or even﻿—er﻿—pig-persons. Can you play the game of pretence?”
“But yes, Monseigneur! Will he pretend, do you think?”
“I have reason to think, child, that he will follow my lead.”
“Why, Monseigneur?”

===Chinese===

公爵大人面带微笑，潇洒地签下了自己的名字。门开了，莱昂妮轻快地走了进来，浑身穿着如浪花般翻滚的白细棉布裙，腰间系着蓝色腰带，发间系着蓝色丝带。
“公爵大人，范妮夫人送我这件漂亮裙子，人真好，不是吗？我看起来挺好看的吧，您觉得呢？”
公爵举起了单片眼镜。
“我的孩子，你看起来楚楚动人。范妮夫人的品味向来无可挑剔。”他站起身，拾起桌上的一个扁平丝绒盒子。“请收下这件小玩意儿吧，小家伙，算是我待你的一点心意。”
莱昂妮蹦蹦跳跳迎上前来。
“又有礼物吗，公爵大人？您待我可真好！到底是什么呢？”
公爵大人打开了盒子。莱昂妮双唇微启，发出无声的惊呼！
“公爵大人！”
公爵从丝绒衬垫上托起那串珍珠，亲手为她戴在颈项间。
“噢，公爵大人，谢谢您！”她屏息惊叹，纤细的手指轻抚着那串长长的珠链，“太美了！我喜欢极了，噢，喜欢得不得了！您是想让我给您行屈膝礼呢，还是只要我亲亲您的手？”
公爵大人微微一笑。
“两样都不必，小家伙。”
“我偏要两样都做，”莱昂妮说着，双膝一弯蹲下身去，裙裾散开，细棉布荷叶边下探出一只玲珑的小脚。接着她吻了吻公爵的手，轻巧地站起身来。最后她打量起公爵的衣着。
“这身衣裳真漂亮，我觉得很好看，”她说。
阿翁欠身施礼。
“我很喜欢，”莱昂妮说，“公爵大人，我现在胆子大得很。等那头猪猡找上门来，您打算怎么收拾他？”
“我有幸会将你引见给他，亲爱的，”阿翁回答，“你只管向他行一个最傲慢冷淡的屈膝礼。这是我们要玩的一出小把戏。”
“是吗？可我一点都不想向他行礼。我想让他追悔莫及。”
“相信我，他日后定会悔不当初，但时机未到。记住，我的女儿，在此之前你可从未见过我这位挚友。”
“哎呀，啐，这算哪门子把戏？”她反问道，“我明明认得他，他也认得我！”
“试着发挥一点想象力吧，”公爵大人叹息道，“那位亲爱的伯爵拐走的是我的侍童莱昂。而你是我的受监护人，德·博纳尔小姐。”
“噢！”莱昂妮将信将疑地说，“总之我得表现得知书达礼，对吧？”
“要极其有礼貌，孩子。记住，你我来到这里纯粹是为了疗养静修。我们对什么绑架、迷药，乃至……呃……猪猡，全都一无所知。这出假装互不相识的把戏，你能演好吗？”
“那当然了，公爵大人！您觉得他也肯跟着演吗？”
“我有充分的理由相信，孩子，他会乖乖跟着我的调子走。”
“为什么呢，公爵大人？”

===Original===

“Because, child, he has a secret which he suspects I share. But since it is a highly discreditable secret he would not like me to think that he had any knowledge of it. We fence, you see, but whereas I see my way clearly, he moves in darkness.”
“Oh, I see!” she said. “He will be surprised to find you, *n’est-ce pas*?”
“I rather think he will,” agreed his Grace. He went to the table and poured out two glasses of canary. One of them he gave to Léonie. “My dear, I drink to your safe deliverance.”
“Oh, I thank you, Monseigneur! What shall I drink to?” She put her head on one side. “*Voyons*, I will just drink to *mon cher seigneur*!”
“Quite neat,” said the Duke. “Gaston? *A la bonne heure!* You will journey back to Avon, Gaston, at once.”
Gaston’s face fell.
“But yes, Monseigneur.”
“Bearing with you this letter to my cousin. She will accompany you to France again.”
Gaston brightened perceptibly.
“Further, you will go to Milor’ Merivale and obtain from him the clothes of Milor’ Rupert. It is understood?”
“All Milor’ Rupert’s clothes, Monseigneur?” asked Gaston, aghast.
“All of them. If he is there, bring milor’s valet also. I had well-nigh forgot Mademoiselle Léonie’s maid. Instruct her to pack the rest of mademoiselle’s clothes, and bring her﻿—and them﻿—to me here.”
Gaston blinked rapidly.
“Yes, Monseigneur,” he said with an effort.
“You will board the Silver Queen, of course, and you will convey your charges by coach to Portsmouth.” His Grace tossed a fat purse to him. “At Portsmouth, on your way to Avon, you will seek out a certain roan horse.”
“*Bon Dieu!*” muttered Gaston. “A roan horse, Monseigneur, yes.”
“A roan horse belonging to one Mr. Manvers of Crosby Hall, sold by Milor’ Rupert on Monday. You will buy it back.” Another purse followed the first. “The price is of no moment. You will have the animal conveyed to Crosby Hall, with Milor’ Rupert’s compliments and﻿—er﻿—thanks. That also is understood?”
“Yes, Monseigneur,” said Gaston dismally.
“*Bien.* This is, I think, Wednesday. You will be here again no later than Monday. Send Meekin to me now. You may go.”
The groom came speedily.
“Your Grace sent for me?”
“I did. You will start for Paris, my friend, within the hour.”
“Ay, your Grace.”
“To apprise the admirable Walker of my coming.

===Chinese===

“因为，孩子，他藏着一个不可告人的秘密，而他怀疑我也知晓内情。然而正因为那是桩见不得人的丑恶勾当，他绝不愿让我知道他竟心知肚明。你瞧，我们这是在交锋过招，只不过我每一步都看得清清楚楚，而他却是在黑暗中摸索。”
“噢，我明白了！”她说，“见到您在这里，他肯定会大吃一惊吧，不是吗？”
“我想他大概会吓破胆，”公爵大人赞同道。他走到桌前倒了两杯（2杯）加那利葡萄酒，将其中一杯递给莱昂妮。“亲爱的，祝贺你平安脱险。”
“噢，谢谢您，公爵大人！那我该为谁干杯呢？”她歪着脑袋琢磨，“得啦，我就为我亲爱的大人干杯吧！”
“很得体，”公爵说，“加斯东？来得正好！加斯东，你立刻启程折返回阿翁庄园。”
加斯东的脸顿时垮了下来。
“遵命，公爵大人。”
“替我带上这封写给我表姐的信。她会随你一同重返法国。”
加斯东脸上明显亮堂起来。
“此外，你去找一趟梅里维尔勋爵，从他那里把鲁珀特勋爵所有的衣物全都取来。听明白了没有？”
“鲁珀特勋爵所有的衣服吗，公爵大人？”加斯东目瞪口呆地问。
“一件不落全带回来。如果勋爵的贴身男仆在那里，把他也一并带来。我差点忘了莱昂妮小姐的侍女。吩咐她将小姐剩下的衣物收拾妥当，把她和箱笼全带到这里来见我。”
加斯东急促地眨巴着眼睛。
“遵命，公爵大人，”他勉强应道。
“你们自然要搭乘‘白银女王号’，然后在朴次茅斯雇马车将人送来。”公爵大人抛给他一个沉甸甸的钱袋。“去阿翁庄园途经朴次茅斯时，去寻找一匹花斑红马。”
“老天爷啊！”加斯东喃喃自语道，“一匹花斑红马，是的，公爵大人。”
“那匹花斑红马属于克罗斯比堂的一位曼弗斯先生，是鲁珀特勋爵在星期一卖掉的。你把它赎买回来。”又一个钱袋抛了过去。“价钱多少无所谓。赎回来后派人将马送回克罗斯比堂，代致鲁珀特勋爵的问候以及……呃……谢意。这桩差事也听明白了吧？”
“明白了，公爵大人，”加斯东垂头丧气地说。
“很好。今天大概是星期三吧。你最迟下周一必须赶回此地。现在去把米金给我叫来。你可以退下了。”
马夫很快赶了过来。
“公爵大人传唤小的？”
“正是。我的朋友，你一个小时内（1小时内）即刻动身前往巴黎。”
“遵命，公爵大人。”
“前去通报令人钦佩的沃克，告知他我即将抵京。

===Original===

You will bring back with you the large berline, the smaller travelling coach, and a light chaise for my Lord Rupert’s baggage. You will arrange for change of horses to await me at Rouen, at Tign, and at Pontoise. I shall rest at the Coq d’Or at Rouen for one night.”
“Very good, your Grace. Which day am I to tell the landlord?”
“I have not the least idea,” said the Duke. “But when I come I shall require four bedchambers, a private parlour, and quarters for my servants. I trust I make myself plain?”
“Yes, your Grace.”
“That is all,” said Avon.
Meekin bowed, and went out.
“Voyons,” said Léonie from her seat by the fire. “It gives me great pleasure to hear you say ‘Do this﻿—do that!’ I like to hear them answer only, ‘Yes, Monseigneur,’ and go so quickly to do your bidding.”
Avon smiled.
“I have only once in my life had a servant in mine employ who dared to question my commands,” he said.
“Oh?” Léonie looked up in all innocence. “Who was that, Monseigneur?”
“A page I had, my dear, by name﻿—er﻿—Léon.”
Her eyes sparkled, but she folded her hands demurely.
“*Tiens!* I wonder he dared, Monseigneur.”
“I believe there was nothing he would not dare,” said Avon.
“Truly? Did you like him, Monseigneur?”
“You are a minx, my dear.”
She laughed, blushed and nodded.
“It is not a compliment,” said his Grace, and came to the fire, and sat down. “I have sent for your duenna, you hear.”
“Yes.” She grimaced. “But she will not come till Monday, will she? Why are we going to Paris?”
“As well Paris as anywhere else,” Avon replied. “Your education is nearly complete. You are going to make your curtsy to the Polite World.”
“Am I, Monseigneur? *Vraiment?* I think it will be *fort amusant*. Shall I go to Vassaud’s?”
The Duke’s brows twitched together.
“No, *ma fille*, you will not. Vassaud’s is one of those places which you will strive to forget.”
Léonie peeped at him.
“And﻿—and the Maison Chourval?”
“Did I take you there?” His Grace was still frowning.
“But yes, Monseigneur, only you sent me to wait for you in the vestibule.”
“I had that much decency left, then. You will most assuredly forget the Maison Chourval. It would be interesting to know what you made of it?”
“Very little, Monseigneur. It is not a nice place, I think.”
“No, infant, you are right. It is not a nice place, nor was I﻿—nice﻿—to take you there. That is not the world you shall enter.”
“Tell me!” begged Léonie.

===Chinese===

你回程时带回那辆大型双门柏林马车、一辆稍小些的旅行马车，以及一辆装载鲁珀特勋爵行李的轻便双轮车。沿途在鲁昂、蒂尼亚和蓬图瓦兹为我安排好沿途备换的马匹。我会在鲁昂的金公鸡客栈留宿一晚（1晚）。”
“遵命，公爵大人。敢问小的该跟掌柜约定在哪一天？”
“我一点也不知道，”公爵说，“但届时我需要四间卧房（4间卧房）、一间私人客厅，以及仆役的下处。我想我的吩咐够清楚了吧？”
“清楚了，公爵大人。”
“下去吧，”阿翁说。
米金躬身行礼退了出去。
“哎呀，”莱昂妮坐在壁炉旁托着腮说道，“听您发号施令‘办这个、办那个’，真叫人心里痛快极了！我就喜欢听他们唯唯诺诺应着‘遵命，公爵大人’，然后屁颠屁颠跑去照办的样子。”
阿翁笑了笑。
“我这辈子雇过的仆人里，唯独只有一个人敢对我的命令阳奉阴违、提出质疑，”他说。
“哦？”莱昂妮一脸天真地抬起头，“那是谁呀，公爵大人？”
“我从前收留的一个侍童，亲爱的，名字叫作……呃……莱昂。”
她双眸灵动地闪了闪，双手端庄地叠放在膝头。
“哎呀！真纳闷他哪来的狗胆，公爵大人。”
“我相信这世上就没有他不敢干的事，”阿翁说。
“当真？您喜欢他吗，公爵大人？”
“你这小滑头，亲爱的。”
她咯咯笑了起来，面飞红霞，用力点了点头。
“这可不是在夸你，”公爵大人说着，踱到壁炉旁坐了下来。“你都听见了，我已经打发人去接你的女看护了。”
“听见了。”她做了个鬼脸，“可她最快也得下周一才能到呢，不是吗？我们干嘛要去巴黎呀？”
“去巴黎和去别处没什么两样，”阿翁回答，“你的规矩学得差不多了。该是你向上流社会行屈膝礼引见的时候了。”
“真的吗，公爵大人？当真如此？我想那肯定好玩透顶。我能去瓦索赌场吗？”
公爵眉头紧紧蹙起。
“不能，我的女儿，你休想。瓦索赌场属于你必须尽力忘得一干二净的地方之一。”
莱昂妮悄悄瞄着他。
“那……那舒瓦尔公馆呢？”
“我还带你去过那儿？”公爵眉头依然紧锁。
“当然去过啦，公爵大人，只不过您让我留在门厅里等您。”
“看来我当时总算还保留着几分起码的体面。舒瓦尔公馆你更是必须忘得干干净净。我很想知道你当时看出什么名堂没有？”
“什么名堂也没瞧出来，公爵大人。我觉得那不是什么好地方。”
“是的，小家伙，你说得对。那确实不是什么正经地方，我带你去那儿也……不正经。那绝不是你今后该涉足的世界。”
“快跟我说说！”莱昂妮央求道。

===Original===

“Shall I go to balls?”
“Certainly, *ma belle*.”
“And will you dance with me?”
“My dear, there will be gallants enough to claim your hand. You will have no need of me.”
“If you do not dance with me I won’t dance at all,” she announced. “You will, Monseigneur, won’t you?”
“Perhaps,” he said.
“I do not like perhaps,” she said. “Promise!”
“You are really very *exigeante*,” he complained. “I am past the age of dancing.”
“*Eh bien!*” Léonie tilted her chin. “Me, I am too young to dance. *Nous voilà!*”
“You, my infant,” said his Grace severely, “are a very naughty, wilful child. I do not know why I bear with you.”
“No, Monseigneur. And you will dance with me?”
“Quite incorrigible,” he murmured. “Yes, infant.”
A horse came clattering up the street, and paused at the inn-door.
“Monseigneur﻿—do you think﻿—is it﻿—*he*?” Léonie asked nervously.
“It seems likely, my dear. The game begins.”
“I am not feeling﻿—*quite* so brave, Monseigneur.”
He rose, and spoke softly.
“You will not disgrace yourself, or me, infant. There is naught to fear.”
“N-no, Monseigneur.”
The landlord entered.
“Monseigneur, it is M. le Docteur to see milor’.”
“How disappointing,” said his Grace. “I will come. Stay here, child, and if my very dear friend should come, remember that you are my ward, and behave with proper courtesy.”
“Yes, Monseigneur,” she faltered. “You will come back soon, won’t you?”
“Assuredly.” His Grace went out with a swish of silken skirts. Léonie sat down again, and regarded her toes. Overhead, in Rupert’s chamber, she heard footsteps and the muffled sound of voices. These signs of the Duke’s proximity reassured her a little, but when again she heard the clatter of hoofs on the cobbled street some of the delicate colour left her cheeks.
“This time it is in very truth that pig-person,” she thought. “Monseigneur does not come﻿—he wants me to play the game a little by myself, I think. *Eh bien, Léonie, courage!*”
She could hear Saint-Vire’s voice upraised in anger outside. Then came a quick, heavy tread, the door was flung open, and he stood upon the threshold. His boots were caked with mud, and his coat bespattered; he carried a riding-whip and gloves, and his cravat and hair were in disorder. Léonie looked at him in some hauteur, copying Lady Fanny’s manner to a nicety.

===Chinese===

“我能去参加舞会吗？”
“当然可以，我的美人儿。”
“那您肯跟我跳舞吗？”
“亲爱的，到时争着邀你跳舞的年轻贵族多的是。你哪用得着我。”
“要是您不跟我跳，我就一支舞也不跳，”她宣布道，“您会跟我跳的，公爵大人，对不对？”
“也许吧，”他说。
“我不喜欢‘也许’，”她说，“快答应我！”
“你未免也太任性难缠了，”他抱怨道，“我都过了跳舞的年纪了。”
“好哇！”莱昂妮扬起下巴，“那我也太年轻了，根本跳不得舞。扯平了！”
“你这小家伙，”公爵大人严肃地训诫道，“真是个无法无天、任性妄为的孩子。我真不知道自己怎么受得了你。”
“就是受得了嘛，公爵大人。那您到底陪不陪我跳舞呀？”
“真是无可救药，”他低语道，“好吧，小家伙。”
街上传来一阵急促的马蹄声，停在了客栈门口。
“公爵大人……您猜……会是……他吗？”莱昂妮紧张地问。
“看样子八九不离十，亲爱的。这出戏开锣了。”
“我心里……没刚才那么勇敢了，公爵大人。”
他站起身，语气轻柔地安抚道：
“你不会让自己丢脸，更不会让我蒙羞的，小家伙。没什么好害怕的。”
“嗯……不怕，公爵大人。”
掌柜推门而入。
“公爵大人，大夫前来看望勋爵了。”
“真叫人失望，”公爵大人说，“我这就来。待在这儿别动，孩子，要是我那位挚友当真赶到了，记住你是我的受监护人，拿出名门小姐该有的礼貌来周旋。”
“好的，公爵大人，”她怯生生地应道，“您很快就会回来的，对不对？”
“自然。”公爵大人伴随着丝绸裙裾的悉窣声迈步出门。莱昂妮重新坐下，低头打量着自己的脚尖。头顶上鲁珀特的卧房里传来了脚步声和隐约的交谈声。意识到公爵就在附近，多少让她宽心了些；然而当石子铺就的街面上再次响起嘚嘚的马蹄声时，她娇嫩的面颊上血色顿时褪去了一半。
“这一回千真万确是那头猪猡了，”她暗想，“公爵大人没赶过来……看来他是想让我自己先演上一会儿。好吧，莱昂妮，拿出勇气来！”
门外传来了圣维尔暴跳如雷的吼声。紧接着是一阵沉重而急促的脚步声，房门被猛地推开，他霍然出现在门槛上。他的靴子上裹满了泥浆，外套溅满了污泥；手里握着马鞭和手套，领巾与头发凌乱不堪。莱昂妮带着几分傲慢神情打量着他，将范妮夫人的神态模仿得惟妙惟肖。

===Original===

For an instant it seemed that the Comte did not recognise her; then he came striding forward, his face dark with passion.
“You thought you had tricked me, madame page, did you not? I am not so easily worsted. I do not know where you obtained those fine clothes, but they avail you nothing.”
Léonie came to her feet, and let her eyes wander over him.
“M’sieur is in error,” she said. “This is a private room.”
“Very prettily played,” he sneered, “but I am no fool to be put off by those airs and graces. Come, where’s your cloak? I’ve no time to waste!”
She stood her ground.
“I do not understand you, m’sieur. This is an intrusion.” She rolled the word off her tongue, and was pardonably pleased with it.
The Comte grasped her arm, and shook it slightly.
“Your cloak! Quickly, now, or it will be the worse for you.”
Much of her icy politeness left Léonie.
“Bah! Take your hand away from my arm!” she said fiercely. “How dare you touch me?”
He pulled her forward, an arm about her waist.
“Have done! The game is up, my dear. You will do better to submit quietly. I shall not hurt you if you do as I say.”
From the doorway came the faint rustle of silk. A cool, haughty voice spoke.
“You mistake, m’sieur. Have the goodness to unhand my ward.”
The Comte jumped as though he had been shot, and wheeled about, a hand to his sword hilt. Avon stood just inside the room, quizzing glass raised.
“*Sacré mille diables*,” swore Saint-Vire. “*You!*”
A slow and singularly unpleasant smile curved his Grace’s lips.
“Is it possible?” he purred. “My very dear friend Saint-Vire!”
Saint-Vire tugged at his cravat as though it choked him.
“You!” he said again. His voice was hardly above a whisper. “Are you in very truth your namesake? Even﻿—here﻿—I find you!”
Avon came forward. An elusive perfume was wafted from his clothes as he walked; in one hand he held a lace handkerchief.
“Quite an unexpected rencontre, is it not, Comte?” he said. “I have to present my ward, Mademoiselle de Bonnard. I believe she will accept your apologies.”
The Comte flushed dark, but he bowed to Léonie, who swept him a magnificent curtsy, and muttered a few incoherent words.
“No doubt you mistook her for someone else?” said his Grace urbanely. “I do not think you have met her before?”
“No. As m’sieur says﻿—I mistook her﻿—*Mille pardons, mademoiselle.*”
His Grace took snuff.
“Strange how one may be mistaken,” he said.

===Chinese===

有一瞬间伯爵似乎没能认出她来；随即他大踏步走上前来，满脸怒容、神色阴沉。
“你以为你耍花招把我蒙过去了，侍童小姐，是不是？我可没那么容易被算计。我不管你从哪儿弄来这一身华服，它们休想救得了你。”
莱昂妮霍然起立，上下打量着他。
“先生认错人了，”她说，“这里是私人雅室。”
“装得倒挺像那么回事，”他冷笑道，“但我可不是傻子，休想拿这副拿腔拿调的做派打发我。少废话，你的斗篷在哪儿？我没工夫跟你磨蹭！”
她毫不退缩。
“我不明白先生在说什么。您这是擅闯私宅。”她咬文嚼字吐出这句话，心里对自己这副措辞颇为满意。
伯爵一把钳住她的臂膀，使劲晃了晃。
“披上斗篷！动作快点，否则有你的苦头吃！”
莱昂妮脸上的冰冷端庄顿时荡然无存。
“呸！拿开你的脏手！”她厉声斥道，“你竟敢碰我？”
他把她往前一拽，一条手臂死死箍住她的腰肢。
“别闹了！戏演完了，亲爱的。老老实实听话对你只有好处。只要你照我说的办，我绝不伤你一根汗毛。”
门口传来了一声极其微弱的丝绸拂动声。一个冰冷而傲慢的嗓音随之响起。
“先生认错人了。请放开我的受监护人。”
伯爵犹如触电般猛地弹跳开来，仓皇转身，一手按住了剑柄。阿翁正悠然伫立在门内，单片眼镜高高举起。
“见了一千个鬼了，”圣维尔咬牙切齿地咒骂道，“竟然是你！”
公爵大人的嘴角缓缓扬起一抹罕见而阴冷的微笑。
“当真可能吗？”他轻柔地说道，“我亲爱的挚友圣维尔！”
圣维尔狠狠扯了扯领巾，仿佛被死死扼住了咽喉。
“你！”他又重复了一句，声音几乎微弱得如同耳语，“难道你当真如你的外号一样阴魂不散吗？哪怕……在这个鬼地方……我也能撞见你！”
阿翁踱步上前。随着他的走动，衣袍间散发出一缕若有若无的高雅暗香；他一手拿着一条蕾丝手帕。
“真是一场意料之外的邂逅，不是吗，伯爵？”他说，“容我向您引见我的受监护人，德·博纳尔小姐。我想她会宽恕您的失礼举动的。”
伯爵脸色涨得猪肝一般紫红，但他不得不向莱昂妮欠身行礼；莱昂妮则向他施了一个高贵华丽的屈膝礼，嘴里含糊地咕哝了几句。
“想必您是把她误认作旁人了吧？”公爵大人温文尔雅地说道，“我想您此前从未见过她吧？”
“没有。正如先生所言……我认错了人……万分抱歉，小姐。”
公爵大人优雅地吸了口鼻烟。
“世事真是奇妙，人怎么竟会认错呢，”他说。

===Original===

“Likenesses are so inexplicable, are they not, Comte?”
Saint-Vire started.
“Likenesses﻿ ﻿… ?”
“You do not find it so?” His Grace drew a fan of lavender silk mounted on silver sticks from his pocket, and waved it languidly. “One wonders what can have brought the Comte de Saint-Vire to this unsophisticated spot.”
“I came on business, M. le Duc. One also wonders what can have brought the Duc of Avon here.”
“But business, dear Comte, business!” said Avon, gently.
“I come to retrieve some﻿—property﻿—I lost at﻿—Le Havre!” said the Comte wildly.
“How singular!” remarked Avon. “I came on precisely the same errand. Our paths seem fated to﻿—er﻿—cross, my dear Comte.”
Saint-Vire set his teeth.
“Yes, m’sieur? On﻿—on the same errand, you say?” He forced a laugh. “Singular indeed!”
“Quite remarkable, is it not! But unlike yours, my property was stolen from me. I hold it in﻿—er﻿—trust.”
“Indeed, m’sieur?” The Comte’s mouth was unpleasantly dry, and it was evident that he was at a loss to know what to say.
“I trust, dear Comte, that you have found your property?” Avon’s tone was silky.
“Not yet,” Saint-Vire answered slowly.
His Grace poured out the third glass of wine, and offered it to him. Mechanically the Comte accepted it.
“Let us hope that I may be able to restore it to you,” said his Grace, and sipped meditatively at his wine.
Saint-Vire choked.
“M’sieur?”
“I shall spare no pains,” continued his Grace. “The village is not a large hunting-ground, to be sure. You know that it is here, I suppose?”
“Yes﻿—no﻿—I do not know. It is not worth your trouble, m’sieur.”
“Oh, my dear Comte!” protested his Grace, “if it is worth so much endeavour”﻿—his eyes flickered to those mud-caked boots﻿—“so much endeavour on your part, I am sure it is also worth my attention.”
The Comte seemed to choose his words carefully.
“I have reason to think, m’sieur, that it is one of those jewels that contain﻿—a flaw.”
“I trust not,” answered Avon. “So it was a jewel? Now that which was stolen from me is in the nature of a weapon.”
“I hope you have had the good fortune to find it,” said Saint-Vire, goaded, but holding fast to his self-control.
“Yes, my dear Comte, yes. Chance favours me nearly always. Strange. Let me assure you that I shall do my utmost to restore your﻿—jewel, I think you said it was?﻿—your jewel to you.”
“It﻿—is not likely that you will find it,” said Saint-Vire, between his teeth.

===Chinese===

“相貌相仿这种事，向来叫人百思不得其解，您说是不是，伯爵？”
圣维尔浑身一震。
“相貌相仿……？”
“您不这么觉得吗？”公爵大人从衣袋中掏出一柄装在银柄上的浅淡紫色丝绸折扇，漫不经心地摇晃起来，“真不知究竟是什么风，竟把圣维尔伯爵吹到了这般偏僻荒凉的乡野角落。”
“我来此公干，公爵大人。我也正纳闷究竟是什么风把阿翁公爵吹到这里来了。”
“自然也是公干，亲爱的伯爵，公干！”阿翁轻描淡写地答道。
“我是来寻回……在勒阿弗尔丢失的……某件财物！”伯爵有些气急败坏地说。
“真是太巧了！”阿翁感叹道，“我正是为了完全相同的差事而来。看来你我的运数注定要在半道上……呃……狭路相逢了，我亲爱的伯爵。”
圣维尔咬紧牙关。
“是吗，先生？为了……完全相同的差事，您是这么说的？”他勉强挤出一丝干笑，“果真是太巧了！”
“简直不可思议，不是吗！不过与您的不同，我的财物是从我手里被人窃走的。我是受人之托代为……呃……保管。”
“当真如此，先生？”伯爵口干舌燥，显然不知该如何招架。
“不知亲爱的伯爵是否已物归原主、寻回失物了？”阿翁语调柔滑如丝。
“尚未寻得，”圣维尔慢吞吞地答道。
公爵大人倒了第三杯（3杯）葡萄酒，递到了他面前。伯爵下意识地接了过来。
“但愿我有幸能替您将失物寻回，”公爵大人说着，若有所思地抿了一口酒。
圣维尔猛地呛了一下。
“先生？”
“我定会不遗余力，”公爵大人继续说道，“这小村落左右不过巴掌大的地方，想来找起来也不算费事。我想您心知肚明失物就在此地吧？”
“这……不……我也不清楚。用不着劳烦先生大驾。”
“哎呀，我亲爱的伯爵！”公爵大人推辞道，“既然值得您如此大动干戈”——他的目光朝那双沾满泥巴的靴子轻轻一瞥——“值得您如此劳师动众，我相信它自然也值得我多加留心。”
伯爵似乎字斟句酌地应付道：
“我有理由认为，先生，那是一件带有……瑕疵的珠宝。”
“但愿并非如此，”阿翁回答，“原来那是一件珠宝？而从我手里被盗走的，却是一件类似兵刃的利器。”
“但愿您能有幸将它找回，”圣维尔被逼得咬牙切齿，却极力维持着克制。
“是的，亲爱的伯爵，正是。冥冥之中运气总是眷顾于我。真是不可思议。请允许我向您担保，我定会竭尽所能替您寻回……我想您方才是说是珠宝吧？……寻回您的珠宝。”
“您……恐怕不大可能找得到它，”圣维尔从牙缝里挤出这句话。

===Original===

“You forget the element of Chance, dear Comte. I am a great believer in my luck.”
“My property can hardly interest you, M. le Duc.”
“On the contrary,” sweetly replied his Grace, “it would afford me great pleasure to be able to assist you in the matter.” He glanced towards Léonie, who stood by the table, listening with a puzzled frown to the quick give and take of words. “I have quite a happy﻿—shall we say, knack?﻿—of finding lost﻿—er﻿—property.”
Saint-Vire turned livid. His hand shook as he raised his glass to his lips. Avon regarded him in exaggerated concern.
“My dear Comte, surely you are unwell?” Again his eyes went to Saint-Vire’s boots. “You must have come a long way, dear Comte,” he said solicitously. “No doubt you are sadly fatigued.”
The Comte spluttered and set down his glass with a snap.
“As you say, I﻿—I am not entirely myself. I have been suffering from a﻿—slight indisposition, which has confined me to my room these last three days.”
“It is really most remarkable,” marvelled his Grace. “My brother﻿—I think you know him? Yes, quite so﻿—is at this very moment above-stairs, also suffering from a slight indisposition. I fear there must be something unhealthy in the air of this place. You find it a trifle sultry, perhaps?”
“Not at all, m’sieur!” snarled Saint-Vire.
“No? These annoying disorders, I believe, have a way of overtaking one in any climate.”
“As my Lord Rupert found,” said Saint-Vire harshly. “I trust his﻿—indisposition has not given him a distaste for my country.”
“Quite the reverse,” said his Grace blandly. “He is agog to proceed to Paris. He and I, dear Comte, believe firmly in that old remedy: the hair of the dog.”
The veins stood out on Saint-Vire’s forehead.
“Indeed? It is to be hoped that my lord does not act rashly.”
“You must not be concerned for him, dear Comte. I stand﻿—as it were﻿—behind him, and I have a wonderfully cool head. So they tell me. But you﻿—ah, that is another matter! You must have a care to yourself, Comte. Let me implore you to relinquish your﻿—search﻿—until you are more yourself.”
Saint-Vire’s hand clenched.
“You are too good, m’sieur. My health is not your concern.”
“You mistake, dear Comte. I take a most lively interest in your﻿—er﻿—health.”
“I believe I shall do very well, m’sieur. My complaint is not so serious, I am glad to say.”

===Chinese===

“您忘了还有机缘巧合呢，亲爱的伯爵。我向来对自己的运气深信不疑。”
“我的私产想必引不起公爵大人的兴致。”
“恰恰相反，”公爵大人甜甜地回答，“若能在此事上为您效劳，我将感到无上荣幸。”他目光扫向伫立在桌旁的莱昂妮——她正微蹙着眉头，困惑地倾听着两人之间连珠炮般的唇枪舌剑。“在寻回失落……呃……财物方面，我向来颇有一番……怎么说呢，独门绝技。”
圣维尔面色惨白。他举杯到唇边时，手不住地颤抖。阿翁则带着夸张的关切神情注视着他。
“我亲爱的伯爵，您莫非身体抱恙？”他的目光再次落在了圣维尔的靴子上，“看来您赶了极远的路程呢，亲爱的伯爵，”他体贴入微地说，“想必此刻早已疲惫不堪了吧。”
伯爵喉咙里咕噜了一声，啪的一声把酒杯重重顿在桌上。
“正如您所见，我……我确实不大舒坦。我受了一点……轻微微恙的困扰，最近三天（3天）一直闭门不出卧床休养。”
“这可真是太不可思议了，”公爵大人啧啧称奇，“家弟……我想您认得他吧？没错，正是如此……此刻恰好也在楼上歇息，同样身染微恙。我真担心此地的水土空气怕是有什么不干净的毛病。您莫非也觉得这里有些闷热难耐？”
“半点都不觉得，先生！”圣维尔恶狠狠地从喉咙里挤出一句。
“是吗？我想这种恼人的微恙，无论在哪种气候下都难免会突然找上门来。”
“正如鲁珀特勋爵的领教一样，”圣维尔语气生硬地说，“但愿他的……微恙没让他对我法兰西祖国心生嫌隙厌恶才好。”
“恰恰相反，”公爵大人慢条斯理地说道，“他如今正急不可耐地盼着启程前往巴黎呢。亲爱的伯爵，他和我都坚信那句古老的医方：解铃还须系铃人。”
圣维尔额头上青筋暴跳。
“当真？但愿勋爵大人行事莫要太鲁莽。”
“您大可不必替他悬心，亲爱的伯爵。我向来……可以说……是站在他身后的坚强后盾，而且旁人都说，我这个人头脑冷静得出奇。但您嘛……啊，那可就是另一码事了！您可得多保重自己的身体啊，伯爵。请容我诚挚奉劝您，在身体彻底康复之前，还是尽早放弃您的……搜寻吧。”
圣维尔双拳紧握。
“您太客气了，先生。我的身子骨用不着您来操心。”
“您误会了，亲爱的伯爵。对于您的……呃……身子骨，我可是关切备至得很呢。”
“我相信我自己应付得来，先生。幸好我的小毛病算不得什么大病。”

===Original===

“Nevertheless, my dear Comte, it is always well to proceed cautiously, is it not? One never knows when these trifling ailments may not grow suddenly to quite large proportions. I have known a mere chill creep to the lungs, and strike a man down in the very prime of life.” He smiled pleasantly upon the Comte, who sprang suddenly to his feet, overturning his chair.
“Curse you, you’ve no proof!” he cried.
Up went his Grace’s brows. His eyes mocked.
“I assure you, dear Comte, I have known such a case.”
Saint-Vire pulled himself together with an effort.
“It will not happen﻿—to me, I think,” he said thickly.
“Why, we will hope not,” agreed the Duke. “I believe that no one is﻿—struck down﻿—before the appointed hour.”
The Comte groped for his whip, and stood wrenching the lash between his hands.
“With your permission, m’sieur, I will leave you. I have wasted enough time already. Mademoiselle, your servant!” He spat the words out, snatched up his gloves, and went blindly to the door.
“So soon?” mourned his Grace. “I shall hope to have the felicity of seeing you in Paris. I must present my ward to your so charming wife.”
Saint-Vire flung open the door, and twisted the handle viciously. He looked back with a sneer.
“You are full of plans, m’sieur. We will hope that none of them go awry.”
“Certainly,” bowed Avon. “Why should they?”
“There is sometimes﻿—a flaw!” snapped Saint-Vire.
“You bewilder me,” said his Grace. “Are we speaking of your lost jewel, or my plans﻿—or both? I should warn you that I am something of a judge of precious stones, dear Comte.”
“Yes, m’sieur?” The flush mounted to Saint-Vire’s face again. “It is possible that you are labouring under a delusion, M. le Duc. The game is not played out yet.”
“By no means,” said the Duke. “Which reminds me that I have not inquired after your so enchanting son. Pray how does he?”
The Comte showed his teeth.
“He is very well, m’sieur. I feel no anxiety on his behalf. Your servant!” The door shut with a slam.
“The so dear Comte!” murmured Avon.
“Monseigneur, you did not do anything to him!” cried Léonie. “I thought that you would punish him!”
“*Ma fille*, the day comes when I shall punish him,” answered Avon, and threw down his fan. His voice had changed, and sounded harsh in Léonie’s ears. “And there will be no mercy for him at my hands.”
Léonie looked at him in awe and some admiration.

===Chinese===

“尽管如此，亲爱的伯爵，小心驶得万年船，您说是不是？谁也说不准这些不起眼的小毛病会不会陡然恶化，演变成弥天大祸。我就曾亲眼见过，一丁点不起眼的微寒侵入肺腑，便让一个正值壮年、年富力强的人命丧黄泉。”他冲着伯爵和蔼可亲地微笑着。伯爵霍然跃起身，猛地撞翻了身后的椅子。
“遭天谴的，你手里根本没有任何证据！”他狂吼道。
公爵大人挑起双眉，眼中满是讥诮。
“我向您保证，亲爱的伯爵，我确实亲历过这样的惨剧。”
圣维尔用尽全身力气稳住心神。
“那种事……绝不会落在我头上，我想，”他粗声粗气地说。
“哎呀，但愿如此吧，”公爵附和道，“我相信不到命定的大限之期，绝不会有人……猝然暴毙。”
伯爵摸索着抓起马鞭，双手死死拧着皮鞭的鞭梢。
“恕我告退了，先生。我已经耽搁了太多宝贵时间。小姐，愿为您效劳！”他恶狠狠地从牙缝里啐出这几个字，抓起手套，头也不回地撞出门去。
“这就走啦？”公爵大人惋惜地叹道，“但愿不久后能在巴黎有幸与您重聚。我必须将我的受监护人引见给您那迷人高贵的夫人呢。”
圣维尔摔开大门，恶狠狠地拧着门把手。他扭过头来冷笑一声。
“先生满脑子都是如意算盘。但愿一件也别落空才好。”
“当然，”阿翁欠身道，“怎么会落空呢？”
“有时候……偏偏会横生瑕疵！”圣维尔厉声道。
“您把我给搞糊涂了，”公爵大人说，“我们眼下谈论的究竟是您遗失的珠宝，还是我的全盘计划……抑或是二者兼有？容我提醒您一句，亲爱的伯爵，对于辨别珍宝瑕疵，我向来颇有眼力。”
“是吗，先生？”血色再次涌上圣维尔的脸庞，“公爵大人，很可能您不过是一厢情愿在自欺欺人罢了。这场博弈可还没下完呢。”
“绝未下完，”公爵说，“这倒提醒了我，我还没向您打听那位天真烂漫的令郎呢。敢问他近来可好？”
伯爵咬牙切齿地咧了咧嘴。
“他好得很，先生。用不着您替他瞎操心。愿为您效劳！”砰的一声，房门被狠狠甩上了。
“这位亲爱的伯爵啊！”阿翁轻声呢喃。
“公爵大人，您居然什么都没对他做！”莱昂妮叫嚷起来，“我还当您会好好惩戒他一番呢！”
“我的女儿，我严惩他的那一天迟早会到来，”阿翁说着，扔下了手中的折扇。他的语调陡然变了，在莱昂妮听来显得异常严厉冰冷，“落在我手里，他休想得到一丝一毫的慈悲宽恕。”
莱昂妮满怀敬畏与钦佩地注视着他。

===Original===

“You look quite angry, Monseigneur!”
His glance came to rest on her face. He went to her, and taking her chin in his hand, looked deep into her eyes. They smiled trustfully up at him. Abruptly he released her.
“I have reason, child. You have seen a villain today.”
“Yes, a pig-person,” she nodded. “You won’t let him take me again, will you, Monseigneur?”
“No, my infant. He shall never again have you in his clutches. That I swear.”
She frowned, watching him.
“You seem different, Monseigneur, I think. You are not angry with me?”
The grimness left his mouth, and he smiled.
“It would be impossible, my dear. We will go now and solace Rupert’s boredom.”

===Chinese===

“您看起来当真动了真怒呢，公爵大人！”
他的目光转而落在了她的面庞上。他走向她，伸出一只手轻轻托起她的下巴，深深凝视着她的眼眸。那双美丽的眸子里正满含信任地冲着他微笑。他猝然松开了手。
“事出有因，孩子。你今天亲眼见识到了一个十恶不赦的恶徒。”
“是的，一头猪猡，”她用力点头，“您再也不会让他把我抢走了，对不对，公爵大人？”
“绝不会，我的小家伙。他休想再把你攥进魔爪里。我对此立誓。”
她微蹙双眉，定定地打量着他。
“我觉得您变了个人似的，公爵大人。您不是在生我的气吧？”
冷峻的神色从他的唇边消散，他微微一笑。
“那绝无可能，亲爱的。走吧，我们现在去替鲁珀特排解排解苦闷。”

## XIII The Education of Léonie / 莱昂妮受教
===Original===

The Duke remained at Avon for over a month, during which time Léonie applied herself energetically to the task of becoming a lady. Madam Field’s ideal of this estate was luckily not Avon’s. He had no wish to see his ward sitting primly over her stitchery, which was just as well, perhaps, for after the first attempt Léonie declared that nothing would induce her to ply a needle. Madam Field was a little flustered by this defection, and by Léonie’s taste for swordplay, but she was far too good-natured and indefinite to do more than murmur nervous remonstrances. She stood very much in awe of her cousin, and although she was by birth an Alastair she felt herself to be a wholly inferior creature. She had been happy enough with her husband, an obscure gentleman with a taste for farming, but she knew that in the eyes of her family she had disgraced herself by marrying him. This had not troubled her much while he lived, but now that he was dead, and she had returned to what had once been her own milieu, she was uncomfortably conscious of the step downwards that she had taken in her foolish youth. She was rather frightened of Avon, but she liked to live in his house. When she looked about her, at faded tapestries, at stretches of velvet lawns, at portraits innumerable, and crossed swords above the doorway, she remembered anew the glory of past Alastairs, and some almost forgotten chord stirred within her.
Léonie was enchanted by Avon Court, and demanded to know its history. She walked with Justin in the grounds, and learned how Hugo Alastair, coming with the Conqueror, settled there, and built himself a fair dwelling, which was destroyed in the troublesome times of King Stephen; how it was built again by Sir Roderick Alastair; how he was given a barony, and prospered, and how the first Earl, under Queen Mary, pulled down the old building and erected the present house. And she learned of the bombardment that partially destroyed the West Wing, when Earl Henry held all for the King against the usurper Cromwell, and was rewarded for it at the Restoration by a dukedom. She saw the sword of the last Duke, the same that he had used in tragic ’15, for King James III, and heard a small part of Justin’s own adventures, ten years ago, for King Charles III.

===Chinese===

公爵在阿翁庄园盘桓了一个多月（1个多月），其间莱昂妮全力以赴投入到成为贵族淑女的学业之中。所幸菲尔德夫人心目中的大家闺秀标准与阿翁的大相径庭。阿翁可不想看见自己的受监护人整天拘谨死板地坐在绣架前飞针走线，这倒也算是件幸事，因为莱昂妮初次尝试之后，便斩钉截铁地宣布天底下没有任何力量能逼她动一下绣花针。莱昂妮的离经叛道以及对击剑的狂热偏好，让菲尔德夫人有些手足无措，但她性子太软弱随和、拿不定主意，除了嘴里发出几句提心吊胆的规劝外，根本无可奈何。她对这位表弟畏之如虎，虽说自己出身阿拉斯泰尔家族，却深感自己是个彻头彻尾的卑下之流。她过去与丈夫过得倒挺快活——对方是个沉迷农事的没落绅士——但她心知肚明，在家族众人眼中，当年嫁给他是让门第蒙羞的丑事。丈夫在世时她对此尚不以为意，如今丈夫过世，她重新回到自己当年的社交圈子，才局促不安地意识到自己少不更事时做出了多么严重的门第降格。她有些惧怕阿翁，但又极喜欢住在他的府邸里。当她环顾四周，看见褪色的壁毯、平整如茵的天鹅绒草坪、数不胜数的祖先肖像，以及门楣上方交叉的古剑时，她便会重新记起昔日阿拉斯泰尔家族的无上荣耀，心底某根沉寂已久的弦也随之悄然拨动。
莱昂妮对阿翁庄园着了迷，非要打破砂锅问到底弄清它的家族历史。她陪同贾斯汀在庭院中漫步，得知当年雨果·阿拉斯泰尔随征服者威廉而来，在此地定居建起了一座美丽的府邸，后来在斯蒂芬国王的动乱年代毁于兵燹；后来由罗德里克·阿拉斯泰尔爵士重建；他又如何获封男爵领地，家族日渐昌盛；玛丽女王统治时期，第一代伯爵拆除旧居，拔地建起了如今这座宏伟宅邸。她还得知了西翼在战火中被炮火摧毁的旧事——当时亨利伯爵誓死为国王抵抗篡位者克伦威尔，后来在王朝复辟时期凭借这份忠诚获封公爵爵位。她瞻仰了上一任公爵佩戴过的宝剑——正是他在悲剧的1715年起义（1715年）中为詹姆斯三世国王浴血奋战时所佩之剑，还听贾斯汀聊起了十年前（10年前）他自己为查理三世国王效力时所经历的一小段冒险往事。

===Original===

Justin touched but lightly on this period of his life; his work in that attempt, Léonie guessed, had been secret and tortuous, but she learned that the true King was Charles Edward Stuart, and learned to speak of the little warlike man on the throne as Elector George.
Her education at Justin’s hands was a source of interest and amusement to her. Up in the long picture gallery he taught her to dance, with an eagle eye for the smallest fault, or the least hint of awkwardness in her bearing. Madam Field came to play on the spinet for them, and watched with an indulgent smile while they trod each stately measure. She reflected that she had never seen her unapproachable cousin so human, as with this laughing sprite of a girl. They danced the minuet, and the long lines of ancestors gazed down upon them indulgently.
Avon made Léonie practise her curtsy, and made her combine her pretty roguishness with some of the haughtiness that characterised my Lady Fanny. He showed her how to extend her hand for a man to kiss, how to use her fan, and how to place her patches. He would walk with her in the pleasaunce, teaching every rule of deportment until she was word perfect. He insisted that she should cultivate certain queenliness of bearing. She soon learned, and would rehearse her newest lesson before him, enjoying herself hugely, radiant if she earned a word of praise.
She could already ride, but astride only. She was disgusted with the sidesaddle, and for a while rebelled against it. For the space of two days her will held fast against Avon’s, but his frigid politeness disarmed her, and on the third day she came to him with head hanging, and faltered:
“I am sorry, Monseigneur. I﻿—I will ride as you wish.”
So they rode together in the grounds until she had mastered this new art, and then they went out over the countryside, and those who saw the Duke beside this beautiful girl cast knowing glances at each other, and shook their heads wisely, for they had seen other beautiful girls with Avon.
Bit by bit the Court, so long bereft of a mistress, began to wear a more cheerful air. Léonie’s glad young spirit pervaded it; she flung back heavy curtains, and consigned ponderous screens to the lumber room. Windows were opened to let in the wintry sun, and bit by bit the oppressive solemnity of the place disappeared.

===Chinese===

贾斯汀对这段人生经历只是一笔带过；莱昂妮猜想，他在那场图谋中所承担的差事定然极其隐秘曲折。不过她明白了查理·爱德华·斯图亚特才是真正的国王，也学会了将宝座上那个好战的矮个子男人轻蔑地称为选帝侯乔治。
在贾斯汀手下接受教导，成了她莫大的乐趣与消遣。在那间长长的画廊里，他亲自教她跳舞，以鹰隼般敏锐的目光审视着她举止中每一个极其细微的瑕疵或丝毫的笨拙。菲尔德夫人过来为他们弹奏击弦古钢琴，带着纵容的微笑注视着他们踏着庄重优雅的节拍起舞。她暗自思忖，在面对这个欢声笑语、精灵般的小姑娘时，自己这位向来令人望而生畏、不可接近的表弟竟流露出了前所未有的人情味。他们翩翩跳起小步舞，壁画上那一排排列祖列宗仿佛也在自上而下慈爱地凝视着他们。
阿翁让莱昂妮反复练习屈膝礼，教她将娇俏的顽皮与范妮夫人身上那种傲慢从容的气度融为一体。他教她如何优雅地伸出手任男士吻手，如何巧用折扇，以及如何贴美人绢痣。他常陪她在林荫花园中漫步，一丝不苟地传授每一道仪态规矩，直到她倒背如流、烂熟于心。他坚持要求她培养出一种宛如女王般高贵尊严的仪态。她学得极快，常在他面前排演最新的仪态功课，玩得不亦乐乎；每每得到他一句赞赏，整张小脸便会容光焕发。
她原本就会骑马，但向来只习惯跨骑。她对侧鞍深恶痛绝，一度强烈抗拒。在整整两天（2天）的时间里，她的倔脾气一直与阿翁对抗，然而阿翁那副冷若冰霜的客气最终将她彻底化解；到了第三天（第3天），她耷拉着脑袋走到他面前，怯生生地喃喃道：
“对不起，公爵大人。我……我愿意按照您的吩咐去骑。”
于是两人一同在庭园里策马，直到她熟练掌握了这项侧骑的新本领；随后他们骑马漫游乡间，凡是撞见公爵身旁伴着这位绝色佳人的人，无不意味深长地相视而笑，心照不宣地摇着头——毕竟他们以往可没少见过伴在阿翁身边的各色美丽姑娘。
渐渐地，这座长年没有女主人的庄园开始焕发出前所未有的生机。莱昂妮欢快明媚的年轻活力浸透了每一处角落；她扯开厚重的窗帘，把笨重沉闷的屏风统统发配到杂物间。一扇扇窗户被敞开，迎进冬日的暖阳，庄园里那种令人窒息的沉闷与森严渐渐荡然无存。

===Original===

Léonie would have none of the stern neatness that was wont to reign there. She tumbled prim cushions, pushed chairs out of place, and left books lying on odd tables, caring nothing for Madam Field’s shocked protests. Justin permitted her to do as she pleased; it amused him to watch her gyrations, and he liked to hear her give orders to his expressionless lackeys. Clearly she had the habit of command: unusual she might be, but never did she exhibit any lack of breeding.
Her lessons were soon put to the test. On one occasion he said suddenly:
“We will suppose, Léonie, that I am the Duchess of Queensberry, and that you have just been presented to me. Show me how you would curtsy.”
“But you cannot be a duchess, Monseigneur,” she objected. “That is ridiculous. You don’t *look* like a duchess! Let us pretend you are the Duke of Queensberry.”
“The Duchess. Show me the curtsy.”
Léonie sank down and down.
“Like this: low, but not so low as to the Queen. This is a very good curtsy I am doing, *n’est-ce pas*?”
“It is to be hoped you would not talk all the time,” said his Grace. “Spread out your skirts, and do not hold your fan like that. Show me again.”
Léonie obeyed meekly.
“It is very difficult to remember everything,” she complained. “Now let us play at piquet, Monseigneur.”
“Presently. Curtsy now to﻿—Mr. Davenant.”
She swept her skirts right regally, and with head held high extended one small hand. Avon smiled.
“Hugh is like to be amazed,” he remarked. “It’s very well, *ma fille*. Curtsy now to me.”
At that she sank down with bent head, and raised his hand to her lips.
“No, my child.”
She rose.
“That is the way I do it, Monseigneur. I like it.”
“It is incorrect. Again, and the proper depth. You curtsied then as to the King. I am but an ordinary mortal, remember.”
Léonie searched in her mind for a fitting retort.
“Lawks!” she said vaguely.
His Grace stiffened, but his lips twitched.
“I﻿—beg﻿—your﻿—pardon?”
“I said ‘lawks,’ ” said Léonie demurely.
“I heard you.” His Grace’s voice was cold.
“Rachel said it,” Léonie ventured, peeping up at him. “She is Lady Fanny’s maid, you know. You do not like it?”
“I do not. I should be glad if you would refrain from modelling your conversation on that of Lady Fanny’s maid.”
“Yes, Monseigneur. Please, what does it mean?”
“I have not the slightest idea. It is a vulgarity.

===Chinese===

莱昂妮对以往统治此地的严苛整饬嗤之以鼻。她把摆放整齐的靠垫揉得一团糟，把椅子推得东倒西歪，书本随手丢在各式各样的小茶几上，对菲尔德夫人大惊失色的抗议全当耳旁风。贾斯汀由着她的性子胡闹；看着她像小陀螺般转来转去，反倒让他觉得趣味盎然，而且他格外喜欢听她向那些面无表情的男仆发号施令。显然，她骨子里天生就带着发号施令的派头：她或许行事与众不同，但骨子里从未流露出半点缺乏教养的粗鄙。
她的功课很快迎来了实战检验。有一次他冷不防开口道：
“莱昂妮，我们假设我是昆斯伯里公爵夫人，而你刚刚被引见给我。演给我瞧瞧你该怎么行屈膝礼。”
“可您哪能当公爵夫人呢，公爵大人，”她抗议道，“这太荒唐了。您长得一点都不像公爵夫人！咱们假装您是昆斯伯里公爵吧。”
“我是公爵夫人。行屈膝礼给我看。”
莱昂妮的身子缓缓下沉，越沉越低。
“就像这样：身子要低，但又不能像朝见王后那么低。我这个屈膝礼行得很标准吧，不是吗？”
“但愿你到时可别从头到尾嘴里嘟囔个不停，”公爵大人说，“裙摆要铺开，折扇不能这么拿。再做一遍给我看。”
莱昂妮乖顺地照办了。
“要把规矩全记牢可真不容易，”她抱怨道，“现在咱们来玩皮克牌吧，公爵大人。”
“待会儿再说。现在向……达弗南特先生行礼。”
她高贵威严地轻拂裙裾，昂首挺胸，优雅地递出一只纤纤玉手。阿翁露出了微笑。
“休怕是要惊掉下巴了，”他评价道，“很好，我的女儿。现在向我行礼。”
闻听此言，她深深躬下身垂着头，将他的手轻轻托起贴在自己的唇边。
“不行，我的孩子。”
她直起身来。
“我平时就是这么向您行礼的呀，公爵大人。我喜欢这样。”

“我听见了，”公爵大人语调冰冷。
“蕾切尔平时就是这么说的呀，”莱昂妮悄悄抬眼打量着他，“她是范妮夫人的侍女，您知道的。您不喜欢这句吗？”
“我不喜欢。你要是能别照搬范妮夫人侍女的粗话做派，我会深感欣慰。”
“遵命，公爵大人。请问这到底是什么意思呀？”

He taught her to fence in the Italian manner, and showed her many subtle passes which he had learned abroad. She experimented with one of them, and since his Grace’s guard, at that moment, was lax, broke through. The button of her foil came to rest below his left shoulder.

Léonie danced in her excitement.

“Not at all,” he said crushingly. “My guard was weak.”
Her mouth dropped.

His Grace relented.
“No, you broke through, *ma fille*.”

Then we have the Cavendish﻿—Devonshire, my dear; and the Seymours, and my Lord Chesterfield, whom you will know by his wit and his dark eyebrows. Whom else? He is not worth your consideration. He lacks polish. There is Colley Cibber, one of our poets, Mr. Sheridan, who writes plays for us, and Mr. Garrick, who acts them; and a score of others. Léonie nodded.
