Of the nobility there is M. de Richelieu, the model of true courtesy, and the Duc de Noailles, famed for the battle of Dettingen, which he lost. Then we have the brothers Lorraine-Brionne, and the Prince d’Armagnac. My memory fails me. Ah yes, there is M. de Belle-Isle, who is the grandson of the great Fouquet. He is an old man now. *Tiens*, almost I had forgot the estimable Chavignard﻿—Comte de Chavigny, child﻿—a friend of mine. I might go on forever, but I will not.”

Machérand has a wife who squints. I need say no more. Château-Mornay will amuse you for half an hour, no longer. Madame de Marguéry’s salons are world-famous. ## XXVII The Hand of Madame de Verchoureux / 德·韦尔舒勒夫人的一手牌
===Original===

And Paris began to talk, in whispers at first, then gradually louder, and more openly. Paris remembered an old, old scandal, and said that the English Duc had adopted a baseborn daughter of Saint-Vire in revenge for past injuries. Paris thought that it must irk Saint-Vire considerably to see his offspring in the hands of his greatest enemy. Then Paris wondered what the English Duc meant to do with Mademoiselle de Bonnard, and found no solution to the riddle. Paris shook its head, and thought that the ways of Avon were inscrutable and probably fiendish.
Meanwhile Lady Fanny swept through the town with Léonie, and saw to it that her social activities this season should not easily be forgotten. Léonie enjoyed herself very much, and Paris enjoyed her even more. In the mornings she rode out with Avon, and two factions sprang up thereafter amongst her admirers. One faction held that the divine Léonie was seen at her best in the saddle; the other faction was firm that in the ballroom she was incomparable. One excitable young gentleman challenged another on this score, but Hugh Davenant was present, and he took both young hotheads severely to task for bandying Léonie’s name about over their cups, and the affair came to naught.
Others tried to make love to Léonie, whereat she was angry, and turned a cold shoulder on their enthusiasms. She could be dignified when she chose, and her admirers were speedily abashed. Learning of their discomfiture one evening when she was helping Léonie to dress, Lady Fanny forgot herself, and exclaimed:
“Oh, splendidly done, my love! What a duchess you will make, to be sure!”
“A duchess, madame?” Léonie said. “How could I be that?”
Lady Fanny looked at her, and then at a new bracelet that lay on the table.
“Don’t tell me you don’t know, puss!”
Léonie was trembling now.
“Madame﻿—”
“Oh, my dear, he’s head over ears in love with you, as all the world must know! I have watched it grow, and﻿—my dearest life, there is no one I would sooner have for my sister than you, I do assure you!”
“Madame, you﻿—you must be mistaken!”
“Mistaken? I? Trust me to read the signs, my love! I have known Justin many years, and never have I seen him as he is now. Silly child, why does he give you all these jewels?”
“I﻿—I am his ward, madame.”
“Pooh!” My lady snapped her fingers. “A fig for that! Tell me why he made you his ward?”
“I﻿—I do not know, madame.

巴黎开始窃窃私语，起初只是私下里的耳语，渐渐声音越来越大，也越来越公开了。巴黎记起了一桩陈年旧日的丑闻，私下传言说这位英国公爵收养圣维尔的私生女，纯粹是为了报复当年的过节。巴黎认定，眼看着自己的亲生骨肉落入死敌的手心里，圣维尔心头必定备受煎熬。接着巴黎又暗自琢磨，这位英国公爵究竟打算如何处置德·博纳尔小姐，却百思不得其解。巴黎摇了摇头，一致认为阿翁公爵的行事手段深不可测，多半也狠毒如恶魔。
与此同时，范妮夫人携着莱昂妮席卷了整个巴黎社交界，确保她这个社交季的风采令人难以忘怀。莱昂妮过得十分快活，而巴黎上流社会也越发沉醉于她的魅力。清晨她常伴随阿翁骑马外出，她的倾慕者中随之分化成了两派人马（2派）。一派坚称，唯有策马飞驰时，才能领略这位绝色莱昂妮最动人的风韵；另一派则坚决认为，在舞厅里的她才是无与伦比的尤物。甚至有一名热血冲动的年轻绅士为此向另一人提出决斗，幸而休·达弗南特在场，严厉训斥了两个在酒后随意拿莱昂妮的名字寻衅闹事的愣头青，那场风波才不了了之。
另有一些人试图向莱昂妮大献殷勤，却惹得她恼怒万分，对他们的狂热嗤之以鼻、冷眼相对。只要她愿意，她随时能摆出极具威严的气派，让那些爱慕者立刻自惭形秽。一天晚上，范妮夫人在帮莱昂妮更衣时听说了这群追求者的狼狈下场，忍不住脱口惊叹道：
“噢，干得太漂亮了，心肝儿！你将来准是个无可挑剔的公爵夫人，这是明摆着的事！”

“Madame﻿—” Léonie pressed her hands together. “I am very ignorant, but I know﻿—I have heard what people say when such as Monseigneur wed﻿—wed ladies of no birth. I am only a tavern-keeper’s sister. Monseigneur could not marry me. I﻿—I had not thought of it.”
“ ’Tis I who am a fool to have put the idea into your head!” said Fanny remorsefully.
“Madame, I beg you will not say it to anyone.”
“Not I, child, but everyone knows that you have Avon in your toils.”
“I have not! I hate you when you talk like that!”
“Oh, my dear, we are but two women! What matter? Justin will count no cost, believe me. You may be born as low as you please, but will he care once he looks into your eyes?”
Léonie shook her head stubbornly.
“I know I am not a fool, madame. It would be a disgrace for him to marry me. One must be born.”
“Fiddle, child! If Paris accepts you without question shall not Avon too?”
“Madame, Monseigneur has no love for those who are lowborn. Many, many times I have heard him say so.”
“Never think of it, child.” Lady Fanny wished that she had not allowed her tongue to run away with her. “Come, let me tie your ribbons!” She bustled about Léonie, and presently whispered in her ear: “My sweet, do you not love him?”
“Oh, madame, madame, I have always loved him, but I did not think﻿—until you made me see﻿—”
“There, child, there! Do not cry, I implore you! You will make your eyes red.”
“I do not care about my eyes!” said Léonie, but she dried her tears, and permitted Lady Fanny to powder her face again.
When they went downstairs together Avon stood in the hall, and the sight of him brought the colour to Léonie’s cheeks. He looked at her closely.
“What ails you, infant?”
“Nothing, Monseigneur.”
He pinched her chin caressingly.
“It is the thought of your princely admirer that makes you blush, *ma fille*?”
Léonie recovered herself at this.
“Ah bah!” she said scornfully.
Condé was not present at Madame de Vauvallon’s rout that night, but there were many others who had come to see Léonie, and not a few who had come early in the hope of securing her hand for a dance.

“我自然不会嚼舌根，孩子，但人人都看得出你把阿翁攥在手心里了。”
“我才没有！您这么说话我真讨厌您！”
“哎呀，亲爱的，屋里就咱们两个女人！怕什么？贾斯汀行事向来不计代价，相信我。哪怕你出身再寒微，只要他望进你的眼眸，他难道还会在乎门第吗？”
莱昂妮倔强地摇了摇头。
“我知道自己并不傻，夫人。娶我对他而言将是奇耻大辱。名门望族必须讲究出身门第。”
“瞎扯，孩子！要是整个巴黎都对你心悦诚服、毫不挑剔，难道阿翁反倒不肯吗？”
“夫人，公爵大人最瞧不起卑贱之流。许多次，许多次我都亲耳听他这么说过。”
“别去胡思乱想了，孩子。”范妮夫人真后悔自己一时嘴快。“来，让我替你把丝带系好！”她在莱昂妮身边忙前忙后，片刻后贴着她的耳畔轻声低语：“心肝儿，难道你当真不爱他吗？”
“噢，夫人，夫人，我一直都深爱着他，可我从不敢多想……直到您方才点破……”
“好啦，孩子，好啦！别哭，我求你了！哭红了眼睛可不好看。”
“我才不在乎眼睛红不红呢！”莱昂妮抽泣着说，但她擦干了眼泪，任由范妮夫人重新替她在脸上扑匀香粉。
当两人一同走下楼梯时，阿翁正伫立在前厅里，一见到他，莱昂妮的面颊上顿时飞起两片绯红。他仔细打量着她。
“怎么啦，小家伙？”
“没什么，公爵大人。”
他亲昵地捏了捏她的小下巴。
“莫非是想起了那位向你求爱的亲王殿下，才害得你面红耳赤的，我的女儿？”
莱昂妮听到这话，立刻恢复了常态。
“哎呀啐！”她轻蔑地哼了一声。
那晚在德·沃瓦隆夫人的晚会上孔代亲王并未出席，但现场依然聚集了大批专程赶来瞻仰莱昂妮芳容的宾客，更有不少人早早到场，只盼能邀得她的玉手共跳一支舞。

===Original===

Avon arrived late, as ever, and Madame de Vauvallon, who had no daughters of marriageable age, greeted him with a laugh, and a gesture of despair.
“My friend, I have a score of young beaux who give me no peace until I promise to present them to *la petite*! Fanny, Marchérand is back! Let me find﻿—oh, la la! I should say choose﻿—a gallant for Léonie, and I’ll tell you the scandal! Come, little one!” She took Léonie’s hand, and led her into the room. “How you have set Paris by the ears! Were my daughters older I should be so jealous! Now child, who will you have to lead you out?”
Léonie looked round the room.
“I do not mind, madame. I will have﻿—Oh, oh, oh!” She let go Madame’s hand, and ran forward. “Milor’ Merivale, Milor’ Merivale!” she cried joyfully.
Merivale turned quickly.
“Léonie! Well, child, and how do you go on?” He kissed her hand. She was radiant. “I hoped I might see you here tonight.”
Madame de Vauvallon bore down upon them.
“Fie, what behaviour!” she said indulgently. “Is this your cavalier? Very well, *petite*. You need no introduction, it seems.” She smiled benignantly upon them, and went back to Fanny’s side.
Léonie tucked her hand in Merivale’s.
“M’sieur, I am very pleased to see you. Is Madame here too?”
“No, child, I am on one of my periodical visits. Alone. I won’t deny that I was drawn hither by certain rumours that reached us in London.”
She put her head on one side.
“What rumours, m’sieur?”
His smile grew.
“Faith, rumours of the *succès fou* that has been achieved by﻿—”
“Me!” she cried, and clapped her hands. “Milor’, I am *le dernier cri*! *Vraiment*, it is so! Lady Fanny says it is. *C’est ridicule, n’est-ce pas?*” She saw Avon coming towards them, and beckoned with pretty imperiousness. “Monseigneur, see whom I have found!”
“Merivale?” His Grace made a leg. “Now why?”
“We have heard things in London,” said Merivale. “Egad, I could not but come!”
“Oh, and we are very glad!” Léonie said enthusiastically.
His Grace offered Merivale snuff.
“Why, I believe my infant speaks for us all,” he said.
“Hey, is it you, Tony, or am I in my cups?” demanded a jovial voice. Lord Rupert came up, and wrung Merivale’s hand. “Where are you staying? When did you come?”
“Last night. I am with De Châtelet. And﻿—” he looked from one to the other﻿—“I am something anxious to hear what befell you all!”
“Ay, you were in our escapade, weren’t you?”

===Chinese===

阿翁一如既往姗姗来迟，而膝下并无适龄待嫁女儿的德·沃瓦隆夫人带着大笑与夸张的绝望姿态迎上前去。
“老友，我身边围了二十几个（20几个）年轻纨绔，软磨硬泡非逼着我把他们引见给这位‘小美人’不可！范妮，马谢朗回来了！等我给莱昂妮挑选……哎呀呀！我是说物色一位舞伴，回头我再跟你细聊那桩风流丑闻！来吧，小家伙！”她拉起莱昂妮的手领她入席，“你可真把全巴黎搅得神魂颠倒！要是我女儿们再年长几岁，我非得嫉妒死不可！来，孩子，你想让谁领你跳头一支舞？”
莱昂妮环视全场。
“我无所谓，夫人。我想选……噢，噢，天哪！”她挣脱夫人的手，欢天喜地地迎上前去。“梅里维尔勋爵，梅里维尔勋爵！”她欣喜若狂地喊道。
梅里维尔猛地转过身来。
“莱昂妮！好啊，孩子，近来一切可好？”他吻了吻她的手。她整个人光彩照人。“我今晚正盼着能在这儿撞见你呢。”
德·沃瓦隆夫人快步跟了过来。
“哎呀，这成何体统！”她纵容地嗔怪道，“这就是你的骑士啦？好吧，小美人。看来用不着我引见了。”她冲着两人慈爱地笑了笑，转身回到了范妮身旁。
莱昂妮把小手挽进梅里维尔的臂弯里。
“先生，见到您真叫人高兴。夫人也一道来了吗？”
“没有，孩子，我只是照例独自过来巡视一趟。一个人。不过我不否认，正是伦敦传来的某些风声把我给勾过来了。”
她歪着脑袋打量他。
“什么风声呀，先生？”
他的笑容更深了。
“老天作证，传闻说某位小姐在巴黎掀起了空前绝后的疯狂追捧……”
“就是我！”她拍手欢呼起来，“勋爵，我成了最时髦的风云人物了！真的，千真万确！范妮夫人也是这么说的。荒唐得很，不是吗？”她看见阿翁正朝他们缓步走来，便带着娇憨的威严招了招手。“公爵大人，快瞧瞧我找着了谁！”
“梅里维尔？”公爵大人长揖施礼，“哪阵风把你吹来了？”
“我们在伦敦听到了些风声，”梅里维尔说，“天哪，我怎能不过来亲眼瞧瞧！”
“噢，我们高兴坏了！”莱昂妮热情地附和道。
公爵大人向梅里维尔递过鼻烟盒。
“我想，我家小家伙的话正代表了我们大家的心声。”
“嘿，是你吗，托尼，还是我喝多了眼花？”一个快活洪亮的嗓门传来。鲁珀特勋爵大步流星赶来，紧紧握住梅里维尔的手。“你下榻在哪儿？什么时候抵京的？”
“昨晚刚到。我住在德·沙特莱公馆。而且……”他看看这个，又看看那个，“我正急着想听听你们这帮人的惊险奇遇呢！”
“是啊，当时那场逃亡你也在场，不是吗？”

===Original===

said Rupert. “Gad, what a chase! How does my friend﻿—stap me if I have not forgot his name again!﻿—Manvers! That’s the fellow! How does he?”
Merivale flung out a hand.
“I beg you’ll not mention that name to me!” he said. “All three of you fled the country, and faith, it’s as well you did!”
“I suggest we repair to the smaller salon,” Avon said, and led the way there. “I trust you were able to satisfy Mr. Manvers?”
Merivale shook his head.
“Nothing less than your blood is like to satisfy him,” he said. “Tell me all that happened to you.”
“In English,” drawled his Grace, “and softly.”
So once again the tale was told of Léonie’s capture and rescue. Then Madame de Vauvallon came in search of Léonie, and bore her away to dance with an ardent youth. Rupert wandered away to the cardroom.
Merivale looked at the Duke.
“And what does Saint-Vire say to Léonie’s success?” he inquired.
“Very little,” replied his Grace. “But he is not pleased, I fear.”
“She does not know?”
“She does not.”
“But the likeness is striking, Alastair. What says Paris?”
“Paris,” said his Grace, “talks in whispers. Thus my very dear friend Saint-Vire lives in some dread of discovery.”
“When do you intend to strike?”
Avon crossed his legs, and eyed one diamond shoe-buckle pensively.
“That, my dear Merivale, is still on the knees of the gods. Saint-Vire himself must supply the proof to my story.”
“It’s awkward, damned awkward!” Merivale commented. “You’ve no proof at all?”
“None.”
Merivale laughed.
“It does not seem to worry you!”
“No,” sighed his Grace, “no. I believe I can trap the Comte through his so charming wife. I play a waiting game, you see.”
“I am glad that I am not Saint-Vire. Your game must be torture to him.”
“Why, so I think,” agreed Avon pleasantly. “I am not anxious to put an end to his agonies.”
“You’re very vindictive!”
There was a moment’s silence; then Avon spoke.
“I wonder if you have realised to the full my friend’s villainy. Consider for a moment, I beg of you. What mercy would you show to a man who could condemn his own daughter to the life my infant has led?”
Merivale straightened in his chair.
“I know nothing of her life. It was bad?”
“Yes, my dear, it was indeed bad. Until she was twelve years old she, a Saint-Vire, was reared as a peasant. After that she lived among the canaille of Paris.

===Chinese===

鲁珀特说，“天哪，那场追逐战多惊险！我那位朋友——该死，我又把他的名字给忘了！——曼弗斯！对，就是那家伙！他近来可好？”
梅里维尔摊开双手。
“求你千万别在我面前提那尊瘟神！”他说，“你们三个全都溜之大吉跑出了国，不过老天作证，你们溜得正是时候！”
“我提议咱们移步小会客厅谈，”阿翁说着引路向前，“想必你设法安抚平息了曼弗斯先生的怒火吧？”
梅里维尔摇了摇头。
“除非饮下你们的鲜血，否则他绝难善罢甘休，”他说，“快跟我细说说你们后来的遭遇。”
“用英文讲，”公爵大人慢条斯理地叮嘱道，“声音放轻点。”
于是，莱昂妮被掳与获救的传奇历险又一次娓娓道来。不多时德·沃瓦隆夫人过来找寻莱昂妮，领着她去陪一位热切的年轻贵族跳舞。鲁珀特则溜达去了纸牌室。
梅里维尔望向公爵。
“眼见莱昂妮在社交界这般大获成功，圣维尔作何反应？”他询问道。
“绝口不提，”公爵大人回答，“不过我料想他心头肯定不是滋味。”
“她自己还蒙在鼓里？”
“她一无所知。”
“可那相貌实在太像了，阿拉斯泰尔。巴黎怎么议论？”
“巴黎嘛，”公爵大人说，“都在暗中窃窃私语。正因如此，我那位亲爱的挚友圣维尔如今终日活在秘密败露的惶恐之中。”
“你打算何时动手清算？”
阿翁翘起二郎腿，若有所思地凝视着自己鞋面上的一颗钻石鞋扣。
“那就要看冥冥之中神明的旨意了，我亲爱的梅里维尔。替我揭开真相提供确凿证据的，必须由圣维尔自己亲自出马。”
“这太棘手了，棘手得要命！”梅里维尔评价道，“你手里当真连一点证据都没有？”
“毫无证据。”
梅里维尔笑了起来。
“可你看起来一点都不发愁！”
“不发愁，”公爵大人叹道，“一点都不。我相信通过他那位高贵美丽的夫人，我定能将伯爵诱入罗网。你瞧，我玩的是以逸待劳的把戏。”
“我真庆幸自己不是圣维尔。你这出慢刀子割肉的把戏对他简直是无休止的酷刑折磨。”
“哎呀，我也是这么想的，”阿翁和颜悦色地表示赞同，“我可不急着让他从痛苦中解脱出来。”
“你可真够记仇狠毒的！”
室内沉默了片刻；随后阿翁再次开口：
“不知你是否当真领教过我那位挚友究竟有多丧心病狂。请你设身处地想一想吧。要是换作有人将自己的亲生骨肉推向我家小家伙曾经历过的那种暗无天日的人间炼狱，你还会对他讲半点慈悲宽恕吗？”
梅里维尔在椅子上坐直了身子。
“我对她以往的身世一无所知。当真那么凄惨？”
“是的，我的老兄，确实凄惨绝伦。直到十二岁（12岁）之前，她堂堂一个圣维尔家族的贵胄骨肉，一直被当成农家村姑抚养。而在那之后，她更是混迹在巴黎最下层的市井流氓之中。

===Original===

Conceive a tavern in a mean street, a bully for master, a shrew for mistress, and Vice, in all its lowest forms, under my infant’s very nose.”
“It must have been﻿—hell!” Merivale said.
“Just so,” bowed his Grace. “It was the very worst kind of hell, as I know.”
“The wonder is that she has come through it unscathed.”
The hazel eyes lifted.
“Not quite unscathed, my dear Anthony. Those years have left their mark.”
“It were inevitable, I suppose. But I confess I have not seen the mark.”
“Possibly not. You see the roguery, and the dauntless spirit.”
“And you?” Merivale watched him curiously.
“Oh, I see beneath, my dear! But then, I have had experience of the sex, as you know.”
“And you see﻿—what?”
“A certain cynicism, born of the life she has led; a streak of strange wisdom; the wistfulness behind the gaiety; sometimes fear; and nearly always the memory of loneliness that hurts the soul.”
Merivale looked down at his snuffbox, and fell to tracing the pattern on it with one finger.
“Do you know,” he said slowly, “I think that you have grown, Alastair?”
His Grace rose.
“Quite a reformed character, in fact,” he said.
“You can do no wrong in Léonie’s eyes.”
“No, it is most amusing, is it not?” Avon smiled, but there was bitterness in his smile, which Merivale saw.
Then they went back into the ballroom, and learned from Lady Fanny that Léonie had disappeared some time ago on Rupert’s arm, and had not since been seen.
She had indeed gone out with Rupert to a small salon where he brought her refreshment. Then had come towards them one Madame de Verchoureux, a handsome termagant who had been all things to Avon when Léonie had first come to him. She looked at Léonie with hatred in her eyes, and paused for a moment beside her couch.
Rupert came to his feet, and bowed. Madame swept a curtsy.
“It is﻿—Mademoiselle de Bonnard?” she said.
“Yes, madame.” Léonie got up, and curtsied also. “I am very stupid, but I cannot at once recall madame’s name.”
Rupert, supposing the lady to be one of Fanny’s friends, lounged back into the ballroom; Léonie was left looking up at Avon’s slighted mistress.
“I felicitate you, mademoiselle,” said the lady sarcastically. “You are more fortunate than I was, it seems.”
“Madame?” The sparkle was gone from Léonie’s eyes. “Have I the honour of madame’s acquaintance?”
“I am one Henriette de Verchoureux. You do not know me.”

===Chinese===

想想看吧，在狭窄阴暗的小巷里开着的一家下等小客栈，老板是个凶神恶煞的恶棍，老板娘是个泼妇，世间最下流肮脏的罪恶污秽，全都在我家小家伙眼皮子底下肆意翻滚。”
“那简直就是……地狱！”梅里维尔感叹道。
“正是如此，”公爵大人欠身道，“是世上最肮脏下流的无间地狱，这一点我深有体会。”
“难得的是，她居然能出淤泥而不染，完好无损地活到现在。”
那双淡褐色的眸子抬了起来。
“可算不上完好无损，我亲爱的安东尼。那些暗无天日的岁月在她身上留下了深刻的烙印。”
“我想这也是在所难免的。不过坦白说，我并未在她身上瞧出什么痕迹。”
“你自然瞧不出来。你所见到的，不过是她的机敏顽皮与无畏骨气罢了。”
“那你呢？”梅里维尔好奇地审视着他。
“噢，我能穿透皮囊看清内里，我的老兄！不过也是，正如你所知，我对女人向来阅历深厚。”
“那你都看到了……什么？”
“是那种在底层挣扎磨砺出的冷酷世故；是一抹异乎寻常的早熟精明；是在欢声笑语掩盖下的怅惘凄楚；有时是心惊胆战的恐惧；而更多时候，则是那种深入骨髓、刺痛灵魂的孤独印记。”
梅里维尔低头看着自己的鼻烟盒，伸手用一根指头描摹着上面的雕花纹路。
“你知道吗，”他慢条斯理地说道，“我觉得你整个人变深沉了，阿拉斯泰尔。”
公爵大人站起身来。
“事实上，简直成了洗心革面的典范，”他说。
“在莱昂妮眼中，你可是无论做什么都绝不会有错的神明。”
“是啊，有趣得很，不是吗？”阿翁微笑着，但笑容中透着一丝苦涩，被梅里维尔敏锐地捕捉到了。
随后他们移步走回舞厅，从范妮夫人口中得知，莱昂妮不久前挽着鲁珀特的臂弯离席去了偏厅，之后便一直没再露面。
其实她是同鲁珀特一道去了小休息室，鲁珀特替她端来了点心茶水。这时，一位名叫德·韦尔舒勒夫人的女子朝他们款款走来。那是一位容貌艳丽却性情泼辣的悍妇，早年在莱昂妮刚投奔阿翁门下时，她曾是阿翁极尽恩宠的情妇。她眼中满含着刻骨仇恨盯视着莱昂妮，在她的睡榻旁停下了脚步。
鲁珀特起身鞠躬行礼。夫人施了一个屈膝礼。
“这位就是……德·博纳尔小姐吧？”她说。
“正是，夫人。”莱昂妮站起身同样行礼，“真是不好意思，我一时想不起夫人的尊姓大名了。”
鲁珀特以为这位贵妇是范妮的某位闺中密友，便信步晃荡回了舞厅；屋里只剩下莱昂妮独自面对这位被阿翁冷落遗弃的昔日情妇。
“恭喜你啊，小姐，”那女人冷嘲热讽地说，“看来你比我当年要有福气得多呢。”
“夫人？”莱昂妮眸子里的神采收敛了，“不知我有幸认识夫人吗？”
“我是亨利埃特·德·韦尔舒勒。你自然不认得我。”

===Original===

“Pardon, madame, but I know of you﻿—much,” Léonie said swiftly. Madame had steered clear of open scandal, but she was somewhat notorious. Léonie remembered the days when Avon had visited her so often.
Madame flushed angrily.
“Indeed, mademoiselle. And of Mademoiselle de Bonnard is also known﻿—much. Mademoiselle is very clever, *sans doute*, but to those who know Avon the so strict chaperon is a poor disguise.”
Léonie raised her eyebrows.
“Is it possible that madame imagines that I have succeeded where she failed?”
“Insolent!” Madame’s hand clenched on her fan.
“Madame?”
Madame stared down at Youth, and knew the pangs of jealousy.
“Brazen it out!” she said shrilly. “You hope to marry in all honour, little fool, but be advised by me, and leave him, for Avon will wed no baseborn girl!”
Léonie’s eyelids flickered, but she said nothing. Madame changed her tactics suddenly, and stretched out her hand.
“My dear, I protest I pity you! You are so young; you do not know the ways of this world of ours. Avon would not be fool enough to wed with one of your blood, believe me. He were surely lost an he dared!” She laughed, covertly watching Léonie. “Even an English Duke would not be received were he wedded to such as you,” she said.
“*Tiens*, am I so base?” Léonie said with polite interest. “I think it is not possible that madame should have known my parents.”
Madame shot her a piercing look.
“Can it be that you do not know?” she asked, and flung back her head, and laughed again. “Have you not heard the whispers? Have you not seen that Paris watches you, and wonders?”
“But yes, madame, I know that I am quite the rage.”
“Poor child, is that all you know? Why, where is your mirror? Where are your eyes? Have you never looked at that fiery head of yours, never asked whence came your black brows and lashes? All Paris knows, and you are ignorant!”
“*Eh bien!*” Léonie’s heart beat fast, but she maintained her outward composure. “Enlighten me, madame! What does Paris know?”
“That you are a baseborn child of the Saint-Vire, my child. And we﻿—*nous autres*﻿—laugh to see Avon all unconsciously harbouring a daughter of his dearest enemy!”
Léonie was as white as her ruffle.
“You lie!”
Madame laughed tauntingly.
“Ask your fine father if I lie!” She gathered her skirts about her, and made a gesture of disdain. “Avon must know soon, and then what comes to you?

“恕我冒昧，夫人，关于您的事我听说过……不少呢，”莱昂妮反应敏捷地反唇相讥。这位夫人虽说极力避开公开的丑闻，但名声向来狼藉不堪。莱昂妮清晰地记得早年阿翁频繁造访她府邸的那些日子。
夫人恼羞成怒地涨红了脸。
“当真如此，小姐。关于德·博纳尔小姐的大名，外头传扬的也……不少呢。小姐确实好手段，毋庸置疑，但对于深知阿翁为人的人来说，找个古板严肃的女伴监护人，遮羞布未免也太拙劣了些。”
莱昂妮挑起眉毛。
“莫非夫人以为，凡是您当年折戟沉沙的地方，我都捷足先登大获成功了吗？”
“放肆无礼的贱人！”夫人手中的折扇骤然捏紧。
“夫人有何指教？”
夫人居高临下地盯着眼前青春焕发的少女，心头翻滚着嫉妒的毒焰。
“别在这儿硬撑门面了！”她尖声叫嚷道，“你这愚蠢的小蹄子，满心巴望着堂堂正正明媒正娶，可听我一句劝，趁早从他身边滚开吧，阿翁绝不可能娶一个私生贱种为妻！”
莱昂妮的眼睑微微颤动了一下，但她死死咬住唇一言不发。夫人突然话锋一转，伸手做出一副语重心长的姿态。
“亲爱的，我发誓我当真同情你！你还那么年轻，根本不懂我们这个上流社会的险恶规矩。相信我，阿翁绝不可能蠢到去迎娶一个流淌着你这种贱血的女人。他要是敢这么干，整个人生就彻底全毁了！”她冷笑着，暗中窥伺着莱昂妮的反应，“哪怕是一位英国公爵，要是娶了你这等下贱货色，整个上流社会也绝不会再容纳接纳他进门。”
“哎呀，难不成我当真就这般下贱？”莱昂妮带着礼貌的兴致反问道，“我想夫人绝无可能认得我生身父母吧。”
夫人射过来两道刺骨的目光。
“难不成你当真还蒙在鼓里？”她问，随即仰起头爆发出尖利的嗤笑，“难道你没听见外头的流言蜚语？难道你没瞧见全巴黎都在盯着你、暗中揣测？”
“这我知道，夫人，我知道自己如今红得发紫。”
“可怜的孩子，难不成你就只知道这个？瞧瞧，你的梳妆镜呢？你的眼睛长哪儿去了？难道你从没端详过你那一头火红的头发，从没纳闷过自己那双漆黑的睫毛浓眉究竟从何而来？全巴黎都心知肚明，唯独你跟个白痴一样一无所知！”
“好哇！”莱昂妮心口狂跳，但表面上依然强撑着波澜不惊，“那就请夫人赐教！巴黎到底知道些什么？”
“知道你是圣维尔那个老色鬼见不得光的私生杂种，我的孩子！而我们这些人——我们大家——每每瞧见阿翁浑然不觉地把死敌的亲生女儿养在自己屋檐下，肚子都快笑破了！”
莱昂妮的面色惨白如颈间的白蕾丝领褶。

===Original===

Little fool, best leave him now while you may do so of your own choice!” She was gone on the word, leaving Léonie to stand alone in the salon, her hands clasped together tightly, her face set and rigid.
Gradually she relaxed her taut muscles, and sank down again upon the couch, trembling. Her impulse was to seek shelter at Avon’s side, but she restrained herself, and stayed where she was. At first she was incredulous of Madame de Verchoureux’s pronouncement, but little by little she came to see the probability of the story’s truth. Saint-Vire’s attempt to kidnap her was thus explained, as was also the interest he had always taken in her. Sick disgust rose in her.
“*Bon Dieu*, what a father I have!” she said viciously. “Pig-person! Bah!”
Disgust gave way to a feeling of horror, and of fright. If Madame de Verchoureux had spoken the truth, Léonie could see the old loneliness stretching ahead, for it was clearly unthinkable that such a one as Avon could marry, or even adopt, a girl of her birth. He came of the nobility; she felt herself to be of mongrel blood. Lax he might be, but Léonie knew that if he married her he would disgrace the ancient name he bore. Those who knew him said that he would count no cost, but Léonie would count the cost for him, and because she loved him, because he was her seigneur, she would sacrifice everything sooner than drag him down in the eyes of his world.
She bit hard on her lip; it was better by far to think herself of peasant blood than a bastard daughter of Saint-Vire. Her world was toppling about her ears, but she rose up, and went back into the ballroom.
Avon came to her soon, and gave her his arm.
“I believe you are tired, my infant. We will find Lady Fanny.”
Léonie tucked her hand in his arm, and gave a little sigh.
“Monseigneur, let us go, and leave Lady Fanny, and Rupert. I do not want them.”
“Very well, infant.” Avon beckoned to Rupert across the room, and when he came to them, said languidly: “I am taking the child home, Rupert. Oblige me by waiting to escort Fanny.”
“I’ll take Léonie home,” offered Rupert with alacrity. “Fanny won’t come away for hours!”
“That is why I am leaving you to look to her,” said his Grace. “Come, *ma fille*.”

===Chinese===

愚蠢的小蹄子，趁着你眼下还能体面抽身，赶紧自谋出路离开他吧！”撂下这句话，她便扬长而去，留下莱昂妮独自僵立在偏厅里，双手死死绞在一起，整张面孔惨白僵硬。
渐渐地，她紧绷的肌肉松弛下来，浑身颤抖着跌坐回睡榻上。她本能地想立刻冲回阿翁身边寻求庇护，但理智硬生生扼制了冲动，迫使她留在原地。起初她对德·韦尔舒勒夫人这番恶毒的说辞将信将疑，但随着思绪抽丝剥茧，这桩身世的真实性逐渐浮出水面。圣维尔此前为何处心积虑要劫持绑架她，为何多年来始终对她格外留心，一切谜团在这一刻全都迎刃而解。一阵阵翻江倒海的恶心与反感涌上心头。
“老天爷啊，我怎么偏偏摊上这么个生父！”她恶狠狠地咒骂道，“一头彻头彻尾的猪猡！呸！”
反感随之被无边的恐惧与绝望所吞噬。倘若德·韦尔舒勒夫人所言非虚，莱昂妮仿佛望见了以往那种无边无际的凄苦孤独再次横亘在眼前——因为像阿翁公爵这般门第显赫的贵胄，无论如何也绝不可能娶她为妻，甚至绝无可能正式收养一个出身这般污秽见不得光的私生女。他出身名门望族，而她却觉得自己血管里流淌着杂种的脏血。他或许行事放浪不羁，但莱昂妮心知肚明，他若是娶了她，必将令他肩负的古老家族门第蒙受永世无法洗刷的奇耻大辱。深知他为人的人都说他行事向来不计代价，可莱昂妮必须替他掂量代价；正因为她深爱着他，正因为他是她心中至高无上、神明般的主人，她宁可粉身碎骨牺牲自己的一切，也绝不愿将他拖入名誉扫地的万劫不复之境。
她死死咬住下唇；哪怕自以为是贫贱农家的孤儿，也远远好过做圣维尔见不得光的私生杂种。她心中的天地已然轰然倒塌，但她依然挺直脊梁站起身，迈步重返喧嚣的舞厅。
不多时阿翁踱步向她走来，递出了自己的臂弯。
“我看你累坏了，我的小家伙。我们去找范妮夫人吧。”
莱昂妮将小手挽进他的臂弯，发出一声极轻微的叹息。
“公爵大人，咱们走吧，别管范妮夫人和鲁珀特了。我不想让他们跟着。”
“好吧，小家伙。”阿翁隔着人群向鲁珀特招了招手，待他走上前来，语气慵懒地吩咐道：“我带这孩子回府了，鲁珀特。劳驾你留在现场护送范妮归府。”
“我送莱昂妮回去吧，”鲁珀特自告奋勇地献殷勤，“范妮那疯性子，没几个钟头（数小时）根本拔不动腿！”
“所以我才留下你照应她，”公爵大人说，“走吧，我的女儿。”

He took Léonie home in his light town chaise, and during the short drive she forced herself to talk gaily of the rout they had left, of this man and that, and a thousand other trivialities. Arrived at the Hôtel Avon she went at once to the library. His Grace followed.
“Well, *ma mie*, what now?”
“Now it is just as it used to be,” Léonie said wistfully, and sat down on a low stool beside the Duke’s chair.
His Grace poured out a glass of wine, and looked down at Léonie with a questioning lift to his brows.
Léonie clasped her hands about her knees, and stared deep into the fire.
“Monseigneur, the Duc de Penthièvre was there tonight.”
“As I saw, infant.”
“You do not mind him, Monseigneur?”
“Not at all, infant. Why should I?”
“Well, Monseigneur, he is not﻿—he is not wellborn, is he?”
“On the contrary, child, his father was a royal bastard, and his mother a de Noailles.”
“That was what I meant,” said Léonie. “It does not matter that his father was a bastard prince?”
“*Ma fille*, since the Comte de Toulouse’s father was the King, it does not matter at all.”
“It would matter if his father were not the King, would it not? I think it is very strange.”
“It is the way of the world, infant. We forgive the peccadilloes of a king, but look askance on those of a commoner.”
“Even you, Monseigneur. And﻿—and you do not love those who are baseborn.”
“I do not, infant. I deplore the modern tendency to flaunt an indiscretion before the eyes of Society.”
Léonie nodded.
“Yes, Monseigneur.” She was silent for a moment. “M. de Saint-Vire was also there tonight.”
“I trust he did not seek to abduct you again?” His Grace spoke flippantly.
“No, Monseigneur. Why did he try to do it before?”
“Doubtless because of your *beaux yeux*, infant.”
“Bah, that is foolish! What was his real reason, Monseigneur?”
“My child, you make a great mistake in thinking me omniscient. You confuse me with Hugh Davenant.”
Léonie blinked.
“Does that mean that you do not know, Monseigneur?”
“Something of the sort, *ma fille*.”
She raised her head, and looked at him straightly.
“Do you suppose, Monseigneur, that he did it because he does not like you?”
“Quite possibly, infant. His motives need not worry us. May I now be permitted to ask you a question?”
“Yes, Monseigneur?”
“There was at the rout tonight a lady of the name of Verchoureux. Did you have speech with her?”
Léonie was gazing into the fire again.

“怎么啦，我的心肝儿，又闹哪一出？”
“如今这屋里又和从前一模一样了，”莱昂妮神色怅惘地喃喃自语，顺从地坐在公爵扶手椅旁的一张矮凳上。
公爵大人斟了一杯酒，挑起双眉，若有所思地垂首打量着莱昂妮。
莱昂妮双手环抱着双膝，定定地凝视着壁炉深处翻滚的烈火。
“公爵大人，今晚庞蒂埃夫勒公爵也在场呢。”
“我瞧见了，小家伙。”
“您并不介意他的出身，对不对，公爵大人？”
“半点都不介意，小家伙。我干嘛要介意？”
“可是，公爵大人，他并……算不上真正出身高贵，不是吗？”
“恰恰相反，孩子，他的生父是王室的私生子，而生母则是德·诺阿耶家族的千金小姐。”
“我指的就是这个，”莱昂妮说，“难道他父亲是个私生子亲王，就一点也不要紧吗？”
“我的女儿，图卢兹伯爵的生身父亲毕竟是尊贵的法兰西国王，自然一点都无伤大碍。”
“可要是生父不是国王，那可就大祸临头了，是不是？我觉得这规矩真够古怪的。”
“这就是人世间的炎凉世态，小家伙。对于帝王将相的风流荒唐，世人向来宽厚包容；可若是换作平民百姓，大家便要侧目鄙夷了。”
“连您也不例外，公爵大人。而且……您向来讨厌那些私生贱种。”
“确实如此，小家伙。我向来痛恨当今世道上那种大张旗鼓在上流社会面前卖弄风流失足的下流习气。”
莱昂妮用力点了点头。
“是的，公爵大人。”她沉默了片刻，“圣维尔先生今晚也在场。”
“但愿他今晚没打算再次下手劫持你吧？”公爵大人语气戏谑地调侃道。
“没有，公爵大人。他当初干嘛非要劫持我呀？”
“想必是被我家小家伙的美貌迷得神魂颠倒了吧。”
“啐，净胡扯！他真正的企图到底是什么呀，公爵大人？”
“我的孩子，你要是以为我无所不知，那可就大错特错了。你分明是把我同休·达弗南特给搞混了。”
莱昂妮眨巴着眼睛。
“这就是说，您其实也不知道喽，公爵大人？”
“差不多是这个意思吧，我的女儿。”
她扬起头，目光直直地望进他的眼眸。
“公爵大人，您猜……他干出这等勾当，会不会纯粹是因为他视您为死敌？”
“大有这种可能，小家伙。他的卑劣动机用不着我们操心。现在容许我也问你一个小问题吧？”
“什么问题呀，公爵大人？”
“今晚晚会上有一位名叫韦尔舒勒的夫人。你同她交谈过吗？”
莱昂妮再次将目光投向了熊熊燃烧的壁炉。

## XVIII The Indignation of Mr. Manvers / 曼弗斯先生义愤填膺
===Original===

Madam Field was worried, for it was after six in the evening and neither Léonie nor Rupert had returned. Considerably flustered at length, Madam sent a messenger to Merivale to inquire whether the truants were there. Half an hour later the lackey returned, with Merivale riding beside him. Merivale went swiftly to the withdrawing-room, and as soon as he entered Madam Field sprang up.
“Oh, Lord Merivale! Oh, and have you brought the child home? I have been in such a taking, for I never saw her after eleven in the morning, or maybe ’twas later, or perhaps a little earlier﻿—I cannot say for sure. And never a sign of Rupert, so I thought mayhap they were with you﻿—”
Merivale broke into the flood of words.
“I’ve not seen either of them since this morning when Rupert set out to come here,” he said.
Madam’s jaw dropped. She let fall her fan, and began to cry.
“Oh dear, oh dear, and Justin telling me to have a care to her! But how could I tell, for sure ’twas his own brother! Oh, my lord, can they﻿—can they have eloped?”
Merivale laid his hat and whip on the table.
“Eloped? Nonsense, madam! Impossible!”
“She was ever a wild piece,” wept Madam. “And Rupert so scatterbrained! Oh, what shall I do, my lord? What shall I do?”
“Pray, madam, dry your tears!” begged Merivale. “I am convinced there’s naught so serious in this as an elopement. For God’s sake, madam, calm yourself.”
But Madam, to his dismay, went into a fit of the vapours. My lord turned to the servant.
“Ride back to Merivale, my man, and request my lady to join me here,” he ordered, with an uneasy eye on the prostrate lady. “And﻿—and send madam’s abigail here! Mayhap the children are playing some trick on us,” he muttered to himself. “Madam, I beg you will not alarm yourself unduly!”
Madam Field’s maid came running with salts and presently the lady recovered somewhat, and lay upon the couch calling on heaven to witness that she had done her best. To all Merivale’s questions she could only reply that she had had no notion of such wickedness, and what Justin would say she dared not think. Came my Lady Merivale, in her chaise, and was ushered into the withdrawing-room.
“Madam! Why, madam, what is this? Anthony, have they not returned? Fie, they are trying to frighten us! Depend upon it, that is it! Never fret, madam, they’ll return soon.” She went to the agitated chaperon, and began to chafe her hands.

===Chinese===

菲尔德夫人心急如焚，因为天色已过了傍晚六点（6点），可莱昂妮和鲁珀特依然踪影全无。夫人终于慌了手脚，派出一名信使赶往梅里维尔府，打听这两个逃学玩耍的年轻人是不是躲在那儿。半个小时后（30分钟后），男仆折返回来，梅里维尔骑马伴随在侧。梅里维尔快步踱进小客厅，一见他进门，菲尔德夫人便猛地弹跳起身。
“哎呀，梅里维尔勋爵！天哪，您把那孩子送回来了吗？我这一整天都快急疯了，因为打从上午十一点（11点）起我就再没见过她的人影，或许比那更晚，又或许还要早一点——我也说不准。而且鲁珀特也连个影儿都没有，所以我想着他们多半是去投奔您了……”
梅里维尔出言打断了她这番滔滔不绝的话潮。
“打从今天早晨鲁珀特动身来这里起，我就再也没见过他们俩，”他说。
夫人的下巴猛地耷拉下来。手中的折扇啪嗒掉在地上，当场失声啼哭起来。
“哎呀老天，天哪，贾斯汀临走前千叮咛万嘱咐叫我照看好她！可我哪能料到会出这种事，毕竟那是他亲弟弟啊！噢，勋爵，他们……他们该不会私奔了吧？”
梅里维尔将帽子和马鞭放在桌上。
“私奔？荒唐透顶，夫人！绝无可能！”
“她向来就是个野丫头，”夫人泣不成声，“而鲁珀特又是那么个少不更事的冒失鬼！天哪，我该怎么办啊，勋爵？我到底该怎么办？”
“求求您，夫人，快擦干眼泪吧！”梅里维尔央求道，“我笃定绝不会是私奔这等严重的事。看在老天的份上，夫人，请您镇定点。”
然而令他手足无措的是，夫人身子一歪，神经衰弱的毛病当场发作了。勋爵转头看向身旁的仆人。
“立刻骑马回梅里维尔府，请我夫人赶过来，”他吩咐道，同时不安地瞥了一眼瘫在沙发上的贵妇，“还有……快把夫人的贴身侍女叫来！兴许这两个小家伙只是在跟我们恶作剧呢，”他暗自喃喃自语，“夫人，我恳求您切莫这般自乱阵脚！”
菲尔德夫人的侍女捧着嗅盐飞奔而来，不多时夫人稍稍缓过气来，斜倚在睡榻上，口口声声指天发誓自己已经尽职尽责。面对梅里维尔抛出的种种疑问，她除了哭诉自己做梦也想不到会发生这等罪过、贾斯汀要是知道了会怎样她连想都不敢想之外，再也说不出半句有用的话来。就在这时，梅里维尔夫人乘着轻便马车赶到了，被径直引进了小客厅。
“夫人！哎呀，夫人，这是怎么闹的？安东尼，他们还没回来吗？嗨，这两个孩子准是存心吓唬咱们呢！放宽心，肯定是这样！别发愁了，夫人，他们很快就会回来的。”她走到心急如焚的女监护人身旁，轻轻揉搓着她冰凉的手。

“Pray, madam, hush. It’s no such great matter, I am sure. Mayhap they have lost their way somewhere, for they are out riding, you may be sure.”
“My dear, Rupert knows every inch of the country,” Merivale said quietly. He turned again to the lackey. “Be good enough to send to the stables and see whether my lord and Mistress Léonie have taken the horses.”
Ten minutes later the man returned with the news that Lord Rupert’s horse was in a loose box, and had been there all day. Whereupon Madam had a fresh attack of the vapours, and Merivale frowned.
“I don’t understand this,” he said. “If they had eloped﻿—”
“Oh, Anthony, can they have done that?” Jennifer cried aghast. “Oh no, surely! Why, the child can think of no one but the Duke, and as for Rupert﻿—”
“Listen!” said my lord sharply, and raised his hand.
Outside they heard horses, and the scrunch of wheels on the gravel. Madam started up.
“Heaven be praised, they have come back!”
With one accord Anthony and Jennifer deserted the ailing lady, and hurried into the hall. The great front-door stood open, and into the house stepped his Grace of Avon, elegant in a coat of fine purple velvet, laced with gold, a many-caped greatcoat, over all, worn carelessly open, and polished top-boots on his feet. He paused on the threshold and raised his eyeglass to survey the Merivales.
“Dear me!” he said languidly. “An unexpected honour. Your ladyship’s devoted servant.”
“Oh lord!” said Merivale, for all the world like a rueful boy.
His Grace’s lips quivered, but Jennifer blushed fiery red. Merivale went forward.
“You must deem this an unwarranted intrusion, Duke,” he began stiffly.
“Not at all,” bowed his Grace. “I am charmed.”
Merivale returned the bow.
“I was summoned to Madam Field’s assistance,” he said. “Otherwise I should not be here, believe me.”
Leisurely the Duke divested himself of his greatcoat, and shook out his ruffles.
“But shall we not repair to the withdrawing-room?” he suggested. “You are saying, I think, that you came to my cousin’s assistance?” He led the way to the withdrawing-room, and bowed them in. Madam Field, seeing him, gave a shriek, and fell back upon her cushions.
“Oh, mercy, ’tis Justin!” she cried.
Jennifer went to her.
“Hush, madam! Calm yourself!”
“You appear to be strangely afflicted, cousin,” remarked his Grace.
“Oh Justin﻿—oh cousin! I have no notion! So innocent they seemed!

“亲爱的，鲁珀特对这方圆几里的每一寸土地都了如指掌，”梅里维尔语气沉稳地说。他重新转向男仆，“劳驾去马厩瞧瞧，看看勋爵和莱昂妮小姐到底有没有牵马出去。”
十分钟后（10分钟后），仆人回禀道，鲁珀特勋爵的马匹整整一天都在马厩的单间里好端端拴着。闻听此言，夫人神经衰弱的毛病再次剧烈发作，梅里维尔眉头紧锁。
“这我就想不通了，”他说，“要是他们真打算私奔……”
“噢，安东尼，他们怎么可能干出这种事来？”珍妮弗大惊失色地惊呼道，“噢绝不会，当真不会！那孩子满脑子除了公爵就容不下旁人，至于鲁珀特嘛……”
“嘘，听！”勋爵语气陡然一凛，抬起手示意肃静。
门外传来了马蹄声，以及车轮碾压砂石路面的清脆声响。夫人猛地惊起。
“谢天谢地，他们总算回来了！”
安东尼与珍妮弗不约而同撇下这位抱恙的贵妇，快步迎向前厅。厚重的前门敞开着，迈步踏进门槛的，赫然是阿翁公爵大人——他身着一袭镶着金滚边的上等紫色天鹅绒外套，外罩一件随意敞开的多层披肩大氅，脚蹬一双擦得锃亮的高筒马靴，尽显高贵典雅。他在门槛前驻足，优雅地举起单片眼镜打量着梅里维尔夫妇。

“噢天哪！”梅里维尔嘟囔了一句，神情活像个自惭形秽的大男孩。
公爵大人的唇角微微抽动了一下，珍妮弗则羞得满面绯红。梅里维尔迎上前去。

“哪里的话，”公爵大人躬身致意，“我深感荣幸。”
梅里维尔回了一礼。
“我是接到菲尔德夫人的求援才匆匆赶来的，”他说，“否则我绝不会冒昧登门，请您相信。”
公爵从容不迫地解下大氅，抖了抖领口的蕾丝褶边。

“噢，慈悲的上苍啊，是贾斯汀！”她哭喊道。
珍妮弗快步走上前去。
“快别出声了，夫人！冷静点！”
“表姐，你看起来似乎受了不小的惊吓呢，”公爵大人随口评价道。
“噢贾斯汀……噢表弟！我完全不知情啊！他们俩平时看着那么天真无邪！

I can scarce believe﻿—”
“Innocent! Of course they were!” snorted Merivale. “Have done with this elopement foolery! It’s mere child’s talk!”
“Oh Anthony, do you think so indeed?” said Jennifer thankfully.
“I do not wish to seem importunate,” said the Duke, “but I should like an explanation. Where, may I ask, is my ward?”
“That,” said Merivale, “is the very root of the matter.”
The Duke stood very still.
“Indeed!” he said softly. “Pray continue. Cousin, I must request you to cease your lamentations.”
Madam’s noisy sobs abated. She clutched Jennifer’s hand and sniffed dolefully.
“I know nothing more than this,” said Merivale. “She and Rupert have been absent since eleven of the clock this morning.”
“Rupert?” said his Grace.
“I should have told you that Rupert has been staying with us these past three weeks.”
“You amaze me,” said Avon. His eyes were as hard as agates. He turned, and put his snuffbox down on the table. “The mystery would seem to be solved,” he said evenly.
“Sir!” It was Jennifer who spoke. His Grace looked at her indifferently. “If you are thinking that﻿—that they have eloped, I am sure﻿—oh, I am sure that ’tis not so! Such a notion was never in either of their heads!”
“So?” Avon looked from one to the other. “Pray enlighten me!”
Merivale shook his head.
“Faith, I cannot. But I would stake mine honour that there’s been no thought of love between them. They are the veriest children and even now I suspect they may be playing a trick on us. More than that﻿—” He paused.
“Yes?” said Avon.
Jennifer broke in.
“Sir, the child can talk of no one but yourself!” she said impetuously. “You have all her﻿—her adoration!”
“So I thought,” answered Avon. “But one may be mistaken. I believe there is a saying that youth will to youth.”
“It’s no such thing,” Merivale averred. “Why, they are forever quarrelling! Moreover they have taken no horses. Mayhap they are hiding somewhere to frighten us.”
A footman came to them.
“Well?” Avon spoke without turning his head.
“Mr. Manvers, your Grace, who desires speech with my Lord Rupert.”
“I have not the pleasure of Mr. Manvers’ acquaintance,” said the Duke, “but you may admit him.”
Entered a little wiry gentleman with red cheeks and bright, angry eyes. He glared at the assembled company, and, singling out the Duke, rapped forth a question.
“Are you Lord Rupert Alastair, sir?”
“I am not,” said his Grace.

“噢安东尼，你当真这么认为吗？”珍妮弗如释重负地说。
“我无意显得咄咄逼人，”公爵大人开口道，“但我很想听个合理解释。冒昧问一句，我的受监护人究竟去哪儿了？”
“那，”梅里维尔说，“正是眼下问题的症结所在。”
公爵静静伫立在原地，纹丝不动。
“当真如此！”他轻柔地说道，“请继续讲下去。表姐，我必须请你收起你的哭天抹泪。”
夫人喧闹的抽泣声收敛了些。她紧紧攥着珍妮弗的手，可怜巴巴地吸着鼻子。
“我所知道的也不过是这些，”梅里维尔说，“她和鲁珀特打从今天上午十一点（11点）起便不知所踪。”
“鲁珀特？”公爵大人挑起眉毛。
“我本该先告诉你，鲁珀特过去三周（3周）一直住在我们府上。”
“真令我惊讶，”阿翁说。他的目光冰冷坚硬得宛如玛瑙。他转过身，将鼻烟盒轻轻放在桌上。“看来谜底似乎揭晓了，”他语气平稳地说道。
“先生！”珍妮弗忍不住开口。公爵神情淡漠地望向她。“如果您是认定……他们俩私奔了，我敢发誓……噢，我敢拿性命担保绝非如此！他们俩脑子里绝不可能有这种念头！”
“是吗？”阿翁目光在两人之间流转，“那就请二位不吝赐教了！”
梅里维尔摇了摇头。
“说真的，我也说不清。但我敢拿我的名誉做担保，他们之间绝没有半点儿女私情。他们简直就是一对彻头彻尾的顽童，哪怕到了这一刻，我都怀疑他们八成是在跟咱们搞恶作剧。更何况……”他欲言又止。
“更何况什么？”阿翁问。
珍妮弗急忙插话：
“先生，那孩子满嘴念叨的除了您就再没别人！”她急切地表白道，“她满心装的……全是对您的崇拜与爱慕！”
“我原本也是这么想的，”阿翁回答，“但人难免会有看走眼的时候。我想民间向来有句俗话，叫作少年自与少年亲。”
“绝无此事，”梅里维尔言之凿凿地断言，“他们俩成天拌嘴吵架！况且他们根本没带走任何马匹。八成是躲在哪个角落里存心吓唬咱们呢。”
一名男仆快步走了进来。
“何事？”阿翁头也不回地发问。
“启禀公爵大人，门外有一位曼弗斯先生，执意要求见鲁珀特勋爵。”
“我素来没有结识曼弗斯先生的荣幸，”公爵说，“不过你可以放他进来。”
进门的是一位个头矮小干练的绅士，两颊涨得通红，眼中闪烁着愤怒的光芒。他怒目环视在场的所有人，随即目光锁定了公爵，劈头盖脸厉声质问道：
“先生，你就是鲁珀特·阿拉斯泰尔勋爵吗？”
“我不是，”公爵大人答道。

The irate little man rounded on Merivale.
“You, sir?”
“My name is Merivale,” Anthony replied.
“Then where is Lord Rupert Alastair?” demanded Mr. Manvers, in a voice of baffled rage.
His Grace took snuff.
“That is what we should all like to know,” he said.
“Damme, sir, do you think to play with me?” fumed Mr. Manvers.
“I have never played with anyone,” said the Duke.
“I am come here to find Lord Rupert Alastair! I demand speech with him! I want an explanation of him!”
“My dear sir,” said Avon. “Pray join our ranks! We all want that.”
“Who the devil are you?” cried the exasperated little man.
“Sir,” bowed his Grace. “I believe I *am* the devil. So they say.”
Merivale was shaken with silent laughter. Mr. Manvers turned to him.
“Is this a madhouse?” he asked. “Who is he?”
“He is the Duke of Avon,” said Merivale unsteadily.
Mr. Manvers pounced on Avon again.
“Ah! Then you are Lord Rupert’s brother!” he said vindictively.
“My misfortune, sir, believe me.”
“What I demand to know is this!” said Mr. Manvers. “*Where is my roan?*”
“I haven’t the least idea,” said his Grace placidly. “I am not even sure that I know what you are talking about.”
“Faith, I am sure I don’t!” chuckled Merivale.
“My roan horse, sir! Where is it? Answer me that!”
“I fear you will have to hold me excused,” said the Duke. “I know nothing about your horse. In fact, I am not, at the moment, interested in your horse﻿—roan or otherwise.”
Mr. Manvers raised his fists heavenwards.
“Interested in it!” he spluttered. “My horse has been stolen!”
“You have all my sympathy,” yawned his Grace. “But I fail to see what concern it is of mine.”
Mr. Manvers thumped the table.
“Stolen, sir, by your brother, Lord Rupert Alastair, this very day!”
His words brought about a sudden silence.
“Continue!” requested his Grace. “You interest us now exceedingly. Where, when, how, and why did Lord Rupert steal your horse?”
“He stole it in the village, sir, this morning! And I may say, sir, that I consider it a gross impertinence! A piece of insolence that infuriates me! I am a calm man, sir, but when I receive such a message from a man of birth, of title﻿—”
“Oh, he left a message, did he?” interposed Merivale.
“With the blacksmith, sir! My groom rode over on the roan to the village, and the horse casting a shoe, he took him to the smith, very properly!

这位暴跳如雷的矮个子绅士猛地扭头转向梅里维尔。

公爵大人优雅地吸了口鼻烟。
“那也正是我们大伙急于知晓的，”他说。
“该死，先生，你莫非存心拿我消遣？”曼弗斯先生暴跳如雷。
“我从不拿任何人消遣，”公爵说。
“我特地赶来这里找鲁珀特·阿拉斯泰尔勋爵！我必须同他当面理论！我要求他给我一个合理的交代！”
“我亲爱的先生，”阿翁说，“那何不加入我们的行列呢？我们所有人都盼着这个交代呢。”
“你到底算哪门子见鬼的人物？”被逼到抓狂的矮个子男人大叫道。
“先生，”公爵大人躬身施礼，“我相信我正是鬼怪恶魔本尊。世人向来这么说。”
梅里维尔被逗得浑身发颤，死死憋着笑。曼弗斯先生转向他。
“这里难道是疯人院吗？”他问，“他到底是何方神圣？”
“他是阿翁公爵大人，”梅里维尔强忍着笑意、声音发颤地回答。
曼弗斯先生再次猛扑向阿翁。
“啊！这么说你是鲁珀特勋爵的亲哥哥了！”他恶狠狠地咬牙道。
“请相信我，先生，这正是我的不幸所在。”
“我现在必须弄个水落石出的只有一件事！”曼弗斯先生咆哮道，“我的花斑红马究竟在哪儿？”
“我一点也不知道，”公爵大人心平气和地答道，“我甚至拿不准自己是否听懂了阁下在谈论些什么。”
“说真的，我反正是半点没听懂！”梅里维尔吃吃笑道。
“我的花斑红马，先生！它在哪儿？快回答我！”
“我恐怕阁下不得不恕我无可奉告了，”公爵说，“我对你的马一无所知。事实上，眼下我对你的马——不论是花斑红马还是别的什么毛色——半点兴致都没有。”
曼弗斯先生愤怒地向天举起双拳。
“半点兴致都没有！”他气得浑身哆嗦，“我的马被人偷了！”
“我深表同情，”公爵大人打了个哈欠，“但我委实看不出这同我有何相干。”
曼弗斯先生重重捶了一下桌子。
“正是令弟，鲁珀特·阿拉斯泰尔勋爵，就在今天干的卑劣勾当，先生！”
他的话如同一记惊雷，让满屋顿时陷入死一般的沉寂。
“请继续讲下去！”公爵大人吩咐道，“你现在总算成功勾起了我们的极大兴致。鲁珀特勋爵究竟是在何处、何时、以何种手段，又因何故偷走了你的宝马？”
“他今天上午就在村子里偷走的，先生！而且不妨直说，先生，我认为这是彻头彻尾的奇耻大辱！这等放肆无礼的行径让我怒火中烧！我平日里是个极沉得住气的人，先生，可当我从一个名门贵胄、堂堂勋爵嘴里收到这等口信时……”
“哦，这么说他还留下了口信？”梅里维尔插话道。
“留在了铁匠铺里，先生！我的马夫骑着那匹花斑红马进村，碰巧马蹄铁脱落了，马夫便极守规矩地牵去送给铁匠打蹄铁！

While Coggin was shoeing the animal my fellow walked on to Fawley to execute my commands.” He breathed heavily. “When he returned, the horse was gone! The smith﻿—damn him for a fool!﻿—tells me that Lord Rupert insisted on taking the horse﻿—*my* horse, sir!﻿—and left his compliments for me, and his﻿—his *thanks* for the loan of my horse!”
“Very proper,” said his Grace.
“Damme, sir, it’s monstrous!”
A gurgling laugh came from Jennifer.
“Oh, was there ever such a boy?” she cried. “What in the world should he want with your horse, sir?”
Mr. Manvers scowled at her.
“Exactly, madam! Exactly! What did he want with my horse? The man’s mad, and should be clapped up! Coggin tells me he came running into the village like one demented, with no hat on his head! And not one of those gaping fools had the sense to stop him from seizing my horse! A set of idiots, sir!”
“I can well believe it,” said Avon. “But I do not yet see how your information can help us.”
Mr. Manvers fought with himself.
“Sir, I am not come here to help you!” he raged. “I have come to demand my horse!”
“I would give it you had I it in my possession,” said his Grace kindly. “Unfortunately Lord Rupert has your horse.”
“Then I want its recovery!”
“Do not distress yourself!” Avon advised him. “No doubt he will return it. What I wish to know is, why did Lord Rupert want your horse, and where did he go?”
“If that dolt of a landlord is to be believed,” said Mr. Manvers, “he has gone to Portsmouth.”
“Fleeing the country, evidently,” murmured his Grace. “Was there a lady with Lord Rupert?”
“No, there was not! Lord Rupert went off at a disgraceful pace in pursuit of a coach, or some such nonsense.”
The Duke’s eyes widened.
“Almost I begin to see daylight,” he said. “Proceed.”
Merivale shook his head.
“I’m all at sea,” he confessed. “The mystery grows.”
“On the contrary,” his Grace replied gently. “The mystery is very nearly solved.”
“I don’t understand you﻿—any of you!” exploded Mr. Manvers.
“That was not to be expected,” said Avon. “Lord Rupert, you say, went to Portsmouth in pursuit of a coach. Who was in that coach?”
“Some damned Frenchman, Fletcher said.”
Merivale started; so also did Jennifer.
“Frenchman?” Merivale echoed. “But what did Rupert﻿—”
His Grace was smiling grimly.
“The mystery,” he said, “is solved. Lord Rupert, Mr. Manvers, borrowed your horse to go in pursuit of M. le Comte de Saint-Vire.”

“该死，先生，这简直是无法无天！”
珍妮弗忍不住发出一阵咯咯娇笑。
“哎呀，天底下哪有这般胡闹的毛头小伙？”她失声叫道，“先生，他到底发了什么疯非要骑您的马呀？”
曼弗斯先生冲她怒目而视。
“问得好，夫人！问得太切中要害了！他干嘛非要骑我的马不可？那家伙准是疯了，该被立刻关进疯人院！科金告诉我，他连帽子都没戴，跟中了邪的疯子一样一路狂奔冲进村子！而那帮伸长脖子发呆的蠢货居然没有一个有脑子去拦下他抢我的马！全是一群无可救药的白痴，先生！”
“这我完全相信，”阿翁说，“但我目前依然看不出你这番高见能对我们有何裨益。”
曼弗斯先生极力克制着胸中的怒火。
“先生，我登门可不是为了给你们效劳的！”他咆哮道，“我是来讨回我的马的！”
“要是马在我手里，我自当奉还，”公爵大人和蔼可亲地说，“遗憾的是，你的马眼下在鲁珀特勋爵手里。”
“那我就要立刻把它追回来！”
“别急躁！”阿翁奉劝道，“他迟早会归还的。我现在想弄清楚的是，鲁珀特勋爵因何非要借你的马不可，他又骑着它去了何处？”
“若是那个笨蛋客栈掌柜的话还能信得过的话，”曼弗斯先生说，“他是往朴次茅斯的方向去了。”
“显然是要逃离出国，”公爵轻声呢喃，“鲁珀特勋爵身旁可曾伴有一位女士？”
“没有，绝无旁人！鲁珀特勋爵以一种伤风败俗的疯狂速度在追赶一辆马车，反正是这等疯疯癫癫的鬼把戏。”
公爵双眸微微睁大。
“事情似乎开始露出端倪了，”他说，“请继续说。”
“我完全被搞糊涂了，”梅里维尔承认道，“谜团反而越滚越大了。”
“恰恰相反，”公爵大人温和地回答，“谜底已然呼之欲出了。”
“我不明白你们……你们所有人的疯话！”曼弗斯先生暴跳如雷。
“这本就怨不得你，”阿翁说，“你说鲁珀特勋爵前往朴次茅斯是为了追赶一辆马车。那辆马车里坐的是谁？”
“弗莱彻说是个遭天谴的法国人。”
梅里维尔浑身一震；珍妮弗也是花容失色。
“法国人？”梅里维尔失声反问，“可鲁珀特究竟……”
公爵大人的嘴角浮现出一抹冷酷的微笑。
“谜底，”他说，“揭晓了。曼弗斯先生，鲁珀特勋爵之所以借走阁下的宝马，是为了去追击圣维尔伯爵大人。”

===Original===

Merivale gasped.
“You knew he was here, then?”
“I did not.”
“Then how a’ God’s name﻿—?”
Again the Duke took snuff.
“Shall we say﻿—intuition, my dear Anthony?”
“But﻿—but why did Rupert pursue Saint-Vire? And﻿—and what was Saint-Vire doing on the road to Portsmouth? He told me he was journeying north to visit a friend! This goes beyond me!”
“What I want to know,” Jennifer said, “is, where is Léonie?”
“Ay, that’s the question,” nodded Merivale.
“Your pardon, sir,” interjected Mr. Manvers, “but the question is, where is my horse?”
They turned to the Duke for enlightenment.
“Léonie,” said the Duke, “is by now on the way to France, in company with the Comte de Saint-Vire. Rupert, I imagine, is also on his way to France, for I do not suppose he was in time to intercept them. Mr. Manvers’ horse is in all probability at Portsmouth. Unless, of course, Rupert has taken it to France with him.”
Mr. Manvers collapsed into the nearest chair.
“Taken﻿—taken my horse to France, sir? Oh, it’s monstrous! It’s monstrous!”
“For God’s sake, Avon, be more explicit!” begged Merivale. “Why has Saint-Vire run off with Léonie? He had not even seen her!”
“On the contrary,” said Avon, “he has seen her many times.”
Jennifer rose to her feet.
“Oh sir, he will not harm her?”
“No, he will not harm her, my lady,” Avon replied, and there was a glint in his eyes. “You see, there will be no time for that. He has Rupert hard on his heels﻿—and me.”
“You’ll go?”
“Of course I shall go. Follow my example, and place your trust in Rupert. It seems I shall live to be grateful to him yet.”
“Alastair, what in God’s name does all this mean?” demanded Merivale. “Rupert himself swore there was a mystery as soon as he saw Léonie’s likeness to Saint-Vire.”
“So Rupert saw that? I appear to have underrated Rupert’s intelligence. I believe I can satisfy your curiosity. Come with me into the library, my dear Merivale.”
Past enmity was forgotten. Anthony went to the door. Mr. Manvers sprang up.
“But all this doesn’t help me to my horse!” he said bitterly.
With his hand on the door Avon paused, and looked back.
“My good sir,” he said haughtily, “I am weary of your horse. It has served its turn, and shall be restored to you.” He went out with Merivale, and shut the door behind him. “So. One moment, Anthony. Johnson!”
The butler came forward.
“Your Grace?”

梅里维尔倒吸了一口凉气。

“我并不知情。”
“那看在老天的份上，你究竟是如何推断出来的……？”
公爵大人再次优雅地吸了口鼻烟。
“莫非该归功于……直觉吧，我亲爱的安东尼？”
“可……可鲁珀特干嘛要去追杀圣维尔？圣维尔又为何会出现在前往朴次茅斯的官道上？他分明亲口告诉我他是要北上拜访挚友的！这简直匪夷所思！”
“我只想知道一件事，”珍妮弗急切地开口，“莱昂妮究竟身在何处？”
“是啊，那才是关键所在，”梅里维尔连连点头。
“恕我冒昧插一句，先生，”曼弗斯先生忿忿不平地打断道，“眼下最要紧的关键，是我的马究竟身在何处！”
他们齐齐将目光投向公爵，寻求指点迷津。
“莱昂妮，”公爵说，“此刻八成已经与圣维尔伯爵一道在前往法国的海路上了。而鲁珀特，我料想多半也正在渡海赴法途中，因为我想他大概没能赶在他们登船前将人截下。至于曼弗斯先生的宝马，多半安然留在朴次茅斯。当然，除非鲁珀特顺手牵羊把它也一并带去法国了。”
曼弗斯先生颓然瘫坐在最近的一张椅子里。
“带……把我的马带去法国了，先生？噢，这简直骇人听闻！骇人听闻！”
“看在老天的份上，阿翁，快把话挑明了吧！”梅里维尔央求道，“圣维尔平白无故为何要拐走莱昂妮？他甚至压根没见过她！”
“恰恰相反，”阿翁说，“他见过她无数次了。”
珍妮弗霍然站起身来。
“噢先生，他不会伤害她吧？”
“不，他伤害不了她，夫人，”阿翁回答，眼中掠过一丝凛冽的寒芒，“因为他根本没有下手的闲暇。鲁珀特正紧咬着他的脚跟不放……还有我。”
“您也要动身？”
“我自然非去不可。学学我的样子，把信任寄托在鲁珀特身上吧。看来我日后少不得要好好酬谢他一番呢。”
“阿拉斯泰尔，看在老天的份上，这一切究竟是怎么回事？”梅里维尔追问道，“鲁珀特自己一瞧见莱昂妮酷似圣维尔的长相，便赌咒发誓说这背后定有天大的阴谋。”
“原来鲁珀特也瞧出来了？看来我以往着实低估了鲁珀特的头脑。我相信自己完全能满足你的好奇心。请随我移步藏书室吧，我亲爱的梅里维尔。”
往日的恩怨嫌隙在这一瞬间烟消云散。安东尼举步走向房门。曼弗斯先生猛地跳了起来。
“可这所有的一切根本帮我找不回马呀！”他悲愤地喊道。
阿翁手搭在门把手上停下了脚步，冷冷回头看他。
“我可敬的先生，”他傲然开口道，“我已经对你的马厌倦透顶了。既然它已物尽其用，事后自当完好奉还。”他与梅里维尔一同走出房间，随手带上了房门。“很好。稍候片刻，安东尼。约翰逊！”
老管家快步上前。
“公爵大人有何吩咐？”

===Original===

“Bid them harness Thunderbolt and Blue Peter to the curricle at once, place my large valise in it, and tell one of the women to pack some clothes for Mistress Léonie. Within half an hour, Johnson.”
“Very good, your Grace,” bowed the old man.
“And now, Merivale, this way.”
“By Gad, you’re a cool devil!” exclaimed Merivale, and followed him to the library.
His Grace went to his desk and extracted from it a brace of gold-mounted pistols.
“Briefly, Anthony, the matter is this: Léonie is Saint-Vire’s daughter.”
“I never knew he had a daughter!”
“No one knew. You thought he had a son, perhaps?”
“Yes. Well, naturally! I’ve seen the boy many times.”
“He is no more Saint-Vire’s son than you are,” said his Grace, snapping the breech of one of his pistols. “His name is Bonnard.”
“Good God, Alastair, do you mean to tell me that Saint-Vire had the audacity to exchange the children? Because of Armand?”
“I am delighted to find that you understand the situation so well,” said the Duke. “I beg you will let it go no further, for the time is not yet.”
“Very well, but what a piece of villainy! Does he know that you know?”
“I had best tell you the whole story,” sighed Avon.
When they at length emerged from the library Merivale’s face was a study of mingled emotions, and he appeared to be speechless. Jennifer met them in the hall.
“You are going, sir? You﻿—you will bring her back?”
“That I cannot say,” Avon replied. “She will be safe with me, my lady.”
Her eyes fell.
“Yes, sir, I feel that that is so.”
His Grace looked at her.
“You surprise me,” he said.
She put her hand out, hesitating.
“She has told me so much. I cannot but be sure of your﻿—kindness.” She paused. “Sir, what﻿—what lies between you and me is past, and should be forgotten.”
His Grace bowed over her hand; his lips were smiling.
“Jenny, if I said that I had forgotten you would be offended.”
“No,” she answered, and a laugh trembled in her voice. “I should be glad.”
“My dear, I desire nothing better than to please you.”
“I think,” she said, “that there is one now who holds a greater place in your heart than ever I held.”
“You err, Jenny. I have no heart,” he replied.
A silence fell. It was broken by a lackey.
“Your Grace, the curricle waits.”
“How will you cross?” Merivale asked.
“In the *Silver Queen*. She lies in Southampton Water. Unless Rupert has already commandeered her.

“立刻吩咐备好双轮轻便双座马车，套上‘霹雳’和‘蓝彼得’，把我的大旅行皮箱装上去，再吩咐一名女仆替莱昂妮小姐收拾几件随身衣物。半个钟头内（30分钟内）必须备妥，约翰逊。”
“遵命，公爵大人，”老仆人躬身领命。
“走吧，梅里维尔，这边请。”
“对着老天发誓，你真他娘是个沉得住气的魔鬼！”梅里维尔惊叹道，随他一同走进藏书室。
公爵大人踱步到书桌前，从抽屉里取出一对精美的金饰手枪。
“长话短说，安东尼，事情的原委是这样的：莱昂妮是圣维尔的亲生骨肉。”
“我从没听说过他居然还有个女儿！”
“全天下无人知晓。想必你以为他膝下唯有一个独子吧？”
“是啊。废话，那是当然！那小男孩我见过无数次了。”
“那个男孩同圣维尔的血缘关系，并不比你多哪怕一厘一毫，”公爵大人说着，啪的一声合上一支手枪的击发机，“那个冒牌货其实姓博纳尔。”
“苍天在上，阿拉斯泰尔，你的意思是说，圣维尔居然胆大包天掉包了亲生骨肉？仅仅是为了防备阿尔芒继承家业？”
“得知你如此通透地洞察了这桩局势，真叫我欣慰，”公爵说，“我恳请你守口如瓶切莫声张，因为摊牌的时机尚未成熟。”
“好吧，可这简直是旷古罕见的丧心病狂！他知道你心知肚明吗？”
“我还是把整件事从头到尾讲给你听吧，”阿翁叹道。
当他们二人终于从藏书室走出来时，梅里维尔脸上写满了复杂难言的震撼神情，整个人仿佛震惊得失了语。珍妮弗在前厅迎上了他们。
“先生，您这就要启程了吗？您……您能把她平安带回来吗？”
“这我眼下尚不敢妄言，”阿翁回答，“但随侍在我身旁，她必定万无一失，夫人。”
珍妮弗垂下眼帘。
“是的，先生，我深信必定如此。”
公爵大人凝视着她。
“你这番话倒让我颇感意外呢，”他说。
她迟疑着伸出手来。
“那孩子对我倾诉了许多。我怎能怀疑您的……仁厚善意。”她停顿了片刻，“先生，以往你我之间的种种纠葛已成过眼云烟，理当就此揭过忘却。”
公爵大人执起她的玉手欠身致敬；唇边挂着优雅的微笑。
“珍妮，要是我说自己早已忘得一干二净，你心头反倒要动怒了。”
“不会的，”她回答，语调中带着轻颤的笑意，“我唯有感到欣慰。”
“亲爱的，我此生唯愿能让你称心如意。”
“我想，”她说，“如今在你心底所占据的分量，早已有一人远远超越了当年的我。”
“你多虑了，珍妮。我这个人从来就没有心肠，”他回答道。
前厅陷入了一片静谧。一名男仆的禀报打破了沉默。
“启禀公爵大人，轻便马车已备妥在门外候命。”
“你打算如何渡海？”梅里维尔问。
“乘‘白银女王号’。她如今正停泊在南安普敦港湾。除非鲁珀特早就抢先一步将她强征了去。

If that should chance to be so, I suppose I must hire a vessel.”

The Duke had donned his great-cloak, and now he picked up his hat and gloves.
“My Lord Merivale will be charmed to assist you,” he said, with the glimmering of a smile. He bowed low to them all, and was gone.
